- **CL-100 (US-42 / T-631, 2026-05-28)** — **Local notifications for newly-published posts: local-vs-push for v1, the recipe-vs-article copy mapping, the toggle-as-single-gate suppression contract, the `userInfo` deep-link payload shape, the foreground-banner choice, and the temporary DEBUG test affordance.** T-630 (US-36 / AC-36.1, CL-62) shipped the Settings "Notify me when new recipes drop" toggle backed by `dod.settings.notificationsEnabled`, explicitly UI-only. T-631 (US-42) is where the toggle does something. This CL records the six decisions behind that wiring. (Renumbered from CL-86 → CL-100 and US-41 → US-42 at the T-631 merge: dad's CloudKit cluster on main owns US-41 / CL-86..CL-99.)

  **(1) Local notifications, NOT Apple Push / APNs (v1).** The candidates were **(a) on-device *local* notifications** (`UNUserNotificationCenter.current().add(_:)` with a `UNTimeIntervalNotificationTrigger`) and **(b) full APNs** (request authorization → `UIApplication.shared.registerForRemoteNotifications()` → device-token handoff to a dutchovendaddy.com WordPress push plugin like OneSignal or WPMobile.app → server fires the payload when a post publishes). The original T-631 stub (under T-630) sketched (b). **Decision: (a) local for v1.** **Why (a):** there is no v1 backend signal for "a new post dropped" — the blog is a stock WordPress install with no push plugin provisioned, and standing one up (plugin selection, APNs key, token storage, opt-in mechanism, payload schema) is a parallel infrastructure decision that does not need to block the *app-side* plumbing. Local notifications let us land the entire device-side contract now — authorization request, the type-aware content builder, the `userInfo` deep-link payload, the tap-routing delegate, and the foreground-banner presentation — all of which a future push task reuses verbatim (the only thing push changes is *who* calls `scheduleNewPostNotification` and *where the trigger comes from*; the content + routing are identical). **Why NOT (b) now:** it couples the app PR to a backend/plugin decision and an APNs entitlement that aren't ready, and it can't be exercised in the simulator without a live push server. The real-world publish trigger is a deferred push follow-up; this CL/task scopes the local plumbing + a temporary test affordance to drive it. **Authorization options:** `[.alert, .sound]` (no `.badge` — v1 has no badge-count source, matching the no-badge posture elsewhere).

  **(2) Type-aware copy keyed on `PostKind` (US-37 / CL-63).** **Decision: a pure content-builder function takes a `PostKind` + the post title and returns the notification title/body strings.** `.recipe` → title **"New Recipe 🍳"**, body **"\<title\> just dropped — tap to start cooking."**; `.article` → title **"New Article 📖"**, body **"\<title\> is up — tap to read."** **Why a pure function:** the copy mapping is the part worth unit-testing (AC-42.2) and it should have zero dependency on `UNUserNotificationCenter` so the L1 suite asserts the exact strings for each kind without scheduling anything. The builder lives in `DODFeatureFeed` (alongside the toggle's `SettingsViewModel`, so the feature package's test target covers it); the `UNUserNotificationCenter` wrapper + delegate live in the app target (composition root) because they touch `UIApplication`/delegate lifecycle. The emoji are part of the spec'd copy (they read as friendly + scannable in Notification Center) and are interpolation-safe.

  **(3) The toggle is the single gate — off ⇒ no notification, ever.** **Decision: `NotificationService.scheduleNewPostNotification(...)` reads `dod.settings.notificationsEnabled` (and the live `UNUserNotificationCenter` authorization status) and short-circuits — scheduling nothing — when either is off/denied.** There is no second path that can post a notification; the test affordance (decision 6) goes through the same `scheduleNewPostNotification`, so toggling off suppresses the test button too (AC-42.4 / AC-42.6). **Why a hard gate at the scheduler:** it makes "off means silence" an invariant of the one choke-point rather than a property each call site must remember to honor — the suppression is unit-tested (AC-42.4) by asserting that with the flag off, a stub scheduler records zero requests. **Why not gate only in the UI:** a UI-only gate would leak if any future caller scheduled directly; gating in the service is the safe default.

  **(4) `userInfo` deep-link payload reuses the existing widget/Spotlight grammar.** **Decision: each `UNMutableNotificationContent.userInfo` carries the deep-link URL string under a stable key (`"dod.deeplink"`): `dod://recipe/<id>` for `.recipe`, `dod://article/<id>` for `.article`.** The tap delegate (`userNotificationCenter(_:didReceive:)`) reads that string, builds a `URL`, and routes it the same way `RootView.onOpenURL` already does — `WidgetDeepLinkParser.parse` for recipe URLs (REG-10 / US-9), falling through to the app's deep-link handling. **Why reuse the grammar:** notifications introduce **no new URL vocabulary** — a tapped notification opens a post by exactly the same contract a widget tap or a Spotlight result does, so there is one routing path to maintain and test. (`dod://article/<id>` for the article kind follows the same host/path shape; the recipe parser already covers `dod://recipe/<id>`, and the article route degrades gracefully to opening the post by id through the same dispatcher.) The payload shape is unit-tested (the builder's `userInfo` carries the expected URL string per kind).

  **(5) Foreground banner via the delegate.** **Decision: `userNotificationCenter(_:willPresent:)` returns `[.banner, .sound, .list]` so a notification firing while the app is foregrounded still shows a banner.** **Why:** without the `willPresent` opt-in, iOS silently swallows foreground notifications — which would make the simulator test (app open, tap "Simulate New Post") appear broken. The banner makes the behavior observable whether the app is background or foreground.

  **(6) Temporary DEBUG "Simulate New Post" test affordance.** **Decision: a `#if DEBUG`-wrapped button labeled "▸ Test: Simulate New Post" directly below the notifications toggle in `SettingsView`, gated by the toggle (decision 3), that fires two sample local notifications ~2s apart** — an **article** ("Best Dutch Oven Recipes (30+ Tried and Tested Favorites)", `.article`, the May-25-2026 real-roundup scenario) and a **recipe** ("Cast Iron Burgers (Easy Skillet Recipe)", `.recipe`), each with a plausible WP post id in its `dod://…/<id>` payload. **Why a temporary test button:** v1 has no server to fire the real "new post" trigger (decision 1), so the only way to exercise the end-to-end path (schedule → fire → banner → tap → deep-link) in the simulator is a manual trigger. It is `#if DEBUG`-gated so it never ships in a release build and is clearly labeled temporary; it is removed (or replaced by the real publish trigger) when the push follow-up lands. The two-kinds-2s-apart design demonstrates both copy variants (decision 2) and both deep-link payloads (decision 4) in one tap.

  **(7) No new analytics; constitution §9 note.** **Decision: T-631 adds NO new `AnalyticsEvent` case (no `notificationOpened` event in v1).** Local notifications are a device-local, system-presented affordance — like the Cook Mode idle-timer and the Live Activity payload, they carry no data off-device and are not a tracked event. Constitution §9 gains only a **clarifying note** (not an allowlist addition) that device-local notifications send nothing to TelemetryDeck and leave the App Privacy questionnaire unchanged. **Why no event:** there is no adoption signal worth the privacy-surface cost in v1, and the change stays bounded (no questionnaire update). A future task that wants a `notificationOpened` signal amends the §9 allowlist then. **Spec/trace:** US-42 / AC-42.1..AC-42.6, consuming `PostKind` (US-37 / CL-63), the `dod://` deep-link grammar (US-9 / `WidgetDeepLinkParser` / REG-10), `DeepLinkDispatcher` + `RootView.handle(...)` (US-10), and the `dod.settings.notificationsEnabled` flag (US-36 / AC-36.1 / CL-62). Implementing PR: T-631 (`feat/T-631-local-notifications`). Touches the composition root + deep-link routing → the PR carries the `e2e` label.

- **CL-101 (US-42 / T-632, 2026-05-29)** — **Notification deep-links must fetch-on-cache-miss, not resolve cache-only: the confirmed T-631 bug, the fetch-on-miss fix, the article-vs-recipe routing via the detail screen's existing classification, and the real-test-id swap.** T-631 (CL-100) shipped the local-notification plumbing end-to-end **except** the tap-to-open actually opened nothing for the feature's real use case. This CL records the root cause + the fix.

  **The confirmed root cause: cache-only resolution.** The notification tap delegate routes through `DeepLinkDispatcher` → `RootView.handle(intent:)` → `resolveRecipeRoute(id:autoStartCookMode:)`, which did a single cache-only lookup: `guard let recipe = try? await store.recipeWithoutTouching(id: id) else { DODLog.app.error("deep link: recipe \(id) not in cache, ignoring"); return nil }`. That is **correct** for the surfaces it was written for — widget (US-9) and Spotlight (US-10) deep links point at recipes the user has already seen, so they are in the cache by construction. But **US-42 notifications announce newly-published posts**, which are *never* in the cache (the user hasn't opened them yet — that's the whole point of being notified). So every notification deep-link hit the `else` branch and silently no-op'd. The feature looked wired (banner fired, tap registered) but opened nothing — broken for its only real use case.

  **(1) The fix: fetch on cache-miss (additive — the cache-hit path is untouched).** **Decision: when `recipeWithoutTouching(id:)` misses, fetch the post from the WP REST API by id (`WPRestClient.post(id:)` → `RecipeListItem`), then route to recipe-detail — mirroring how a normal feed-row tap hydrates an uncached recipe.** `WPRestClient.post(id:)` is a new single-post endpoint (`GET /wp/v2/posts/<id>?_embed=wp:featuredmedia`) projecting the post to a `RecipeListItem` carrying `canonicalURL` + inline hero — the same projection `posts()` already uses for list rows. The detail screen then runs the existing JSON-LD-parse / article-classify fetch path (AC-4.11 / AC-37.2) to hydrate. **Cache-first stays:** only a miss triggers a fetch; a hit returns the route with no network call and no recently-viewed-LRU touch, so widget/Spotlight behavior (and REG-10) is unchanged. **Why reuse `WPRestClient.post(id:)` + the detail fetch instead of reinventing:** the recipe-detail open is already the canonical "hydrate an uncached post by URL" path; the deep-link resolver just needs the post's `canonicalURL`, which the by-id fetch supplies, after which the detail screen does the rest. **Graceful fallback preserved:** if the fetch fails (post truly deleted, or the JSON-LD + article-body extraction both fail per CL-9 / AC-4.11), the resolver still returns `nil` ("ignore") — now logged distinctly as `deep link: recipe \(id) fetch failed` so a fetch failure is diagnosable separately from a plain cache miss.

  **(2) Articles route through the same `.recipe(item:)` case — PostKind is honored by the detail screen, not a new route.** **Decision: a post without parseable Recipe JSON-LD is an article (PostKind `.article`, US-37 / CL-63 / T-640), and it resolves through the *same* `RecipeRoute.recipe(item:)` the recipe path uses — `RecipeDetailViewModel.fetchAndParse()` classifies it and transitions to the `.article` load state, which `RecipeDetailView` renders via `ArticleDetailView`.** So tapping an article notification opens the article detail, not "ignore" and not a broken recipe-shaped screen. **Why no separate article route case:** `RecipeRoute` has only `.recipe` / `.category`; the recipe-vs-article distinction already lives entirely in the detail screen's fetch path (T-640 made the list-row presentation identical for both kinds — see CL-63 decision 3). Adding an `.article` route case would force every push site to discriminate a difference that only matters one screen deeper. Routing the article's `RecipeListItem` (with its `canonicalURL`) to `.recipe(item:)` *is* the article-aware route — the detail VM does the honoring. The resolution policy is lifted out of the SwiftUI `View` into a standalone `RecipeRouteResolver` (two injected I/O edges: cache lookup + remote fetch) so it is unit-testable without a host — the L1 suite drives cached-hit (no fetch), uncached-miss (fetch + route), uncached-article (fetch resolves an article → still a `.recipe(item:)` route carrying the article's post), and fetch-failure (→ `nil`).

  **(3) The real-test-id swap.** **Decision: the DEBUG "Simulate New Post" affordance fires *real* WP post ids so the now-live fetch path has a real target.** CL-100 decision 6 used plausible-but-placeholder ids (article 18342, recipe 17615 / "Cast Iron Burgers"). With cache-only resolution those ids never mattered — the tap no-op'd before any fetch. Now that the tap fetches, the ids must resolve on the live blog. **Article → id `23406`** ("Best Dutch Oven Recipes (30+ Tried and Tested Favorites)" — a real roundup post with no parseable Recipe JSON-LD, so it resolves as an article and opens `ArticleDetailView`). **Recipe → id `21238`, title "Garlic Butter Skillet Corn (Easy 15-Minute Side Dish)"** — the fictional **"Cast Iron Burgers (Easy Skillet Recipe)" does not exist on the blog**, so it is swapped for this real recipe (the same one the `WPRestClientPostsTests` fixture already uses) and the title string is updated to match. Both ids are verified to exist via `GET /wp/v2/posts/<id>` at PR time. **Why this matters:** the simulator test is the only way to exercise schedule → fire → banner → tap → fetch → detail in v1 (no server trigger), so the test ids being *real* is load-bearing for the manual verification.

  **Spec/trace:** REG-20 (the fetch-on-miss + article-routing regression pin), US-42 / AC-42.6 (the DEBUG affordance now fires real ids), AC-4.11 + AC-37.2 (the recipe-vs-article classification the detail screen runs), CL-9 (the graceful "ignore" fallback on a truly-missing post), REG-10 (cache-hit path + LRU untouched). Implementing PR: T-632 (`fix/T-632-notification-deeplink-fetch`). Touches the composition root + deep-link routing → the PR carries the `e2e` label.

- **CL-103 (US-34 / T-634, 2026-05-29)** — **Recipe card long-press context menu becomes state-aware: a saved card shows "Unsave" with the outline `bookmark` glyph; an unsaved card shows "Save" with `bookmark.fill`. Reverses CL-60 decision (2)'s "no Unsave branch in v1".** Round-7's CL-60 deferred the per-row `isSaved` query on the deferral cost basis: Feed/Categories/Search viewmodels don't carry a saved-IDs observation surface, so always-"Save" was the lowest-friction shape. Owner feedback in round 9 reframes the trade-off: the Saved tab is the high-value surface (every card IS saved by definition — zero plumbing cost there to know `isSaved == true`), and long-pressing a saved card to see "Save" reads as broken even though the underlying toggle works. **Decision: extend `recipeCardContextMenu` with an `isSaved: Bool` parameter and rename the closure to `onToggle` to reflect that the action toggles in both directions.** Helper body branches the `Label` text and `systemImage`: `isSaved ? "Unsave" : "Save"` paired with `isSaved ? "bookmark" : "bookmark.fill"`. **Call-site resolution.** **(a) Saved tab:** `isSaved: true` constant — every card in `SavedView`'s `LazyVGrid` is by definition saved (the tab's source is `viewModel.recipes`, which is `RecipeStore.savedRecipes()`). Zero plumbing cost. **(b) Categories, Feed, Search:** `isSaved: false` constant with a TODO marker — `RecipeListItem` has no `isSaved` field (it's a Domain DTO from the WP REST `Post.toRecipeListItem(...)` mapping), and no feature viewmodel currently maintains a `Set<Int>` of saved IDs hydrated alongside the result set. Wiring the per-card query would require either (i) extending `RecipeListItem` with an `isSaved: Bool` field populated by a store lookup, (ii) per-row `task { isSaved = try await dependencies.isSaved(id:) }` on appear with mutation observation, or (iii) viewmodel-owned saved-IDs sets hydrated on appear with notification-driven refresh. All three are non-trivial; CL-60's path-(c) framing remains the right longer-term answer if user feedback surfaces the asymmetry on these surfaces. T-634 ships the high-value Saved-tab fix and leaves a clearly-marked TODO at the other three call sites so the follow-up is easy to find. **Toggle semantics confirmed.** `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` already toggles in both directions (`Packages/DODPersistence/Sources/DODPersistence/RecipeStore.swift:152` — `row.isSaved.toggle()`). The composition-root callback in `TabStack.saveFromCard(item:store:)` routes through the same seam, so tapping "Unsave" in the Saved tab's context menu correctly transitions the row to `isSaved == false` (and the LRU eviction path may then trim it per the existing `toggleSaved` contract). No store-side change required. **What CL-103 does NOT touch:** `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` (no signature/behavior change — it already toggles), `RecipeStore.isSaved(id:)` (already exists but is not used at the new call sites in this CL), the `recipeCardTap` modifier (REG-DOD-LIST-SCROLL preserved — `.contextMenu` is still gesture-distinct from `.onTapGesture`), the `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher.publish()` shape (the same publish-on-save call fires for both save and unsave transitions — the saved-recipes widget snapshot regenerates correctly either way), any analytics event (no new event; the existing `recipeSaved` / `recipeUnsaved` events still fire from `RecipeDetailViewModel.toggleSaved()` on the detail-screen path — the card-context-menu path remains telemetry-free per CL-60's same out-of-scope framing), `RecipeListItem` / `Recipe` Domain types (no new `isSaved` field — Feed/Categories/Search keep the TODO marker rather than threading state through the DTO). **Considered:** thread `isSaved` through `RecipeListItem` (CL-60's option-a). Rejected for T-634 — the DTO is sourced from network responses and threading a persistence-state field into a network-shaped type inverts the layering. **Considered:** add per-card `task { isSaved = ... }` on Feed/Categories/Search (CL-60's option-b). Rejected for T-634 — high allocation churn (N concurrent tasks per visible page), no mutation-observation seam in v1, and the value at non-Saved surfaces is lower than at the Saved tab. **Considered:** viewmodel-owned saved-IDs sets (CL-60's option-c — still the right longer-term answer). Deferred — T-634 is a < 20-minute polish task and option-c needs viewmodel surgery on three feature modules; the Saved-tab win lands now and the option-c follow-up can pick up the TODOs later. **Considered:** keep "Save" + `bookmark.fill` everywhere and call it done. Rejected — that is exactly the CL-60 status quo this CL reverses. **Considered:** swap the icon convention (`bookmark.fill` for saved, `bookmark` for unsaved) to match `RecipeDetailView.swift:261`'s `viewModel.isSaved ? "bookmark.fill" : "bookmark"` mapping. Rejected — the menu's `Label` is the *action* (the user is about to perform "Save" or "Unsave"), so the action-icon convention here is "show the icon that represents the destination state" — `bookmark.fill` for the save action, `bookmark` (outline) for the unsave action. The detail-screen nav-bar icon is the *current-state indicator* (a different convention — it shows the state the recipe is currently in), so the two surfaces intentionally diverge on icon polarity. **Spec/trace:** US-34 / AC-34.4 amended in `spec.md` (the always-"Save" line is struck through with a CL-103 marker + a new AC adds the state-aware contract); CL-60 decision (2) struck through with a "Reversed by CL-103 / T-634, 2026-05-29" lineage marker (decision (1) preserved unchanged). Implementing PR: T-634 (`fix/T-634-context-menu-unsave`). Single helper signature change + 4 call sites + 1 new L1 test in `DODDesignSystemTests` + spec-text amendments; no composition-root edit, no protocol surface change, no `e2e` label.

- **CL-102 (US-42 / T-633, 2026-05-29)** — **The temporary DEBUG "Simulate New Post" test affordance is removed now that the end-to-end path is verified; notification copy is em-dash-free per the owner's request.** Two small follow-ups to the T-631/T-632 notifications work. **(1) Remove the test scaffold.** CL-100 decision 6 added a `#if DEBUG` "▸ Test: Simulate New Post" button in `SettingsView` (wired through `FeedView` → `TabStack` → `NotificationService.simulateNewPosts()`) so QA could fire both notification kinds in the simulator — v1 has no server "new post" trigger (CL-100 decision 1). CL-101 decision 3 then swapped its placeholder ids for real ones (article `23406`, recipe `21238`) so the now-live fetch-on-miss path (REG-20) had real targets. **Decision: with schedule → fire → banner → tap → fetch → detail confirmed working in the simulator, the scaffold is deleted** — the button, its `onSimulateNewPosts` closure plumbing (`SettingsView`/`FeedView`/`TabStack`), and `NotificationService.simulateNewPosts()`. The real `scheduleNewPostNotification(title:postKind:recipeID:)` API is **unchanged** and remains the seam the deferred push follow-up reuses; AC-42.4's single suppression gate is untouched (it just no longer has a DEBUG caller). **Why now:** the affordance was always labeled temporary and `#if DEBUG`-gated; keeping dead scaffolding around invites drift (e.g. the placeholder-id bug CL-101 had to correct). **(2) Em-dash-free copy.** **Decision: the AC-42.2 notification bodies use a period + space instead of " — " (em dash):** recipe body **"\<postTitle\> just dropped. Tap to start cooking."** and article body **"\<postTitle\> is up. Tap to read."** Titles are unchanged. **Why:** owner request for plain, period-separated sentences; no em or en dashes anywhere in the user-facing notification copy. (The post title is still interpolated verbatim, so a title that itself contains a dash is passed through unaltered — that is the caller's content, not our copy.) **Spec/trace:** US-42 / AC-42.6 (struck through in `spec.md` with a "removed in T-633" note), AC-42.2 (copy amended in place), AC-42.4 (gate note trimmed). Implementing PR: T-633 (`fix/T-633-remove-test-button`). Deletion + copy only — does not touch the composition root's deep-link routing meaningfully, so **no `e2e` label**.

- **CL-105 (US-29 / US-33 / T-636, 2026-05-29)** — **Search-tab simplification + icon polish bundle: the "All categories" and "Recently viewed" filter chips are removed (duplicative of Categories tab + Recent searches section), the idle "Try" pill glyph swaps from `tag.fill` to `magnifyingglass` (it's a tap-to-search affordance, not a category tag), and the long-press "Clear" context-menu trash icon is explicitly tinted red (`.tint(.red)`) so the SF Symbol stops inheriting the orange ancestral accent and matches the destructive title color.** Four small Search-tab polish items bundled into one PR because each touches `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/` (`SearchView.swift` or `IdleSuggestionsView.swift`) and the snapshot baseline churn is shared.

  **(1) "Try" suggestion pill glyph: `tag.fill` → `magnifyingglass`.** **Decision: `IdleSuggestionsView.body`'s "Try" section pill uses `systemImage: "magnifyingglass"` instead of `systemImage: "tag.fill"`.** Per CL-49 / AC-29.1 (T-500), tapping a "Try" pill no longer sets `filters.categoryID` — it populates the search field with the category name and runs a normal REST text query via `viewModel.selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)`. The visual `tag.fill` glyph was a leftover from the pre-CL-49 path when the pill *was* a category filter setter; today the action is "perform a search," so the glyph should communicate "search" rather than "tag a category." `magnifyingglass` is already the search-field glyph at the top of the screen (`SearchView.searchField`), and reusing the symbol reinforces the visual association — "tap a pill, it goes into the search field, the magnifyingglass icon is the same as the field's." The pill's text, accessibility label, surface-elevated capsule, and tap behavior are unchanged.

  **(2) Remove the "All categories" filter chip from `FilterChipRow`.** **Decision: `FilterChipRow.categoryChip` (the `Menu`-backed chip that listed every WP category and was used to filter results by category ID) is removed entirely from the `FilterChipRow` HStack, and the `categoryChip` computed property along with the unused `selectedCategoryName` helper and the now-unused `categories` field on `FilterChipRow` are deleted.** **Why:** the Categories tab in the bottom nav is the canonical "browse all categories" surface, and per CL-49.4 (T-500) the in-menu "All categories" row was already recognized as redundant against that tab. Round-9 owner review reframes the trade-off: if "All categories" inside the menu was redundant, the entire chip surface (which is the only place inside Search to scope to one category) is also redundant — the Categories tab covers picking-a-category-then-searching, and within the Search tab the dominant intent is text-query + cook-time. The chip was rarely used and visually crowded the row. **`filters.categoryID` is retained on the `SearchFilters` model** (defaults to `nil`, always the "all categories" pipeline path because no UI mutates it) so the search merge logic (`SearchFilters.apply(...)`, `SearchResultMerger.merge(...)`, `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` flow) stays byte-identical — removing the model field would ripple into the merger and the L1 `SearchFiltersTests.categoryFilterTreatsUnknownAsMiss` and `SearchResultMergerTests.composeCategoryAndCookTimeFiltersForFreshRESTResults` (REG-17) contracts, which is out of scope for a polish task. CL-53's REG-17 wire-data round-trip is preserved unchanged: the `categories` field still arrives on `WPDTO.Post`, still maps into `RecipeListItem.categoryIDs`, still round-trips through `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)`. The data infrastructure stays live; only the UI affordance to mutate `filters.categoryID` is gone.

  **(3) Remove the "Recently viewed" toggle chip from `FilterChipRow`.** **Decision: `FilterChipRow.recentlyViewedChip` (the toggle that flipped `filters.recentlyViewedOnly`) is removed entirely from the HStack and the `recentlyViewedChip` computed property is deleted.** **Why:** the Recent searches section in `IdleSuggestionsView.recentsSection` (US-29 / AC-29.2 / CL-49.2 — the "Recent" pills above the "Try" section) already surfaces the user's recent activity. The "Recently viewed" filter chip is a different semantic ("show me results that match this query AND that I've opened recently") but the dominant intent it served was "go back to a recent thing," which the Recent section covers more directly. The toggle was rarely used and visually competed with the Recent section for the same affordance space. **`filters.recentlyViewedOnly` is retained on the `SearchFilters` model** (defaults to `false`, the no-op filter path because no UI mutates it) for the same reason `filters.categoryID` is retained: removing the field would ripple into `SearchFilters.apply(...)` and the `recentlyViewedIDs` parameter the merger threads in. The data plumbing (`RecipeStore.recentlyViewedRecipeIDs(...)` from the cache's `lastViewedAt` index) stays live; only the UI to flip the filter is gone.

  **(4) Destructive context-menu trash icon tinted red.** **Decision: the `Button(role: .destructive)` inside `IdleSuggestionsView.recentsSection`'s per-pill `.contextMenu` gets `.tint(.red)` applied on the Button, so both the SF Symbol trash glyph and the "Clear" label render in red.** **Why:** the pre-T-636 rendering had the trash icon orange (inheriting `DODColor.accent` via the app-level `.tint(DODColor.accent)` from `RootView`, which is the same path that gives "Clear All" its CL-57 brand-orange treatment per US-33 / AC-33.1) while the "Clear" label text rendered red (SwiftUI's automatic destructive-role treatment), so the menu read as visually mismatched. SwiftUI's `role: .destructive` tints the title text red but the SF Symbol icon inherits the ancestral tint (the orange brand accent). Forcing `.tint(.red)` on the Button cleanly colors both — `tint` overrides the inherited app-level accent at the button scope and `role: .destructive` still pulls red for the label text, producing a uniformly-red destructive menu item. **Why not `.foregroundStyle(.red)` on the Label:** `tint` is the semantically correct lever for SF Symbol tinting in menus and it composes with `role: .destructive` rather than overriding it; `foregroundStyle` would override the role's automatic styling and risk fighting the system in future iOS versions. `tint` is the minimal-risk SwiftUI-idiomatic fix.

  **What CL-105 does NOT touch:** `SearchFilters.categoryID` / `SearchFilters.recentlyViewedOnly` (retained on the model with default values so the search pipeline stays unchanged — CL-53 / REG-17's category data round-trip is preserved), `SearchFilters.apply(...)` (still composes AND-wise across all three slices — the two retained slices just always evaluate to "no filter" because nothing mutates them), `SearchResultMerger.merge(...)` (ranking + dedupe unchanged), `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` flow (the REST + local + merge + filter chain is byte-identical because the filters default to no-op), the 300ms debounce (AC-3.1 still locks it), the telemetry hash contract (AC-3.6 still locks the SHA-256 hash), `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` (CL-53's wire-data round-trip stays live), `RecipeStore.recentlyViewedRecipeIDs(...)` (the cache's `lastViewedAt` index stays live — the data is still computed; just not consumed by a chip toggle), the "Clear All" affordance in the Recent section header (US-33 / AC-33.1's brand-orange `DODColor.accent` treatment is preserved), the per-term `.contextMenu` Clear behavior (US-33 / AC-33.2 / AC-33.3 — the menu still fires `removeRecentSearch(_:)`; only the icon tint changes), the Categories-tab bottom-nav entry (the canonical "browse all categories" surface stays the canonical surface), the cook-time chip (the only remaining filter chip — visually fine as a single-chip row for v1; if the lone-chip layout looks weird in production a future polish task can revisit, but no change in T-636).

  **Considered: delete `filters.categoryID` and `filters.recentlyViewedOnly` from `SearchFilters` entirely.** Rejected — that ripples into the `SearchFilters.apply(...)` signature, the merger's `recentlyViewedIDs` parameter, the REG-17 round-trip contract, and the L1 `SearchFiltersTests` and `SearchResultMergerTests` suites. Polish tasks should not invalidate test contracts that pin behavior the merger still needs to support if the chips ever come back. **Considered: keep the "All categories" chip but route it to a deep-link to the Categories tab instead of a Menu.** Rejected — that's a US-12 / AC-12.2 contract change (the chip-row affordance is documented) and a new navigation pattern the user didn't ask for; if the Categories tab is the answer, the chip is just clutter pointing at it. **Considered: keep the "Recently viewed" chip but rename it to clarify its semantic vs. the Recent section.** Rejected — same clutter argument; the data layer stays available if a future story brings the affordance back, but for v1 the Recent section is enough. **Considered: swap the trash icon to `.foregroundStyle(.red)` on the `Label`.** Rejected per decision (4)'s rationale — `tint` composes with `role: .destructive`, `foregroundStyle` overrides it. **Considered: remove the entire `FilterChipRow` since only `cookTimeChip` remains.** Rejected — the cook-time filter is the single most-used Search filter per the round-6/round-9 owner feedback that motivated this CL (it's why the chip stays), and removing the row entirely would require moving the cook-time affordance somewhere else (the toolbar? the searchfield?), which is a US-12 / AC-12.2 contract change. A one-chip row reads fine.

  **Spec/trace:** US-29 / AC-29.4 amended in `spec.md` — strike-through the "All categories menu-row removal" line with a "superseded by CL-105 / T-636: the entire category chip is now removed" lineage marker (the menu-row-removal sub-decision is moot now that the whole chip is gone); US-12 / AC-12.2 amended in place — strike-through the "Category … chip" and "Recently viewed toggle" mentions in the chip-row enumeration with CL-105 lineage markers (the cook-time chip stays, and the model fields stay for forward compatibility); US-33 amended in place — a new AC-33.5 records the `.tint(.red)` destructive-icon contract; CL-49.4 preserved unchanged (its menu-row sub-decision is now mooted by CL-105 but its rationale is the foundation CL-105 builds on); CL-53 / REG-17 preserved unchanged (the wire-data round-trip stays live). Implementing PR: T-636 (`fix/T-636-search-polish`). Two source files + one snapshot test file repurposed + 8 stale baseline PNGs deleted (for re-record) + spec-text amendments; no composition-root edit, no protocol surface change, no `e2e` label.

- **CL-106 (US-12 / US-29 / T-637, 2026-05-29)** — **Three-part Search page fix bundle: (1) the `FilterChipRow` is hidden during the `.idle` state so the chip row doesn't crowd the "Try" / "Recent" idle-suggestions layout; (2) the cook-time filter now hydrates `totalSeconds` from the network on cache miss so the filter actually narrows uncached REST results (the pre-fix `SearchViewModel` populated `lastTotalSecondsByRecipe` from `RecipeStore.totalSeconds(forRecipeIDs:)` which is cache-only, so most search results — being uncached — fell through the "missing = MISS" rule and were filtered out entirely); (3) the "Latest Recipes" Try-pill is special-cased to fetch the N most-recent recipes via `client.posts(page: 1)` instead of running a literal text search for the string "Latest Recipes" (which returned garbage because "Latest Recipes" is a real WP category — id 1590, slug `latest-recipes` — that appears in the top-categories list, and the pre-fix path passed the name through `selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)` which fires the normal REST search path).** Bundled as one PR because each fix lives in `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/` (`SearchView.swift` for the chip gating, `SearchViewModel.swift` + `SearchDependencies.swift` for the cook-time hydration and Latest-Recipes branch).

  **(1) Hide `FilterChipRow` until a search is active.** **Decision: `SearchView.body`'s `VStack` only renders `FilterChipRow(filters: $viewModel.filters)` when `viewModel.state != .idle` (i.e. one of `.searching`, `.results`, `.noResults`, `.offline`).** **Why:** the idle state already renders the "Try" / "Recent" suggestions layout in `IdleSuggestionsView` — adding a filter chip row above that surface is visually noisy (the user hasn't entered a query yet, so there's nothing to filter), and the previous behavior of always rendering the chip row crowded the idle layout. The chip stays available the moment a search is in flight so the user can refine narrowing while results are coming back. No model change; just a `@ViewBuilder` gate around the row.

  **(2) Cook-time filter hydrates `totalSeconds` from the network on cache miss.** **Diagnosis (`SearchFilters.apply` is correct — it applies `cookTime.contains(totalSeconds:)` and treats absent map entries as MISS per AC-12.3's documented contract). The bug is the `SearchViewModel.lastTotalSecondsByRecipe` population: `dependencies.totalSeconds(forRecipeIDs:)` routes to `RecipeStore.totalSeconds(forRecipeIDs:)` which is a cache-only `FetchDescriptor<CachedRecipe>` query. For most fresh search results the user has never opened the recipe detail, so the cache lacks `CachedRecipe.totalSeconds`, the map comes back empty, and the cook-time filter rejects every uncached row.** **Decision: add `SearchDependencies.fetchTotalSeconds(forRecipeIDs:) async throws -> [Int: Int]` (network path: per-id `RecipePageFetcher` + `JSONLDRecipeParser.parseISO8601Duration` of the JSON-LD `totalTime` field). When the cook-time filter is active and the post-search `lastTotalSecondsByRecipe` map is missing entries for items in the current result set, `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` kicks off a hydration task (`Task { await self.hydrateMissingTotalSeconds() }`) AFTER the initial result render. The hydration task fetches the unknown items (capped at 20 — one page of results, mirroring `WPRestClient.defaultPageSize`), merges the result into `lastTotalSecondsByRecipe`, persists the parsed total time back into `RecipeStore` via the existing `mergeDetail(...)` path so subsequent filter flips don't re-fetch, then calls `reapplyFilters()` so the now-known items either pass or fail the cook-time bucket constraint cleanly.** **Why this approach over WP REST custom field (`wprm_recipe_total_time`):** the WPRM plugin's custom-field exposure varies by install and pulling it requires either `?_embed=wprm` (heavier wire shape) or per-id calls to `/wp-recipe-maker/v1/recipe/<id>` (separate REST surface not currently in the client). The existing JSON-LD fetch path already works for every recipe page (it's what `RecipeDetailViewModel.fetchAndParse()` does on detail open), parses cleanly via `JSONLDRecipeParser.parseISO8601Duration` of the `totalTime` field (the same field that populates `CachedRecipe.totalSeconds` on detail open), and reuses the existing `RecipePageFetcher` + `WPRestClient` plumbing — zero new REST endpoint surface, zero plugin-version risk. **Cap = 20 items (one page).** This bounds the fan-out so a single filter toggle can't hammer the API; if a search returns more than 20 results and >20 are unknown, the top-20 by current rank order get hydrated and the rest stay filtered out (the user can scroll the result set and the chip can be toggled off if they want to see them). The fetch is fire-and-forget — the initial result render is **not blocked** on hydration (we show what we have, then re-apply the filter when hydration lands). **Cache write-back:** each hydrated `Recipe` is passed through `dependencies.mergeDetail(_:)` (mirrors how `RecipeDetailViewModel` persists the parsed detail) so subsequent filter flips for the same recipes hit the L1 cache path without a network round-trip.

  **(3) "Latest Recipes" Try-pill fetches the N most-recent recipes instead of running a literal text search.** **Diagnosis: "Latest Recipes" is a real WP category on `dutchovendaddy.com` — id 1590, slug `latest-recipes` (pinned by `Packages/DODDomain/Tests/DODDomainTests/SubTypeTests.swift:8`). The category appears in `availableCategories` (loaded by `loadCategoriesIfNeeded()`) and surfaces as one of the top-5 by count in `topCategorySuggestions`. The pre-fix `IdleSuggestionsView.onCategoryTap` callback passes `category.name` ("Latest Recipes") straight into `viewModel.selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)`, which runs a normal REST text search via `client.search(query: "Latest Recipes")`. The WP REST `search=` parameter does a fulltext match against post title + content; "Latest Recipes" as a phrase happens to be in the boilerplate of many roundup-style article posts, so the results are a near-random scatter of unrelated articles. Same shape would happen for any category whose name is a generic English phrase. The 1590 id is the load-bearing signal — the WP category exists, but the user's intent on tapping the pill is "show me what's new," not "search the literal string."** **Decision: special-case the tap in `IdleSuggestionsView.onCategoryTap`. When the tapped category matches the "Latest Recipes" pill (case-insensitive name match against `"Latest Recipes"` OR id match against `1590` — case-insensitive name match is the canonical check because it's robust against future renames of the WP category, and the id check is a belt-and-suspenders fallback in case the name drifts), the view-model branches to a new `SearchViewModel.surfaceLatestRecipes(limit:)` async method that fetches the N most-recent posts via `dependencies.fetchLatestRecipes(limit:)`. The dependency routes to `WPRestClient.posts(page: 1, perPage: limit)` — the existing date-desc-by-default endpoint. The viewmodel renders the result set directly (sets `items`, transitions `state = .results`), does NOT call `recents.record(...)` (mirroring the `queryFromCuratedTap` exclusion path), and tags itself with a new `lastSurface = .latestRecipes` flag so a subsequent filter mutation correctly re-renders against the latest-recipes set rather than running `reapplyFilters()` against an empty `lastQuery`.** **Limit = 5** (one screen of cards is plenty for "newest and maybe a few more recent" — the user's framing). **Over-fetch and drop articles.** Per `WPRestClient.search(...)`'s wire shape, post responses don't carry the `PostKind` discriminator (it's a JSON-LD parse outcome, not a REST field). We can't filter articles at the REST boundary. **Decision: fetch `ceil(limit * 1.5)` posts (so 8 for `limit: 5`), and drop articles in the viewmodel using the existing `CachedRecipe.jsonLDFailedAt`-derived `PostKind` if the recipe is already in the cache; for uncached fresh-fetch posts, surface them as-is** (we don't know the kind yet — same shape the rest of the feed has always used; the detail-screen path classifies on open per US-37 / AC-37.2 / T-640). The over-fetch is the cheap safeguard for the most common case (one or two of the latest posts being articles); in the rare case where 4+ of the latest 8 are articles, the user sees a shorter list — preferable to running a literal text search. **What "Latest Recipes" looks like in the UI.** Visually identical to the other Try pills — same `magnifyingglass` icon (per CL-105 decision (1)), same surface-elevated capsule, same accessibility label. Only the tap behavior changes. **`recentSearches` exclusion.** The Latest-Recipes path NEVER writes to the recent-searches store (no `recents.record(...)` call) — mirrors the curated-tap exclusion from REG-19 / CL-66 / T-670 (the user did not type the term; persisting a category name as a recent would surface the term on future idle renders and "Clear All" would not actually clear them). The viewmodel sets `lastSurface = .latestRecipes` explicitly so the existing `queryFromCuratedTap`-driven exclusion logic in `performSearch()` is not re-used (no `performSearch()` call happens at all on the Latest-Recipes branch — the fetch and render are direct).

  **What CL-106 does NOT touch:** `SearchFilters.apply(...)` (correct as-is — the "missing = MISS" rule still holds; what changes is that the missing-set is now hydrated when the cook-time filter is active), `SearchResultMerger.merge(...)` (unchanged — the Latest-Recipes path skips the merger entirely because it's not a text query; it goes straight from REST → `items`), the 300ms debounce (AC-3.1 still locks the typed-search path; the Latest-Recipes tap fires a one-shot fetch with no debounce because there's no query to debounce against), the telemetry hash contract (AC-3.6 — the Latest-Recipes path does NOT call `sendSearchTelemetry(queryHash:)` because it's not a text query and there's no hash to send; the curated category pills that DO route through `selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)` still send the hash per the existing contract), `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` (CL-53 / REG-17 wire-data round-trip preserved — the Latest-Recipes fetch result is still cached via `dependencies.cache(listItems:)` like any other REST hit, so the `RecipeListItem.categoryIDs` round-trip stays live), `RecipeStore.totalSeconds(forRecipeIDs:)` (cache-only — unchanged; the new `fetchTotalSeconds(...)` is the network-side companion, called only when the cache returns less than full coverage AND the cook-time filter is active), the curated-tap exclusion logic for normal Try pills (REG-19 / CL-66 / T-670 preserved — `selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)` still does NOT persist a Recent; the new Latest-Recipes branch is independent and also does not persist), the snapshot test re-record cadence (the chip-gating change shifts the idle-state `SearchView` baseline; `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` is unaffected because it locks the chip row in isolation — the row still exists and renders identically when shown), `LiveSearchDependencies` shape (one new method `fetchTotalSeconds(...)` + one new method `fetchLatestRecipes(...)` are added — additive surface, no breaking changes), `LiveSearchDependencies` composition wiring (no new dependency — both methods use the existing `client: WPRestClient` and reuse `RecipePageFetcher` indirectly via a new internal helper; the existing `init(client:store:monitor:)` signature is preserved).

  **Considered: WP REST custom field via `?_embed=wprm` or `/wp-recipe-maker/v1/recipe/<id>`.** Rejected — pulls in WPRM plugin-version risk (the field may not be registered on every install / may rename across plugin upgrades), requires a new REST client extension, and doesn't reuse the existing JSON-LD parse path that already works. The HTML+JSON-LD approach is heavier per-request than a pure REST `?_fields=` slice but bounds the cap at 20 and reuses zero-risk infrastructure. **Considered: pre-fetch cook times for every search result, regardless of whether the cook-time filter is active.** Rejected — runs 20 page fetches on every search, even when the user doesn't need cook-time data. The cook-time filter is the trigger so the cost is paid only when the user opts in. **Considered: hydrate synchronously before the initial render (block on hydration).** Rejected — delays the result render by up to 20 sequential page fetches' worth of time; the user sees a long "Searching…" spinner. The fire-and-forget pattern is the right shape (render what we have, narrow when hydration lands). **Considered: cap hydration at the visible page (10 items if the user hasn't scrolled).** Rejected for v1 — the result `LazyVGrid` doesn't expose a "what's currently visible" hook in a clean way, and the 20-item cap (one REST page) is a simple, defensible bound that matches the existing `WPRestClient.defaultPageSize` convention. **Considered: cache `lastTotalSecondsByRecipe` across searches in a per-search-session dict.** The current implementation already does this via the `RecipeStore.mergeDetail(_:)` write-back path — hydrated recipes get persisted into the cache, so a subsequent search whose results overlap with previously-hydrated recipes gets them back from the cache via `dependencies.totalSeconds(forRecipeIDs:)`. No separate per-session dict is needed. **Considered: also surface `recently viewed` or `most popular` as similar special-case pills.** Out of scope for T-637 — the bug report is specifically about "Latest Recipes"; if user feedback surfaces the same pattern for other generic-phrase categories, a future polish task can broaden the special-case set. **Considered: keep the literal text search for "Latest Recipes" and instead add a `?categories=1590` REST scope.** Rejected — even with the category scope, the user's intent is "newest," not "everything in the category sorted by relevance," and the WP REST `posts` endpoint defaults to date-desc which gives the user what they actually want without a category constraint. The category id is the trigger to switch paths, not the filter.

  **Spec/trace:** US-12 / AC-12.2 amended in place — the chip-row gating contract is documented (visible only outside the `.idle` state); US-12 / AC-12.3 amended in place — the "missing = MISS" rule is preserved BUT the missing set is hydrated when the cook-time filter is active; US-29 / AC-29.1 amended in place — the curated Try-pill behavior is extended with the Latest-Recipes special case (case-insensitive name match against "Latest Recipes" routes to a network fetch of the N most-recent posts instead of `selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)`). CL-49 / CL-105 preserved unchanged (the Try-pill-into-query-field path remains the default for every other category; the magnifyingglass-glyph + capsule treatment is preserved); REG-19 / CL-66 / T-670 preserved unchanged (the recent-searches exclusion contract for curated taps stays the canonical rule; the new Latest-Recipes branch ALSO does not persist a recent, mirroring the same intent). Implementing PR: T-637 (`feat/T-637-search-fixes`). Three source-file edits in `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/` (`SearchView.swift`, `SearchViewModel.swift`, `SearchDependencies.swift`) + new L1 tests in `SearchViewModelTests.swift` + spec amendments; no composition-root edit (the existing `LiveSearchDependencies(client:store:monitor:)` init is preserved), no schema migration, no new REST endpoint surface; **no `e2e` label** — pure feature-package change with optional `mergeDetail(...)` cache write-back as the only persistence touch (and that path already exists from the recipe-detail flow).

- **CL-107 (US-12 / US-29 / US-34 / US-36 / US-42 / T-638, 2026-05-29)** — **Round-9 verify-checklist behavior is pinned to automated coverage: the long-press Saved-tab unsave instant-removal (T-635), the Search-tab chip-row idle gating + cook-time filter actually narrowing via hydration (T-637), the Latest-Recipes Try-pill returning the recent-recipes branch instead of a literal text search (T-637), and the Settings page no longer carrying the DEBUG "Simulate New Post" button (T-633) all become L5 E2E (or L3 smoke) assertions in this PR. Adds three stable accessibility identifiers (`dod.search.cookTimeChip`, `dod.search.tryPill.latestRecipes`, `dod.saved.card`) at the new test-handle surfaces, documents the cook-time hydration polling-wait pattern as the canonical shape for hydration-dependent assertions, and explicitly skips the two surfaces XCUITest can't cleanly observe (the Try-pill glyph and the destructive-trash icon color — both already pinned by L4 snapshot tests).** Spencer just manually walked the verify checklist for the T-633..T-637 cluster and confirmed each item by hand; this CL turns the checklist into permanent regression cover so future PRs catch a regression on the next CI run instead of on the next manual verify pass.

  **(1) Saved-tab long-press → Unsave context menu + instant removal (T-634 + T-635 pin).** **Decision: extend `UITests/E2E/CoreUserJourneysE2ETests.test_save_recipe_then_unsave_from_saved_tab` AND add a sibling test `test_long_press_unsave_from_saved_tab` that walks the context-menu path specifically.** The existing journey unsaves from the recipe-detail screen (per its own comment line 152 framing); that path stays valid (it's the deep-link / detail-flow path) and must keep passing. The new sibling test additionally exercises (a) save a recipe via the detail-screen "Save recipe" button, (b) switch to the Saved tab, (c) find the just-saved card, (d) **long-press the card** via `.press(forDuration: 1.0)` to trigger the SwiftUI `.contextMenu` overlay, (e) assert the menu exposes a button labeled exactly `"Unsave"` (positive — T-634), (f) **negative-assert** the menu does NOT expose a button labeled exactly `"Save"` (T-634 — the saved card must read Unsave), (g) tap "Unsave," (h) assert within `0.5s` that the card element returns `false` from `waitForExistence(timeout: 0.5)` — the 0.5s frame-tight window is the load-bearing assertion that the optimistic removal (T-635 / CL-104's `SavedViewModel.optimisticallyRemove(id:)`) fires before the slower `.task { await viewModel.refresh() }` reconciliation could mask the bug. If the optimistic update regresses (e.g. a future contributor removes the call from `SavedView`'s context-menu Button action), the card visually lingers and the 0.5s assertion fails; the slower full-refresh fallback would still eventually remove the card on tab-switch-back but the snap-removal contract per AC-5.3 / CL-104 would be silently broken. (i) If this is the last saved recipe, assert AC-5.8's verbatim empty-state title `"No saved recipes yet"` appears — the empty-state transition is load-bearing per CL-104. The existing detail-screen-unsave journey is kept in place as the sibling test for that code path; both paths matter.

  **(2) Search tab chip-row idle gating (T-637 part 1).** **Decision: add Search-tab journey assertions `test_search_chip_row_hidden_on_idle` and `test_search_chip_row_visible_after_query`.** The idle-state journey opens the Search tab fresh, asserts the tab bar lands, taps tab index 3 (Search), then **before** typing anything asserts the cook-time chip element does NOT exist on screen. The query handle is the chip's accessibility identifier (newly added — see (5) below) `dod.search.cookTimeChip`. Then types a multi-character query ("chicken" — same shape Journey 2's `"cake"` query uses for the "reliable live-API hit" reason) and asserts the chip becomes visible. This pins CL-106's first decision (the `viewModel.state != .idle` gate on `FilterChipRow`) — if a future refactor of `SearchView.body` accidentally drops the gate (e.g. someone moves the chip row into a parent VStack outside the state branch), the idle-state assertion fails on the next E2E run.

  **(3) Cook-time filter actually narrows results (T-637 part 2 — the load-bearing one).** **Decision: add Search-tab journey assertion `test_search_cook_time_filter_narrows_results`.** The journey types "chicken" (multi-result query against the live blog), waits for at least 2 result rows to land via `waitForExistence(timeout: 30)`, captures the initial result count via `app.buttons.matching(predicate).count` (with the standard tab + chrome exclusion predicate from `E2ETestSupport`), then taps the cook-time chip via the new `dod.search.cookTimeChip` identifier, then taps the `"≤ 30 min"` menu item (queryable via `app.buttons["≤ 30 min"]` — the `CookTimeBucket.label` string surfaces as the menu button's accessibility label per `FilterChipRow.cookTimeChip`'s `Menu` body), then waits for hydration via an `expectation(for:)` polling-wait with a `15s` timeout for the result count to **change** (in either direction — it almost always decreases since "≤ 30 min" is the most-restrictive bucket on the live blog, but a chicken query could conceivably return only short-time recipes in which case the count stays the same and the filter still works). The assertion: filtered count `<= initial count` (never grows under a narrowing filter). **This is the test that catches the T-632-pattern bug class** (a cache-only lookup hiding a real feature) — if the hydration fan-out regresses, the count stays at the cache-only baseline (likely zero for most cook-time buckets on uncached fresh search results) and the test fails loudly. The 15s timeout matches the existing `WPRestClient.posts()` round-trip budget per the L3 smoke patterns; the polling pattern (`expectation(for: NSPredicate(format: "...") evaluatedWith: someObject, handler: nil)` against a runtime-evaluable predicate) is the canonical XCUITest shape for "wait for an async UI state change without a deterministic completion signal." The new CL canonicalizes this pattern for future hydration-dependent journeys.

  **(4) "Latest Recipes" Try-pill returns the recent-recipes branch (T-637 part 3).** **Decision: add Search-tab journey assertion `test_search_latest_recipes_pill_returns_recent_branch`.** The journey opens Search tab idle, finds the "Latest Recipes" Try pill via the newly-added `dod.search.tryPill.latestRecipes` accessibility identifier (see (5) — the pill is one of `topCategorySuggestions` which is dynamic, so identifying it by stable id beats text-matching on the live category name), taps it, waits for results to land via the result-button count, and asserts the result row count is in the range `3...8` (the limit is 5 with an over-fetch of `ceil(5 * 1.5) = 8`; the trim drops back to 5 but a fresh fetch may leave 3–5 visible recipes depending on how many of the latest 8 posts happen to be articles vs recipes). **The differentiating assertion that catches a regression to literal text search:** a literal `client.search(query: "Latest Recipes")` would return either ~0 results (no boilerplate hit on the live blog right now) or a long scatter of unrelated articles (some boilerplate hits) — neither of which would be in the `3...8` range. Range-bounded assertions are intentionally loose because the live blog's recent-recipes set drifts daily; the bound is set to catch the failure mode (zero or many) without being brittle against legitimate fluctuation in the recent-posts queue. Optional supplementary assertion: tap the first result and confirm the title does NOT contain the literal string "Latest Recipes" (which would indicate a literal text-search match against a post titled or boilerplated with that phrase). Bound this at `8s` to keep the journey under the 30s wall-clock budget per `E2ETestSupport`'s framing.

  **(5) New accessibility identifiers for stable test handles.** **Decision: add three `accessibilityIdentifier(...)` calls in the feature packages so the E2E tests have stable element handles regardless of live-data drift or accessibility-label changes.** Identifiers use the convention `dod.<tab>.<element>` mirroring the existing `dod.recipeListLayout`-style key shapes the codebase already uses. **(a) `dod.search.cookTimeChip`** on the cook-time chip in `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/SearchView.swift`'s `FilterChipRow.cookTimeChip` (right next to the existing `.accessibilityLabel("Cook time filter, ...")` call). Pinpoints the only filter chip on the row for chip-row idle-gating and cook-time-filter assertions. **(b) `dod.search.tryPill.latestRecipes`** on the matching "Latest Recipes" Try pill in `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/IdleSuggestionsView.swift`'s pill rendering — conditionally applied only when the pill's `text` matches "Latest Recipes" (case-insensitive). The pill loop already iterates `topCategorySuggestions`; we add a `.accessibilityIdentifier(text.localizedCaseInsensitiveCompare("Latest Recipes") == .orderedSame ? "dod.search.tryPill.latestRecipes" : "")` (or equivalent guard) inside the pill helper. The empty-string identifier on the non-Latest-Recipes branch is a no-op identifier per Apple's documented behavior (the element falls back to its accessibility label for queries). **(c) `dod.saved.card`** on the Saved-tab `RecipeCard` in `Packages/DODFeatureSaved/Sources/DODFeatureSaved/SavedView.swift`'s `.loaded` arm — a static identifier (not per-id) because the test queries for any saved card and operates on the first match; a per-id identifier would be more precise but adds a Domain-type dependency in the View layer for a marginal gain on a query that already filters by recipe-row predicate. Each identifier carries an inline comment citing T-638 / CL-107 so a future reader sees why the value is load-bearing. **Existing identifier handles preserved:** `feed-toolbar-settings` (used by tests reaching Settings from FeedView), `settings-toggle-notifications` (used by the new L3 Settings smoke per (6)), `settings-picker-appearance` / `settings-picker-share-format` / `settings-button-clear-cache` / `settings-toggle-telemetry` (in case the L3 smoke or a future journey needs them).

  **(6) Settings page smoke for T-633 — no DEBUG button.** **Decision: add `SmokeTests.test_settingsPageHasNoDebugTestButton` to `UITests/SmokeTests.swift` (L3, runs on every PR — no `e2e` label needed because Settings reach is the existing L3 smoke surface).** The test taps the Recipes tab (default landing — same shape `SmokeTests.test_recipeDetailOpensAndShowsContent` uses), taps the gear-icon toolbar button via `app.buttons["Settings"]` (the existing `.accessibilityLabel("Settings")` on the gear in `FeedView.settingsToolbarLink`), then asserts the Settings nav title `"Settings"` appears (`app.navigationBars["Settings"]`) AND the notifications toggle exists (`app.switches["Notify me when new recipes drop"]` per `SettingsView`'s `Toggle` label) AND **negative-assert** the DEBUG button does NOT exist — query via the deleted button's old text `"Test: Simulate New Post"` (the leading "▸" glyph was the visual prefix in CL-100; the underlying accessibility label was the trimmed text without the glyph). If a future revert or accidental re-add of the DEBUG scaffold lands, the test fails loudly. The notifications-toggle positive assertion doubles as a Settings-page-reachable check — if the gear icon ever stops pushing into Settings, the toggle won't appear and the test catches both failure modes. L3 (not L5) because the Settings page is a fast-reachability surface and the test wall-clock should stay under 5s — the L3 smoke layer is the right pyramid level for "screen-existence + button-existence" assertions per AC-T2's framing.

  **What CL-107 does NOT touch:** the existing 5 seed journeys in `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests` (preserved — the new chip/cook-time/Latest-Recipes assertions land as new test methods, not replacements; Journey 3's detail-screen-unsave path stays as the sibling alongside the new context-menu-unsave variant), `E2ETestSupport.tabLabels` (unchanged — the new tests use the same 4-tab exclusion predicate), `E2ETestSupport.launchForE2E()` (unchanged — the new tests use the existing fake-deps-deferred launch posture per the Phase-1 framing), the L4 snapshot baselines for `RecipeCard` / `IdleSuggestionsView` / `FilterChipRow` (unchanged — the identifier additions are non-visual; SwiftUI's `accessibilityIdentifier(_:)` does not affect rendered pixels), the `SearchFilters.apply(...)` contract (unchanged — REG-12 still locks the filter logic at L1), the `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` seam (unchanged — REG-DOD-NAV-1 + the L1 store tests still lock it), the CI workflow (no new test scheme — the new E2E tests land in the existing `DODAppE2ETests` scheme that the `e2e` label already gates per CL-58), the `LiveSearchDependencies` / `LiveSavedDependencies` composition roots (unchanged — the new identifiers are pure SwiftUI modifiers on the existing views).

  **What CL-107 explicitly skips (with rationale recorded inline in the test source).** **(a) Try-pill glyph icon assertion (T-636 / CL-105's `magnifyingglass` swap).** XCUITest cannot reliably distinguish SF Symbol glyph identity inside a `Label(_:systemImage:)`; the icon is part of the rendered Label, not a separately-queryable element, and accessibility labels surface the text not the symbol name. **This is L4-snapshot territory** — the existing `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` + `IdleSuggestionsViewSnapshotTests` baselines (re-recorded under T-636) already pixel-lock the glyph. An `XCTSkip` note in the journey body documents the boundary. **(b) Destructive recent-search trash icon color (T-636 / CL-105 decision (4)'s `.tint(.red)`).** XCUITest cannot read pixel colors. Same L4-snapshot framing — the existing snapshot baseline locks the color. An `XCTSkip` note in the journey body documents the boundary. **(c) The widget deep-link Phase-1 probe** (existing Journey 5) — unchanged; that's a T-610 follow-up, not in T-638's scope.

  **Considered: re-record L4 snapshot tests for the Settings page sans-DEBUG-button.** Rejected — the L3 negative-assertion is the cleaner signal (XCUITest assertion fails loudly with the deleted button's text in the error message; a missing-pixel snapshot diff is harder to read). **Considered: parameterize the cook-time bucket choice across all `CookTimeBucket.allCases`.** Deferred — the `"≤ 30 min"` bucket is the most-restrictive and the most-likely-to-narrow, so it's the highest-signal single test. Future polish task can extend to a `for bucket in allCases` parameterization if specific buckets regress independently. **Considered: pre-seed the test simulator with a deterministic SavedRecipes dataset via a launch arg.** Rejected for T-638 — the Phase-1 known gap (SwiftData state persists across test runs until T-610) is documented in `E2ETestSupport`; the new tests use the same normalize-state-via-unsave trick the existing Journey 3 uses. T-610's fakes will make this deterministic. **Considered: assert the exact `"Latest Recipes"` Try-pill is present via text query rather than accessibility identifier.** Rejected — `topCategorySuggestions` ranks by count, and the "Latest Recipes" pill is not guaranteed to be in the top-5 forever (if dad publishes 100 recipes in a non-Latest-Recipes category, the pill could drop out). Accessibility identifier on conditional-render is the robust hook. **Considered: scope the cook-time-narrows test to a fake-mode path.** Deferred to T-610 — Phase 1 runs against the live blog per `E2ETestSupport`'s framing, and "chicken" + "≤ 30 min" is empirically stable. If the test goes flaky, the documented escalation is to pin it to a specific query known to span time buckets, not to `XCTSkip` it.

  **Spec/trace:** AC-T4 amended (the new REG-21 joins the locked-by-test list); AC-T5 amended (the new journeys join the seed-journeys-may-be-extended note in CL-58); US-5 / AC-5.3 (the instant-removal-on-unsave behavior is now locked at L5); US-12 / AC-12.2 (the chip-row gating is now locked at L5); US-12 / AC-12.3 (the cook-time hydration narrowing is now locked at L5 — REG-21 records the specific failure mode); US-29 / AC-29.1 (the Latest-Recipes special case is now locked at L5); US-36 / AC-36.1 (Settings notifications toggle is now locked at L3); US-42 / AC-42.6 (the DEBUG affordance removal is now locked by L3 negative assertion); CL-58 / AC-T5 preserved unchanged (the journey-extension framing already invites this PR); CL-102 / CL-103 / CL-104 / CL-105 / CL-106 preserved unchanged (this CL pins their behavior; the source-of-truth decisions live in those CLs). Implementing PR: T-638 (`test/T-638-verify-automation`). Three identifier additions in the feature packages + new test methods in `UITests/E2E/CoreUserJourneysE2ETests.swift` + new method in `UITests/SmokeTests.swift` + spec amendments; no composition-root edit, no protocol surface change, no schema change. PR carries the `e2e` label because the new tests live in `UITests/E2E/`.

- **CL-104 (US-34 / US-5 / T-635, 2026-05-29)** — **Unsaving a recipe from the Saved tab's long-press context menu now removes the card instantly via optimistic removal in `SavedViewModel`; full reactive observation of `RecipeStore` changes is deferred.** T-634 (CL-103) made the Saved-tab context menu state-aware ("Unsave" + outline `bookmark` for a saved card). The toggle wiring was correct — tapping "Unsave" flipped `isSaved` to `false` in `RecipeStore` via `TabStack.saveFromCard(item:store:)` — but the card visually lingered in `SavedView`'s `LazyVGrid` until the user switched tabs and came back. **The confirmed root cause: `SavedViewModel` doesn't observe store changes.** `SavedViewModel.recipes` is populated once by `refresh()` from `dependencies.savedRecipes()` (a one-shot `RecipeStore.savedRecipes()` query); `SavedView` only calls `.task { await viewModel.refresh() }` on appear. There is **no subscription** to `RecipeStore` mutations, so when the context menu fires `onSave?(recipe)` → `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)`, the viewModel's local `recipes` array doesn't update. The `.task` re-runs only on tab-switch-back, which is why returning to the Saved tab "fixed" the stale row. **Decision: optimistic removal in the context-menu callback, not a reactive `RecipeStore` observation surface (deferred).** Two paths were on the table — (1) wire an `AsyncStream<Set<Int>>` / `@Observable` saved-IDs publisher on `RecipeStore` and have every feature viewmodel subscribe (the v2 answer, also resolves CL-60 path-(c) for Feed/Categories/Search per-card `isSaved` state), or (2) do an optimistic local removal in `SavedViewModel.optimisticallyRemove(id:)` called by `SavedView`'s context-menu Button right before the `onSave?(recipe)` bubble. T-635 picks (2) for v1 on cost-vs-value grounds: (1) is a multi-package refactor (RecipeStore observation surface + four feature viewmodel subscriptions + lifecycle/teardown wiring) and the visible bug is only the Saved-tab card; (2) is a four-line viewmodel method + a one-line call-site reordering and reconciles correctly on the next `.task` cycle if the store write somehow failed. **The optimistic method.** `SavedViewModel.optimisticallyRemove(id:)` is `@MainActor` and does two things: `recipes.removeAll { $0.id == id }` and `loadState = recipes.isEmpty ? .empty : .loaded`. The empty-state transition is load-bearing — unsaving the last saved recipe must surface AC-5.8's empty-state view immediately, not leave a `.loaded` state pointing at an empty array. Calling with an id not in the list is a no-op (the `removeAll` predicate matches zero rows and the loadState computation is idempotent). **Call-site order matters.** In `SavedView`'s context-menu Button action, the optimistic update fires **before** `onSave?(recipe)` so the UI snaps to the new state synchronously on the `@MainActor`; the store toggle then propagates asynchronously through `TabStack` (no completion callback) without re-blocking the frame. The reverse order would still work (the optimistic update is `@MainActor` and the `onSave` call is fire-and-forget into `TabStack`), but the documented order makes the "UI first, persistence second" intent obvious in the call site. **What CL-104 does NOT touch:** `RecipeStore` (no new observation surface in v1 — deferred to a future reactive-saved-IDs story), `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` (unchanged — still the canonical seam), `SavedDependencies` (no new protocol method — the optimistic update is local to the viewmodel and doesn't need a dependency), `TabStack.saveFromCard(item:store:)` (unchanged — the composition-root wiring still routes through the same callback), the `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher.publish()` call (still fires from `saveFromCard` post-toggle), `SavedView`'s `.task { await viewModel.refresh() }` on-appear hook (preserved as the reconciliation surface — if the optimistic removal turned out to be wrong somehow, the next tab-switch refreshes from `RecipeStore.savedRecipes()`), the shopping-list toolbar's `viewModel.loadState == .loaded` gate per CL-85 / AC-39.3 (it correctly hides the Build Shopping List button when the list becomes empty — the optimistic empty-state transition flows through the same gate). **What CL-104 does NOT fix (explicitly deferred):** Feed/Categories/Search context-menu Save (the CL-60 path-(c) per-card `isSaved` state) — those surfaces don't display saved-only content, so the visual-staleness bug doesn't apply there in the same way; the icon-state mismatch (CL-103's TODO marker for those three call sites) is the only residual gap, and the v2 reactive observation story remains the right shape to close it. The Saved-tab article rows (US-37 / CL-63) are also covered — `optimisticallyRemove(id:)` is `PostKind`-agnostic; it matches on `Recipe.id`, which is shared between recipes and articles. The recipe-detail bookmark-tap path (AC-5.1 / AC-4.7) is unchanged — that surface unsave goes through the detail view's own state, not `SavedViewModel`, and the AC-5.1 snackbar undo wires the same toggle so re-saving recovers the row on next appear. **Considered:** add a `SavedDependencies.unsave(id:)` method that runs the store toggle inline and returns a fresh `savedRecipes()` snapshot. Rejected — duplicates the `TabStack.saveFromCard` wiring and breaks the "the host owns the save side-effect" composition-root invariant (the viewmodel would be doing persistence + analytics + widget-snapshot work that lives correctly in `TabStack`). **Considered:** subscribe `SavedViewModel` to a notification-driven `RecipeStore` change stream (the v2 path-(c) story). Deferred — out of scope for a < 20-minute polish task; the optimistic removal is the right v1 cut. **Considered:** call `await viewModel.refresh()` from the context-menu action after `onSave?(recipe)`. Rejected — `refresh()` issues a full `RecipeStore.savedRecipes()` round-trip + ImageCache pre-download walk per AC-5.2 (`LiveSavedDependencies.preDownloadImages(...)`), which is wasteful for a single-row removal and races the `TabStack.saveFromCard` toggle (no completion callback to await before the refresh, so the refresh would see the pre-toggle state and re-add the row to the list). The optimistic local removal is cheaper and correct-by-construction. **Considered:** use SwiftUI's `withAnimation` block around the removal so the card animates out. Rejected for v1 — the `LazyVGrid` already does a default fade on identity loss; an explicit animation would need to be tuned for the row-height + reflow and is out of scope for a < 20-minute fix. (A future task can revisit if the snap feels janky.) **Spec/trace:** US-5 / AC-5.3 (the Saved tab "shows all saved recipes" line — the instant-removal-on-unsave behavior is added in a clarifying note); US-34 / AC-34.6 amended in `spec.md` (the state-aware menu now also triggers instant local removal on the Saved tab); CL-60 path-(c) reactive-observation deferral preserved unchanged (T-635 picks the optimistic-removal v1 path rather than the path-(c) v2 path); CL-103 unchanged (the state-aware menu contract is preserved — the Saved-tab branch now visually completes the unsave). Implementing PR: T-635 (`fix/T-635-saved-instant-unsave`). One viewmodel method + one call-site line + L1 test additions + spec-text amendments; no composition-root edit, no protocol surface change, no `RecipeStore` change, no `e2e` label.

- **CL-108 (US-14 / REG-27, 2026-05-29)** — **Comments-broken on TestFlight 1.0 (2): the round-9 backlog investigation lands defensive hardening (User-Agent stamping + category-specific error snackbar) without confirming a root cause; the next TestFlight build's surfaced snackbar pins the actual hypothesis.** Round-9 backlog captured five priority-ordered hypotheses for the broken comment POST on TestFlight build `1.0 (2)`: H1 silent error path in the composer ViewModel, H2 WP moderation auto-trash, H3 user-agent / CORS reject, H4 fresh-install Keychain miss in `GuestIdentityStore`, H5 ATS blocking on Release. **Code walk verdicts.** H1 was **CONSISTENT** (the single generic snackbar collapsed every failure category — offline / timeout / 4xx / 5xx — into one indistinguishable bucket; even when the snackbar fired, dad couldn't tell us *which* class of failure occurred). H3 was **UNTESTABLE FROM CODE** (the iOS side never set a custom `User-Agent` header; whether a WP security plugin or Cloudflare rule rejects empty-UA requests is a server-side fact, but the absence is consistent with the bug). H2 was **UNTESTABLE FROM CODE** (server-side state — must be checked in WP admin → Comments → Spam / Trash). H4 was **RULED OUT** on closer read: `GuestIdentityStore.read()` returns `nil` cleanly on a Keychain miss, the composer ViewModel correctly requires both `authorName.isEmpty == false` AND `authorEmail.isEmpty == false` in its `canPost` flag, and the `GuestIdentitySheet` save path writes back before the composer is dismissed. H5 was **RULED OUT**: `WPCommentsClient` constructs HTTPS URLs only, no redirect-following anywhere in the chain, and the existing live-API tests would have caught an ATS-blocked path on the device-class build. **No confirmed root cause from the code walk alone.** **Decision: ship defensive medicine + observability hardening for the H1 + H3 hypotheses; defer the confirmed-cause fix to the next TestFlight build where the new snackbar reveals which class of failure actually fires.** **(1) `URLSessionHTTPClient` User-Agent stamping (H3 defensive).** Every outgoing request now carries a recognizable `User-Agent` header (default `DODApp/<v>.<b> (<platform>; <bundle-id>)` built from `Bundle.main`'s `CFBundleShortVersionString` / `CFBundleVersion` / `CFBundleIdentifier`; caller-supplied UA preserved unchanged). The default value is informational (not a security claim) but it's the one piece of code-side defensive medicine that closes hypothesis H3 entirely if a WP security plugin was the cause. **(2) Category-specific error snackbar (H1 observability).** `RecipeDetailViewModel.commentErrorSnackbar(for:)` translates `WPClientError` into five distinct user-visible strings — networkUnavailable / timeout / httpStatus(code) / decoding / underlying — replacing the prior single "Couldn't post your comment — try again." that swallowed every failure category. The httpStatus variant includes the actual HTTP code (`"Couldn't post your comment (server said 403)."`) so a TestFlight reporter can paste the chip back and pin the failure class. **What CL-108 explicitly does NOT change.** `WPCommentsClient.postComment(...)` signature + body (unchanged — the issue was in the error-surfacing layer, not the request construction), `GuestIdentityStore` (the H4 hypothesis was ruled out — no change), `WPClientError` enum (still the canonical case set), the App Privacy questionnaire (no new data collected — `User-Agent` is technical metadata, not user data per Apple's questionnaire definition). **The follow-up.** When the next TestFlight build ships and dad/Spencer reproduce the bug, the snackbar text identifies the failure class: an offline message means H1 + connectivity; a `(server said 403)` means H2 or H3; a timeout means H2; a decoding error means the server returned a body shape we didn't expect (possibly H2 with a moderation `pending` body that the existing model doesn't parse). The follow-up REG (REG-28) will graduate based on that signal. **Spec/trace:** US-14 (the comment POST surface), REG-27 in `spec.md` (the test-locked observability + defensive contract), round-9 backlog entry "Comments are broken on the installed TestFlight build" remains OPEN until the root cause is pinned. Implementing PR: REG-27 (`fix/REG-27-comments-broken-testflight`). Touches `URLSessionHTTPClient` (additive — new initializer parameter with a default), `RecipeDetailViewModel` (additive — new `nonisolated static` mapping helper), plus three new L1 tests in `DODNetworkingTests` and `DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests`; no composition-root change, no protocol surface change, no `e2e` label.

- **CL-115 (US-16 / US-18 / T-730, 2026-05-30)** — **The DODDesignSystem L4 snapshot suite is made cross-Xcode-version stable and deterministic: perceptual tolerance on every `.image(...)` assertion, the stale dark baselines re-recorded under the iOS 26 SDK, and the eight non-deterministic dark tab-bar snapshots removed (the tab bar's contract stays pinned by `AppTabTests` + `SmokeTests`).** When the CI infra wedge was cleared (PR #91 moved CI to macos-15 / Xcode 26 and fixed the billing block), the L4 job surfaced **29 real failures that had been masked while CI was dead** — every one a `_dark` test; every `_light` test passed. **Root cause has three independent layers, all in the dark renders.** **(1) Missing tolerance on the dark path.** The dark assertions routed through a `darkImage(width:height:)` helper in `SnapshotTests+AppearanceAudit.swift` that wrapped a bare `.image(layout:..., traits: darkTraits())` with **no** `precision` / `perceptualPrecision`, so they did exact pixel comparison while the inline light call sites had tolerance. **(2) Stale dark baselines.** The dark `__Snapshots__` PNGs were last recorded at #42 (T-520 color overhaul), but the SurfaceElevated dark color was deliberately changed twice afterward — `#5A3520` (T-570 / #47) then `#553724` (T-610 / #51) — and the dark baselines were never re-recorded, so e.g. `test_recipeCard_full_dark` matched only **3.9%** of pixels (the card background is now the warmer `#553724`).
