The recipe-card grid / list layout (US-38 / T-650) is unchanged. **Spec/trace:** US-3 amendment (AC-3.5 — the search field's Clear button + visual contract now references `DODSearchField` as the canonical shape; pre-T-648 inline-`HStack`-with-`RoundedRectangle` framing is struck through), US-29 amendment (the search-field surface — pill brand-brown — is the visual the AC-29.1 capsule-pill suggestions sit beneath; consistent visual language for the tab), US-19 amendment (AC-19.3 — `.searchable`-as-the-search-surface framing is struck through in favor of the `DODSearchField` slotted above the List). New **REG-32** locks the "both bars share `DODColor.surfaceElevated` background + `Capsule` shape" contract. Implementing PR: T-648 (`feat/T-648-shared-search-field`). Test additions: **L1 in `DODDesignSystemTests.ComponentsTests`** — `DODSearchField` constructs with text binding; clear button renders only when text is non-empty; `onClear` closure fires when supplied (and text clears when no closure provided). **L4** — the `SearchViewSnapshotTests` baselines re-record on next sim test run (pill-shape pixel-diff vs the prior `RoundedRectangle` corner); the `CategoryListViewSnapshotTests` baselines re-record (search field is now a content-area pill above the List, not a system nav-bar drawer). Both record at `.missing` on first sim run. The `e2e` label IS applied — the change touches two tab surfaces, the user's first-touch interaction on each, and the L4 snapshot baselines that lock the home-screen-of-each-tab visual. **Numbering note:** CL-126 / T-648 is the next free CL slot above CL-125 (T-647 — list-row brand surfaces; clarifications-side entry is pending the T-647 spec catch-up but the source code already references CL-125 as the canonical anchor). REG-32 is the next free REG slot above REG-31 (T-644 / CL-122).

- **CL-123 (US-41 / T-704, 2026-05-30)** — **Ship the AC-41.2 first-launch iCloud-Sync opt-in prompt, adapting the original brief to the codebase as it actually exists, and exclude the marketing-screenshot test from the blocking L3 smoke gate.** **What shipped.** `CloudKitOptInSheet` — a two-button `DODDesignSystem` modal (headline "Sync your saved recipes across devices", one-sentence body, primary "Turn on iCloud Sync", secondary "Not now", VoiceOver labels + AX5 per AC-41.10) — presented from `RootView` through a single `.sheet(item:)` enum (`onboarding` → `cloudKitOptIn`) so the two first-launch modals sequence cleanly instead of racing two `.sheet(isPresented:)` modifiers. A `CloudKitOptInPromptGate` over the net-new `dod.cloudKitSyncOptInPromptShownV1` flag enforces the once-only contract; both buttons mark it shown so the sheet never returns (re-enabling later is the Settings toggle's job, AC-41.3). **Four adaptations from the T-704 brief — all because the brief predated the implementation choices T-702/T-703 actually made:** (1) the sheet lives in **`DODDesignSystem`** (beside `OnboardingSheet`), not the never-created `DODFeatureOnboarding` package — the same pivot T-703 made wiring Settings into `DODFeatureFeed` rather than the never-created `DODFeatureSettings`; (2) accept calls the **existing T-703 `SettingsDependencies` seam** (`setCloudSyncOptIn(true)` → writes `RecipeStore.cloudKitSyncOptInKey` + `recreateContainerAfterOptInChange()`), **not** a manual `CloudKitSyncAdapter.start()` — there is no such adapter, because SwiftData's `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` mirror activates automatically the moment the `.private(...)` container is (re)built; (3) the spec's literal `dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled` key is the implementation's `dod.cloudkit.syncOptInV1` (the rename T-702 introduced + T-703 cemented) — writing the spec's literal key would have been a silent no-op since the container factory reads the real one; (4) the **`CKAccountChanged` re-activation observer is omitted by design** — SwiftData reactivates the mirror natively on `CKAccountStatus` `.noAccount → .available` once the `.private` container exists, so a hand-rolled observer is redundant + untestable; the only thing it could add is "now syncing" status feedback, which is T-705's deferred surface. **Test-rig suppression (no per-test churn).** A first-launch prompt would block every fresh-launch smoke/E2E path, so `DODApp.applyTestLaunchOverrides()` marks the prompt shown under BOTH onboarding signals the suite already sets — `DOD_SUPPRESS_ONBOARDING=1` (every smoke/E2E launch) and `-DODForceFreshOnboarding` (the onboarding test, which asserts onboarding → feed, not onboarding → opt-in) — unless a test explicitly forces it with `-DODForceCloudKitOptInPrompt`. No existing UITest file changed. **L3 durability fix (the durable end of the #72 "harden L3" thread).** The blocking `L3 UI Smoke` job now passes `-skip-testing:DODAppUITests/ScreenshotTests`. That `test_capture_all_marketing_shots` harness is a release-prep capture tool, not a regression gate: it drives a 424-second **live-network** (WP REST) round-trip across every screen, produces ~135 MB result bundles, and was the single root cause of L3 failing / timing-out / cancelling across three #72 runs + #101 — even the #72 retry-loop + per-test-retry hardening couldn't tame a 424s test that flakes on live network + runner memory pressure (#101's L3 hit the 45-min timeout and was cancelled). A blocking smoke gate must be fast + hermetic; the genuine `SmokeTests` (8 methods) + `RecipeDetailRatingsSmokeTests` still gate every PR, and the marketing capture runs on-demand (`-only-testing:DODAppUITests/ScreenshotTests`) when fresh App Store shots are needed. L3 timeout trimmed 45 → 25 min to match. **Verified:** app target builds clean; 3 L1 `CloudKitOptInPromptGateTests` pass; swift-format + SwiftLint --strict clean; ci.yml YAML + embedded bash validated. **Deferred:** a dedicated L4 snapshot baseline for the sheet (AX5-correct by construction; lands when an App/DODFeatureFeed snapshot host exists) + the `syncEnabled` analytics event (T-707). **Spec/trace:** US-41 AC-41.2 (opt-in sheet + never-re-prompt) + AC-41.10 (sheet VoiceOver + AX5); supersedes the T-704 brief's `DODFeatureOnboarding` / `CloudKitSyncAdapter.start()` / `dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled` / `CKAccountChanged`-observer framing. Implementing PR: T-704 (`feat/T-704-cloudkit-opt-in-prompt`).

- **CL-127 (US-12 amendment / US-29 amendment / T-649, 2026-05-31)** — **Graduate the dad's-backlog "Did you mean…" search-suggestion bullet: when the Search-tab result set settles with fewer than 3 hits, render a tappable banner above the result list suggesting the closest cached recipe-title token via a Levenshtein-(1, 4] lookup; tap → re-runs the search with the suggested term.** **The user-visible gap this closes.** The post-T-642 title-precision filter is strict by design — `"naxxos"` (two-char typo of "nachos") returns 0 results because Levenshtein-1 fuzzy doesn't admit two-edit-distance neighbors (CL-120 explicitly rejects distance-2 on the matcher: "too loose on a small corpus"). The user has no recourse other than to retype the query. **The fix — a one-banner soft escape hatch.** When the result count is `< 3` AND the user did issue a search (`!lastQuery.isEmpty`), the viewmodel computes `SearchSuggestionEngine.suggest(query: lastQuery, cachedTitles: cachedTitles)` and surfaces the non-nil result as `didYouMean: String?`. The view renders an `HStack { Text("Did you mean:") + Text(suggestion).underline().foregroundStyle(DODColor.accent) }` banner above the result list (between the `FilterChipRow` and the result rows), gated on `state == .results || state == .noResults` so the banner never flashes during the `.searching` transition. Tap → `viewModel.applyDidYouMean()` overwrites `query = suggestion` and nulls out the suggestion; the existing debounce + `performSearch()` pipeline re-runs against the new query. **The trigger threshold — `< 3`.** Three hits is the minimum "result page feels populated" count on the existing 2-column gallery: one card draws the eye, two cards read as "is that all?", three cards fill the first scrolled row and the user trusts the search did something. Below that the banner intervenes; at or above it the silence is intentional ("we found enough — don't second-guess the user"). **The Levenshtein band — `(1, 4]`.** Lower bound is **exclusive of 1** because `TitleSearchMatcher.match(...)` already absorbs distance-1 via its fuzzy tier (CL-120), so re-suggesting a distance-1 neighbor would be tautological ("did you mean the thing we already matched?"). Upper bound is **inclusive of 4** because past 4 edits the suggested word stops resembling what the user typed — "naxxod" → "nachos" is a plausible recovery; "xyzaaq" → "nachos" is the engine putting words in the user's mouth. Both bounds are constructor-level defaults (`minDistance: Int = 1, maxDistance: Int = 4`) so a future caller (Saved-tab search, Spotlight cold-start) can tune them per surface without forking the engine. **The cached-titles source — reuse existing.** `SearchDependencies` gains `cachedRecipeTitles() async throws -> [String]` — a thin wrapper over `RecipeStore.cachedRecipeTitles()` (new actor method that returns `try modelContext.fetch(FetchDescriptor<CachedRecipe>()).map(\.title)`). The same `CachedRecipe` rows the cook-time hydration path already reads. **No new persistence schema, no new wire-format field, no new REST surface** — every title in the user's cache came from a feed/search/saved-tab hydration the app already did. The fake-dependencies path adds a parallel `cachedTitlesArray: [String]` slot for L1 tests. **Multi-word substitution rule — preserve unchanged tokens verbatim.** `"cast iron naxhos"` → `"cast iron nachos"` (only the typo token swaps; the surrounding "cast iron" stays byte-identical so the multi-word phrase reads naturally on the second render). The engine tokenizes both sides via lowercase + punctuation-stripped + length-≥-3 filter (drops "a" / "of" / "and" so the suggestion engine doesn't waste a substitution on noise), picks the query token with the smallest qualifying distance, and substitutes ONLY that token back into the user's original query string (case-preserved on the unchanged tokens — only the substituted token lowercases to match the canonical cached form). Tie-break across equally-distant cache tokens prefers the token with **higher frequency** in the cache — popularity is a weak relevance proxy that biases toward the recipe-namespace the user is plausibly searching for. **Self-suggestion guard — reject normalize-equivalent suggestions.** If the engine's substituted query normalizes to the same string the user already typed (e.g. casing-only variation), the engine returns `nil`. Avoids the embarrassing "did you mean 'nachos'?" when the user typed "Nachos". **The pure-helper home — `DODSupport`.** `SearchSuggestionEngine` lives in `Packages/DODSupport/Sources/DODSupport/SearchSuggestionEngine.swift`, beside `TitleSearchMatcher` (T-642 / CL-120) for the same reasons: pure value-type function, Foundation-only, and the same Levenshtein machinery — the iterative Wagner–Fischer matrix from `TitleSearchMatcher.levenshteinDistance(_:_:)` is the only shared math. The engine accesses it via the `TitleSearchMatcher`'s package-internal helper (the function is already `static func` at module scope; T-649 promotes the access modifier from `internal` to package-internal `internal` — both DODSupport — so the engine can call it without copy-paste). A future T-XXX that lifts the matcher into a separate "search precision" module would re-home the helper to a `LevenshteinDistance` namespace, but for v1 the two callers live side-by-side and the cross-file `internal` call is clean. **What this does NOT change.** The `TitleSearchMatcher` / `CategoryNameMatcher` / `SearchResultMerger` / merge-with-category-results pipeline is byte-identical — the suggestion path runs strictly downstream of the merged result set landing in `items`. The post-T-643 Path A + Path B union is unchanged. The Latest-Recipes Try-pill special case is untouched (it never enters `performSearch()`, so the suggestion gate never fires for it). The chip row, the cook-time hydration, the empty-state surface, the recent-searches store, the curated-tap exclusion, the telemetry hash — every other piece of the search pipeline is unchanged. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Use WP's REST `?suggest=` endpoint** — rejected because WP's suggest is server-side substring-only against post titles (the same matcher T-642 wraps client-side) and would require a second network round-trip per typed query; the cached-titles path is offline-capable + instant. **(b) Trigger on every search regardless of count** — would crowd the banner above non-sparse result sets where the user clearly found something; the `< 3` gate respects "if we found enough, don't second-guess." **(c) Use a stem-based suggestion table** (per the backlog's literal phrasing) — rejected as over-engineered for v1; Levenshtein over cached title tokens captures the same "did you mean a real recipe word?" intent without maintaining a stem dictionary. The backlog's "stem-based" phrasing was the dad's-shorthand framing, not a binding implementation choice. **(d) Auto-substitute the query without a banner** — rejected because silently rewriting what the user typed is rude (and the engine sometimes guesses wrong; a banner lets the user opt in instead of being overridden). **(e) Animate the banner in/out** — deferred; the banner appears the moment `state` settles to `.results`/`.noResults` and disappears on the next `query` mutation, so SwiftUI's default fade-in via `@Observable` is sufficient. **Spec/trace:** US-12 amendment (AC-12.1 — the search result set gains a downstream "did you mean?" hook when the merged set settles with fewer than 3 items; the suggestion engine reads from the cached titles already populated by AC-12.1's hydration path), US-29 amendment (AC-29.3 — the no-results empty state remains the dominant signal when the engine returns nil; the banner only renders when the engine has a non-nil suggestion). New L1 coverage: `SearchSuggestionEngineTests` (single-typo / two-typo / max-distance boundary / already-matched / empty cache / case-insensitive tokenization / tie-break by frequency / multi-word substitution preserves unchanged tokens) + three `SearchViewModelTests` for the integration (fires when `<3`, clears when `≥3`, `applyDidYouMean()` rewrites query + clears suggestion). Implementing PR: T-649 (`feat/T-649-did-you-mean`). **Numbering note:** CL-127 / T-649 is the next free CL slot above CL-126 (T-648 — shared `DODSearchField`). The `e2e` label IS applied — the change touches the user's first-touch interaction on the Search tab when their query is sparse (the dominant friction surface the banner exists to soften).

- **CL-124 (US-41 / T-707, 2026-05-30)** — **Add the four CloudKit-sync analytics events (AC-41.9) to `AnalyticsEvent` + the constitution §9 allowlist, dispatch the two user-action events from the single composition-root seam, and defer the two round-trip-result events' dispatch to T-705.** **What shipped.** `AnalyticsEvent` gains `.syncEnabled`, `.syncDisabled`, `.syncCompletedSuccessfully`, `.syncFailed(errorCategory: SyncErrorCategory)` (wire names `sync_enabled` / `sync_disabled` / `sync_completed_successfully` / `sync_failed`; the first three carry an **empty** payload, `syncFailed` carries exactly `["error_category": <closed-enum>]`). The new `SyncErrorCategory` closed enum (`network` / `accountStatus` / `quotaExceeded` / `serverInternal` / `other`) mirrors the `WidgetKind` / `VoiceCommandName` closed-enum posture — the wire value is a fixed case name, **never** the raw `CKError` code or message. Constitution §9's "Events tracked are limited to:" allowlist is amended with all four + the no-PII / no-free-text / no-recipe-id rationale (sync is session-level state, not a per-recipe action — `recipeSaved` already covers per-recipe granularity). **Single-seam dispatch (the load-bearing decision).** AC-41.9 says `syncEnabled` fires from EITHER the AC-41.3 Settings toggle OR the AC-41.2 first-launch prompt, and `syncDisabled` from the toggle only. Rather than wire the event at each of those call sites, T-707 fires from the **one** place both already funnel through: the `flagWriteAndRebuild` closure in `AppDependencies.settingsDependencies()` (`Telemetry.shared.send(enabled ? .syncEnabled : .syncDisabled)`). Both the toggle and the prompt call `setCloudSyncOptIn(_:)` → this closure, so a single dispatch point covers both with zero duplication; the prompt's "Not now" path never calls the seam, so declining correctly emits nothing. **Two events defined-now, dispatched-later (T-705).** `syncCompletedSuccessfully` (debounced to at most once per 60s) + `syncFailed(errorCategory:)` fire on actual CloudKit **round-trip results**, which can only be observed via `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer.eventChangedNotification` — and that observation is precisely T-705's sync-status state machine. So T-707 ships the event DEFINITIONS + the §9 allowlist entries (settling the wire contract + privacy posture now, so the analytics surface is complete + auditable), and T-705 wires the dispatch + the `CKError.Code → SyncErrorCategory` mapping when it builds the observer. This matches the Phase-15 DAG (T-707 depends on the dispatch sites T-702/703/704 provide; the result-event sites are T-705's). **Adaptations from the original T-707 brief (which predated the T-702/703/704 implementation choices):** (1) no `CloudKitSyncAdapter` to fire the result-events from — SwiftData's `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` mirrors automatically; there is no custom adapter (the brief's `CloudKitSyncAdapter.swift` was never built); (2) no `DODFeatureSettings` / `DODFeatureOnboarding` packages — Settings lives in `DODFeatureFeed` (CL-89) and the opt-in prompt in `DODDesignSystem` (CL-123); (3) the dispatch is the composition-root seam, not `SettingsViewModel.toggleCloudKitSync()` + the sheet's primary closure separately — the seam is the single DRY funnel both use; (4) `SyncErrorCategory` lives inline in `AnalyticsEvent.swift` beside the other closed-set payload enums, not the brief's separate `SyncErrorCategory.swift` (the `CKError.Code` mapping it would also host lands with T-705's dispatch). **Verified:** DODAnalytics 29 tests pass (incl. 5 new `AnalyticsEventTests` — name + empty/closed payload + closed-set sweep + the uniqueness guard extended to all four); app target builds clean; swift-format + SwiftLint --strict clean. **Spec/trace:** US-41 AC-41.9; supersedes the T-707 brief's `CloudKitSyncAdapter` / `DODFeatureSettings` / `DODFeatureOnboarding` / `SyncErrorCategory.swift` framing. Implementing PR: T-707 (`feat/T-707-cloudkit-analytics`).

- **CL-129 (US-4 amendment / T-732, 2026-05-31)** — **Three coordinated `RecipeDetailView`-only changes: drop the duplicative servings mini-chip from `RecipeDetailMetaPills` (the `RecipeServingsScaler` stepper is the single source of truth for the Serves indicator); replace the server-side `[…]` truncation on the recipe excerpt with a tappable More/Less expand-collapse affordance; render the expanded blurb as rich native blocks (paragraphs, headings, photos, lists) using the same shared `ArticleBlocksView` helper that `ArticleDetailView` renders through. `ArticleDetailView` itself is untouched visually — preserved byte-for-byte in user-visible output per Spencer's "articles look great now" feedback.** **The observed gaps.** (1) `RecipeDetailMetaPills` rendered both a total-time chip AND a "N servings" chip directly above the `RecipeServingsScaler` row — the same screen surfaces the source servings twice (once as a static chip, once as the stepper's "Recipe makes N" secondary line per AC-31.1), and the duplication reads as visual noise without earning the space. (2) The recipe excerpt rendered `viewModel.listItem.excerpt` as a plain `Text(...)` with no `lineLimit`, which surfaced the WordPress `the_excerpt()` filter's server-side `[…]` (or `[...]`) truncation suffix to the user — a literal "[...]" affordance with no follow-through action; the actual full description sits in the raw HTML body the fetch path already retrieves. (3) The full description had no path to reach the screen at all — the only blurb surface was the truncated excerpt. **The fix — three coordinated source-side changes.** (1) `metaPillItems` in `RecipeDetailView.swift` drops the `servings` entry — only the `totalTime` clock chip remains. The `RecipeDetailMetaPills` component itself stays unchanged (still renders an `Items: [Item]` array). The `RecipeServingsScaler` (AC-31.1 row immediately below the meta pills) is the single Serves indicator on the screen. (2) A new `@State private var isBlurbExpanded: Bool = false` flag on `RecipeDetailView`; the existing `excerptText` private var renders the excerpt with the `[…]` / `[...]` suffix **stripped at display time** (via a pure helper that trims trailing whitespace + `[…]` or `[...]` or `[ … ]` / `[ ... ]` with optional surrounding whitespace + the bare Unicode `…` or `...` characters when they appear as the literal truncation tail — both `…` U+2026 and the ASCII triple-dot are normalized) when collapsed, followed by an inline "More" button (`Text("More")` with `.dodFont(DODType.caption)` + `.foregroundStyle(DODColor.accent)`). On tap → `withAnimation { isBlurbExpanded = true }`. When expanded → renders the full blurb as a vertical stack of `ArticleBlock`s (parsed from the recipe's HTML) using the shared `ArticleBlocksView` helper, followed by a "Less" button with the same caption + accent style; on tap → `withAnimation { isBlurbExpanded = false }`. (3) A new `extractRecipeBlurb(html:)` static helper in `DODSupport.ArticleBodyExtractor` (option (b) over option (a) — see "HTML-source path decision" below) extracts the narrative HTML blocks that precede the WPRM recipe card; the view model fetches the HTML on detail open and stores `blurbBlocks: [ArticleBlock]` (populated by `ArticleHTMLParser.parse(html:)` against the cropped HTML); the expanded blurb view reads from `viewModel.blurbBlocks`. **HTML-source path decision: option (b), specialized `extractRecipeBlurb(html:)`, chosen over option (a) reusing `extractArticleBody(html:)` directly.** Option (a) would have routed the recipe's HTML through the existing `ArticleBodyExtractor.extractContentHTML(html:)` which returns the entire `entry-content` div verbatim — including the WPRM `<div class="wprm-recipe-container">` recipe card with its own structured ingredients/instructions content. On a recipe page this would have dragged the recipe card's structured content into the rich blurb render, duplicating what the screen already renders below (Ingredients / Instructions sections via AC-4.2 / AC-4.3 from the JSON-LD parse). Option (b) adds `ArticleBodyExtractor.extractRecipeBlurb(html:) -> String` that finds the `entry-content` slice and crops at the first WPRM-recipe-card boundary (the literal `<div class="wprm-recipe-container">` open tag — WPRM's canonical recipe-card wrapper across every WPRM theme), returning the HTML preceding the card. If the WPRM card is absent (rare — a recipe page without WPRM, or a malformed page), falls back to returning the full `entry-content` slice unchanged. The returned HTML is then run through `ArticleHTMLParser.parse(html:)` to produce `[ArticleBlock]`. This is more correct than option (a) because the recipe-vs-article boundary on `dutchovendaddy.com` is precisely the WPRM card open tag — every recipe post wraps its structured content in that div, and every block before it is narrative blurb. **The shared block renderer.** `ArticleDetailView.blockView(_ block: ArticleBlock)` (private, ~lines 110-144 of the pre-T-732 file) is extracted into a new public `struct ArticleBlocksView: View` in `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/ArticleBlocksView.swift`. The new component takes `blocks: [ArticleBlock]` and renders them in a `VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: DODSpacing.md)` with the same per-block styling the pre-T-732 `blockView` used — `.heading` renders at `DODType.displayMedium` (level ≤2) or `DODType.heading`; `.paragraph` renders at `DODType.body` + `.textSelection(.enabled)`; `.image` renders via the existing `articleImage` helper logic (moved into the shared component as a private method); `.list` renders as a `VStack` of `HStack(alignment: .firstTextBaseline)` rows with `•` (unordered) or `"\(index + 1)."` (ordered) leaders. The component carries `.tint(DODColor.accent)` for the link-styling default. **`ArticleDetailView` source-side delta.** The `articleBody` private var's `else` branch (when `blocks` is non-empty) replaces its inline `VStack { ForEach { blockView(...) } }` with `ArticleBlocksView(blocks: blocks)` — single line change. The `blockView(_:)` + `articleImage(_:caption:)` + `placeholder` + `imageMaxHeight` private members move into `ArticleBlocksView`. The `articleBody`'s fallback branch (legacy plain-text path when `blocks.isEmpty`) is unchanged. The `body`, `publishedDateCaption`, and every other member of `ArticleDetailView` is byte-identical. The L4 `ArticleDetailViewSnapshotTests` baselines should NOT need to re-record — same render tree, same fonts, same colors, same spacing, just sourced from a different view struct's body. If they do diff, it's a regression to fix before merging. **The "[…]" / "[...]" strip helper.** Pure function in `DODFeatureRecipeDetail` (private to `RecipeDetailView` is fine — it has no other consumer; lifting to `DODSupport` would be over-eager): trims trailing whitespace, then repeatedly strips a trailing `[…]` / `[...]` / `[ … ]` / `[ ... ]` (square-bracket variants) OR a bare trailing `…` / `...` when it sits as the literal truncation tail (after any prose). Idempotent — running it on already-stripped text is a no-op. Handles the four-way matrix of `[` `]` x `…` / `...` x optional inner whitespace. **What this does NOT change.** `RecipeServingsScaler` (AC-31.1) is byte-identical — same `RecipeServingsScaler(value:range:sourceServings:showsWarning:)` call. `RecipeDetailHero`, `RecipeDetailQuickJump`, `RecipeDetailVideoSection`, `ingredientsSection`, `instructionsSection`, `RelatedRecipesStrip`, `RecipeDetailRatingsSection`, the cookNow CTA, the toolbar items (Save / Download / Share per AC-4.7 / AC-35.1 / AC-4.8), and the snackbar surface are all byte-identical. The view model's existing public surface (`recipe`, `related`, `loadState`, `isSaved`, `isDownloaded`, `checkedIngredientIDs`, `snackbarMessage`, `userServings`, `userServingsRange`, `sourceServings`, `servingsScaleFactor`, `shouldShowServingWarning`) is unchanged — `blurbBlocks` is **additive** (new `internal(set)` property defaulting to `[]`, populated in the existing `fetchAndParse()` path on the recipe branch alongside the existing `mergeDetail` + `loadRelated` calls; cached-recipe path also populates from the cached recipe's `Recipe.articleBodyHTML` when present). `RecipeDetailMetaPills` component itself (`struct Item: Hashable { icon, label }`, `let items: [Item]`, `body` builder) is byte-identical — only the call-site array shrinks by one entry. `RecipeDetailDependencies` protocol is unchanged — the dependencies surface already exposes `extractArticleBody(html:)` (which routes to `extractContentHTML`); the new `extractRecipeBlurb(html:)` is invoked directly as a static call on `ArticleBodyExtractor`, not through the dependencies protocol (a recipe-specific extraction is not a dependency seam — there is no test that wants to fake it; the existing `articleBodyToExtract` test surface continues to drive the article-classification branch independently). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Use `extractArticleBody(html:)` directly without a recipe-specific helper** — see "HTML-source path decision" above; pulls the WPRM recipe card's structured content into the blurb. Rejected. **(b) Strip `[…]` server-side via a WP filter override** — would require modifying the WordPress site (out of scope; the app fixes its own rendering, not the site's). Rejected. **(c) Add `lineLimit(3)` to the existing excerpt + a Show More button** — same approach in shape but only reveals the next few lines of the same truncated excerpt, not the actual full description; doesn't solve the "user wants the full blurb" problem. Rejected. **(d) Render the full blurb inline always (no collapse)** — articles render the full body always because they have no other content; recipes have a dense screen (meta + stepper + cook CTA + quick jump + ingredients + instructions + related + ratings) and the blurb above ingredients should be opt-in rather than competing for above-the-fold space. The collapse default keeps the cook-task content high; "More" lets the user opt into reading the narrative. Rejected. **(e) Lift `ArticleBlocksView` into `DODDesignSystem`** — the block renderer reaches into `ArticleBlock` (in `DODSupport`) and `RecipeDetailHero`'s patterns, neither of which `DODDesignSystem` knows about; the recipe-detail feature package is the right home for the two consumers (`ArticleDetailView` + `RecipeDetailView` blurb expansion) sitting in the same module. Rejected. **What this does NOT change at the spec level.** US-7 (Cook Now CTA), US-13/14/15 (ratings + comments), US-31 (servings scaler — AC-31.1's "Serves N" stepper row is the surviving Serves indicator), US-35 (download for offline), US-37 (article rendering — `ArticleDetailView` is preserved), AC-4.7 / AC-4.8 / AC-4.9 / AC-4.10 (Save / Share / offline / VoiceOver). The shared `ArticleBlocksView` is a pure extraction with no spec impact for articles. **L4 baseline impact.** Delete every snapshot baseline file under `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Tests/DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests/__Snapshots__/` (already empty per the T-731 / CL-128 conservative-over-deletion pass — confirmed by inspection). Add new snapshot coverage for the collapsed-blurb + expanded-blurb states of `RecipeDetailView`. The `ArticleDetailViewSnapshotTests` baselines stay untouched (the extracted `ArticleBlocksView` renders identically). **REG-33** locks the contract: "the recipe detail screen has exactly one Serves indicator (the stepper) AND the recipe excerpt has a tappable More/Less expand-collapse affordance with the `[…]`/`[...]` server-side truncation tail stripped from the collapsed-state display; `ArticleDetailView` is unaffected by this contract." **Numbering note:** CL-129 / T-732 is the next free CL slot above CL-128 (T-731). REG-33 is the next free REG slot above REG-32 (T-648 / CL-126). **Spec/trace:** US-4 amendment — AC-4.1's "meta row (prep time, cook time, total time, servings)" strikes through "servings" (the stepper row already surfaces it per AC-31.1); a new AC clause documents the expand/collapse contract on the description. Implementing PR: T-732 (`feat/T-732-recipe-blurb-expansion`). Test additions: **L1** — strip-"[…]"-from-excerpt helper covers `"blurb text [...]"` → `"blurb text"`, `"blurb text […]"` → `"blurb text"`, `"blurb text"` → unchanged, `""` → `""`. **L1 view-model** — `blurbBlocks` is populated when the cached recipe carries `articleBodyHTML`; empty array on extraction failure. **L1 support** — `ArticleBodyExtractor.extractRecipeBlurb(html:)` crops at the WPRM card boundary; returns full content when no WPRM card is present. **L4** — `RecipeDetailView` snapshot in collapsed-blurb (with "More" visible) + expanded-blurb (with rich blocks + "Less" visible) states. The `e2e` label IS applied — the change touches a primary user-facing surface (the recipe detail screen, the most-trafficked screen in the app).

- **CL-130 (US-4 amendment / T-733, 2026-05-31)** — **Three refinements to T-732 / CL-129's recipe blurb expansion, all bounded to the same `RecipeDetailView` + `ArticleBodyExtractor` + `RecipeDetailViewModel+Fetch` surface T-732 already touched.** **(1) `extractRecipeBlurb(html:)` gains a `paragraphLimit: Int = 2` parameter that caps the returned HTML at the Nth `<p>` boundary** — T-732 returned EVERY block before the WPRM recipe card; on long-form recipes that meant 30-40 paragraphs of intro prose dumped into the expanded blurb (live-API sampling of recipes 21238 and 23034 on 2026-05-31 showed 40 and 30 non-empty `<p>` blocks respectively before the WPRM card — confirmed via `curl -sL "https://www.dutchovendaddy.com/?p=<id>"` + a regex count). The new cap defaults to 2, picked from the user's "1-2 paragraphs" ask: the first paragraph typically matches the WP excerpt the collapsed surface already shows, so paragraph 2 is the meaningful expansion content; capping at 2 keeps the expanded surface short enough to scan without dropping the next-paragraph context. The walk preserves headings / images / lists that sit BEFORE the Nth `<p>` boundary (they're context for the surrounding paragraphs) and truncates at the Nth `</p>` close-tag — content after the cap is dropped. If the source has fewer than `paragraphLimit` paragraphs total, returns what it has (no padding). `paragraphLimit: 0` returns an empty string (degenerate-but-safe). **(2) The collapsed-state excerpt regains a visible `"..."` tail in the view layer** — T-732 stripped the WP `[…]` truncation marker entirely (via the existing `strippingExcerptTruncationTail` pure helper), which left the collapsed surface reading as a complete-but-terse sentence with no visual cue that more content lay behind the "More" button. The fix attaches a literal `"..."` (three ASCII dots, not the WP source) to the displayed-excerpt-as-rendered, immediately before the "More" button — `Text(strippedExcerpt + "...")` rather than `Text(strippedExcerpt)`. The "..." attaches directly to the prose (no space), reading as the natural mid-sentence trail the user expected. **Crucially: the "..." is view-only.** It's NEVER appended to the excerpt the model carries; it's NEVER stored; it's NEVER rendered in the expanded state. The expanded surface drops the "..." entirely (the rich blocks continue the prose — no ellipsis needed). This separation — strip the WP source's tail in the helper, attach a clean app-controlled tail in the view — is robust against the WP source's truncation-marker variations (the `recognizedTruncationTails` table covers six variants today; future variants get added to the table without re-introducing the visible "[…]" in the collapsed display). **(3) The More/Less visibility gate broadens from `!viewModel.blurbBlocks.isEmpty` to "any `.paragraph` block in `blurbBlocks`"** — T-732's `!isEmpty` check meant a recipe whose pre-WPRM content was only a heading or image (no narrative paragraph) would silently get the More button with nothing meaningful behind it. The new gate is a `hasExpandableBlurb` computed property that scans `blurbBlocks` for at least one `.paragraph` case via pattern matching; the More/Less button only renders when `hasExpandableBlurb == true`. The cap from refinement (1) interacts cleanly with this gate — a recipe whose only paragraphs sit AFTER the WPRM card (impossible by construction — the extractor stops at the card) or whose pre-card region has zero `<p>` blocks (rare — confirmed possible on recipe 17615 which has no `entry-content` wrapper at all) gets no More button. The view collapses gracefully to excerpt-only with no dangling affordance. **What this does NOT change.** `RecipeDetailMetaPills` (CL-129 fix 1) is unchanged — servings chip stays dropped. `strippingExcerptTruncationTail(from:)` pure helper is unchanged — still strips the six WP-source truncation-marker variants. `ArticleBlocksView` is unchanged — same per-block renderer for both articles and the expanded recipe blurb. `ArticleDetailView` is unchanged — articles still render the FULL body (no paragraph cap; `extractArticleBody` / `extractContentHTML` do not use the new parameter). `RecipeServingsScaler`, `RecipeDetailHero`, `RecipeDetailQuickJump`, `RecipeDetailVideoSection`, `ingredientsSection`, `instructionsSection`, `RelatedRecipesStrip`, `RecipeDetailRatingsSection`, `cookNowSection`, the toolbar items (Save / Download / Share), the snackbar surface — all byte-identical. `RecipeDetailViewModel`'s public surface — `recipe`, `related`, `loadState`, `isSaved`, `isDownloaded`, `checkedIngredientIDs`, `snackbarMessage`, `userServings`, `userServingsRange`, `sourceServings`, `servingsScaleFactor`, `shouldShowServingWarning`, `blurbBlocks` — is unchanged (cap is applied inside `fetchAndParse()` before assignment; the property type signature `[ArticleBlock]` is identical). `RecipeDetailDependencies` protocol is unchanged. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Cap at the parsed `[ArticleBlock]` level instead of the HTML string.** Implementation-wise cleaner (count `.paragraph` cases by enum pattern match, slice the array at the Nth match) but the spec brief explicitly puts the parameter on the extractor (`extractRecipeBlurb(html:paragraphLimit:)`), which keeps the cap as a property of the extracted HTML and not a view-model concern. Rejected — extractor-side cap is the spec-prescribed shape, and the `<p>` boundary in HTML space is unambiguous (every `<p>` is a paragraph; no need to round-trip through the parser to count them). **(b) Default `paragraphLimit` to 1.** A single additional paragraph after the excerpt was considered as a more aggressive cap, but the live-API sampling showed that the first paragraph typically restates the recipe-card summary and paragraph 2 is where the actual narrative starts; defaulting to 1 would hide the narrative entirely. Rejected. **(c) Default `paragraphLimit` to 3 or higher.** Considered for blogs that front-load context across multiple paragraphs before getting to the meat, but the user's brief explicitly says "1-2 paragraphs" and the cap is a default — callers wanting a longer blurb can pass an explicit higher value. The recipe-detail fetch path uses the default and never overrides. Rejected. **(d) Reuse the WP source's "[…]"as the visible tail.** Considered for the "..." restoration, but WP's tail varies across the six `recognizedTruncationTails` entries (Unicode `[…]`, ASCII `[...]`, padded variants, bare ellipses) and reads as awkward UI furniture rather than natural sentence flow. A clean app-controlled "..." attached at display time decouples the visible tail from the WP source's truncation-marker drift. Rejected. **(e) Render the "..." as a separate `Text` view styled differently from the excerpt.** Considered for visual emphasis, but the natural mid-sentence trail reads best when concatenated to the same prose run (same font, same color, same baseline). The "More" button immediately after carries the affordance — the "..." is just the typographic cue. Rejected. **(f) Use a `Bool` flag like `hasExpandableBlurb` directly on the view model rather than a computed property.** Equivalent in observable behavior, but a derived computation off `blurbBlocks` keeps the source-of-truth single (the array) and avoids a write-when-write-also redundancy. Rejected — computed property is the cleaner shape. **REG-33 stays unchanged** — the contract "the recipe detail screen has exactly one Serves indicator (the stepper) AND the recipe excerpt has a tappable More/Less expand-collapse affordance with the `[…]`/`[...]` server-side truncation tail stripped from the collapsed-state display; `ArticleDetailView` is unaffected" continues to hold — the "..." tail is the APP's visible cue, not the WP server-side `[…]` (which is still stripped). **Live-API sampling result** (2026-05-31, recipes drawn from the production WordPress site): recipe 17615 → 0 `<p>` before WPRM card (no `entry-content` wrapper found — likely an archive or special page; the visibility gate correctly hides More); recipe 21238 → 42 `<p>` blocks (40 non-empty), 31 `<h*>` blocks; recipe 23034 → 33 `<p>` blocks (30 non-empty), 32 `<h*>` blocks. Both 21238 and 23034 are unambiguous "blog-style long narrative" recipes where the user's complaint about "too much" was the literal pre-cap behavior. With `paragraphLimit: 2`, the expanded blurb on both shrinks to 2 paragraphs + any heading/image that sat between them — a comfortable 1-screen-tall read. **Numbering note:** CL-130 / T-733 is the next free CL slot above CL-129 (T-732). REG-33 stays unchanged. **Spec/trace:** US-4 amendment — AC-4.12 amended via strike-through + replacement framing to document the 1-2 paragraph cap on the expanded blurb (replacing "everything before the recipe card") and the literal "..." tail on the collapsed display (in addition to the existing WP-source-strip). Implementing PR: T-733 (`fix/T-733-recipe-blurb-polish`). Test additions: **L1 support** — `extractRecipeBlurb(html:paragraphLimit:)` with a 5-paragraph fixture + `limit: 2` returns the first 2 paragraphs only; `limit: 2` on a 1-paragraph fixture returns 1 paragraph (no padding); `limit: 0` returns empty (degenerate-but-safe); `limit: 2` with mixed `[paragraph, heading, paragraph, image, paragraph]` returns the first 2 paragraphs plus the heading between them (heading preserved because it sits before the 2nd `</p>` close); the existing `extractRecipeBlurbCropsAtWPRMCard` / `extractRecipeBlurbReturnsFullContentWhenNoWPRMCard` / `extractRecipeBlurbReturnsEmptyWhenNoEntryContent` / `extractRecipeBlurbHandlesWPRMCardWithSiblingClasses` tests pass unchanged (default `paragraphLimit` of 2 covers the 2-paragraph fixtures these tests use). **L1 view-model** — `hasExpandableBlurb` returns `true` when `blurbBlocks` contains at least one `.paragraph` case; `false` when `blurbBlocks` is empty; `false` when `blurbBlocks` contains only `.heading` / `.image` / `.list` cases (no expansion-worthy paragraph content). The `e2e` label IS applied (inherited from T-732 / CL-129 — same user-facing surface).

- **CL-131 (US-4 amendment / T-734, 2026-05-31)** — **Unify recipe-blurb styling across the collapsed and expanded states so both render via the same `ArticleBlocksView` rich-block path that already drives `ArticleDetailView`. Bounded to `RecipeDetailView+Blurb.swift`. T-732 / CL-129 established the rich-block expanded surface; T-733 / CL-130 added the "..." tail + paragraph cap; T-734 closes the visual-consistency gap between the two states.** **The observed gap.** The collapsed surface rendered `Text(strippedExcerpt + "...")` (plain `String` from `viewModel.listItem.excerpt`) with `.dodFont(DODType.body)` + `.foregroundStyle(DODColor.labelSecondary)` (lighter gray), while the expanded surface rendered `ArticleBlocksView(blocks: viewModel.blurbBlocks)` which paints `.paragraph` blocks with `DODColor.label` (full label color) at `DODType.body` and carries `.tint(DODColor.accent)` for inline link runs in the `AttributedString` — plus the parser preserves `<strong>` → bold, `<em>` → italic spans in the `AttributedString`'s inline attributes. Net effect: tapping "More" shifted the color from gray to label color AND introduced bold / italic / accent-colored link runs that were absent in the collapsed surface. User read the two states as visually inconsistent — same content surface, two different typographic registers, which read as polish drift. **The fix — single source-file change.** `RecipeDetailView+Blurb.swift`'s collapsed branch now also renders through `ArticleBlocksView`. When `viewModel.hasExpandableBlurb == true` (the dominant case — recipes whose pre-WPRM HTML carries at least one `<p>` block, the T-733 / CL-130 gate), the collapsed surface renders the first paragraph block from `viewModel.blurbBlocks` via `ArticleBlocksView(blocks: [firstBlockWithEllipsisTail])` — same view struct, same per-block styling, same `DODColor.label` foreground, same inline bold / italic / link treatment. The literal `"..."` tail (T-733's view-only continuation cue) attaches to the first paragraph's `AttributedString` via a pure helper — `appendingEllipsis(to: ArticleBlock) -> ArticleBlock` — that pattern-matches `.paragraph(AttributedString)`, mutates a copy by `append(AttributedString("..."))`, and returns `.paragraph(updated)`. Non-`.paragraph` first blocks (impossible by the `hasExpandableBlurb` gate's `.paragraph`-only requirement, but safe under the pattern match) pass through unchanged. The `AttributedString.append` preserves the source string's inline attributes for the prose before the appended run — bold / italic / link spans that ended before the end of the paragraph remain bold / italic / linked; the appended `"..."` carries no attributes (plain run, inherits the paragraph's base styling). **The expanded branch is unchanged** — `ArticleBlocksView(blocks: viewModel.blurbBlocks)` already renders all 1-2 paragraphs via the T-733-capped extractor output. The "..." tail is NOT rendered in the expanded state (the rich blocks continue the prose). **The empty-`blurbBlocks` fallback (the rare case — recipes whose pre-WPRM region has no `<p>` blocks, e.g. recipe 17615 per the CL-130 live-API note).** When `blurbBlocks` is empty, `hasExpandableBlurb` is `false`, no More button is rendered, and the WP excerpt is the only blurb surface. T-734's fallback renders `Text(strippedExcerpt)` with `.dodFont(DODType.body)` and `.foregroundStyle(DODColor.label)` (full label color, matching `ArticleBlocksView`'s `.paragraph` treatment) — the lighter `.labelSecondary` is removed from the blurb-rendering path entirely. No `"..."` tail (nothing to expand into per the T-733 gate; a dangling tail with no More button would read as broken). **The first-paragraph-as-collapsed consequence (deliberate).** The pre-T-734 collapsed surface showed the WordPress `the_excerpt()` server-snipped text (typically 1-2 sentences, ~150-250 chars), which is shorter than a typical full first paragraph from `<p>` extraction (~300-500 chars). T-734's collapsed surface now shows the FULL first paragraph from `blurbBlocks[0]`, which is generally LONGER than the WP excerpt. **This is intentional.** The user's stated goal is visual consistency across the two states (same content surface, same styling); the trade-off is the collapsed view shows more text than it did pre-T-734. The user accepted this consequence in the brief — visual consistency wins over collapsed-state brevity. The "Less" button still collapses back from the full 1-2-paragraph expansion to the first paragraph + "..." + "More", so the expansion still adds the second paragraph + any heading/image/list between them. **Why first-paragraph-only and not first-N-sentences.** Considered cropping `blurbBlocks[0]`'s `AttributedString` at the first sentence boundary (`. ` followed by capital-letter heuristic) to better match the WP excerpt's brevity. Rejected — adds parser complexity, breaks the "same content unit as the expanded view" invariant (a sub-paragraph crop would render as a sentence fragment when bold/italic spans cross the boundary), and the user's brief explicitly prescribes "paragraph 1" not "first N sentences." The first-paragraph-as-unit shape keeps the rendering pure (no sub-paragraph splitting) and the `AttributedString` attributes intact. **Why inline-append the "..." vs separate-Text-below.** Considered rendering the first paragraph unchanged then a separate `Text("...")` below it. Rejected — reads as a separate sentence rather than a mid-paragraph continuation marker, and breaks the "natural prose flow" feel T-733 / CL-130 explicitly chose for the tail. Inline `AttributedString.append` keeps the "..." in the same line of prose as the last word of the paragraph, reading as the natural trail-off the user expected. **Why ASCII `"..."` and not Unicode `…` U+2026.** T-733 / CL-130 picked ASCII three-dot for the tail; T-734 preserves that choice for visual consistency with the existing tail behavior and to match the user's "..." preference stated in the spec brief. The Unicode `…` was considered for typographic cleanliness but the user's brief explicitly accepts ASCII per the existing T-733 convention. **What this does NOT change.** `extractRecipeBlurb(html:paragraphLimit:)` (T-733 / CL-130 — unchanged; same 2-paragraph cap). `hasExpandableBlurb` computed property (T-733 / CL-130 — unchanged; same `.paragraph`-presence gate). `strippingExcerptTruncationTail(from:)` pure helper (CL-129 — unchanged; the WP source's `[…]` / `[...]` tail is still stripped from the empty-`blurbBlocks` fallback path). `ArticleBlocksView` (T-732 / CL-129 — unchanged; same per-block styling for both consumers). `ArticleDetailView` (unchanged — articles already render via the rich-block path that recipes now match). `RecipeDetailMetaPills` (CL-129 fix 1 — unchanged). `RecipeServingsScaler`, `RecipeDetailHero`, `RecipeDetailQuickJump`, `RecipeDetailVideoSection`, `ingredientsSection`, `instructionsSection`, `RelatedRecipesStrip`, `RecipeDetailRatingsSection`, `cookNowSection`, the toolbar items (Save / Download / Share), the snackbar surface — all byte-identical. `RecipeDetailViewModel` public surface — unchanged (no new properties; the rendering decision lives in the view layer). `RecipeDetailDependencies` protocol — unchanged. `RecipeDetailViewModel+Fetch.swift` — unchanged (the fetch path's existing `blurbBlocks` population is reused; no new fetch). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Render `Text(strippedExcerpt + "...")` with `.foregroundStyle(DODColor.label)` (just swap the color, keep the plain-text shape).** Closes the color drift but NOT the bold/italic/link drift — the WP excerpt is plain text with HTML entities decoded, so bold / italic / linked runs that were marked up in the source HTML render as plain text in the excerpt. The user's complaint was the full styling difference (bold, italic, link color), not just the gray-vs-label distinction. Rejected — incomplete fix. **(b) Render the first paragraph as a plain `String` extracted from `blurbBlocks[0].paragraph.characters`.** Same shape as (a) — drops the inline attributes (bold / italic / link). Rejected for the same reason. **(c) Build a new `BlurbStyledText` view that takes a `String` excerpt + applies the same color/font as `ArticleBlocksView`'s `.paragraph` case.** Adds a parallel rendering path that has to be kept in sync with `ArticleBlocksView` over time (DRY violation). The clean fix is to reuse `ArticleBlocksView` itself for the collapsed case too. Rejected. **(d) Drop the "..." tail entirely now that the first paragraph is fully rendered (the rich blocks visibly continue across "More").** The "..." remains a clear typographic cue that more content lies behind the "More" button — without it, the collapsed surface reads as a complete paragraph and the "More" button reads as a less-discoverable expand-to-next-paragraph affordance. Keeping "..." preserves the T-733 / CL-130 mid-sentence-trail invariant. Rejected. **(e) Show the first 2 paragraphs collapsed (= same content as expanded).** Defeats the collapse-expand affordance entirely — there'd be nothing to expand into. Rejected. **(f) Show first paragraph collapsed + a fade-out gradient at the bottom.** Adds visual furniture (gradient mask) that doesn't compose with the dynamic-type / dark-mode contracts. Rejected — `"..."` is the cheaper cue. **REG-33 stays unchanged.** The contract "the recipe detail screen has exactly one Serves indicator (the stepper) AND the recipe excerpt has a tappable More/Less expand-collapse affordance with the `[…]`/`[...]` server-side truncation tail stripped from the collapsed-state display; `ArticleDetailView` is unaffected by this contract" continues to hold — the visual unification is additive to the contract (still tappable, still strips WP truncation, still leaves articles alone). **Numbering note:** CL-131 / T-734 is the next free CL slot above CL-130 (T-733). REG-33 unchanged. **Spec/trace:** US-4 amendment — AC-4.12 amended (the collapsed surface's content source becomes the first `blurbBlocks` paragraph when `hasExpandableBlurb == true`, rendered via the same `ArticleBlocksView` the expanded surface uses; the fallback for empty `blurbBlocks` renders the WP excerpt with `.label` color, not `.labelSecondary`). Implementing PR: T-734 (`fix/T-734-blurb-styling-unify`). Test additions: **L1 helper** — `appendingEllipsisTail(to:)` static helper on `RecipeDetailView` takes an `ArticleBlock`, pattern-matches `.paragraph(AttributedString)`, appends `"..."` to the `AttributedString` (preserving inline attribute runs for the prose before the appended text), returns `.paragraph(updated)`. Test fixtures: a `.paragraph` with no inline attributes + a `.paragraph` with a bold run mid-string (bold span is preserved in the output, "..." is plain). **L1 view-model integration** — re-uses the existing `RecipeDetailViewModelBlurbTests` fixtures: with non-empty `blurbBlocks`, the collapsed-display blocks (via a new `collapsedBlurbBlocks(from:)` static helper on `RecipeDetailView` for testability) = `prefix(1)` of `blurbBlocks` with the last block's `AttributedString` ending in `"..."`; with empty `blurbBlocks`, the helper returns `[]` (the view falls back to the plain-`Text` WP-excerpt path). **L4** — the existing `RecipeDetailViewSnapshotTests` baselines (currently empty per `__Snapshots__/.gitkeep`) will pixel-diff once re-recorded; the collapsed-state snapshot now shows the rich first paragraph rather than the WP excerpt. The `e2e` label IS applied (inherited from T-732 / CL-129 — same user-facing surface).

- **CL-132 (US-4 amendment / T-735, 2026-05-31)** — **Two coordinated fixes to the recipe-blurb expansion shipped by T-732 / T-733 / T-734: (a) filter `.image` cases out of the blurb-rendering path so the collapsed and expanded surfaces show TEXT ONLY (no Pinterest-preview / lazy-load placeholder photos that the live HTML embeds between intro paragraphs); (b) broaden the extractor's WPRM-boundary detection so recipes that nest the WPRM card inside a Gutenberg `wp-block-group` wrapper (or use a sibling `wprm-recipe` snippet div with a "Jump to Recipe" link as the de-facto card start) get a clean pre-card slice without the snippet-button + lazy-image junk leaking in. T-735 is bounded to `RecipeDetailView+Blurb.swift` (the view-level image filter) + `ArticleBodyExtractor.swift` (the broadened boundary detection). Spec contract REG-33 unchanged.** **The observed gaps (live-API audit, 2026-05-31).** Probe of 11 production recipe URLs (`?p=17615, 21238, 23034, 274, 5016, 736, 524, 23406, 19319, 21869, 18342`) on `dutchovendaddy.com` via the production extractor (`ArticleBodyExtractor.extractRecipeBlurb` + `ArticleHTMLParser.parse`) revealed: **(1) Image blocks dominate the blurb on 3 of 9 successfully-fetched recipes.** Recipe 274 returns 4 blocks = `[image, image, paragraph, paragraph]` — the first paragraph is at index 2; `collapsedBlurbBlocks(from:)` calls `blocks.first` (which is a `.image`), `appendingEllipsisTail(to:)` no-ops on `.image`, so the collapsed surface renders a Pinterest preview image (often a `data:image/svg+xml` placeholder while the lazy-load swap is pending) with no `...` tail and no visible affordance to expand. Recipe 5016 returns `[image, paragraph, paragraph]`; recipe 524 returns 6 blocks `[image, image, paragraph, image, list, paragraph]` — the same image-first problem plus images interleaved with the prose in the expanded view. The pre-T-735 collapsed render on these recipes was an opaque image where text was expected; the expanded render was a mix of Pinterest social-preview images, lazy-load svg placeholders, and actual prose, which read as junk-filled rather than a narrative intro peek. The user's stated brief: "blurb is a narrative intro peek, not a media gallery." **(2) The "Jump to Recipe" snippet div leaks into the blurb on 8 of 9 successfully-fetched WPRM recipes.** The site embeds `<div class="wprm-recipe wprm-recipe-snippet wprm-recipe-template-snippet-icons">` (with a "Jump to Recipe" button) ABOVE the actual `<div id="wprm-recipe-container-XXXXX" class="wprm-recipe-container">` card. The existing `sliceBeforeWPRMCard` matcher correctly targets only the `wprm-recipe-container` class token (NOT the broader `wprm-recipe` token) — so the slice STARTS from `entry-content` and includes the leading snippet div + a Gutenberg `wp-block-group` wrapper before the prose. The text-only filter from fix (1) drops the snippet div's tags + button text (it has no `<p>` so it contributes no `.paragraph` blocks), so this gap is INDIRECTLY closed by fix (1) — the only blocks the parser surfaces are paragraphs, and the snippet-div HTML structure produces zero paragraph blocks. No code change is needed for this specific symptom beyond fix (1); the extractor broadening per fix (b) below is for the rarer no-WPRM and Gutenberg-only paths. **The fix (a) — view-level text-only filter on the blurb-rendering path.** New computed property on `RecipeDetailView` extension: `textOnlyBlurbBlocks: [ArticleBlock]` returns `viewModel.blurbBlocks.filter` that admits `.paragraph` + `.heading` + `.list` and drops `.image`. The collapsed render (`canRenderCollapsedRich` gate via `collapsedBlurbBlocks(from:)`), the expanded render (`ArticleBlocksView(blocks: ...)`), AND the `hasExpandableBlurb` gate visibility check all switch to operating on this filtered set. The `hasExpandableBlurb` view-side gate is no longer a direct read of `viewModel.hasExpandableBlurb` — it becomes a local computed property `hasExpandableTextBlurb` that scans `textOnlyBlurbBlocks` for at least one `.paragraph`. A blurb whose ONLY content is `.image` cases now correctly hides the More button (nothing text-y to expand into). This is a view-level filter — the view-model's `blurbBlocks` array remains structurally faithful for any future consumer that wants the full block sequence (e.g., a future "show all media from the intro" affordance). `ArticleDetailView` is NOT touched — articles still render `.image` blocks as part of their rich body per dad's earlier #113 / US-37 / AC-37.3 contract. **The fix (b) — broadened boundary detection in `extractRecipeBlurb`.** `sliceBeforeWPRMCard` was the only boundary token. T-735 extends the boundary scan to ALSO match three additional class tokens, in priority order: `tasty-recipes`, `mv-create`, `wp-block-tasty-recipes-recipe-card` — these surface on the small fraction of recipes that use the Tasty Recipes or Meal Vista plugin instead of WPRM. A new helper `sliceBeforeRecipeCard(in:)` scans `<div ...>` tags for ANY of the four boundary tokens (`wprm-recipe-container` first, then the Tasty / MV / wp-block-tasty alternates) and crops at the FIRST match. The existing `sliceBeforeWPRMCard` becomes a thin alias that calls the broadened helper for source-compatibility (one inline call site, unchanged signature). Live-API audit found ZERO of the 11 sampled recipes use Tasty Recipes or Meal Vista today — the broadening is defensive, anticipating future plugin migrations or guest-post recipes on the same WP install. The audit also found ZERO recipes in the sample use Gutenberg block-comment markup (`<!-- wp:paragraph -->`); existing `<p>` parsing handles both classic and Gutenberg output uniformly via the `<p>...</p>` tag pair, so no additional Gutenberg handling is needed. **What this does NOT change.** `extractContentHTML` / `extract` (the article-body paths used by `ArticleDetailView`) are unchanged — articles continue to render images. The view-model's `blurbBlocks` array still contains the FULL block sequence including images (the filter is a view-side concern). `appendingEllipsisTail(to:)` / `collapsedBlurbBlocks(from:)` (T-734 / CL-131) are unchanged — they keep their pure-function shape; the collapsed gate just now feeds them the text-only filtered list. `strippingExcerptTruncationTail(from:)` (CL-129) is unchanged. `ArticleBlocksView` is unchanged — same per-block renderer for both consumers. `RecipeDetailMetaPills`, `RecipeServingsScaler`, `RecipeDetailHero`, `RecipeDetailQuickJump`, `RecipeDetailVideoSection`, `ingredientsSection`, `instructionsSection`, `RelatedRecipesStrip`, `RecipeDetailRatingsSection`, `cookNowSection`, the toolbar items, the snackbar surface — all byte-identical. `RecipeDetailViewModel` public surface — unchanged (no new properties; the filter is view-layer-only). `RecipeDetailViewModel+Fetch.swift` — unchanged. The `paragraphLimit: 2` default on `extractRecipeBlurb` is unchanged. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Filter at the extractor level (`extractRecipeBlurb` strips `<img>` / `<figure>` tags from the returned HTML).** Cleaner for callers but breaks the layered contract — the extractor's job is structural fidelity (return the full HTML slice), the parser's job is structural parsing (produce all blocks), and rendering choices belong in the view. Future consumers of `extractRecipeBlurb` (a future "show all media" feature, an L2 test that asserts the HTML structure) would lose access to the image blocks. Rejected. **(b) Filter at the view-model (`blurbBlocks` setter drops `.image` cases on assignment).** Same problem as (a) at a lower layer — the view-model becomes lossy. Rejected. **(c) Render image blocks at a smaller size or with a thumbnail treatment in the blurb.** Adds rendering complexity (new SwiftUI variant of `ArticleBlocksView` with a `compact: Bool` flag) for a surface the user explicitly described as text-only. Rejected. **(d) Move the filter onto a `static` helper on `RecipeDetailView` (parallel to `collapsedBlurbBlocks`) instead of a computed property.** Computed property + a `static` testable helper is the cleaner shape — the property uses the testable helper internally so L1 covers the filter logic via `static func textOnlyBlurbBlocks(from: [ArticleBlock]) -> [ArticleBlock]`. Both shapes coexist; the computed property is just sugar over the static helper. Adopted (this is the production shape; the rejection above is for the no-static variant). **(e) Add a Gutenberg `<!-- wp:paragraph -->` block-comment scanner to the extractor.** Live-API audit found ZERO recipes use Gutenberg block-comment markup today; the existing `<p>...</p>` scan covers both classic-editor and Gutenberg-rendered HTML uniformly (Gutenberg emits `<p>` tags wrapped in HTML comments — the comments are noise the existing scan ignores). Rejected — no observed need. **Live-API audit results (2026-05-31, `dutchovendaddy.com` production WordPress).** Pre-fix audit table (per-recipe block counts after `extractRecipeBlurb` + `ArticleHTMLParser.parse`, default cap=2): `21238 → blocks=2, paragraphs=2, images=0`; `23034 → 2/2/0`; `274 → 4/2/2 (image-first!)`; `5016 → 3/2/1 (image-first!)`; `736 → 2/2/0`; `524 → 6/2/3 (image-first + interleaved!)`; `23406 → 2/2/0 (no-WPRM fallback path)`; `19319 → 2/2/0`; `21869 → 2/2/0`. Two recipes (17615, 18342) returned HTTP 301 redirects to slug URLs that served a non-HTML payload — not a Swift extractor concern (the production path uses `URLSessionHTTPClient` which follows redirects per HTTP spec). After fix (a) the image counts collapse to 0 on every recipe; `hasExpandableTextBlurb` resolves true on all 9 successfully-fetched recipes (the user gets a More button on every recipe with at least one `<p>` of intro narrative). After fix (b) the audit is unchanged (no Tasty / Meal Vista recipes in the sample); the broadening is defensive. **REG-33 stays unchanged.** The contract "the recipe detail screen has exactly one Serves indicator (the stepper) AND the recipe excerpt has a tappable More/Less expand-collapse affordance with the `[…]`/`[...]` server-side truncation tail stripped from the collapsed-state display; `ArticleDetailView` is unaffected by this contract" continues to hold — image filtering + boundary broadening are additive to the existing contract. **Numbering note:** CL-132 / T-735 is the next free CL slot above CL-131 (T-734). REG-33 unchanged. **Spec/trace:** US-4 amendment — AC-4.12 amended (the collapsed and expanded blurb surfaces filter `.image` blocks before passing to `ArticleBlocksView`; the More button visibility gate operates on the filtered set so a blurb-with-only-images correctly hides the affordance; the extractor's boundary detection broadens to Tasty Recipes / Meal Vista / wp-block-tasty as additional crop boundaries beyond WPRM). Implementing PR: T-735 (`fix/T-735-blurb-images-coverage`). Test additions: **L1 view helper** — new `textOnlyBlurbBlocks(from: [ArticleBlock]) -> [ArticleBlock]` static helper on `RecipeDetailView` (mixed input `[paragraph, image, paragraph]` → returns `[paragraph, paragraph]`; image-only input → `[]`; empty input → `[]`); `hasExpandableTextBlurb`-equivalent assertion (filtered blocks containing zero `.paragraph` cases produce no More button — covered by the existing `hasExpandableBlurb` L1 tests adapted to the filtered set). **L1 extractor** — new `extractRecipeBlurbCropsAtTastyRecipesBoundary` fixture (a recipe page with `<div class="tasty-recipes ...">` as the card boundary returns the pre-card slice); the existing WPRM-boundary tests pass unchanged (WPRM remains the highest-priority boundary). **L4** — the existing `RecipeDetailViewSnapshotTests` baselines re-record naturally (the collapsed surface now never shows an image even on recipes where one is in `blurbBlocks`). The `e2e` label IS applied (inherited from T-732 / CL-129 — same user-facing surface).

- **CL-133 (US-4 amendment / T-736, 2026-05-31)** — **Recipe blurb is missing on the cache-hit re-open path: `viewModel.blurbBlocks` is view-local state that's only ever populated by `fetchAndParse()`, but `RecipeDetailViewModel.onAppear()` short-circuits to the cache-hit fast path when `dependencies.cachedRecipe(id:).hasDetail == true`, returning early via `loadState = .ready` WITHOUT calling `fetchAndParse()` or otherwise populating `blurbBlocks`. The view's empty-`blurbBlocks` fallback (`Text(strippedExcerpt)` per CL-131 / T-734 lines 124-134 of `RecipeDetailView+Blurb.swift`) renders correctly but visibly differs from the rich `ArticleBlocksView` render (no "..." tail, no More button, no inline bold/italic/link spans), and on every recipe the user has previously opened — i.e. nearly every recipe after a few minutes of browsing — the second-open and onwards renders the flat fallback rather than the rich blurb. The user perceived this as "lots of recipes" missing the rich blurb (framed initially as dark-mode broken, then verified by T-736 audit to be cache-path-specific and orthogonal to mode). T-736 audit (live API, 2026-05-31): `extractRecipeBlurb` + `ArticleHTMLParser.parse` succeed on 100% of 50 newest + 100% of 50 oldest + 99% of 100 mid-catalog recipes (one transient HTTP fail) — extractor coverage is effectively perfect, so the gap is exclusively in the view-model's cache-hit branch, not in the extractor or the parser. Bounded to `RecipeDetailViewModel.onAppear()` (the cache-hit branch) + `RecipeDetailViewModel+Fetch` (a new background-refresh helper). REG-33 unchanged.**

  **The mechanism.** `RecipeDetailViewModel.onAppear()` (Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/RecipeDetailViewModel.swift lines 117-148) reads `dependencies.cachedRecipe(id: listItem.id)`; when the cached recipe has `hasDetail == true` (the recipe was opened at least once before in this install — onboarding seed feeds, every prior nav-push, every Saved tap, every long-press preview, every widget deeplink), the body sets `recipe = cached` + `loadState = .ready` and returns. `fetchAndParse()` is the only call site that populates `blurbBlocks` (line 47-51 in `RecipeDetailViewModel+Fetch.swift`: `let blurbHTML = ArticleBodyExtractor.extractRecipeBlurb(html: html); blurbBlocks = blurbHTML.isEmpty ? [] : ArticleHTMLParser.parse(html: blurbHTML)`). The cache-hit branch never touches `blurbBlocks` — the property holds its initializer default `[]` for the lifetime of the view. The view's `excerptText` builder (RecipeDetailView+Blurb.swift line 82+) computes `textOnlyBlocks` from `viewModel.blurbBlocks` (always `[]` on cache hits), so `canRenderCollapsedRich = false`, `hasExpandableTextBlurb = false`, and the rendering falls through to the `Text(strippedExcerpt)` fallback at line 124-134. The fallback uses `DODColor.label` + `DODType.body` (T-734 / CL-131 — visually matches the rich path's color register) so the dark-mode appearance is correct, but the visible-content gap remains: no inline rich attributes, no "..." continuation cue, no More button.

  **Why the audit data wasn't enough to spot this earlier.** T-735 / CL-132's audit ran the extractor directly against fetched HTML and found 9 of 11 sampled recipes had non-empty post-filter block sequences — that test correctly verifies the extractor / parser path. It did NOT exercise the view-model's `onAppear()` lifecycle, which is where the cache-hit branch lives. T-736's broader audit (50 newest + 50 oldest + 100 mid-catalog) confirms the extractor side is at 100% coverage, isolating the gap to the cache-hit code path.

  **The fix — background-refresh on cache hit (recipe kind only).** `onAppear()`'s cache-hit branch for `cached.kind == .recipe` (line 128-131) now ALSO kicks off a background `await refreshBlurbBlocks(forCanonicalURL: canonicalURL)` after the immediate `loadState = .ready` + `loadRelated` calls. The new helper `refreshBlurbBlocks(forCanonicalURL:)` on `RecipeDetailViewModel+Fetch`: (1) gates on `await dependencies.isOnline()` so offline cache-hits skip the network call entirely (graceful — the fallback `Text` path is acceptable when there's no fresh data anyway); (2) calls `try? await dependencies.fetchHTML(for: canonicalURL)` and ignores fetch errors (transient failures shouldn't disturb the already-rendered cached view); (3) on success runs the same `ArticleBodyExtractor.extractRecipeBlurb` + `ArticleHTMLParser.parse` pipeline `fetchAndParse()` uses; (4) assigns `blurbBlocks` only if the result is non-empty (don't overwrite a successful population with an empty result if a later refresh fails). The view's `@Observable`/`ObservableObject` binding picks up the `blurbBlocks` assignment and re-renders the blurb surface from fallback-`Text` to rich-`ArticleBlocksView` with the "..." + More affordance. The pattern matches `loadRatingsAndComments` (line 147 — same cached-first-then-refresh shape).

  **Why not persist `blurbBlocks` in the cache.** Considered extending `Recipe` to carry a `blurbHTML: String?` or `blurbBlocks: [ArticleBlock]` field that `mergeDetail` would persist. Rejected — (a) it expands the public `Recipe` surface area (Codable encoding, SwiftData migration if applicable, all consumers that initialize a `Recipe` literal would need to thread the new field through); (b) `ArticleBlock` carries an `AttributedString` which is not trivially `Codable` (would need a custom encoding round-trip); (c) the live-blurb refresh on cache hit costs one HTTP fetch per recipe-open while online — the same fetch the `fetchAndParse` path already makes for fresh recipes; the user's existing TTI experience is unchanged (the cached view renders instantly; the blurb fills in on a background task); (d) when offline the cached view still renders (just via the fallback), preserving the offline-first contract.

  **Why not always call `fetchAndParse()` on every open.** Considered dropping the cache-hit branch entirely and letting every `onAppear()` re-run the full JSON-LD parse. Rejected — the cache-hit fast path is load-bearing for the snappy navigation feel (cached recipes show instantly with all structured content while a network call would block render); the only field missing from the cached payload is `blurbBlocks`, and that's a strictly-additive UI enhancement that can layer on top of the cached render without delaying it.

  **Why gate the refresh on `isOnline()`.** Offline cache-hits already render correctly via the fallback path (the user has the WP excerpt + title + cached hero + cached ingredients + cached instructions; the blurb is the narrative-intro nice-to-have, not load-bearing for cook-mode flows). Issuing a network call we know will fail wastes battery + adds spurious error-log noise; gating on `isOnline()` keeps the offline experience identical to today.

  **Why fail-silent on fetch errors.** `try?` + drop. The cached view is already on screen; surfacing a snackbar or alert for a background refresh would feel like the app is broken in a context where it manifestly is not. The next online open re-attempts the refresh. This matches the existing `loadRatingsAndComments` no-op-on-failure pattern (REG-14 / AC-14.6 — cached state in place on network failure).

  **Why guard the post-fetch assignment on non-empty.** A transient parse failure that returns `[]` would otherwise overwrite a previous successful population with empty, defeating the purpose. The guard is: `if !newBlocks.isEmpty { blurbBlocks = newBlocks }`. (The view-model lives for the duration of the `RecipeDetailView`; a second `onAppear` from a deep-link push would re-run the refresh anyway, so worst case is one stale render — but the guard means an empty parse never *downgrades* a previously-successful render.)

  **The fallback rendering check (Step 4 of the T-736 spec brief).** Per the brief, audit `RecipeDetailView+Blurb.swift` line 124-134 (the empty-`blurbBlocks` fallback path) for dark-mode visual register: confirmed pre-T-736 already uses `DODColor.label` (NOT `.labelSecondary` — that was fixed in T-734 / CL-131) + `DODType.body`. Same color + font register as `ArticleBlocksView`'s `.paragraph` case. No additional change needed. The dark-mode "feels broken" perception is purely the visible-content gap (no rich attributes, no "..." tail, no More button) which the background-refresh fix closes by populating `blurbBlocks` from the network — once filled, the view re-renders via the rich `ArticleBlocksView` path with the full styling.

  **What this does NOT change.** `extractRecipeBlurb` / `ArticleHTMLParser.parse` / `ArticleBlocksView` — unchanged. `appendingEllipsisTail(to:)` / `collapsedBlurbBlocks(from:)` / `textOnlyBlurbBlocks(from:)` (T-734 / T-735) — unchanged. `RecipeDetailView+Blurb.swift` — unchanged (the empty-`blurbBlocks` fallback path stays exactly as T-734 / CL-131 / T-735 / CL-132 left it; the fix populates the array from outside the view). `RecipeDetailViewModel`'s public surface — unchanged (no new public properties; the new helper is private). `RecipeDetailDependencies` protocol — unchanged (re-uses the existing `fetchHTML` / `isOnline` dependency methods). `Recipe` data model — unchanged (no new fields, no persistence change). `ArticleDetailView` (US-37) — unchanged. The fresh-fetch path (`fetchAndParse`) — unchanged (no cache hit means no behavior change). The article cache-hit path (`cached.kind == .article` line 132-138) — unchanged (articles persist `articleBodyHTML` in `Recipe` already, so cached articles render rich on re-open without the background refresh — the asymmetry between recipes and articles is now closed via the new path).

  **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Persist `blurbBlocks` in `Recipe` (or in a separate cache table keyed by recipe ID).** See "Why not persist" above — surface area + AttributedString-encoding cost + offline cache-hits already render via fallback. **(b) Persist the raw blurb HTML string (`blurbHTML: String?` on `Recipe`) and re-parse on cache hit.** Less invasive than persisting `[ArticleBlock]` but still expands the `Recipe` surface area and adds a SwiftData migration. The background-refresh pattern reuses the existing dependency contract with zero data-model touch. Rejected. **(c) Run the background refresh ALSO on fresh `fetchAndParse()` paths to keep the code shape uniform.** Pointless — `fetchAndParse` already populates `blurbBlocks` directly from the same fetched HTML, and adding a second redundant fetch wastes a request. The refresh helper is specifically for the cache-hit path where the HTML wasn't fetched in the first place. Rejected. **(d) Issue the refresh BEFORE the cache hit renders.** Defeats the cache-fast-path purpose (the view would block on a network fetch even when offline). Rejected. **(e) Show a placeholder "loading blurb..." state during the refresh.** Adds visible UI motion for a background nice-to-have refresh. The fallback path is already a valid render; swapping in the rich render when ready is the cleaner UX. Rejected. **(f) Update the refresh to also re-trigger `loadRelated` if a category-id change is detected in the freshly-parsed JSON-LD.** Out of scope — `loadRelated` is already called on cache hit with the cached category-id; T-736 is bounded to blurb-blocks population. A future refresh-related-on-detail-change pass would be its own CL.

  **L1 test additions.** **`RecipeDetailViewModelBlurbRefreshTests`** (new file in `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Tests/DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests/`): (a) `cacheHitWithOnlineDependencyPopulatesBlurbBlocksFromBackgroundFetch` — seeds a `FakeRecipeDetailDependencies` with a cached `Recipe` (`hasDetail == true`, `kind == .recipe`), `isOnline() == true`, and `fetchHTML` returning known-good HTML with `<div class="entry-content"><p>blurb para</p><div class="wprm-recipe-container">...</div></div>`; awaits `onAppear()`; asserts `blurbBlocks` is non-empty after the background refresh completes. (b) `cacheHitWithOfflineDependencySkipsRefreshAndLeavesBlurbBlocksEmpty` — same cached recipe but `isOnline() == false`; asserts `blurbBlocks` stays `[]` (offline path is correctly silent). (c) `cacheHitWithFetchErrorLeavesBlurbBlocksEmptyAndDoesNotSurfaceError` — `isOnline() == true` but `fetchHTML` throws; asserts `blurbBlocks` stays `[]` AND `snackbarMessage` is unchanged (no user-visible error from a background refresh). (d) `cacheHitWithEmptyParseResultDoesNotOverwritePreviousNonEmptyValue` — pre-set `blurbBlocks = [.paragraph(AttributedString("seeded"))]` before `onAppear`, then trigger a refresh that returns HTML with no `<p>` blocks; assert the pre-set value survives (the guard prevents downgrade). (e) `freshFetchPathStillPopulatesBlurbBlocks` — sanity test that the existing fresh-fetch behavior is unchanged (cache miss → `fetchAndParse` → `blurbBlocks` populated as before).

  **L4 / L5 impact.** L4 snapshot baselines for `RecipeDetailViewSnapshotTests` are unchanged — the snapshot tests use a pre-seeded `RecipeDetailViewModel` with `blurbBlocks` populated directly, so the visible surface in the snapshot already matches the post-refresh rich-blurb state. L5 seed-journey tests are unchanged — the journey tests assert the initial onAppear-and-render path, and the cached recipes they exercise are seeded with `hasDetail == false` so they take the fresh-fetch branch (always did, still do).

  **Numbering note:** CL-133 / T-736 is the next free CL slot above CL-132 (T-735). REG-33 unchanged.

  **Spec/trace:** US-4 amendment — AC-4.12 amended (the cache-hit branch of `onAppear()` for `cached.kind == .recipe` now ALSO triggers a background blurb-blocks refresh via `refreshBlurbBlocks(forCanonicalURL:)`, gated on `dependencies.isOnline()`, fail-silent on fetch error, non-empty-guarded on parse-result assignment; the resulting `blurbBlocks` re-renders the blurb surface from the fallback `Text` path to the rich `ArticleBlocksView` path on re-open). Implementing PR: T-736 (`fix/T-737-blurb-coverage-broad` — branch name preserves the original T-737 marker used during the implementation session; every spec/clarification/task reference now reads T-736 per the CL-133 numbering). The `e2e` label IS applied (inherited from T-732 / CL-129 — same user-facing surface).
- **CL-134 (US-32 amendment / T-738, 2026-05-31)** — **Graduate DUT-14 ("About Ned section — shorter paragraph + photo") by replacing T-550's placeholder About destination with the verbatim DUT-14 copy + Ned's photo bundled as a local asset.
