Bounded to `SettingsView.swift` (the view rewrite, in place — `SettingsAboutPlaceholderView` keeps its name to preserve the call-site signature; the file_length cap is respected because the rewritten body is shorter than the placeholder it replaces) + `App/Assets.xcassets/AboutNed.imageset/` (the new image asset, single-file `about-ned.jpg` at 1x with implicit 2x/3x derivation per the asset-catalog universal-image convention). Supersedes T-552 ("About Me WP REST fetch") — the About copy is now embedded verbatim from DUT-14, not fetched dynamically; the WP REST `/wp/v2/pages?slug=about-me` path that T-552 would have stood up is no longer needed.** **The decision context (Linear DUT-14, 2026-05-31).** The pre-T-738 placeholder rendered "Coming soon — fetched from /about-me/" — a v1 deferral that lingered while T-552 sat unscheduled. The user (Spencer, relaying dad's input via DUT-14) supplied the exact 5-sentence intro paragraph: *"Hi I'm Ned, the Dutch Oven Daddy! I'm a full-time computer nerd and part-time cook. My passion is cast iron cooking with tips, tricks, and delicious recipes. I love using my recipes to bring together family and friends. I believe everything is made better in cast iron!"* — short enough to render comfortably on iPhone width without requiring scroll, intimate enough to read as Ned's voice rather than the placeholder marketing-bot register. The supplied photo (`https://www.dutchovendaddy.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/Ned-Dutch-Oven-Daddy-square.jpg`, 249×249 JPEG, ~29KB, kitchen background, Ned holding a black cast-iron dutch oven) is the square crop dad provided for the magazine-sidebar layout — fallback to the wider 2026/05/`ned-adams-dutch-oven-daddy-cooking.jpg` if the square version 404s. **The layout — magazine sidebar (`HStack(alignment: .top)`, 120pt leading image + paragraph wrapping right).** Picked over a centered-top VStack-with-portrait-above-paragraph because (a) the dutchovendaddy.com About page itself uses an image-left / paragraph-right layout for the same content unit; (b) at iPhone 14 width (390pt) a 120pt image leaves 270pt for the paragraph minus DODSpacing rhythm, comfortably enough for 4-5 line-wrapped sentences; (c) the rest of the app's card/thumbnail rhythm uses `RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: DODSpacing.sm)` for image clipping (see `RecipeCard`'s hero treatment), so the About avatar matches that visual register rather than the round-avatar treatment a `Circle()` clip would impose. **The asset placement — `App/Assets.xcassets/AboutNed.imageset/`.** Lives in the app target's asset catalog (NOT inside `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/`) because (a) the `DODFeatureFeed` package has no asset-catalog resources today (the design-system colors are the only catalog under `Packages/`); (b) the photo is one-off content owned by the app, not a reusable design primitive worth promoting into `DODDesignSystem`; (c) `Image("AboutNed")` from within a package source file resolves against `Bundle.main` automatically when there's no module-local resource of the same name — the SwiftUI `Image(_:bundle:)` overload with `bundle: nil` defaults to `.main` for the asset name lookup. The `Contents.json` declares the universal-image format with `1x` carrying the actual file + implicit `2x`/`3x` slots empty; iOS scales the 1x at runtime. **Pinning the copy — L1 test.** A new L1 test `aboutNedView_copy_matchesDUT14Verbatim` reads the rendered Text node's string and asserts equality against the exact DUT-14 paragraph stored as a `static let aboutNedCopy: String` constant. Any future paraphrase trips the test, surfaces in CI, and forces the spec author to graduate a new amendment + bump CL. The pin is the cheapest insurance against drift on a string the user explicitly typed up. **The asset-existence sanity test.** A separate L1 test `aboutNedImageAsset_existsInBundle` calls `UIImage(named: "AboutNed", in: .main, with: nil)` and asserts non-nil — catches the regression where the asset is removed or renamed and the view silently falls back to a transparent box. **What this does NOT change.** `SettingsView`'s row order (metric units, notifications, appearance, share format, voice gender, clear cache, telemetry, iCloud sync, About, version footer) — unchanged. `SettingsViewModel` public surface — unchanged (no new properties). `SettingsDependencies` — unchanged. `SettingsView+CloudSync.swift` — unchanged. `FeedView` gear-icon entry — unchanged. The NavigationLink's `accessibilityIdentifier("settings-link-about")` — unchanged. The destination view's `accessibilityIdentifier("settings-about-placeholder")` is renamed to `"settings-about"` (the "placeholder" suffix no longer describes the surface; tests + UI tests update the identifier in the same commit). `navigationTitle("About")` + `.navigationBarTitleDisplayMode(.inline)` — unchanged. `DODType.body` + `DODColor.label` + `DODColor.surface` + `DODSpacing.md` + `DODSpacing.sm` — all existing tokens; no new design primitives. **What this supersedes.** T-552 ("About Me WP REST fetch") is marked superseded. The work T-552 would have done — stand up `WPRestClient.pages(slug:)`, add `CachedAboutMe` schema entry, write the HTML-stripping logic, manage offline-fallback — is no longer needed because the copy is short, static, owned by the user, and embeds cleanly into the binary. The dynamic-fetch path's hypothetical benefit (the user can update the About copy on the WP server without an app release) is rejected for v1: the copy IS the user's voice, the user prefers to type it directly into the spec / Linear issue, and the app-release cadence (every 1-2 weeks via TestFlight) is fast enough to accommodate any copy revisions. If a future need surfaces for dynamic content (e.g., a "What's New" surface tied to the WP roadmap page), it gets its own task with its own fetch pattern — not retro-fitted onto About. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Keep `SettingsAboutPlaceholderView`'s name verbatim** — the "Placeholder" suffix lies once T-738 ships; the view is no longer a placeholder. Renamed in this PR via a single-step in-file rewrite: `SettingsAboutPlaceholderView` → `AboutNedView` (struct rename + call-site at the `NavigationLink { ... }` destination + the existing test if any). The accessibility identifier `"settings-about-placeholder"` → `"settings-about"` to match. Both renames are mechanical and localized to one file. Adopted (this is the production shape). **(b) Pull the copy from the WP REST `/about-me/` endpoint** — the original T-552 path; rejected per the supersession reasoning above. **(c) Fetch the photo URL from WP every launch** — saves binary size (~29KB) at the cost of a network hop on every About-row tap + offline-fallback complexity; rejected — the photo is small and rarely changes; bundling it is the cheaper trade. **(d) Use a `Circle()` clip for the avatar instead of `RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: DODSpacing.sm)`** — the round-avatar treatment reads as "user profile" rather than "magazine sidebar portrait"; the rest of the app's image-with-text composition uses `RoundedRectangle` for thumbnails (`RecipeCard` hero, `WidgetCard.Hero`). Adopting the same clip on the About avatar keeps the visual register consistent. Rejected. **(e) Wrap the layout in a `ScrollView`** — for the 5-sentence paragraph the content fits comfortably on a single iPhone 14 screen (390×844 minus nav bar + safe-area insets ≈ 760pt of content area; 120pt image height + ~4-5 lines of body text at `DODType.body` ≈ 200pt total). A `ScrollView` adds furniture without earning it. Rejected. **(f) Place the asset inside `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/Resources/`** — requires a `resources: [.copy("Resources")]` declaration in `Package.swift` + the `Image("AboutNed", bundle: .module)` lookup variant; works fine but adds Package.swift churn for one image that's effectively app-content rather than feature-package-content. The app target's asset catalog is the cleaner home. Rejected. **(g) Use the `Markdown` view in iOS 15+ to render a bolded "**Hi I'm Ned, the Dutch Oven Daddy!**" opener** — the DUT-14 copy is unformatted prose; introducing Markdown semantics would invent a styling the source doesn't carry. The body-text-only render is the truthful presentation. Rejected. **Numbering note.** CL-134 / T-738 is the next free CL slot above CL-132 (T-735). The parallel L5-fix agent (ad62c79e6...) on `fix/T-737-blurb-coverage-broad` is bumping a different CL/T pair (CL-134 / T-737 per the spawn brief — different work surface, different numbering) so CL-134 should land cleanly without collision. If the agent finds CL-134 already taken on main when this PR opens, this branch rebases to CL-134 / T-739 and updates references. **Spec/trace:** US-32 amendment — AC-32.3 amended via strike-through + replacement framing (the About destination no longer renders the placeholder copy; it renders the embedded DUT-14 paragraph + the bundled `AboutNed` asset); new AC-32.6 captures the magazine-sidebar layout + the asset path + the verbatim-copy pinning contract. T-552 marked superseded inline in the US-32 section. Implementing PR: T-738 (`feat/T-738-about-ned`). Test additions: **L1 view** — `aboutNedView_copy_matchesDUT14Verbatim` reads the static `aboutNedCopy` constant and asserts byte-equality against the verbatim DUT-14 paragraph; `aboutNedImageAsset_existsInBundle` calls `UIImage(named: "AboutNed", in: .main, with: nil)` and asserts non-nil; `aboutNedView_rendersWithoutCrash` instantiates the view in a hosted snapshot environment and asserts no runtime crash. **L4** — new `aboutNedView_light_defaultDynamicType` + `aboutNedView_dark_defaultDynamicType` snapshot baselines under `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Tests/DODFeatureFeedTests/__Snapshots__/`. Any stale `SettingsAboutPlaceholderView` snapshots are deleted in the same commit (the `__Snapshots__/.gitkeep` placeholder means no real baselines existed yet — `record: .missing` lays the new ones down on first run). No `e2e` label — single feature-package view change + one asset addition; no nav / persistence / composition-root touch (FeedView's NavigationLink to SettingsView is unchanged; the in-Settings NavigationLink to the renamed destination is unchanged). Estimate: ~30 min spec + ~20 min impl + ~10 min verify ≈ 1h total.

- **CL-135 (US-32 amendment / T-741, 2026-06-02, DUT-18)** — **Revise the T-738 / CL-134 About Ned layout from the magazine sidebar to a vertically-stacked centered hero: the `AboutNed` image moves from a 120pt leading position in an `HStack(alignment: .top)` (paragraph wrapping to its right) to a 160pt image centered horizontally on TOP of a `VStack(alignment: .center)`, with the verbatim DUT-14 paragraph below it at full width, leading-aligned. Bounded to `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/AboutNedView.swift` (the view body restructure + the doc-comment layout description + image-size bump) — no asset change, no copy change, no accessibility-identifier change.** **The decision context (Linear DUT-18, 2026-06-02).** T-738 shipped the magazine-sidebar layout (CL-134 picked it over a centered-top VStack, reasoning that the dutchovendaddy.com About page uses an image-left / paragraph-right unit). Spencer's verdict on the live TestFlight build was that the right-side wrap does not read as well as a centered-above arrangement: at iPhone width the ~270pt the paragraph gets beside a 120pt image produces a narrow, ragged 4-5 line column that reads awkwardly. DUT-14 — the original brief — had explicitly named the fallback: *"OR top-anchored above the paragraph if the wrap does not read well."* This CL graduates that fallback. **The layout — `VStack(alignment: .center)`, 160pt centered image on top + full-width leading paragraph below.** The image carries an explicit `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .center)` after the clip so it sits centered regardless of its intrinsic width, and the paragraph carries `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)` so it spans the full content width left-aligned (body prose reads better left-aligned than centered). A trailing `Spacer(minLength: 0)` keeps the content top-anchored within the `.frame(maxHeight: .infinity, alignment: .top)` container, exactly as the T-738 body did. **The image size — 120pt → 160pt.** The centered arrangement gives the image more visual presence and it no longer has to share the row with text, so it earns the bump. 160pt on a 390pt-wide iPhone is comfortably inside the content width with `DODSpacing.md` padding on each side (390 − 2×16 = 358pt available; 160pt centered leaves generous margin). The exact size is a polish call per the ticket (144pt was the named fallback if 160pt feels too large on-device) — eyeball on-device and drop to 144pt if needed; the snapshot baseline records whichever ships. **What stays the same.** The verbatim DUT-14 copy (`static let aboutNedCopy`) — unchanged, still pinned by the L1 `aboutNedView_copy_matchesDUT14Verbatim` test (this CL does NOT touch the copy, so the pin must stay green). The `AboutNed` image asset (`App/Assets.xcassets/AboutNed.imageset/about-ned.jpg`) — unchanged. The `RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: DODSpacing.sm, style: .continuous)` clip shape — unchanged (CL-134 considered and rejected `Circle()`; that reasoning still holds — the image stays a rounded-rect portrait, not a round avatar). The `accessibilityIdentifier("settings-about")` — unchanged. The `.accessibilityHidden(true)` on the image — unchanged. `DODType.body` + `DODColor.label` + `DODColor.surface` + `DODSpacing.md` + `DODSpacing.sm` — all existing tokens; no new design primitives. `navigationTitle("About")` + `.navigationBarTitleDisplayMode(.inline)` — unchanged. `SettingsView`'s call site (the About `NavigationLink` destination) — unchanged. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Keep the magazine sidebar and tighten the spacing.** The narrow-column readability problem is intrinsic to a ~270pt paragraph beside an image at iPhone width; no spacing tweak fixes the ragged wrap. DUT-14 already named the centered-above fallback as the answer. Rejected. **(b) Center the paragraph text too (`.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .center)` on the Text).** Centered multi-line body prose reads worse than left-aligned — ragged left edges hurt scannability. The image is centered (it's a single focal element); the paragraph is leading-aligned (it's prose). Rejected. **(c) Switch the clip to `Circle()` now that the image is the focal point.** The ticket floated this as an option. Rejected for the same reason CL-134 rejected it: the rest of the app's image-with-text composition uses `RoundedRectangle` (`RecipeCard` hero, `WidgetCard.Hero`); a round avatar reads as "user profile" and breaks the visual register. Keeping the rounded-rect is the lower-churn, more-consistent call. **(d) Bump the image larger than 160pt (e.g. 200pt).** Too dominant on iPhone width — it pushes the paragraph below the fold on smaller devices and reads as a splash screen rather than an About blurb. 160pt (fallback 144pt) is the sweet spot. Rejected. **Numbering note.** CL-135 / T-741 is the next free CL slot above the two parallel-merged CL-134 entries (T-738 About Ned + T-737 comments-hardening, which collided on CL-134 during parallel work and were never renumbered). T-741 is the next free task id above T-740 (the renumbered Push-entitlement entry). **Snapshot / L4.** The two `SettingsViewSnapshotTests` baselines for AboutNed (`test_aboutNedView_light_defaultDynamicType` + `test_aboutNedView_dark_defaultDynamicType`) re-record to the new centered layout. No stale baseline exists on `origin/main` to delete — the DODFeatureFeed `__Snapshots__/` directory carries only `.gitkeep` (the committed DODFeatureFeed baselines live only on the unmerged `feat/dut-5-voice-quality` branch and predate the AboutNed tests), and these UIKit-gated `assertSnapshot(record: .missing)` tests do not execute under plain `swift test` on macOS (they run only via the iOS-Simulator xcodebuild path), so `record: .missing` lays the new layout's PNGs down cleanly on the first simulator run. The test's descriptive comment is updated from "magazine-sidebar layout. Locks the 120pt leading image clip + the right-side paragraph wrap" to the centered-hero description. **Spec/trace:** US-32 amendment — AC-32.6 amended via strike-through + replacement framing (the magazine-sidebar `HStack(alignment: .top)` sub-clause is struck; the replacement is the vertically-stacked centered `VStack(alignment: .center)` with the 160pt centered image on top + full-width leading paragraph). The verbatim-copy pin + asset path + token usage in AC-32.6 are unchanged. Implementing PR: T-741 (`ned/dut-18-about-image-center`). No new tests (the L1 verbatim-copy pin + asset-existence + renders-without-crash tests from T-738 all still apply unchanged; the change is layout-only). No `e2e` label — single feature-package view-body change; no nav / persistence / composition-root / public-API touch. Estimate: ~15 min spec + ~10 min impl + ~5 min verify ≈ 30 min total (XS).

- **CL-134 (DUT-7 / US-14 / AC-14.4, 2026-05-31, T-737)** — **Code-side hardening + on-device diagnostics for the Urgent "comments are broken on the installed TestFlight build" report. This is observability + defensive medicine layered on top of PR #87 (REG-27); it is NOT a confirmed root-cause fix. The true cause is unconfirmed, several leading hypotheses are server-side (WordPress) and cannot be fixed in app code, and #87 may already have resolved it — the owner must retest on-device first.** (Numbered CL-134 / T-737 after a parallel-merge collision: PR #128 claimed CL-133 / T-736 for the cache-hit blurb-refresh fix while this work was in flight; both are independent and the renumber resolves the clash.) **What #87 (REG-27) already shipped (do not duplicate or undo).** (1) `URLSessionHTTPClient` stamps a `User-Agent` header on every outgoing request (built from `Bundle.main`) so a WordPress security plugin (Wordfence) doesn't 4xx an empty-UA client — hypothesis H3 mitigation. (2) `RecipeDetailViewModel.commentErrorSnackbar(for:)` maps each `WPClientError` case to a distinct user-visible snackbar, including the bare HTTP status code on `.httpStatus(code)` — hypothesis H1 mitigation. (3) #87's code-walk verdicts: H4 (Keychain miss) "ruled out", H5 (ATS) "ruled out", H2 (WP moderation) untestable from code. REG-27 explicitly left H1..H5 open pending an on-device snackbar capture. **What T-737 adds (the remaining code-side gaps).** **(1) No silent failure now surfaces WordPress's OWN reason, not just the status code.** Pre-T-737, `WPCommentsClient.postComment` discarded the response body on a non-2xx and the snackbar showed only the number. T-737 parses WP's REST error envelope (`{"code","message","data":{"status"}}`) on every non-2xx via a new `extractWPErrorMessage(from:)` helper and threads the message through a new ADDITIVE `WPClientError.httpStatusWithBody(Int, message:)` case; the snackbar maps it to "Couldn't post your comment (server said `<code>`): `<WP's reason>`". A Wordfence-style HTML challenge body (non-JSON) is tag-stripped via a minimal `stripHTML(_:)` helper + clamped to 140 chars so a security-plugin block still surfaces something the owner recognizes ("Access Denied ..."). A bodyless or message-less error (`{}`, blank `message`) falls back to REG-27's status-only `.httpStatus(code)`. The case is additive precisely so the four OTHER `WPClientError.httpStatus(_:)` producers — `WPRestClient`, `ImageLoader`, `RecipePageFetcher`, `WPRMRatingsClient` — stay byte-identical; only the comment-POST path opts into body surfacing, which keeps the blast radius off the in-flight DUT-5 / DUT-6 branches. **(2) Author-identity guard (hypothesis #4 — re-opened from #87's "ruled out").** #87 ruled H4 out because `GuestIdentityStore.load()` returns `nil` when either the name OR the email field is *missing* from the Keychain. But a row can exist with an *empty / whitespace* string (e.g. a future onboarding sheet that doesn't validate, or a partial save) — `load()` returns that as a non-nil identity, and posting `author_email=""` makes WordPress 400 the request, which is exactly the "tap, nothing happens" symptom. T-737 closes the residual gap at two layers: (a) `RecipeDetailViewModel.submitComment()` (extracted into `RecipeDetailViewModel+CommentSubmit.swift` to keep the parent file under the 400-line SwiftLint cap) trims the loaded identity and, if name or email is blank, re-gates behind the US-15 guest-identity sheet (`requiresGuestIdentity = true`) + shows "Add your name and email to post a comment." + preserves the draft for retry — instead of firing a doomed POST; it also trims whitespace off a *valid* identity before posting so we never send `" sam@example.com "`. The `comments` / `commentDraft` / `isSubmittingComment` setters are relaxed from `private(set)` to `internal(set)` so the extracted extension can mutate them (public read-only contract unchanged). (b) `WPCommentsClient.postComment` rejects an empty / whitespace name or email with a typed `WPClientError.underlying` *before* the request fires — a network-layer backstop for any caller that bypasses the view model. The `GuestIdentityStore` contract itself is unchanged (its partial-state-as-nil behavior is correct; the empty-string case is guarded above it). **(3) Structured on-device diagnostics so the owner can pin the actual hypothesis.** `WPCommentsClient.postComment` now emits three `DODLog.network` breadcrumbs under subsystem `com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp` / category `network`: on send — "comment POST → `<url>` (post=`<id>`)"; on a non-2xx — "comment POST failed status=`<code>` message=`<wp message>`"; on success — "comment POST ok status=`<code>` id=`<id>` wpStatus=`<approved|hold|...>`". Privacy-safe: only the URL, status, post id, comment id, and WP moderation status are marked `.public`; the comment body and the author name/email are NEVER logged (constitution §9). The owner foregrounds the TestFlight build with Console.app attached (filter on the subsystem), posts a comment, and reads which branch fired + the status/message to determine whether the failure is a 4xx (→ check Wordfence UA / rate limits, hypothesis H3), a `wpStatus=hold`/`spam` success (→ check WP admin Comments → Pending / Spam / Trash, hypothesis H2 / Akismet), a 400 with an "empty email" message (→ hypothesis H4), or no failure at all (→ #87 already fixed it). **(4) ATS (hypothesis H5) verified — no code change.** `App/Info.plist` declares no `NSAppTransportSecurity` key, so the app uses Apple's default ATS posture: HTTPS allowed, cleartext HTTP forbidden. The comment endpoint is `https://www.dutchovendaddy.com/wp-json/wp/v2/comments` → ATS does not block the POST (consistent with REG-27's H5 ruling). Documented caveat for the owner: if WordPress ever issues a 301/302 redirect on the POST to a non-HTTPS host, ATS would block the redirected request — but the canonical host is HTTPS-only today and `URLSession` follows same-scheme redirects transparently, so no redirect-related ATS failure is expected. **The owner-verification checklist (what code CANNOT do — must be done by the owner).** SERVER-SIDE: (a) WP admin → Comments → check Pending / Spam / Trash for the test comments (Akismet auto-trash is hypothesis H2); (b) Wordfence (or whichever security plugin is installed) → Live Traffic / Blocking → confirm the app's `DODApp/<v>.<b> (iOS; ...)` User-Agent is not rate-limited or 403'd, and check the "block fake Google crawlers / block by UA" rules. ON-DEVICE: foreground the installed TestFlight build with Console.app attached (subsystem `com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp`), post a comment, and capture the request URL + response status + which error branch the new log printed. **What is CODE-HARDENED in this PR vs. what still needs owner verification.** CODE-HARDENED: every comment-POST failure path now produces a distinct, specific, user-visible message (no silent fail); a blank author identity can no longer produce a doomed 400 POST; the request/response/branch is logged for on-device diagnosis. STILL NEEDS OWNER VERIFICATION: whether the live failure is server-side (WP moderation / Akismet / Wordfence) or was already resolved by #87 — the on-device Console capture is the deciding evidence. **Tests (all comment-write coverage at L1/L2 against the fake clients — NO write reaches the live blog, constitution §6).** L1/L2 network: `DODNetworkingTests.WPCommentsClientPostFailureTests` — WP JSON error body surfaced as `httpStatusWithBody(400, "Comment content is invalid.")`; HTML challenge tag-stripped + status preserved; 401/403/422 bodyless fall back to `httpStatus`; empty email and empty name each rejected before the POST with a captured-request-is-empty assertion; `extractWPErrorMessage` covered for JSON-with-message / empty-body-nil / blank-message-nil / long-HTML-clamped-to-140. L1 snackbar: `DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests.CommentErrorSnackbarTests.httpStatusWithBodySurfacesCodeAndServerMessage`. L1/L2 view-model: `DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests.RecipeDetailCommentSubmitTests` — blank-email re-gates + shows the prompt + fires no comment-submitted telemetry (proves no POST); whitespace-wrapped valid identity is trimmed then posts successfully; a `httpStatusWithBody(403, ...)` injected via the fake's new `postCommentError` surfaces the full "server said 403: <message>" snackbar end-to-end with the draft preserved. **No `e2e` label** — comment-write E2E must never touch the live blog (constitution §6); the coverage is deliberately held at L1/L2. **What this does NOT change.** PR #87's User-Agent stamping (unchanged, not duplicated); the four other `WPClientError.httpStatus(_:)` call sites (unchanged); `WPCommentsClient.postComment` signature + JSON request-body shape (REG-27 constraint — unchanged; the change is in error surfacing + a pre-flight guard + logging); `GuestIdentityStore` (unchanged); the `comments` GET path (unchanged); `CommentComposer` (unchanged — already blocks empty-content submits client-side); `RecipeDetailViewModel` public read-only surface (unchanged — only the module-internal setters of three comment properties widened); T-736's cache-hit blurb-refresh path (unchanged — orthogonal); ATS / `App/Info.plist` (verified, intentionally unchanged). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Widen the existing `WPClientError.httpStatus(Int)` to `httpStatus(Int, serverMessage: String?)`.** Cleaner conceptually but ripples to ~17 construction + pattern-match sites across 5 source files and 6 test files (every client + every client test), and would collide with the in-flight DUT-5 / DUT-6 branches — directly against the "keep changes localized to the comment path" constraint. Rejected in favor of the additive `httpStatusWithBody` case that only the comment path produces and only the snackbar consumes. **(b) Surface the WP message through the existing `.underlying(message:)` case.** Loses the numeric status the user/owner needs to pin the hypothesis. Rejected. **(c) Block the empty-identity case ONLY at the view model.** Leaves the network client able to fire an empty-email 400 for any other caller (e.g. a future Cook-Mode comment affordance). Defense-in-depth at both layers is cheap and correct. Adopted both. **(d) Log the comment body / author email for richer diagnosis.** Violates constitution §9 (no PII in logs / telemetry). Rejected — status + ids + WP moderation status are sufficient to pin the failure class without PII. **(e) Add an L5 E2E that posts a real comment and asserts it lands.** Violates constitution §6 (E2E must never write to the live blog — it would spam dad's production comment moderation queue). Rejected — comment-write stays at L1/L2 with the fake client. **Numbering note:** CL-134 / T-737 is the next free CL slot above CL-133 (T-736, PR #128) after the parallel-merge renumber. **Spec/trace:** US-14 / AC-14.4 (every comment-POST failure path produces a distinct, specific, user-visible message; success and failure are visibly distinct) + US-15 / AC-15.1 (blank identity re-gates behind the guest-identity sheet). New DUT-7 entry in the AC-T4 regression list in `spec.md`. Implementing PR: T-737 (`fix/dut-7-comments`). The `e2e` label is NOT applied (comment-write coverage deliberately held at L1/L2 per constitution §6).

- **CL-128 (US-43 amendment / T-731, 2026-05-31)** — **Revert CL-113's SF Rounded family swap on `DODType.heading`, `DODType.caption`, and `DODType.brand` — the entire app now uses one font (the default System font, SF Pro). The CL-113 `.bold` weight shift on `displayLarge` + `displayMedium` and the `.medium` weight on `caption` stand; only the family-design portion of CL-113 reverses.** **The decision context.** Joint @adamsned (dad) + @spencer0706 (Spencer) offline conversation today: after seeing CL-113's rounded-on-heading-and-caption ship to a real device for ~48 hours, both agreed that the mixed-family register (rounded card titles + chips + brand against default body) reads as visually inconsistent rather than friendly. Uniform System font across the app is the preferred direction. **Brand register comes through weight, color, and spacing instead of via SF Rounded specifically** — the `.bold` weight on display tokens (preserved from CL-113), `DODColor.accent` burnt-orange for affordances, and `DODSpacing.md` / `DODSpacing.lg` rhythm carry the brand register without the font-family swap. **The three token swaps:** `DODType.heading` from `.system(.headline, design: .rounded)` → `.system(.headline)`; `DODType.caption` from `.system(.caption, design: .rounded, weight: .medium)` → `.system(.caption, weight: .medium)`; `DODType.brand` from `.system(size: 22, weight: .bold, design: .rounded)` → `.system(size: 22, weight: .bold)`. **What stays from CL-113.** Move (1) — `displayLarge` + `displayMedium` `.semibold` → `.bold` — preserved unchanged. Move (3) — the new `DODType.brand` token's existence (size 22, weight `.bold`, reserved for wordmark moments) — preserved; only the rounded-design family revert. The `.medium` weight on `DODType.caption` — preserved. `body` + `bodyEmphasized` were never touched by CL-113 — unchanged. **Why a wholesale revert and not a partial.** Considered keeping rounded on `brand` (the wordmark surface is where a friendlier register most plausibly earns its keep) and reverting only `heading` + `caption`. Rejected: `brand` is a wordmark surface where consistency with surrounding chrome matters more than friendliness — the splash + About + share-sheet preview surfaces all sit next to other System-font copy and a per-token family switch would re-introduce the same mixed-register critique at a smaller surface. Uniform > partial. **Why now, not at the next major redesign.** The CL-113 family swap is unconditional — every screen that resolves `heading` or `caption` picks up the rounded family on next launch. Deferring the revert means an entire TestFlight cycle of mixed-register UI lands on dad's device before the next round of work, and the revert is one-line-per-token + a snapshot-baseline-delete. Cheaper to revert immediately than to ship the mixed-register state another week. **L4 baseline impact.** The font-family swap pixel-diffs every snapshot baseline that renders text via `heading`, `caption`, or `brand` — the chip surfaces, the card titles, the time stamps. Per the CL-115 / T-730 pattern, **delete the affected baselines and let CI re-record** via the existing `record: .missing` workflow. Conservative over-deletion is preferred — under-deletion leaves stale baselines that block CI. Six feature/package directories had baselines deleted: `DODDesignSystem`, `DODFeatureFeed`, `DODFeatureSaved`, `DODFeatureSearch`, `DODFeatureCategories`, `DODFeatureRecipeDetail`. **No spec change beyond AC-43.7 + AC-43.8 amendment.** AC-43.6 (display weight bold) + AC-43.9 (L4 baseline re-record contract) + AC-43.10 (L1/L2/L3 no regression) stay verbatim. AC-43.7 amended via strike-through + replacement framing (uniform System font for `heading` + `caption`; brand register via weight/color/spacing). AC-43.8 amended via strike-through on the font literal (`design: .rounded` portion removed; size 22 + `.bold` weight stand). **What this does NOT change.** Every view-side call site that references `dodFont(.heading)` / `dodFont(.caption)` / `dodFont(.brand)` is byte-identical — the `Font` instance type signature didn't change, only the resolved family. `RecipeCard` / `FilterChipRow` / `EmptyState` / every snapshot host is unchanged source-side. `DODColor` tokens are unchanged. `DODSpacing` tokens are unchanged. `Spacing.swift` / `Colors.swift` are untouched. The L1 `TypographyTests` (if any assert on font instance equality) are robust to the swap because they compare the `Font` value, not its design family. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Keep `brand` rounded** — see "Why a wholesale revert" above; uniform > partial. **(b) Introduce a `DODType.useRoundedFamily` flag** — over-engineered for a binary brand decision the team has already made twice; a flag is the wrong shape for a brand-identity question. **(c) Defer the revert to Phase b (T-711's magazine RecipeCard variant)** — the family swap is unconditional today; deferring locks the mixed-register UI on every screen until T-711 ships. Revert now is one-line-per-token + a snapshot-baseline-delete. **(d) Re-record the snapshot baselines manually in this PR** — adds review burden + the baselines are deterministic from the new tokens; `record: .missing` is the cheaper path per the CL-115 / T-730 pattern. **Spec/trace:** US-43 amendment (AC-43.7 amended — `heading` + `caption` revert to default System font family; AC-43.8 amended — `brand` font literal drops `design: .rounded`). Implementing PR: T-731 (originally landed as T-650 in PR #118 — renamed in a fast-forward docs PR after the merge to resolve a collision with the prior T-650 (Gallery↔List view toggle, PR #58). The `feat/T-650-uniform-system-font` branch name + the squash-merge commit subject preserve the original T-650 marker for git-history traceability; every spec/clarification/task/source-comment reference now reads T-731). **Numbering note:** CL-128 / T-731 is the next free CL slot above CL-127 (T-649). The `e2e` label IS applied — the change is a user-facing visual change across many surfaces (every screen that renders card titles, chips, or time stamps).

- **CL-136 (US-44 / DUT-36 Phase a / T-739, 2026-06-02)** — **Graduate DUT-36 ("User profile + gated write surfaces") as a four-phase delivery, with Phase a (T-739) landing the data model + Settings → Profile section + edit view + Sign Out + Delete Profile. Phases b (photo picker + crop), c (Ratings gate + first-write popup), and d (comment composer auto-fill) ship in separate PRs under separate task IDs.** **The decision context (Linear DUT-36, 2026-06-02).** Pre-T-739 the app's only on-device identity surface was the US-15 guest identity (display name + email captured in the comment composer's `GuestIdentitySheet`, persisted in the Keychain as two generic-password rows under `com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp.guest`). The guest identity was sufficient for the "post a comment / rating" flow but missing the affordances a returning cook expects: no way to see "this is me" in the app, no photo, no clear sign-out path, and the App Store 5.1.1(v) "Delete Account" requirement was unaddressed because there was no UI surface where the user could remove their identity. T-739 lands the profile data model + the Settings entry surface; subsequent phases gate the write surfaces and add the photo flow. **The locked decisions (the user resolved each up-front so the implementation didn't re-litigate them).** **(1) Storage: local-only.** ``UserProfile`` persists as a single JSON-encoded Keychain row (service `com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp.profile.v1`, account `"profile"`, `kSecAttrSynchronizable: false` — device-local, matches the US-15 / CL-15 / DUT-30 posture). Survives a wipe-and-reinstall via OS-managed iCloud Keychain *backup* (if the user has it on at the OS level), but does NOT cross-device-sync through iCloud Keychain. SwiftData was considered + rejected — the profile is a single record with no relational schema and no CloudKit-sync need (the user's identity travels with their device, not across them, in v1); a SwiftData entity adds migration burden for no benefit. **(2) Email: required.** Both display name and email are non-optional fields, validated by ``UserProfile/validateDisplayName(_:)`` (non-whitespace) and ``UserProfile/validateEmail(_:)`` (basic regex `^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$` — intentionally loose, server-side validation is authoritative). The client-side guard prevents `author_email=""` / malformed shapes from ever reaching the network, matching the CL-134 / DUT-7 empty-identity guard rationale on the comment path. **(3) Default avatar: initial-letter circle.** ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` renders a `DODColor.accent`-filled circle carrying the uppercase first letter of the trimmed display name (skipping emoji / digits / punctuation — `"🌟 Spencer"` → `"S"`, `"3 Sam"` → `"S"`); empty / all-emoji / whitespace-only names fall back to `"?"`. 60pt diameter in the Settings section header, scalable via the `diameter` parameter. Per-name color hashing was considered + rejected for Phase a — the accent fill is a single unambiguous "this is you" cue and a hash → palette mapping is a polish call that can layer on later if a stronger identity register is wanted. **(4) Guest comments retroactive attribution: NO.** Old guest comments stay attributed to the guest identity that posted them (the US-15 row's display name + email); only new comments after profile creation use the profile. Going through the WP REST API to re-attribute past comments was rejected — it's a write-heavy network-coupled operation that would require server-side coordination + a moderation re-pass + a privacy disclosure for the back-fill, and the user explicitly said "old guest comments stay attributed to the guest UUID." **(5) Sign Out vs Delete Profile: both ship in Phase a, identical behavior for local-only v1.** Both clear the Keychain row + photo file (when Phase b lands the photo). App Store 5.1.1(v) requires an explicit "Delete Account" affordance whenever an app collects user data — ``ProfileEditView`` ships a `Button(role: .destructive)` with a confirmation alert reading "Delete your profile? This will remove your display name, email, and any future comments will be attributed to a guest." The accent-color "Sign Out" button is the friendlier wording for the common case (no confirmation alert — the user can always re-set-up). When DUT-16 lands and adds backend state, the two diverge: Sign Out keeps server data (the user can sign back in later and pick up where they left off), Delete Profile nukes both local + server state. **The bridge to US-15.** ``UserProfile/id`` is the canonical "this device's commenter" UUID — the same UUID semantic the comment + rating POSTs already use. CL-136's design has the composition root reuse the existing on-device guest UUID when wrapping a new profile, so old guest comments stay associated with the same identity even though the row label in WP admin reads the old guest's name + email rather than the profile's. Today's US-15 `GuestIdentityStore` (in `Packages/DODSupport/Sources/DODSupport/GuestIdentityStore.swift`) stores `displayName` + `email` only — it does NOT surface a UUID — so Phase a's `UUID()` generation at first-profile creation is a fresh value, and the `saveWithSeededUUIDPreservesIdentity` test pins the round-trip contract so a future task can thread an existing on-device UUID through without breaking the bridge. **The package shape.** New `Packages/DODFeatureProfile/` matches the `DODFeatureFeed` / `DODFeatureSearch` layout: `Package.swift` with `iOS 17` + `macOS 14` targets, dependencies on `DODSupport` + `DODDesignSystem` (the source side) + `swift-snapshot-testing` (test-only), source files `UserProfile.swift` / `ProfileStore.swift` / `ProfileSection.swift` / `ProfileEditView.swift` / `InitialLetterAvatarView.swift` / `ProfilePhotoView.swift`, and tests under `Tests/DODFeatureProfileTests/` covering `UserProfile` validation, `ProfileStore` round-trip + clear + idempotency + UUID bridge, and `InitialLetterAvatarView` letter extraction. The Keychain-backed ``KeychainProfileStore`` is an `actor` (matches `RecipeStore`'s `@ModelActor` posture); the test-only ``InMemoryProfileStore`` is also an `actor` (modeled originally as a class with `NSLock` then converted to `actor` because `NSLock.lock()` is unavailable from async contexts under strict concurrency). The ``ProfileStoring`` protocol declares `load() async -> UserProfile?` / `save(_:) async throws` / `clear() async throws` / `var hasProfile: Bool { get async }` — the same async-protocol shape `RecipeStore`'s public surface uses. **The composition-root wiring.** `App/AppDependencies.swift` instantiates one `KeychainProfileStore` per process and threads it through `App/TabStack.swift` → `App/SettingsToolbar.swift` → `SettingsView`'s `SettingsViewModel` init. The view-model carries `profile: UserProfile?` (seeded from a synchronous initial load is impossible because the store is `async`; init kicks off a `Task { await self?.refreshProfile() }` so the row paints empty on first frame and updates on next runloop tick — same lazy-warmup pattern `refreshCloudSyncStatus()` uses). `ProfileEditView`'s `onProfileChanged` callback fires after every save / sign-out / delete to drive the view-model's `refreshProfile()`. **The Profile section's row treatment.** Empty state: a single "Set up your profile" `Text` + system-chevron via `NavigationLink`'s default label. Populated state: `HStack(spacing: DODSpacing.md)` carrying the 60pt avatar + `VStack(alignment: .leading)` with display name (`DODType.body`/`DODColor.label`) on top + email (`DODType.caption`/`DODColor.labelSecondary`) below + trailing `Spacer(minLength: 0)` so the chevron stays at the row's right edge. `.listRowBackground(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` matches the T-647 / CL-125 brand-brown treatment shared with every other Settings section. **The edit view's surface.** `Form`-based (matches the existing Settings styling); four sections — Identity (display name + email TextFields with proper content types + autocap/autocorrect overrides), Profile Picture (stub row with avatar trailing; TODO marks the Phase b wire-in point), Sign Out (accent button), Delete Profile (destructive role + confirmation alert). Cancel + Done toolbar items live in `.cancellationAction` + `.confirmationAction` placements. Done is disabled until `isFormValid` (display name non-whitespace AND email regex passes). On a save error from validation, the form surfaces a humane prompt in the Identity section footer ("Enter a valid email address.") rather than a generic toast. **Phase a is constitution-amendment-free.** No new analytics events (Phase d will add `profileCreated` if telemetry is required). No new App Privacy disclosure (local-only Keychain entry — no data leaves the device in Phase a). No new dependency (Apple `Security` framework used directly). The §1 "mostly read-only with two write surfaces" framing is preserved — the Profile section is identity surface, not a new write surface. **The deferred-phase scope (NOT touched by Phase a — these are separate PRs).** **Phase b (photo picker + crop, separate task ID).** Wire `PhotosPicker` into the Profile Picture row; on selection run a crop step (CropView or `PHAsset`-based square crop); persist the cropped JPG to the app's Documents directory as `<UUID().uuidString>.jpg`; populate ``UserProfile/photoFilename``; update `ProfilePhotoView` to render the loaded `UIImage(contentsOfFile:)` inside the circular frame (falling back to ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` when the file is missing). The `// TODO(2026-06-02, spencer)` comment in `ProfileEditView.photoSection` marks the wire-in point. **Phase c (Ratings gate + popup, separate task ID).** Gate the Star Rating tap on the Recipe Detail screen behind `await profileStore.hasProfile`; when no profile exists, present a first-write popup that explains "Set up your profile to leave a rating" and offers a sheet-presented `ProfileEditView`. The §1 amendment lands with Phase c (the write surfaces gain a Profile prereq). **Phase d (comment composer auto-fill, separate task ID).** Replace the `GuestIdentitySheet` flow in `CommentComposer` with a `profileStore.load()` pre-flight that auto-fills the name + email when a profile exists; when no profile exists, gate behind the same Phase c popup. The US-15 guest-identity sheet stays in the codebase as a fallback for users who explicitly choose "post as guest" after Phase d (Linear DUT-36 sub-discussion suggests we may keep that escape hatch — that's a Phase d design call). **What this does NOT change.** US-15's `GuestIdentityStore` / `KeychainGuestIdentityStore` / `InMemoryGuestIdentityStore` (unchanged — Phase a coexists with the guest identity row; Phase d will eventually retire the user-facing guest sheet but the storage layer survives as a fallback). The US-14 comment composer + the US-13 rating composer (unchanged in Phase a — both still consume the guest identity; Phase c + Phase d wire the profile bridge). The `dod.cloudkit.*` keys and the iCloud Sync row (unchanged — the profile is intentionally NOT in CloudKit sync per the locked decision). The `dod.settings.*` keys (unchanged — the profile lives in Keychain, not UserDefaults). The four existing tabs / their `SettingsToolbarModifier` (unchanged surface — the gear opens the same Settings sheet; the modifier just gains a `profileStore` parameter). The constitution §1 / §3 / §9 sections (Phase a is amendment-free; Phase c will amend §1; Phase d may amend §9 if a `profileCreated` event is required). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) SwiftData persistence for the profile.** Rejected — single-record, no relations, no CloudKit-sync need, Keychain is the canonical place for identity-bearing data on iOS. SwiftData adds migration burden + a CloudKit-vs-local fork in the container factory for zero benefit. **(b) Per-name color hash for the default avatar.** Rejected for Phase a — the accent fill is a single unambiguous "this is you" cue; a hash → palette mapping is a polish call that can layer on later if a stronger identity register is wanted. The Phase b photo flow makes this moot for users who upload a photo anyway. **(c) Retroactive guest comment re-attribution.** Rejected — server-side coordination required, a re-moderation pass required, privacy disclosure required, low user value (most guest comments are old enough that the user wouldn't notice). Old guest comments stay attributed to the guest. **(d) Sign Out only (no Delete Profile) in Phase a.** Rejected — App Store 5.1.1(v) requires an explicit "Delete Account" surface whenever an app collects user data; shipping only Sign Out fails review. **(e) Delete Profile only (no Sign Out) in Phase a.** Rejected — Sign Out is the friendlier everyday UX for the common case where the user wants to swap identities without nuking everything; the destructive-alert friction belongs only on the irreversible-when-DUT-16-lands path. **(f) Inline the Profile section UI inside `DODFeatureFeed`'s SettingsView source.** Rejected — the Profile surface will gain Phase b/c/d additions (photo picker, Ratings gate popup, comment composer integration) that belong in a dedicated feature package; the `DODFeatureFeed` package is already at the SwiftLint file_length cap on `SettingsViewModel.swift` (the iCloud Sync / Voice section splits prove the cap is a real constraint), and a new package matches the codebase's per-feature ownership pattern. **(g) Single combined "Account" button that toggles between Sign Out and Delete Profile based on long-press.** Rejected — discoverability is bad (users don't long-press buttons by default), accessibility is bad (VoiceOver users can't easily switch), App Store reviewers may not find the Delete affordance during review. Two buttons is the boring-but-correct shape. **(h) Place the Profile section BELOW Use Metric Units rather than above.** Rejected — every iOS user has been trained by Settings.app to expect "Apple ID" at the top; placing the Profile section below the metric units toggle would read as "this is a secondary setting" rather than "this is me." The user explicitly said "first section." **The asset placement — no new assets in Phase a.** No image-set additions (the default avatar is pure SwiftUI; Phase b's photo flow will write to the Documents directory, not the asset catalog). **Numbering note.** CL-136 / T-739 is the next free CL slot above CL-135 (T-741) and the next free task slot above T-741. The `e2e` label IS applied — composition root touch (`AppDependencies`), new Settings UI surface (Profile section row at the top of the list). **Spec/trace:** new US-44 entry in `spec.md` with AC-44.1..AC-44.7 owning the Phase a contract (Profile section placement, required Display Name + Email, Photo deferred to Phase b, Sign Out + Delete Profile semantics, initial-letter default avatar, US-15 UUID bridge, no retroactive attribution). AC-44.8..AC-44.13 reserved for Phase b/c/d. Implementing PR: T-739 (`feat/T-739-profile-phase-a`). Tests: L1 `UserProfileValidationTests` (display name + email regex), L1 `ProfileStoreTests` (round-trip / clear / idempotent / UUID bridge), L1 `InitialLetterAvatarViewTests` (letter extraction rules), L4 `InitialLetterAvatarViewSnapshotTests` (single-letter + two-word + emoji-prefix + fallback + dark mode — recorded via `record: .missing` on first iOS-Simulator xcodebuild run, no stale baseline to delete). Estimate: ~45 min spec + ~30 min impl + ~10 min verify ≈ 90 min total.
- **CL-138 (US-44 / DUT-36 Phase c / T-741, 2026-06-02)** — **Gate the Ratings & Reviews WRITE composer (star picker + review text + submit) on profile existence: blur + popup overlay when `hasProfile == false`, with the CTA presenting ``ProfileEditView`` as a modal sheet OVER the recipe (not a push). Reactive ungating when the sheet dismisses with a profile populated.** Phase c of DUT-36 — extends CL-136 + CL-137. **The architecture choice (locked up-front so the implementation didn't re-litigate).** **(1) Modal sheet OVER the recipe (not a push).** The CTA presents ``ProfileEditView`` via `.sheet(isPresented:)` from `RecipeDetailRatingsSection`. Rationale: the recipe view stays mounted underneath, so dismissing the sheet returns the user to the exact same scroll position on the same recipe — they immediately see the gate disappear and can leave a review without re-navigating. A push would have unwound a navigation stack entry and confused the spatial model ("Where did I come from? Settings? No, the recipe"). The Settings → Profile entry path stays a push (Phase a wired that up via `ProfileSection`'s `NavigationLink`) — two presentation styles for two distinct entry semantics is fine and matches Apple HIG (sheet = "do this thing, return", push = "navigate deeper into hierarchy"). **(2) Gate scope: rating + review WRITE composer only.** The blur + popup overlay wraps the `rateAndReviewCard` only — the existing reviews list (`commentsList`) stays unchanged and readable in all states. Users can still browse what others wrote without a profile; only the WRITE surface (star picker + review text box + author fields + submit button) is gated. Phase d (T-742) handles the comment composer auto-fill separately — Phase c does NOT touch comment rendering or composition. **(3) Blur via SwiftUI primitives.** `.blur(radius: 10)` on the composer + `Rectangle().fill(.ultraThinMaterial).allowsHitTesting(false)` overlay where the popup sits. The Material overlay does NOT eat touches (the popup sits in the same ZStack and IS the interactive surface); the underlying composer is `.disabled(!viewModel.hasProfile)` so even if a touch leaks through the blur won't accidentally trigger an action. Radius 10 is enough to make the text+stars unreadable without being so heavy it visually wipes the layout cue — testers can still see "stars + text editor live below" through the haze, which signals what they get post-setup. **(4) Reactive ungating via @Observable + onDisappear hook.** When the sheet dismisses with a profile saved, `RecipeDetailViewModel.refreshProfile()` fires from `.onDisappear { Task { await ... } }` — re-reads the profile store, assigns to the `@Observable` `profile: UserProfile?` property, SwiftUI re-renders `RecipeDetailRatingsSection`, `hasProfile` flips, the gate disappears. No manual callback wiring from `ProfileEditView` to the ratings section — the `.onDisappear` + `@Observable` re-render is sufficient and avoids coupling the two views. **(5) Popup copy and CTA.** "Set up your profile" (mirrors the Settings → Profile empty-state row label from Phase a / CL-136). "Set Up Profile" CTA button. Body: "To leave a rating and review, set up your profile in Settings." — explicit about WHY the gate exists + that the same profile lives in Settings (so a returning user who set up a profile on first launch but later signed out understands the connection). Avoids "log in" / "sign in" framing — that's DUT-16's territory (backend identity); Phase c is strictly the local-only profile gate. **(6) `RecipeDetailViewModel` gains `profile: UserProfile?` + `hasProfile: Bool` + `refreshProfile() async`.** The dependency surface (`RecipeDetailDependencies`) gains `loadUserProfile() async -> UserProfile?` so the view model reads through the protocol seam (test fakes provide a canned profile). Called from `onAppear` so the gate is computed before the user can scroll to the ratings section, and from `.onDisappear` on the sheet so dismissing with a populated profile reactively ungates. **(7) ProfileEditView `existingProfile:` parameter is reused unchanged.** The view already accepts `existingProfile: UserProfile?` (Phase a / CL-136) — for the first-time-setup case (which is the only case the gate ever fires for, by definition: `hasProfile == false`) we pass `nil` so the view renders in "New Profile" mode. No `ProfileEditView` API changes in Phase c. **(8) Accessibility posture.** The blurred composer carries `.accessibilityHidden(!viewModel.hasProfile)` so VoiceOver users skip past the unreadable haze cleanly — only the popup card is announced. The CTA carries `accessibilityIdentifier("dod.ratings.gate.cta")` for future L5 E2E coverage (Phase c does NOT add a new E2E journey — existing L5 journeys don't tap the rating composer, so the gate is invisible to them; the identifier is registered now so a future "create profile then rate" journey can drive the gate without revisiting the implementation). **(9) Composition-root wiring.** `App/AppDependencies.swift` already creates the singleton `KeychainProfileStore` (Phase a). The `LiveRecipeDetailDependencies` initializer gains a `profileStore: any ProfileStoring` parameter that the live `loadUserProfile()` reads through. The fake dependencies in tests get a `profileToLoad: UserProfile?` field. **The flow end-to-end (no profile).** User opens recipe → scrolls to Ratings & Reviews → sees blurred composer + popup overlay → taps "Set Up Profile" → sheet slides up presenting `ProfileEditView` in NavigationStack (so Cancel + Done toolbar render correctly) → user fills name + email → Done → sheet dismisses → `.onDisappear` runs `refreshProfile()` → `viewModel.profile` flips non-nil → `hasProfile == true` → gate disappears → composer is now interactive → user rates + reviews → submit. The user never leaves the recipe scroll position. **The flow end-to-end (profile exists).** User opens recipe → scrolls to Ratings & Reviews → composer is interactive immediately (no gate ever shown) → user rates + reviews → submit. **What this does NOT change.** The existing `commentsList` (existing reviews stay readable). The comment composer + author fields auto-fill (Phase d's job). `ProfileEditView` API surface (no new parameters). `SettingsView` push to `ProfileEditView` (unchanged — still a push, the locked decision was push from Settings + sheet from recipe-detail). `ProfileSection` Settings row (unchanged). The Keychain `service = com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp.profile.v1` (unchanged). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Push `ProfileEditView` from the gate CTA instead of a sheet.** Rejected — would have unwound the recipe-detail navigation stack entry and the user would have had to manually navigate back to "this recipe" after profile setup, losing the scroll position. The sheet preserves recipe-mount + scroll, so post-dismiss the user sees the gate dissolve right where they were. **(b) Block the entire Ratings & Reviews section (including reading existing reviews) on profile existence.** Rejected — readers who haven't set up a profile should still see what others wrote; gating reads behind setup is hostile UX. Only the WRITE surface is gated. **(c) Inline `ProfileEditView`-equivalent setup form below the popup (no sheet).** Rejected — would have duplicated the form code, conflicted visually with the recipe layout, and forced a re-implementation of the validation + photo + Sign Out / Delete Profile controls inside `RecipeDetailRatingsSection`. The sheet reuses the existing canonical edit view. **(d) Manual callback (`onProfileSaved: () -> Void`) from `ProfileEditView` to the ratings section instead of `.onDisappear` + `@Observable`.** Rejected — adds coupling between two unrelated view layers; the `.onDisappear` hook is just as reactive (the sheet's dismissal IS the signal) and reuses the existing observable pipeline. **(e) Use `accessibilityHidden(false)` on the blurred composer so VoiceOver still announces "5 star rating, disabled" etc.** Rejected — announcing the disabled surface is noise; VoiceOver users get a cleaner experience hearing only the popup ("Set up your profile. To leave a rating and review, set up your profile in Settings. Set Up Profile button."). **(f) Pin the gate behind an additional explicit "must rate to leave a review" rule.** Rejected — the ratings + reviews surfaces are already consolidated (DUT-24) and the gate runs on profile existence, not on rating-state. The user can leave EITHER a star rating OR a written review (or both); the gate is binary on profile, not on intent. **(g) Add an L5 E2E journey for the gate → setup → ungate flow.** Rejected — existing L5 journeys don't interact with the ratings composer at all (they end at "Ingredients" header), so the gate is invisible to them; adding a new full E2E journey for a feature this self-contained is over-investment per CL-58. The `dod.ratings.gate.cta` identifier is exposed so a future journey CAN drive it if a new use case arises. **Numbering note.** CL-138 / T-741 is the next free CL slot above CL-137 (T-740, Phase b) — Phase d (CL-139 / T-742) will land separately with the comment composer auto-fill. The `e2e` label IS applied — new write-surface gating behavior end-to-end (read recipe → trigger gate → setup → return → gate disappears) + composition-root touch (`AppDependencies` threads the profile store into `LiveRecipeDetailDependencies`). **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendment — AC-44.10 added (write surface gated on profile existence with blur + popup + modal sheet presentation) + AC-44.11 added (existing reviews stay readable in all states). Implementing PR: T-741 (`feat/T-741-profile-phase-c`). Tests: L1 `RecipeDetailViewModel` (hasProfile false when store empty, true when populated, `refreshProfile()` re-reads the store), L1 `RatingsProfileGate` view body (renders the copy + CTA correctly, callback fires on tap), L4 `RecipeDetailRatingsSection` snapshots (gated state vs ungated state, light + dark mode). Estimate: ~30 min spec + ~30 min impl + ~15 min verify ≈ 75 min total.
- **CL-139 (US-44 / DUT-36 Phase d / T-742, 2026-06-02)** — **Remove the editable Display Name + Email TextField inputs from the rating/review/comment composer; replace them with a static `PostingAsHeader` sub-view that renders the user's profile identity (avatar + display name + email) as plain text above the empty comment editor. Source `author_name` + `author_email` for the WP REST POST payload from `profile.displayName` + `profile.email` directly. Render the current user's ``ProfilePhotoView`` on their own comment rows via a case-insensitive `RecipeComment.authorEmail == profile.email` match.** Final phase of DUT-36 (Phase d of 4) — extends CL-136 + CL-137 + CL-138 and closes the parent Linear issue. **The user-spec clarification (Spencer, 2026-06-02, during T-742 setup).** "The name and email field doesn't need to show up as an editable field in the ratings and review section, the name and email should automatically be just strings of text above the comment field that is empty and ready for input." Translation: wherever the composer currently has `TextField("Name", ...)` and `TextField("Email", ...)` inputs, remove them entirely and replace with a static text header. The user only types the comment text (and picks stars for reviews). Phase d implements that surgery. **The locked decisions (the user resolved each up-front so the implementation didn't re-litigate).** **(1) Removal scope: `authorFields` deleted, view-model fields retired.** ``RecipeDetailRatingsSection+AuthorFields.swift`` is deleted in full — the file existed to host the `authorFields` `@ViewBuilder` + the `AuthorFieldKind` / `AuthorFieldSpec` helpers + the `authorInput` / `authorField` builders, all of which are gone now. `RecipeDetailViewModel.commentAuthorName` / `commentAuthorEmail` / `setCommentAuthorName(_:)` / `setCommentAuthorEmail(_:)` / `prefillAuthorIdentity()` / `persistAuthorIdentity(name:email:)` / `isAuthorNameValid` / `isAuthorEmailValid` / `isAuthorIdentityValid` are all retired — the form fields they backed no longer exist. The Phase c gate guarantees `profile` is non-nil whenever the composer is interactive, so the empty-identity guard the retired form fields used to enforce now collapses to "is `profile` non-nil + does the user have a rating OR a comment draft" via a simplified `canSubmitRatingOrComment`. **(2) `PostingAsHeader` shape — 32pt avatar + display name + email + Spacer.** A new private `PostingAsHeader` sub-view inside `RecipeDetailRatingsSection.swift` renders an `HStack(spacing: DODSpacing.xs)` carrying `ProfilePhotoView(profile: profile, diameter: 32, photoStore: photoStore)` leading + `VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 2)` containing the display name (`DODType.bodyEmphasized` / `DODColor.label`) on top + email (`DODType.caption` / `DODColor.labelSecondary`) below + a `Spacer(minLength: 0)` trailing. Padding-bottom of `DODSpacing.sm` separates the header from the comment editor. The header is non-interactive — no tap handler, no `Button`, no `TextField`. Reads as "you're posting as this person", not as another input. The 32pt diameter is smaller than the 60pt Settings avatar + the 40pt comment-row avatar so the header sits comfortably inside the composer card without dominating it visually. **(3) Composer layout becomes — PostingAsHeader → star picker → comment editor → Submit.** No more "Display name" / "Email" rows between the stars and the comment field. The star picker stays the same `StarRatingInput` Phase c shipped. The comment editor stays the same `TextEditor` Phase c shipped (empty + ready for input, placeholder "Share your tips, substitutions, or thoughts."). The Submit button stays the same `.borderedProminent` button. **(4) WP REST payload source change — `profile.displayName` + `profile.email`, NOT user-typed.** `RecipeDetailViewModel+CommentSubmit.swift`'s `submitRatingAndComment()` + `submitComment()` + `recordRatingAlongsideComment(stars:)` paths read `profile.displayName` + `profile.email` (trimmed) and pass those as `author_name` + `author_email` to `postComment(...)` + `postRating(...)`. The WP REST endpoint, the JSON payload shape (`{ "post": id, "author_name": "...", "author_email": "...", "content": "...", "meta": {...} }`), and the network seam (`WPCommentsClient.postComment(...)`) are byte-identical to pre-Phase-d — only the SOURCE of the two author fields changes. The Phase c gate guarantees `profile` is non-nil whenever Submit can fire, so the submit paths assert `profile != nil` and route through `profile.displayName` / `profile.email` without re-validating (the profile-creation flow already validated both fields). **(5) Own-comment photo rendering — case-insensitive `authorEmail` match.** ``CommentRow`` rendering inside `commentsList` shows the current user's ``ProfilePhotoView`` (40pt, matching the existing Gravatar avatar frame) for comments where `comment.authorEmail.lowercased() == profile.email.lowercased()`. Per RFC 5321 email local parts CAN be case-sensitive but the dominant industry convention (and every major mail provider in practice) is case-insensitive — the canonical form Spencer types in his profile may differ in case from what WP echoes back, and the user reasonably expects "his own comment" to match. Other users' comments fall through to the existing Gravatar `AsyncImage` path (or the `person.crop.circle.fill` system fallback when no Gravatar URL). Old guest-attributed comments with empty `authorEmail` naturally fall through to the existing avatar path because empty doesn't equal a real profile's email — AC-44.7 (no retroactive attribution) is preserved by construction, no special case needed. **(6) The WP GET response doesn't include `author_email` — local-stamp on the just-posted comment.** WordPress's public `/wp/v2/comments` GET DOES NOT return `author_email` in the response payload (privacy — email is moderation-only data, only visible to admins via the moderation queue or the authenticated `/wp/v2/comments?context=edit` view; the app uses the anonymous public endpoint). So `RecipeComment.authorEmail` is empty for every comment FETCHED via the GET path. To make own-comment photos work for comments the user POSTS in-session, the submit path stamps `RecipeComment.authorEmail = profile.email` LOCALLY before inserting the just-posted comment into `viewModel.comments` via ``insertPostedCommentIfNew(_:)``. The `WPDTO.Comment.toDomain()` decoder maps the wire-format comment to an empty `authorEmail` (preserving the privacy posture), and the submit path then rewrites that field on the returned comment before insertion. The row renders with the profile photo immediately. **(7) Relaunch trade-off — the in-session stamp is lost.** The cached comments (via `RecipeStore.cacheComments(...)`) round-trip through `RecipeComment.Codable` so the stamped email IS persisted to disk for as long as the session's in-memory `comments` array is the source of truth — but on next app launch the comments cache is hydrated from the network via the same `WPDTO.Comment.toDomain()` path (which maps to empty `authorEmail`), so a relaunch loses the device-local stamp and own previously-posted comments fall back to the existing avatar (Gravatar / initial-letter / system fallback) path. This is the LOCKED TRADE-OFF — keeping the device-local stamp in the cache would require either (a) a separate "this device's posted comment IDs" Keychain row mirroring the API ID + matching at render time, or (b) a CloudKit / Keychain schema bump that ships own-comment metadata next to the cached comments — both over-investment for a strictly-presentational signal. Spencer's profile photo will render on every comment he posts WITHIN the same session; after relaunch his comments will render with the Gravatar (if his email has one) or the system fallback. If a returning-user persistence story becomes important we can revisit — the data shape (RecipeComment.authorEmail) is in place to support it without an API change. **(8) `RecipeComment` gains `authorEmail: String`.** New optional-defaulting-to-empty field on the domain model. `WPDTO.Comment.toDomain()` maps it to empty (`""`) since the wire payload doesn't include it. `RecipeComment.Codable` round-trips it cleanly (Codable synthesis handles the new field; existing on-disk caches decode with the default `""` because the field is absent from old JSON). The cache layer (`CachedComment.swift` in `DODPersistence`) gains the field too so the round-trip is honest end-to-end. **(9) Pass `currentProfile: UserProfile?` to the `CommentRow` callsite — view-model owns the threading.** `RecipeDetailRatingsSection`'s `commentsList` iterates `viewModel.comments` and renders each via a new `commentRow(for:)` private builder that takes the comment + reads `viewModel.profile` (already wired via Phase c) + decides which avatar surface to render — the existing Gravatar `AsyncImage` path OR `ProfilePhotoView(profile: profile, diameter: 40, photoStore: viewModel.profilePhotoStoreForGate)`. `CommentRow`'s public API gains an `avatarOverride: AnyView?` parameter (default `nil`, preserves backward compatibility) so the section can swap the avatar without forking CommentRow itself; when non-nil the override renders in place of the AsyncImage path. **(10) Accessibility posture.** `PostingAsHeader` carries `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)` + a combined `.accessibilityLabel("Posting as \(profile.displayName), \(profile.email)")` so VoiceOver announces it as a single read-only header rather than three independent elements (avatar + name + email). Own-comment avatar carries `.accessibilityHidden(true)` matching the existing CommentRow avatar (decorative). **What this does NOT change.** The Phase c gate (preserved verbatim — when `profile == nil` the composer is still blurred + popup-overlaid; AC-44.12 only changes the composer body that's revealed WHEN unlocked). The existing Gravatar / initial-letter / system-fallback avatar rendering for OTHER users' comments (unchanged path). The WP REST endpoint + payload shape (same `POST /wp/v2/comments` with `author_name` / `author_email` / `content` / optional `meta.wprm_comment_rating`). The US-15 `GuestIdentityStore` / the Phase a/b/c profile flows (unchanged — Phase d is a presentation-layer surgery on the composer + comment row, not a storage or networking change). `ProfileEditView` / `ProfileStore` / `ProfilePhotoStore` / `ProfilePhotoView` / `InitialLetterAvatarView` / `ProfilePhotoCropView` / `ProfileSection` / `RatingsProfileGate` (all unchanged). The Settings → Profile entry path (push, unchanged from Phase a). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Keep the editable Name + Email inputs as "override" fields that pre-fill from the profile but let the user override per-comment.** Rejected — the user-spec was explicit ("just strings of text above the comment field"). The Phase a/b/c story is "your profile is your identity in the app"; an override field would let the user post comments under fake identities, breaking the "one canonical commenter" model and re-introducing the empty/malformed-identity submit risk that the form-based fields had to guard against (DUT-7 / CL-134). **(b) Render the PostingAsHeader as a single sentence ("Posting as Spencer Adams · spencer@example.com") instead of a stacked HStack.** Rejected — the stacked layout (avatar + name on top + email below) is more scannable, mirrors the existing Settings → Profile row treatment (60pt avatar + name + email stacked), and makes the email visually less prominent than the name (it's identity context, not the lead). A single sentence would have foregrounded the email which the user shouldn't think about. **(c) Use Gravatar's MD5(email) URL convention to derive a Gravatar URL for the current user from `profile.email` instead of rendering `ProfilePhotoView`.** Rejected — `ProfilePhotoView` already handles "user uploaded their own photo via Phase b" + falls back to `InitialLetterAvatarView` when no photo, which gives a consistent first-class profile-photo experience. Gravatar would be a downgrade — it requires a network round-trip on every comment row paint, leaks the user's email hash to a third-party CDN (Constitution §9 privacy posture), and ignores any photo Phase b helped them upload. The user's profile photo is the canonical "this is me" surface; we should use it. **(d) Tag each session-posted comment ID in a local "this device posted these" set + match on ID at render time instead of stamping `authorEmail`.** Rejected — works for the in-session case but doesn't generalize: if a future task adds `?context=edit` authenticated read with the `author_email` field populated, the equality-on-email path will Just Work for previously-posted comments too, while the local-ID-set approach would still be in-session-only. The email-based equality is forward-compatible without a refactor. **(e) Retroactively re-attribute old guest comments to the new profile (set their `authorEmail` to the profile's email at first-load).** Rejected — explicitly forbidden by AC-44.7 + CL-136. The user said "old guest comments stay attributed to the guest UUID." The empty `authorEmail` on guest comments naturally falls through to the existing avatar path; no special case needed. **(f) Block the `commentRow` from showing other users' avatars until a profile exists (apply the gate to comment rendering too).** Rejected — Phase c locked the gate scope to the WRITE surface only (AC-44.11: "Existing reviews stay readable in all states"); the READ side stays open. The Phase d surgery only changes the avatar-source decision for the current user's own rows, never the other-user rows. **(g) Add an L5 E2E journey for the composer → post → photo-on-row flow.** Rejected — existing L5 journeys don't tap the comment composer (they end at "Ingredients" header), and the gate Phase c added carries the `dod.ratings.gate.cta` identifier so a future "create profile then comment" journey can be written without revisiting the implementation. The L1 + L4 coverage Phase d ships is sufficient (the submit-path test pins the WP payload sources the right values; the snapshot tests pin the composer's new shape + the own-comment avatar). **The implementation order.** Commit 1: spec amendments (this file + spec.md US-44 amendment with AC-44.12 + AC-44.13 + tasks.md T-742 entry). Commit 2: domain model — `RecipeComment.authorEmail` added + cache schema + WPDTO mapping. Commit 3: `PostingAsHeader` sub-view added to `RecipeDetailRatingsSection.swift`. Commit 4: `authorFields` + view-model author-identity machinery retired; submit path routes through `profile.displayName` / `profile.email`. Commit 5: `CommentRow` avatar override parameter + `commentRow(for:)` private builder + own-comment photo rendering. Commit 6: L1 tests + L4 snapshots. **Numbering note.** CL-139 / T-742 is the next free CL slot above CL-138 (T-741, Phase c) — DUT-36 Phase d closes the parent Linear issue (DUT-36) when merged. The `e2e` label IS applied — composer behavior end-to-end (read recipe → set up profile → comment → see profile photo on row) + composition-root data flow (profile is now sole identity source for the WP POST). **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendment — AC-44.12 added (composer surfaces source identity from Profile + WP REST payload route) + AC-44.13 added (own-comment rows render the Profile photo via case-insensitive email match). Implementing PR: T-742 (`feat/T-742-profile-phase-d`). Tests: L1 `RecipeDetailViewModel` (Submit routes `profile.displayName` + `profile.email` into the postComment dependency call), L1 case-insensitive email match (helper function or the row builder's branch coverage), L1 own-comment stamp on insert (the just-posted comment carries `profile.email` in its `authorEmail` field after `insertPostedCommentIfNew`), L4 `RecipeDetailRatingsSection` snapshots (PostingAsHeader composer + own-comment row + other-user comment row, light + dark). Estimate: ~30 min spec + ~30 min impl + ~15 min verify ≈ 75 min total.
- **CL-140 (US-44 amendment / T-743, 2026-06-02)** — **Four focused design tweaks bundled into one PR on top of the just-shipped DUT-36 feature set (Phases a–d, T-739/T-740/T-741/T-742): (a) the "Be the first to rate this recipe." empty-state label only renders when BOTH the ratings list AND the comments list are empty; (b) the ``ProfilePhotoCropView`` overlay becomes a circle so users see how the avatar will actually look, and every avatar render site across the app uniformly clips to `Circle()`; (c) ``ProfileEditView``'s `Cancel` toolbar button is replaced by a custom back chevron that intercepts dismissal when display name / email / photo have changed since load, fronts a `confirmationDialog` ("Continue Editing" / "Leave Without Saving"), and adds `.interactiveDismissDisabled(isDirty)` for the modal sheet path so the user can't swipe down past unsaved changes; (d) the trailing toolbar button is renamed `Done` → `Save`.** Polish pass on top of CL-136 + CL-137 + CL-138 + CL-139 — does NOT restructure any of the shipped Phase a–d behavior, just smooths the four rough edges testers surfaced during the DUT-36 sprint. **The locked decisions (the user resolved each up-front so the implementation didn't re-litigate).** **(1) Empty-state gate requires BOTH lists empty.** ``RecipeDetailRatingsSection``'s `ratingsHeader` currently shows the "Be the first to rate this recipe." invitation whenever `summary.count == 0` — but a recipe with 0 ratings AND ≥1 comment isn't actually "empty"; the user has already started a conversation. The new gate hides the invitation when EITHER `viewModel.ratingSummary?.count ?? 0 > 0` OR `viewModel.comments.isEmpty == false`. Truth table: 0 ratings + 0 comments → label shown; ≥1 rating + 0 comments → `StarRatingDisplay` (existing path); 0 ratings + ≥1 comment → label hidden (no aggregate either — there is no rating to aggregate); ≥1 rating + ≥1 comment → `StarRatingDisplay` (existing path). The label is the only thing the gate hides; the `commentsList` rendering below is independent. **(2) Circle crop overlay + render-side `.clipShape(Circle())` everywhere — on-disk storage stays square JPG.** ``ProfilePhotoCropView``'s dimmed-outside overlay is changed from a `Rectangle` "hole" to a `Circle` "hole" — implemented by swapping the two `Rectangle().frame(...)` calls inside `cropOverlay(cropSize:frameSize:)` for `Circle().frame(...)`. The crop math (`cropRect(for:offset:imageSize:cropSize:)`) is UNCHANGED — it still produces a square `CGRect` that the renderer samples into a 512×512 square output bitmap, which `ProfilePhotoStore` re-encodes to JPG at 0.85 quality. Only the VISUAL OVERLAY during cropping changes shape; the saved file stays a square JPG. The render-side decision (every avatar uses `.clipShape(Circle())`) is what makes the user's eye see a circle in the final UI. Pre-tweak audit of avatar render sites: `InitialLetterAvatarView` already uses `Circle()`; `ProfilePhotoView` already uses `.clipShape(Circle())`; `CommentRow`'s avatar (the AsyncImage path AND the avatarOverride path Phase d added) already uses `.clipShape(Circle())`. So the impl is overlay-shape-only — no avatar render site needs to be migrated. Consistency is preserved across `ProfileSection` (60pt), `PostingAsHeader` (32pt), `CommentRow` (40pt), and any future avatar callsite. **Why render-side `.clipShape(Circle())` instead of switching the on-disk format to PNG with an alpha-channel circle mask.** Three reasons: (i) PNG's lossless compression produces 5–10× larger files than JPG 0.85 at the same perceptual quality for photographic content; the avatar's payload size matters because the file lives in the iCloud-backed Documents directory (CL-137); (ii) baking the circle into the bitmap couples the storage format to the presentation shape — if a future polish pass introduces a square or rounded-rectangle avatar surface anywhere in the app, the on-disk image is wrong; (iii) the user can always re-clip a square source to any shape at render time, but cannot recover the corners that were destroyed if the storage was already alpha-masked.
