Render-side clipping is the canonical iOS pattern (Apple Mail, Messages, Contacts all store square photos and clip-at-render). **(3) Custom back chevron + dirty-state `confirmationDialog`.** The `ProfileEditView`'s `.topBarLeading` `Cancel` button is removed entirely; replaced by a `ToolbarItem(placement: .topBarLeading)` carrying `Button { ... } label: { Image(systemName: "chevron.left") }` (no text — matches iOS-native back-button convention; the visible page title still reads "Profile" / "New Profile"). The system back chevron is suppressed via `.navigationBarBackButtonHidden(true)` so the custom button is the only leading affordance. **Dirty state.** Captured via three `@State` initial-value snapshots (`initialDisplayName`, `initialEmail`, `initialPhotoFilename`) seeded once on `.onAppear` (alongside the existing field-seeding from `existingProfile`). The computed `isDirty` returns `true` iff any of `displayName != initialDisplayName || email != initialEmail || inFlightPhotoFilename != initialPhotoFilename`. **Custom back action.** Taps trigger `if isDirty { showLeaveConfirmation = true } else { dismiss() }`. The dialog uses `.confirmationDialog("You have unsaved changes", isPresented: $showLeaveConfirmation, titleVisibility: .visible)` with two buttons: `Button("Continue Editing") { showLeaveConfirmation = false }` (cancel out, stay on the form) and `Button("Leave Without Saving", role: .destructive) { showLeaveConfirmation = false; dismiss() }` (acknowledge the data loss, dismiss). The role is `.destructive` because the user is throwing away their in-flight edits — iOS surfaces destructive buttons in red. **`interactiveDismissDisabled(isDirty)` for the modal sheet path.** Phase c presents `ProfileEditView` as a `.sheet(isPresented:)` from the recipe-detail gate CTA; without this guard a user could drag the sheet down to dismiss WITHOUT going through the custom back button, bypassing the unsaved-changes confirmation. `.interactiveDismissDisabled(isDirty)` blocks the drag-to-dismiss gesture when the form is dirty so the user has to tap the back button (and therefore see the confirmation). When the form is clean (`isDirty == false`) the drag-down gesture works normally — no need to gate a no-op dismissal. The Settings push-entry path is unaffected (push back uses the same custom back button). **(4) Sign Out + Delete Profile bypass the dialog.** Both buttons already call `store.clear()` then `dismiss()` directly — neither consults `isDirty` or sets `showLeaveConfirmation`. After `clear()` the profile is `nil` so the "unsaved changes" concept doesn't apply (there's no profile to be unsaved against). The dialog only fires through the custom back button's intercept logic, which these two buttons do not route through. Explicit verification: their `Button { ... }` closures' action paths are `Task { await handleClear() }` (Sign Out) and `showDeleteConfirmation = true` → `Button("Delete", role: .destructive) { Task { await handleClear() } }` (Delete Profile, with the existing destructive confirmation alert preserved). `handleClear()` calls `try await store.clear()` + `await onProfileChanged()` + `dismiss()` — no `isDirty` check, no `showLeaveConfirmation` set. (The existing Delete Profile alert "Delete your profile?" stays as a separate explicit destructive confirmation — distinct from the new unsaved-changes confirmation; the two never co-fire.) **(5) "Done" → "Save" rename.** `.topBarTrailing` `Button("Done")` becomes `Button("Save")`. Action unchanged (calls `handleSave()` which validates + persists via `store.save(...)` + dispatches `onProfileChanged()` + `dismiss()`). Disabled state unchanged (`.disabled(!isFormValid || isSubmitting)`). Accessibility identifier `"profile-edit-done"` is renamed to `"profile-edit-save"` to keep the identifier consistent with the visible label (future L5 E2E journeys + L1 tests look up by identifier; the rename keeps the contract honest). **Why "Save" reads better.** "Done" is iOS's default toolbar confirmation but "Save" is unambiguous about what the button does (persist this form). "Done" works in informational dismissal contexts ("I'm done reading this") but the Profile edit screen is a data-input form — "Save" is what the user is actually doing. **What this does NOT change.** The Phase c `gatedRateAndReviewCard` gate (preserved verbatim — the gate fires when `profile == nil`; Tweak 1 is a separate, lower-down `ratingsHeader` empty-state). The `RatingsProfileGate` popup copy + CTA (unchanged). The on-disk image format (JPG, 512×512, 0.85 quality — see (2)). `App/Info.plist` (no new `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` — `PhotosPicker` still doesn't need it, locked per CL-137 (1)). The `ProfilePhotoStore` save / load / clear surface (unchanged). The Sign Out + Delete Profile semantics (unchanged — both still clear Keychain + photo file + dismiss). The Settings → Profile push entry path (unchanged — `ProfileSection`'s `NavigationLink` still pushes; the custom back button works the same way for both push and sheet contexts). The validation regex / `isFormValid` / `handleSave()` body (unchanged). The `existingProfile == nil` vs non-nil branch in the navigation title ("New Profile" vs "Profile" — unchanged). The Phase d composer (`PostingAsHeader` + `commentRow(for:)` + own-comment photo rendering — unchanged). The `e2e` label IS applied (UI surface changes the user sees + Profile edit modal behavior change). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Bake the circle into the on-disk image (switch JPG → PNG with alpha mask).** Rejected — file size cost (5–10× per CL-137 (c)), couples storage format to presentation shape (forecloses any future non-circle avatar surface), and the user can always re-clip a square source at render time. Constitution §3 / CL-137 trade-off stays. **(b) Make the empty-state gate require "≤2 comments" instead of "0 comments".** Rejected — arbitrary threshold; the user-spec said "only when truly empty," meaning ZERO of both. A small-comment-count recipe still has someone the user can read; the invitation is misleading there. **(c) Keep the "Cancel" text label but add the dialog behind it.** Rejected — the user-spec explicitly asked to replace Cancel with a back button (custom chevron, no text). "Cancel" reads as "discard everything"; a back-button + dialog reads as "go back, confirm if you have unsaved work." The back-button affordance is what iOS-native edit forms (Settings.app, Mail compose) use. **(d) Use a system-style `.alert` instead of `.confirmationDialog` for the unsaved-changes prompt.** Rejected — `.confirmationDialog` is the iOS-canonical pattern for "two-way destructive choice tied to a navigation action" (Apple HIG); alerts are for informational warnings + recoverable error reporting. The dialog also surfaces from the bottom of the screen on iPhone (less spatially jarring than an alert's centered modal). **(e) Pre-populate `initialPhotoFilename` from `existingProfile?.photoFilename` and treat the in-flight crop as "always dirty" until Save.** Rejected — a user who opens the photo confirmation dialog and taps "Cancel" should not be considered to have dirtied the form (no actual change was committed). The `inFlightPhotoFilename` state already only updates when the user completes the crop flow OR taps Remove, so it tracks the user's actual intent. The `initialPhotoFilename` snapshot is the right comparison point. **(f) Add an L5 E2E journey for the unsaved-changes dialog → leave / continue editing flow.** Rejected — existing L5 journeys don't reach Profile edit at all; adding a new full E2E for a polish tweak this self-contained is over-investment per CL-58. The L1 coverage (the `isDirty` computed property's combinations + the save button label) is sufficient. **(g) Apply `interactiveDismissDisabled(isDirty)` to the Settings push-entry path too.** Rejected — push doesn't have a swipe-down-to-dismiss gesture; the only way out of a push is via the back button (which already routes through the dialog). `interactiveDismissDisabled` is a no-op on push and SwiftUI just ignores it, so the modifier is safe to apply unconditionally — but the rationale is sheet-only. **(h) Rename "Done" to "Save Profile" for extra clarity.** Rejected — Apple's toolbar buttons run short by convention (Settings.app uses "Save", Mail uses "Save Draft" only when context demands it), and the navigation title already says "Profile" so "Save Profile" duplicates the noun. "Save" is the canonical one-word affirmative for a form. **The implementation order.** Commit 1: spec amendments (this file + spec.md US-44 amendment with AC-44.14 + AC-44.15 + AC-44.16 + tasks.md T-743 entry). Commit 2: ratings empty-state gate (Tweak 1). Commit 3: circle crop overlay (Tweak 2 — the audit confirmed all avatar render sites already use Circle, so this is overlay-only). Commit 4: unsaved-changes confirmationDialog + custom back chevron + `interactiveDismissDisabled(isDirty)` (Tweak 3). Commit 5: Done → Save rename + accessibility identifier rename (Tweak 4). **Numbering note.** CL-140 / T-743 is the next free CL slot above CL-139 (T-742, Phase d) — DUT-36 is fully closed; T-743 is a polish pass, not a new phase. The `e2e` label IS applied — Profile edit modal behavior is visibly different (back button + dialog + Save) and the Ratings empty-state copy gate is a UI rule change the user sees. **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendment — AC-44.14 added (empty-state label gate requires BOTH lists empty) + AC-44.15 added (uniform `Circle()` clipping across avatar render sites + circle crop overlay) + AC-44.16 added (custom back button + `confirmationDialog` on dirty dismiss + `interactiveDismissDisabled(isDirty)` for the modal-sheet context + Sign Out / Delete Profile bypass + `Save` rename). Implementing PR: T-743 (`fix/T-743-profile-ratings-design-tweaks`). Tests: L1 `RecipeDetailRatingsSection` ratings-header gate (the 4 combinations: 0/0 shows, >0/0 hides via aggregate, 0/>0 hides, >0/>0 hides via aggregate). L1 `ProfileEditView` save button label assertion + `isDirty` computed property combinations + `initial*` snapshot capture. The crop overlay shape change is validated transitively via the existing `cropRect(for:)` math tests (math unchanged) + manual visual smoke-test. Estimate: ~20 min spec + ~25 min impl + ~10 min verify ≈ 55 min total.
- **CL-137 (US-44 / DUT-36 Phase b / T-740, 2026-06-02)** — **Wire the Profile Picture row to a SwiftUI ``PhotosPicker`` + a custom SwiftUI square crop view + a UUID-keyed JPG in Documents at 512×512 @ 0.85 quality + on-disk loading via ``ProfilePhotoView`` with ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` graceful-degradation fallback.** Phase b of DUT-36 — extends CL-136. **The architecture choice (locked up-front so the implementation didn't re-litigate).** **(1) PhotosPicker (SwiftUI, iOS 16+) for selection — chosen for privacy.** ``PhotosPicker`` uses Apple's privacy-preserving picker, so **no `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` Info.plist entry is required and no permission prompt is shown**. The user picks a single image without granting the app full library access. The picker hands back a ``PhotosPickerItem``; the row loads its `Data` via `loadTransferable(type: Data.self)` and surfaces a `UIImage` to the crop sheet. **The legacy alternative — `UIImagePickerController(allowsEditing: true)` — was considered + rejected.** UIImagePickerController has a built-in square-crop affordance for free (the system's native cropper), but it requires `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` + triggers the permission alert, which is a worse first-time-photo-upload UX than the PhotosPicker's zero-prompt flow. Privacy posture > free-system-crop. **(2) Custom SwiftUI square crop view — chosen over third-party libraries.** ``PhotosPicker`` does NOT include built-in editing or cropping (that's the trade we accept for the privacy posture), so the app needs its own crop UI. Constitution §3 (default-no on new third-party dependencies) — Mantis + BSImagePicker were both considered + rejected. The custom ``ProfilePhotoCropView`` is bounded — `GeometryReader`-sized container + `Image` with `.scaleEffect` + `.offset` modifiers + `MagnificationGesture` (1.0..4.0 clamp range) + `DragGesture` (pan within bounds) + a dimmed overlay outside the square crop frame — and fits under 200 lines. The Done toolbar button renders the cropped image at the user's transform via `UIGraphicsImageRenderer` and hands the resulting `UIImage` back through a callback; Cancel dismisses without committing. **(3) Output: 512×512 JPG at 0.85 quality.** 512×512 is good enough for the 60pt Settings avatar at @3x (180px) with headroom for any larger future surface (a 100pt header or a 256pt full-screen profile view). 0.85 JPEG quality produces ~50–150 KB per photo — small enough to keep the Documents directory clean even with many Replace cycles. Output ratio is always 1:1 (square) — the crop frame is a fixed square aspect, no portrait/landscape branch. **(4) Storage: UUID-keyed filename in Documents (`profile-photo-<UUID().uuidString>.jpg`).** A UUID-derived name is more robust than a fixed `profile-photo.jpg`: Replace-Photo writes a new UUID and clears the old file, so we never have a stale write half-overwriting the previous JPG on a partial failure. ``ProfilePhotoStore`` is an `actor` (matches the ``KeychainProfileStore`` concurrency posture) with `save(_ image: UIImage) async throws -> String` (returns the filename), `load(filename: String) async -> UIImage?`, `clear(filename: String) async throws`. Documents directory chosen over Caches because the photo is user content not regeneratable cache (Caches would let iOS evict on low storage). **(5) Replace + Remove confirmation dialog.** When `photoFilename` exists the row tap presents a `confirmationDialog` with three options — Replace Photo (re-presents the picker + crop), Remove Photo (clears the in-flight `photoFilename` + calls `ProfilePhotoStore.clear(filename:)` for the previous file → row falls back to the initial-letter avatar), Cancel. When `photoFilename` is nil the row tap goes straight to the picker. **(6) Sign Out + Delete Profile also clear the photo file.** ``ProfileStoring/clear()`` is amended to also delete the on-disk photo file when the current profile carries a `photoFilename` (read profile pre-clear → call `ProfilePhotoStore.clear(filename:)` → clear Keychain). So Sign Out and Delete Profile leave both Keychain + Documents in the same clean state — no orphan JPGs. This requires the production ``KeychainProfileStore`` to gain a `photoStore: ProfilePhotoStoring?` collaborator + a `init(service:accessGroup:photoStore:)` overload. **(7) Photo never leaves the device.** Constitution §9 — never POSTed to any backend, never sent to TelemetryDeck, never serialized into the comment-submitted analytics payload (Phase d will only auto-fill name + email from the profile, not the photo). **The flow end-to-end.** Profile Picture row tap (when `photoFilename == nil`) → `PhotosPicker` sheet → user picks → `selectedItem` `Binding` updates → `.onChange(of: selectedItem)` loads `Data` via `loadTransferable` + creates a `UIImage` → presents ``ProfilePhotoCropView`` as a sheet with the picked image → user pinch/pan → Done → `cropAndExport()` renders a 512×512 JPG at 0.85 quality → `ProfilePhotoStore.save(image)` returns a UUID-derived filename → the in-flight ``UserProfile`` gets `photoFilename` updated → crop sheet dismisses → on next Done-save the new filename round-trips through the Keychain. Replace path is identical except the row first calls `ProfilePhotoStore.clear(filename:)` on the previous file after the new one is saved (write-then-clear-old, never the reverse, so a mid-flow failure leaves the old file intact). **The crop math (the `cropRect(for transform:)` helper).** Pure function so the L1 test suite can pin the transform math without a view host: input is `(scale: CGFloat, offset: CGSize, imageSize: CGSize, cropSize: CGFloat)`, output is the source-image rect (in image coordinates) the crop should sample. Returns a square rect centered on the user's transform after clamping. The renderer uses this rect via `UIGraphicsImageRenderer(size: CGSize(width: 512, height: 512))` to draw the source image scaled+translated into the 512×512 output. Pinned by `ProfilePhotoCropMathTests` (centered + zoomed-in + clamped-to-edge cases). **The renderer (``ProfilePhotoView``).** Loads the image lazily in `.task` from `ProfilePhotoStore.load(filename:)` when `profile?.photoFilename != nil`; caches the loaded `UIImage` in `@State` so re-renders don't re-touch disk. Renders `Image(uiImage:).resizable().scaledToFill().frame(width: diameter, height: diameter).clipShape(Circle())`. When the file is missing (graceful degradation — the Keychain still has `photoFilename` but Documents was wiped by the user via Files.app, or a partial sync from a future cross-device push) the view falls back to ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` — same fallback as the no-photo state. **What this does NOT change.** US-15 guest identity (unchanged). The Comment composer + Rating composer (unchanged — Phases c/d will wire the profile bridge). `App/Info.plist` (no new privacy keys — `PhotosPicker` doesn't need `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription`). `App/DODApp.entitlements` (no new entitlements). The Keychain `service = com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp.profile.v1` (unchanged). The `Documents` directory location semantics (still per-app sandbox, still backed up to iCloud as user content — that's the standard iOS posture and the locked decision per CL-137). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) `UIImagePickerController(allowsEditing: true)` for the free system crop.** Rejected — requires `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` + permission prompt, worse privacy posture than `PhotosPicker`'s zero-prompt access. **(b) Mantis / BSImagePicker third-party crop libraries.** Rejected — constitution §3 default-no respected; the custom SwiftUI crop is bounded and reads naturally next to the existing SwiftUI view code. **(c) PNG output.** Rejected — PNG's lossless compression produces 5–10× larger files than JPG 0.85 at the same perceptual quality for photographic content; the avatar surfaces don't need the alpha channel. **(d) Fixed `profile-photo.jpg` filename.** Rejected — UUID-keyed is more robust to partial-failure Replace cycles (no stale half-write); also future-proofs against a hypothetical "multiple profiles" feature without forcing a migration. **(e) Save the cropped image inline to Keychain as a Data blob.** Rejected — Keychain rows have a strict size budget (target <4KB per row; macOS allows larger but iOS is the constraint), 50-150KB JPG payloads would breach. Documents directory is the canonical iOS location for user-content blobs. **(f) Saves and clears synchronously on the main actor.** Rejected — the `ProfilePhotoStore` `actor` matches the ``KeychainProfileStore`` posture so the disk I/O happens off the main thread; the UI awaits the actor calls without blocking touch handling. **(g) Snapshot test ``ProfilePhotoCropView`` directly.** Rejected — the crop view is gesture-driven with dynamic `GeometryReader` layout that doesn't reproduce cleanly under `UIHostingController` snapshot harnesses; coverage of the underlying `cropRect(for:)` transform math via L1 unit tests gives the same regression guarantee with less flake. ``ProfilePhotoView`` does get L4 snapshots (photo present + photo missing fallback + no-photo nil branch). **(h) Always overwrite a single fixed filename, no UUID.** Rejected — see (d); UUID-keyed Replace cycles are atomic by construction (write new → save filename → clear old), the fixed-name flow would have to write to a tmp file first then mv-overwrite, an extra step with the same effective complexity. **Numbering note.** CL-137 / T-740 is the next free CL slot above CL-136 (T-739, Phase a) and the next free task slot above T-741. The `e2e` label IS applied — new Settings UI behavior end-to-end (picker → crop → save → render). **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendment — AC-44.3 amended to wire the picker + crop + Documents-store + render flow; AC-44.8 (Replace + Remove dialog) + AC-44.9 (Sign Out + Delete Profile clear the photo file) added. Implementing PR: T-740 (`feat/T-740-profile-phase-b`). Tests: L1 `ProfilePhotoStoreTests` (save-then-load round-trip, clear removes the file, load of missing returns nil, oversized image still works), L1 `ProfilePhotoCropMathTests` (centered, zoomed-in, clamped-to-edge), L1 `UserProfile` extension (photoFilename Codable round-trip), L1 `ProfileStoreTests` integration (clear() with a populated photoFilename also calls `ProfilePhotoStore.clear(filename:)` — pinned via mock photo-store), L4 `ProfilePhotoViewSnapshotTests` (no photo → InitialLetterAvatarView; photo loaded; photo file missing → graceful fallback). `ProfilePhotoCropView` itself is covered transitively by the `cropRect(for:)` math tests; no gesture-driven snapshot test. Estimate: ~30 min spec + ~35 min impl + ~10 min verify ≈ 75 min total.
- **CL-141 (US-44 amendment / T-744, 2026-06-03)** — **Two XS polish tweaks bundled into one PR on top of T-743 / CL-140's design-tweak pass: (a) hide the email row from ``PostingAsHeader`` in the rate/review/comment composer so the composer surface displays avatar + display name only — published comments never expose `author_email`, so the composer's own-email display was inconsistent with that privacy posture; (b) make the ``RatingsProfileGate`` popup card read as a solid, on-brand surface — wrap the popup body in `.background(RoundedRectangle.fill(DODColor.surface).shadow(...))` so the SwiftUI compositor knows the card is fully opaque and the underlying `.ultraThinMaterial` overlay can't tint the card's interior.** Polish pass on top of CL-138 (Phase c — `RatingsProfileGate`) + CL-139 (Phase d — `PostingAsHeader`) + CL-140 (T-743 — DUT-36 polish). Closes Linear DUT-37 + DUT-38. Bounded entirely to `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/` — `RecipeDetailRatingsSection+PhaseD.swift` (the `PostingAsHeader` struct's body) + `RatingsProfileGate.swift` (the popup card's background composition). **The decision context (Spencer, 2026-06-03).** During post-T-743 manual smoke-testing two rough edges surfaced on the just-shipped surfaces — both XS-scope polish, both isolated to a single render-site, both deliverable in one ≤30-min PR. Spencer surfaced them as DUT-37 + DUT-38 and asked for a single bundled PR (not two separate ones — they share the same package, the same file ownership, and the same review surface). **(1) DUT-37 — hide email from ``PostingAsHeader``.** The composer surface today renders ``ProfilePhotoView`` (32pt) + ``VStack { Text(profile.displayName); Text(profile.email) }`` + `Spacer`. The two-line `VStack` shows the user their own email back at them — a privacy posture mismatch with the rest of the comment surface. Verified at 3 layers that the published-comment path never exposes `author_email`: (i) WordPress REST `/wp/v2/comments` GET redacts the field server-side per the existing WP privacy posture (matches the `RecipeComment` doc-string already on `authorEmail`: "GET does NOT include `author_email` in the response (privacy — email is moderation-only)"); (ii) ``WPCommentDTO`` zeroes the field on parse so the in-memory cache mirrors what WP returned (empty string); (iii) ``CommentRow`` has no email-render path — only `authorName`, `avatarURL`, `relativeDate`, `bodyText`, `ratingValue`, and the avatar override. So OTHER users never see anyone else's email; the only place the composer shows AN email is the current user's own — to themselves — pulled from the in-memory ``UserProfile``. Spencer's framing: "Email is a private piece of identity the user manages in Settings → Profile + ``ProfileEditView``; the composer should read as 'you're posting as this person', not as a billboard showing the address back to them. Settings is where email is actively managed; the composer is where email is incidental context." The fix is the smallest possible surgery: delete the `Text(profile.email)` line, collapse the inner `VStack` to a single `Text(profile.displayName)`, and simplify the surrounding `HStack` to drop the now-unneeded vertical-stack wrapper (one line of text, no need for a `VStack`). The VoiceOver `accessibilityLabel` is amended from "Posting as `<displayName>`, `<email>`" to "Posting as `<displayName>`" — the email phrase is dropped since it's no longer rendered (announcing it would be misleading to non-sighted users). **The WP REST submission path is NOT changed.** ``RecipeDetailViewModel+CommentSubmit`` still pulls `author_name` = `profile.displayName` and `author_email` = `profile.email` from the profile and POSTs both to `/wp/v2/comments`; the network call is unchanged. The L4 snapshot expectation (`commentAuthorEmail == "spencer@example.com"` in the test setup) stays valid — the test asserts the view-model's mirrored value for downstream WP REST submission, not the UI render. Only the UI display changes. **(2) DUT-38 — gate popup solid surface background.** During smoke-testing on dark mode Spencer noticed the ``RatingsProfileGate`` popup card reads as grey-brown haze instead of a solid surface. **The investigation order (per the task brief's `Investigation step 1` → `step 2` sequence).** Step 1 confirmed the popup card's `.background(...)` modifier already uses `DODColor.surface` (not `surfaceElevated` — the token-confusion hypothesis ruled out; the surfaces diverge in dark mode `#1B140E` deep brown vs `#281F19` lighter brown, but the popup was already pinned to `.surface`). Step 2 examined the ZStack composition in `gatedRateAndReviewCard`: `ZStack { rateAndReviewCard.blur(...); Rectangle.fill(.ultraThinMaterial).allowsHitTesting(false); RatingsProfileGate(...) }`. The popup sits ABOVE the Material in z-order, so the Material itself does NOT z-stack-blend through the popup — but the popup's `.background(...)` shape was rendering with the `RoundedRectangle.fill(DODColor.surface)` AND `.shadow(...)` chained on the SAME shape inside the background closure. In SwiftUI, applying `.shadow(...)` to a filled shape inside a `.background(...)` causes the shape to be rendered into an off-screen layer with the shadow blurred — and that layer's compositing can blend with the underlying Material's tint at the edges + corners (the cumulative effect of the shadow's antialiased perimeter + the popup's own slight rendering translucency at the rounded corners is enough for the user's eye to read the whole card as grey-brown haze, not as a solid surface). **The chosen root cause (documented for the impl commit message).** The popup card's `.background(RoundedRectangle.fill(.surface).shadow(...))` chain renders the shadow + fill as a single composite layer, and that composite layer doesn't read as fully opaque to SwiftUI's depth-aware Material blending. **The chosen fix shape.** Hoist the shadow OUT of the fill chain — apply `.shadow(...)` as a SEPARATE modifier ON the `.background(RoundedRectangle.fill(.surface))` background result, so the fill is the primary opaque layer and the shadow is applied to the composited card AFTER the opaque-fill commitment. In SwiftUI prose: change `.background(RoundedRectangle.fill(.surface).shadow(...))` to `.background(RoundedRectangle.fill(.surface))` + `.shadow(...)` on the OUTSIDE of the VStack (so the shadow renders against the final composed card, not the unfilled rect). Equivalent fix: wrap the popup card in `.background { RoundedRectangle.fill(.surface) }.shadow(...)` so the fill commits to its own layer before the shadow is applied. **The ZStack structure itself is preserved** — the task brief locked "Don't change the gate ZStack structure unless required" + the Material overlay over the composer + popup card on top is the right shape per AC-44.10. The fix is entirely INSIDE `RatingsProfileGate.body` — the call-site in `gatedRateAndReviewCard` is untouched. **What this does NOT change.** The WP REST submission payload (locked per task brief — `author_email` still flows through as `profile.email`; only the composer's UI hide changes). The Phase c gate ZStack composition (preserved — Material on top of blurred composer, popup on top of Material). The popup card's CTA copy / accessibility identifier / `onSetUpProfile` callback (unchanged — `RatingsProfileGate(onSetUpProfile:)` constructor stable). The ``ProfileEditView`` modal sheet presentation path (unchanged). The DUT-36 / DUT-37 backstory on email in Settings + ``ProfileEditView`` (unchanged — Spencer manages the email there, and Settings → Profile section header still renders `displayName` + `email`). The recipe-detail page background (still `DODColor.surface`). The L4 snapshot tests for `test_section_postingAsHeader_renders` + `test_section_gatedState_renders` + `test_section_ungatedState_renders` re-record their baselines via `record: .missing` after the stale `.1.png` is deleted (no test-shape change — just delete-and-regenerate). **The other DUT-36 surfaces are NOT touched** (locked per task brief — Profile edit view, photo crop, comment-row rendering for OTHER users). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Swap the popup card to `DODColor.surfaceElevated`.** Rejected — the popup floats over the recipe page; in dark mode the page itself is `.surface` `#1B140E` and the popup needs to read as "elevated" relative to that. But `.surfaceElevated` `#281F19` is only marginally lighter and the popup already gets visual elevation from the `.shadow(...)` modifier; switching to `.surfaceElevated` would muddy the on-brand contrast against the dark recipe-detail page without resolving the underlying compositor concern. **(b) Add `.compositingGroup()` to the popup card to force a flat layer before the Material composites.** Rejected — `.compositingGroup()` is a sledgehammer fix that hurts SwiftUI's draw-order optimization; the cleaner fix is to hoist the shadow OUT of the fill chain so the fill commits to its own layer naturally (which is what the chosen fix does). **(c) Replace the `.ultraThinMaterial` `Rectangle` overlay with a solid `Rectangle().fill(DODColor.label.opacity(0.4))` "scrim" instead.** Rejected — the Material dim is the iOS-canonical "you can't interact with what's underneath" affordance (Apple HIG modal dim pattern). Swapping it out changes the gate's visual affordance language (matches share sheets, action sheets, photos picker). The popup card itself is the surface that needs to read solid — not the dim layer. **(d) Hide the email from the Profile Settings section header + ``ProfileEditView`` too (consistency across surfaces).** Rejected — Settings → Profile is exactly WHERE email is managed; hiding it there would conflict with the user's mental model (where do I see / edit my email?). The composer is incidental context; Settings is the canonical edit surface. **(e) Make the email render path conditional (e.g. only show if `profile.email != profile.displayName` or some heuristic).** Rejected — adds branching for no clear signal; the user's display name and email are independent fields. The clean answer is "composer shows display name only, Settings shows both." **The implementation order.** Commit 1: spec amendments (this file + spec.md AC-44.10 + AC-44.12 amendments + tasks.md T-744 entry). Commit 2: DUT-37 hide email from ``PostingAsHeader`` body (the smallest surgery: delete the email `Text`, collapse the inner `VStack`, simplify the `HStack`, drop the `accessibilityLabel` email phrase). Commit 3: DUT-38 ``RatingsProfileGate`` solid surface background (hoist `.shadow(...)` OUT of the `.background(...)` fill chain so the fill commits as its own opaque layer). Commit 4: re-record the L4 snapshot baselines that change shape (`test_section_postingAsHeader_renders` + `test_section_gatedState_renders` + `test_section_ungatedState_renders`) — delete the stale `.1.png` baselines + let `record: .missing` regenerate. **Numbering note.** CL-141 / T-744 is the next free CL slot above CL-140 (T-743, polish pass). The `e2e` label IS applied — composer surface render changes (email no longer visible) + popup card render changes (solid background) are both UI rule changes the user sees. **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendment — AC-44.10 amended (popup card uses solid `DODColor.surface` background, no Material bleed-through) + AC-44.12 amended (`PostingAsHeader` shows avatar + display name only, no email; VoiceOver label collapses to "Posting as `<displayName>`"). Implementing PR: T-744 (`fix/T-744-composer-email-and-gate-bg`). Tests: L1 `PostingAsHeader` assertion added — pin that the `PostingAsHeader` view body builds + does NOT include the email render path (the prior T-742 task did not ship a dedicated L1 for `PostingAsHeader`; T-744 adds one here). L4 `test_section_postingAsHeader_renders` + `test_section_gatedState_renders` + `test_section_ungatedState_renders` baselines re-recorded via stale-baseline delete + `record: .missing`. The popup card's solid-surface rendering is validated transitively via the gated-state snapshot. Estimate: ~10 min spec + ~10 min impl + ~10 min verify ≈ 30 min total.
- **CL-142 (US-44 amendment / T-745, 2026-06-03)** — **Two related changes to the Profile Picture row in ``ProfileEditView`` bundled into one PR, closes Linear DUT-39: (a) **direct picker on placeholder** — when no photo exists (`profile.photoFilename == nil`), tapping the Profile Picture row opens ``PhotosPicker`` directly instead of presenting the action sheet first (today the user has to tap "Remove Photo" as a workaround to enter the picker, since "Replace Photo" is the only Replace path and the action sheet shows even with no photo to replace); (b) **Edit Photo** option — when a photo IS set, the action sheet adds a new **Edit Photo** option that loads the user's existing source image and re-opens ``ProfilePhotoCropView`` so they can adjust the crop without re-picking from the photo library. Enabling (b) requires storing the **picked original** alongside the cropped 512×512 derivative — today only the cropped square is on disk, so a meaningful re-crop has nothing to re-crop FROM (re-cropping the already-cropped 512×512 throws away half the source pixels and degrades quality further). The fix adds a second on-disk artifact: the original picked image, downscaled to a longest-dimension cap of 2048×2048 at 0.85 JPEG quality (~300-500KB), saved alongside the existing cropped 512×512 derivative. ``UserProfile`` gains an optional `photoOriginalFilename` field; ``ProfilePhotoStore`` gains `saveOriginal` / `loadOriginal` / `clearOriginal` methods; Replace + Edit + Remove + Sign Out + Delete Profile flows all extend to handle both files. **The decision context (Spencer, 2026-06-03 via Linear DUT-39).** During post-T-744 manual smoke-testing two rough edges surfaced on the Profile Picture row: (1) the action-sheet-on-placeholder behavior reads as a bug — the action sheet's "Replace Photo" + "Remove Photo" + "Cancel" options assume a photo exists, but with no photo set the only meaningful path is to pick one, and that's exactly what `PhotosPicker` does directly; "Replace Photo" via the workaround-tap path still works but the extra confirmation tap is friction without purpose; (2) the absence of an "Edit Photo" path means a user who picked a not-quite-right crop has to re-pick the same image from their library, re-tap Done, re-crop — a full picker round-trip for what should be a single re-crop tap. Both rough edges are minor in isolation but compound on top of T-740 / Phase b's substantial photo-flow investment; the user-facing complaint is "I picked a photo and the crop is off-center — why do I have to re-pick the same image to fix it?" **The two-file storage decision (the substantive half).** Option (i) **save only cropped** — current state; can't meaningfully re-crop (re-cropping the 512×512 destroys image quality at every cycle). Option (ii) **save only original** — would require crop-on-render at every avatar surface (60pt Settings + 32pt PostingAsHeader + 40pt own-comment row + future surfaces), which is expensive (every render pays the crop tax) AND couples presentation to source-image quality (large source = slower render). Option (iii) **save both** — chosen: cropped 512×512 for fast renders (every avatar surface reads the pre-cropped derivative, no per-render crop math), original (downscaled to 2048 max) for re-crop. **Cost.** Adds ~300-500KB per profile in the Documents directory (a 2048×2048 JPEG at 0.85 is in that ballpark; portrait or landscape originals at 2048×1536 / 1536×2048 are smaller). Total profile storage today is ~50-150KB (cropped only); with the original added it grows to ~350-650KB total. Negligible on modern iOS devices (Documents directory is unbounded except by total device storage; the photo is a single user-managed asset, not a per-row cache). The 2048 cap is enough for meaningful re-crop on any device the app supports (iPhone 12 mini's 2436×1125 Retina display is the largest pixel surface; 2048 covers it with headroom), while staying small enough to keep the Documents directory tidy. **Quality trade-off.** The downscale from the original (any size) to 2048-max is a one-time loss; the cropped 512×512 is rendered from the 2048 source via the existing crop pipeline. Re-crop operations sample the 2048 source repeatedly without further quality loss (every re-crop reads the same source, samples a sub-rect, re-renders the 512 derivative). **Why not preserve the full original.** Original camera photos can be 4032×3024 (iPhone 13 Pro main camera) — ~3-5MB each. Saving the full original would push the per-profile Documents footprint to ~3-5MB, dominated by the original. The 2048 cap is the sweet spot: large enough for every re-crop the user will realistically do (they can't pinch-zoom past the source's actual resolution anyway — the crop view clamps at scale 4.0 which on a 2048 source is 512×512 visible window, exactly the cropped output resolution), small enough for the Documents directory to stay tidy. **Why JPEG 0.85 again (not 0.9, not lossless).** Matches the cropped derivative's quality (CL-137 locked 0.85 as the perceptually-lossless threshold for photographic content), and JPEG is the on-disk format for both files so a future debugger sees the same encoding on both. **Filename shape.** `profile-photo-original-<UUID>.jpg` — UUID-keyed (not a fixed name) for the same atomic-Replace rationale as the cropped derivative (CL-137 decision (h)): a Replace flow writes the new UUID then clears the old file via `clearOriginal(filename:)`, never the reverse, so a mid-flow failure leaves the previous original intact. Same prefix-suffix convention as the cropped derivative so the Documents directory's photo files are easy to spot. **`UserProfile.photoOriginalFilename` Codable.** New optional `String` field; the JSON decoder uses `decodeIfPresent` semantics (Swift's auto-synthesized Codable handles optional fields gracefully — a missing key decodes to `nil` rather than throwing). Legacy profiles persisted before this change (only `photoFilename`, no `photoOriginalFilename`) decode cleanly into the new struct shape. New encodings include the field. **Legacy users with only the cropped 512×512.** When a returning user with a pre-T-745 profile taps Edit Photo, `photoOriginalFilename` is nil — the Edit flow falls back to loading `photoFilename` (the cropped 512×512) and re-crops that. Documented quality limitation: re-cropping a 512×512 produces a smaller effective source for the crop view (max zoom is 4.0 on a 512 source = 128×128 visible window, less than the 512 output target, so the re-crop is upscaling). Functional but degraded. The user can break out of this by tapping Replace Photo to re-pick from the library, which captures a fresh original at 2048 — Edit Photo becomes meaningful again on the next iteration. **The conditional picker entry (the easy half).** ``profileEditPhotoSection``'s `Button` action today is `if inFlightPhotoFilename != nil { showPhotoActionDialog = true } else { isPickerPresented = true }` — that's actually already the right conditional shape (T-740 / Phase b's original implementation), so re-verifying the existing code confirms this is a no-op restructuring; the bug must be elsewhere. **Re-examination.** Inspecting `ProfileEditView+Photo.swift` lines 39-46: the button's `action` closure IS already conditional — `if inFlightPhotoFilename != nil { showPhotoActionDialog = true } else { isPickerPresented = true }`. So the "bug" the user filed (action sheet on placeholder) is NOT in the production code today — either the issue was resolved in T-740 and the Linear ticket is stale, OR the bug exists in a different code path. **Investigation (run before writing the fix).** Re-test on simulator: open Profile (no photo set), tap row → confirm whether action sheet OR picker presents. If picker (no bug), the spec entry documents the verification + closes DUT-39 as already-fixed. If action sheet (bug confirmed), trace the call path to find the root cause. **Resolution path.** Per the task brief's "find the row + action sheet wiring" + "restructure to a conditional" instructions, the fix shape is the same regardless — the conditional `inFlightPhotoFilename != nil` is the right gate. If the code already has it, no edit. If not, add it. Either way, the bug-fix commit message documents the state on inspection. **Edit Photo flow.** Tap action sheet → tap **Edit Photo** option (new) → `loadPhotoForEditing()` async closure: (a) if `photoOriginalFilename != nil`, `await profilePhotoStore.loadOriginal(filename:)` returns the 2048-max source; (b) else `await profilePhotoStore.load(filename: photoFilename!)` returns the 512×512 cropped (legacy fallback); (c) present `ProfilePhotoCropView` with the loaded image via the existing `cropCandidate` `@State` + `.sheet(item:)` plumbing; (d) on crop Done, `handleCroppedImage(_:)` (unchanged) calls `await profilePhotoStore.save(croppedImage)` which writes a NEW UUID-keyed cropped file; (e) since the cropped filename in `inFlightPhotoFilename` is now different (UUID changed), the existing Replace-flow machinery in `handleCroppedImage` marks the old cropped filename for `photoFilenameToClearOnSave` so post-save cleanup deletes the orphan. **The original stays as-is** — only the cropped derivative is overwritten on Edit. **UI re-render after Edit.** The cropped filename changes (new UUID) so SwiftUI's view-state diff naturally invalidates the avatar — no special re-render machinery needed. The avatar reads `inFlightPhotoFilename` via the live `previewProfile` computed property in `ProfileEditView+Photo.swift`. **Replace Photo flow extends to save the original.** ``loadPickedImage(_:)`` today loads the picker's `Data` → `UIImage` → presents `cropCandidate`. The extended flow ALSO calls `await profilePhotoStore.saveOriginal(image)` to persist the original alongside, before presenting the crop sheet. The returned `originalFilename` is held in a new `@State var inFlightPhotoOriginalFilename: String?` — distinct from the existing `inFlightPhotoFilename` so the diff machinery can track both filenames independently. On Save, both filenames flow into the `UserProfile` constructor as `photoFilename` + `photoOriginalFilename`. **Remove Photo flow extends to clear the original.** ``handleRemovePhoto()`` today marks the cropped filename for `photoFilenameToClearOnSave` then nulls `inFlightPhotoFilename`. The extended flow ALSO marks `photoOriginalFilename` for clean-up via a new `photoOriginalFilenameToClearOnSave` `@State` — and nulls `inFlightPhotoOriginalFilename` alongside. The post-save cleanup in `handleSave` extends to call `await photoStore?.clearOriginal(filename:)` on the marked original filename (matching the existing `clear(filename:)` call on the cropped). **Sign Out + Delete Profile flow extends to clear the original.** ``KeychainProfileStore.clear()``'s `clearPhotoIfPresent` private helper today reads the existing profile pre-clear and calls `await photoStore.clear(filename: profile.photoFilename)` if non-nil. The extended helper ALSO calls `await photoStore.clearOriginal(filename: profile.photoOriginalFilename)` if non-nil. The `InMemoryProfileStore.clear()` mirror does the same. **``ProfilePhotoStoring`` protocol additions.** Three new methods: `saveOriginal(_ image: UIImage) async throws -> String` (downscales if longest dimension > 2048, JPEG 0.85, returns filename); `loadOriginal(filename: String) async -> UIImage?` (parallel to existing `load`); `clearOriginal(filename: String) async throws` (parallel to existing `clear`). Both production `ProfilePhotoStore` and `InMemoryProfilePhotoStore` implement all three. **Downscale algorithm for `saveOriginal`.** Compute `let longest = max(image.size.width, image.size.height)`. If `longest <= 2048`, save at original resolution (no resize). Else compute `let scale = 2048 / longest`, render via `UIGraphicsImageRenderer` at `CGSize(width: image.size.width * scale, height: image.size.height * scale)` with `format.scale = 1.0` (so the output is in pixels not points), draw the source into that frame. Preserves aspect ratio (both dimensions scaled by the same factor). JPEG 0.85 via `jpegData(compressionQuality: 0.85)`. **Constitution + no-new-deps respected.** No new third-party dependency — uses existing `UIGraphicsImageRenderer` (same primitive `ProfilePhotoStore.save` uses for the 512 crop). No `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` change — `PhotosPicker` still doesn't need it (locked per CL-137 (1) + reiterated in the task brief). **What this does NOT change.** ``ProfilePhotoCropView``'s public interface (still accepts a `UIImage` + `onComplete` + `onCancel`; both Replace and Edit flows feed it different sources but the view itself is unchanged). The cropped 512×512 JPEG @ 0.85 format (locked per CL-137; the new field is opt-in). The action-sheet copy when a photo IS set (still "Replace Photo" / "Remove Photo" / "Cancel" PLUS the new "Edit Photo" option). The Phase a + b + c + d behavior (preserved verbatim — this is amendments to AC-44.8 + AC-44.9 + the underlying storage shape, NOT a phase). The picker presentation when no photo is set (`PhotosPicker` direct entry — this IS the change being documented). The validation regex / `isFormValid` / `handleSave()` body (unchanged). The Settings → Profile push entry path + the Phase c gate-CTA sheet path (unchanged). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Preserve the full original (no 2048 cap).** Rejected — per-profile Documents footprint balloons to 3-5MB; 2048 covers every realistic re-crop scenario on iOS devices; saves storage with no user-perceptible quality difference. **(b) Save only the cropped 512×512 (existing state) — Edit Photo re-crops the cropped.** Rejected — every re-crop iteratively degrades the image (each cycle the cropped is the source for the next cropped). The substantive complaint the user filed (re-crop without re-picking) requires a meaningful source to re-crop from; the only way to do that without a full picker round-trip is to keep the original on disk. **(c) Switch the cropped derivative to a PNG with crop-rect metadata, render at presentation time.** Rejected — same arguments as CL-140's "PNG with alpha mask" rejection (file size 5-10× larger, couples storage to presentation, foreclosed by future non-circle surfaces). Plus crop-on-render is expensive (CL-137 (3) rationale — fast renders, no per-render crop math). **(d) Use a UUID-keyed pair where the original and cropped share a UUID stem (e.g., `profile-photo-<UUID>-original.jpg` + `profile-photo-<UUID>-cropped.jpg`).** Rejected — couples the two filenames to a shared identifier; the Replace flow + Edit flow + Remove flow all manage them independently. Separate UUIDs are cleaner for the existing atomic-Replace pattern (CL-137 (h)). **(e) Add a new section to the spec instead of amending AC-44.8 + AC-44.9.** Rejected — the conditional picker entry IS what AC-44.8 already describes ("when `photoFilename` is nil the row tap goes straight to the picker (no dialog)"); the Edit Photo + original-storage feature is a natural extension of AC-44.8's Replace/Remove/Cancel triad. Amending the existing ACs keeps the spec coherent rather than fragmenting the photo-flow story across multiple sections. **(f) Persist `photoOriginalFilename` only when the user has explicitly used Edit Photo (opt-in lazy save).** Rejected — defeats the purpose. The 2048 cap is small enough to be invisible; the original-saved-on-pick path means a user who picks a photo and immediately taps Edit can re-crop without a library round-trip. Opting in later would require a re-pick to populate the original, which is the exact friction this CL fixes. **(g) Add a "Reset to default" option to the action sheet alongside Edit Photo.** Rejected — "Reset to default" doesn't make sense in this context (the default is the initial-letter avatar, which is what Remove Photo already produces). Adding it would duplicate Remove Photo's behavior under a different label, confusing the user. **The implementation order.** Commit 1 (spec): this file + spec.md US-44 amendments to AC-44.8 + AC-44.9 + new ACs covering the original-image storage + the Edit Photo flow + tasks.md T-745 entry. Commit 2 (data model): `UserProfile.photoOriginalFilename` field + Codable round-trip + ``ProfilePhotoStore`` original methods (saveOriginal / loadOriginal / clearOriginal) on both production + in-memory + the protocol. Commit 3 (UI bug fix): conditional picker entry (verify existing code or restructure if needed). Commit 4 (Edit Photo): action sheet option + `loadPhotoForEditing` async closure + re-crop flow + `inFlightPhotoOriginalFilename` `@State` + Replace flow extension to save original. Commit 5 (Remove + Sign Out + Delete): extend `handleRemovePhoto` + `handleSave` post-save cleanup + `KeychainProfileStore.clear` + `InMemoryProfileStore.clear` to handle both files. **Numbering note.** CL-142 / T-745 is the next free CL slot above CL-141 (T-744, polish pass). The `e2e` label IS applied — Profile edit view photo-flow behavior is visibly different (action sheet skip on placeholder + Edit Photo option + storage layout extension), and the changes are user-observable. **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendments — AC-44.8 amended (direct picker entry when no photo + new Edit Photo option in action sheet); AC-44.9 amended (clear both cropped + original on Sign Out / Delete Profile); AC-44.17 added (`photoOriginalFilename` field + `saveOriginal` / `loadOriginal` / `clearOriginal` methods + the 2048-max downscale + the legacy fallback). Implementing PR: T-745 (`feat/T-745-photo-picker-direct-and-edit`). Tests: L1 `UserProfile` Codable for `photoOriginalFilename` (round-trip + legacy-missing-field decodes to nil); L1 `ProfilePhotoStore.saveOriginal` downscale math (4096×4096 → 2048×2048; 3000×4000 → 1536×2048 aspect preservation); L1 `ProfilePhotoStore.loadOriginal` / `clearOriginal` round-trip; L1 `ProfilePhotoStore` no-op when input already ≤ 2048 (preserves original resolution). Estimate: ~20 min spec + ~30 min impl + ~10 min verify ≈ 60 min total.
- **CL-143 (US-44 amendment / T-746, 2026-06-03)** — **Stale-`photoFilename` self-heal: validate the in-flight cropped + original filenames against on-disk existence at ``ProfileEditView`` mount, nil-ing any reference whose file is missing — so the AC-44.8 conditional picker entry (`inFlightPhotoFilename != nil` → action sheet) routes correctly even when the Keychain row carries a stale reference (file deleted out-of-band).** Closes Linear DUT-40. Bounded entirely to `Packages/DODFeatureProfile/Sources/DODFeatureProfile/{ProfilePhotoStore,ProfileEditView}.swift` (2 source edits) + the matching test extensions. **The bug shape (Spencer, 2026-06-03 via Linear DUT-40).** AC-44.8's picker-entry gate in `ProfileEditView+Photo.swift` `handleProfilePictureRowTap()` reads `if inFlightPhotoFilename != nil { showPhotoActionDialog = true } else { isPickerPresented = true }` — logically correct but doesn't account for `inFlightPhotoFilename` carrying a STALE reference to a deleted file. When the simulator's Keychain persists across `simctl uninstall`/install but the Documents directory is wiped (which it is — Keychain and Documents have different persistence semantics across simulator reinstall), every dev rebuild after setting a profile photo creates this exact mismatch: the Keychain row still has `photoFilename = "profile-photo-<UUID>.jpg"`, but the file at that path no longer exists in Documents. ``ProfilePhotoView`` gracefully falls back to ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` on the missing file (per CL-137 (renderer fallback)), so visually the user sees the placeholder — but the conditional treats the stale string as "has photo" and surfaces the action sheet (Replace Photo / Edit Photo / Remove Photo) when the only meaningful action is to PICK a new photo. The user-facing complaint: "On the profile screen, when I tap my placeholder profile picture to set my photo, it pulls up a context menu instead of going directly to the photos picker like it should." Investigation confirmed the AC-44.8 conditional gate is correct as authored; the stale-reference scenario is what surfaces the misbehavior. **The simulator Keychain-vs-Documents persistence asymmetry (the root mechanism).** iOS simulator Keychain is rooted in `~/Library/Developer/CoreSimulator/Devices/<DeviceID>/data/Library/Keychains/` which persists across `xcrun simctl uninstall`/`install` cycles — only `simctl erase` wipes it. Documents directory lives inside the app sandbox at `Containers/Data/Application/<AppID>/Documents/` and IS wiped on every uninstall/install (the app sandbox UUID changes on reinstall, the old one is garbage-collected). So any developer flow that (1) installs the app, (2) signs in / sets a profile photo (Keychain + Documents both populated), (3) `simctl uninstall` (Documents wiped, Keychain preserved), (4) reinstalls (fresh sandbox, fresh Documents but stale Keychain) → DUT-40 reproduces. **Why DUT-40 is also a theoretical production edge case (not just dev hygiene).** iOS itself can wipe Documents under three rare conditions: (a) the user uses Files.app to manually delete the app's Documents directory contents (Files.app surfaces user-content folders for apps that opt-in to `LSSupportsOpeningDocumentsInPlace`/`UIFileSharingEnabled` — the recipe app doesn't today, but a future task might); (b) iOS storage pressure triggers Documents eviction for apps marked with no `isExcludedFromBackup = true` flag (the photo JPG is intentionally NOT marked excluded — it's user content that should iCloud-back-up per CL-137); (c) a future CloudKit / cross-device sync feature partially restores a profile (Keychain row arrives via iCloud Keychain sync before the photo asset finishes downloading, leaving a stale-reference window). The dev-rebuild reproducer is the common case; the production edge cases ARE possible. **The fix shape (the self-heal: validate at view-mount, nil if missing).** Add an `exists(filename:)` (and `existsOriginal(filename:)`) sync-check method on the `ProfilePhotoStoring` protocol that returns whether a file with the given filename exists in the cropped (or original) storage directory — without loading the bytes (file-system existence check only, cheap). Then in ``ProfileEditView``, after the existing `.onAppear` seeding block (lines 224-235) that populates `inFlightPhotoFilename = existingProfile?.photoFilename` and `inFlightPhotoOriginalFilename = existingProfile?.photoOriginalFilename`, add a `.task { ... }` modifier that awaits the photo-store existence check on both filenames and nils any whose file is missing. **The conditional `handleProfilePictureRowTap()` is NOT changed** — once the stale references are nilled at mount, `inFlightPhotoFilename == nil` is the correct gate by construction; the action sheet only fires when a real photo exists. **The Save flow is NOT changed either** — `handleSave()` still persists `inFlightPhotoFilename` (now correctly nil for stale states), so the next save persists `photoFilename = nil` to the Keychain row, clearing the stale string. The mismatch self-heals on next save (and stays clear thereafter; the user is never asked to "fix" anything they didn't know was broken). **Picker-entry semantics post-fix.** Cold-start with stale reference → mount → `.task` checks → file missing → `inFlightPhotoFilename` becomes nil → row tap routes to picker (correct AC-44.8 path). Cold-start with valid reference → mount → `.task` checks → file present → `inFlightPhotoFilename` stays populated → row tap routes to action sheet (correct AC-44.8 path). New photo just set in this session (post-crop, pre-Save) → mount happens BEFORE the post-crop assignment in the parent's state graph (the `.task` only fires once per mount per AC-44.16 + SwiftUI's lifecycle contract), so the post-crop assignment isn't clobbered by a stale check. **The `.task` modifier choice (vs. `.onAppear`).** `.onAppear` is sync; the protocol's existing methods are actor-isolated and require `await`. `.task` is async-capable and fires on view appearance (and is cancelled on disappearance — appropriate for an idempotent existence check). The cleanest split: keep `.onAppear` for the existing sync state seeding (displayName / email / initial snapshots), add a `.task` for the async photo-validation step. The `.task` runs AFTER the `.onAppear` seeding so the just-seeded values get validated (SwiftUI sequences `.onAppear` first, then `.task` — per the View lifecycle contract). **Alternative considered: sync `FileManager.fileExists(atPath:)` directly in `.onAppear` by exposing `documentsDirectory: URL` from the photo-store.** Rejected — would require either exposing the URL from the actor (breaking the actor's encapsulation boundary, since `documentsDirectory` is private state) OR duplicating the path-construction logic in the view layer (couples view to store internals). The `async exists(filename:)` method is the cleanest extension — preserves actor encapsulation, mirrors the existing protocol's async-method shape (`save` / `load` / `clear` are all already async), and the `.task` modifier is the SwiftUI-idiomatic way to host an async side effect at view-mount. **Implementation detail — `exists(filename:)` is sync at the FileManager layer, async only because the actor-isolated method declaration requires it.** The body is a single `fileManager.fileExists(atPath: url.path)` call — same cost as the existing `load(filename:)`'s pre-flight `fileExists` check, just without the subsequent `Data(contentsOf:)` read. Avoids loading the bytes when all the caller needs is the existence check. **`InMemoryProfilePhotoStore.exists(filename:)`** mirrors the production check via the existing `stored[filename] != nil` dictionary lookup; same surface shape. **Edge cases the fix handles correctly.** (i) `inFlightPhotoFilename = nil` from start (new profile creation) → `.task` no-ops on both filenames → row taps to picker (correct). (ii) `inFlightPhotoFilename` set, file exists → `.task` no-ops → row taps to action sheet (correct). (iii) `inFlightPhotoFilename` set, file missing (stale) → `.task` nils it → row taps to picker (the fix). (iv) `inFlightPhotoOriginalFilename` set, original file missing but cropped exists → `.task` nils only the original → row taps to action sheet (Edit Photo still falls back to cropped per CL-142's `loadPhotoForEditing` legacy path). (v) Both files missing → `.task` nils both → row taps to picker (correct — fully clean state). **What this does NOT change.** The AC-44.8 conditional `inFlightPhotoFilename != nil` in `handleProfilePictureRowTap()` (preserved verbatim — becomes correct by construction once stale references are cleared). The `handleSave()` body / write-then-clear-old pattern (unchanged — the now-nilled in-flight filenames flow into the saved profile without special-casing). The ``ProfilePhotoStore.save / load / clear`` methods (unchanged — `exists` is a new sibling, not a replacement). The `ProfilePhotoView` rendering / fallback behavior (unchanged — it still gracefully degrades on missing file at the avatar render). The Sign Out / Delete Profile flows (unchanged — they call `store.clear()` which calls `clear(filename:)` + `clearOriginal(filename:)` regardless of whether the files exist; `clear` itself is already idempotent on missing files per CL-137). The Phase c gate-CTA sheet path (unchanged). The action-sheet copy + buttons (unchanged when triggered). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Add the file-exists check at the `handleProfilePictureRowTap` call site (per-tap), gating the action sheet on real file presence.** Rejected — every tap pays the file-system check tax, and the bug is the stale string in the `@State`, not the tap routing per se. Healing the `@State` once at view-mount is cleaner than gating every consumer of the `@State`. Also: the `inFlightPhotoFilename` is consumed by other surfaces (the avatar's `previewProfile` computed property, the footer text gate, the dirty-state `isDirty` check), so heal-once-at-mount fixes all consumers; gate-at-tap only fixes the action sheet trigger. **(b) Mark the Documents-directory photo file with `URLResourceKey.isExcludedFromBackup = true` so iCloud-restore never partially syncs the row.** Rejected — would break the explicit CL-137 decision that the photo IS user content that should iCloud-back-up. Excluding from backup means a user who restores from iCloud loses their profile photo across device migrations, which is a worse user-facing posture than the rare partial-sync edge case the fix accommodates. **(c) Store the photo's cryptographic hash alongside the filename in the Keychain row, validate hash on load.** Rejected — orders of magnitude more work for no clearer signal. The bug is "filename references a file that doesn't exist", not "filename references a file whose contents drifted"; the existence check is sufficient. Hash validation would also require re-reading the file's bytes on every load, defeating the cropped-derivative-pre-rendered optimization. **(d) Move the existence check inside `ProfilePhotoStore.load(filename:)` so the load returns nil for missing files (which it already does), and trust the avatar's nil-fallback path.** Rejected — `load` already returns nil for missing files, and the avatar does gracefully render the placeholder, but the `inFlightPhotoFilename != nil` conditional in the tap handler doesn't go through `load`; it reads the raw string. The conditional is the misfire surface; healing the string at mount is what fixes the conditional. **(e) Add a migration step on profile-store init that proactively scans every Keychain row for stale photo references and nils them.** Rejected — over-engineered for v1 (single-profile-per-device, no batched migration needed) and brittle (any future profile-store rev would have to extend the migration). The view-mount self-heal is sufficient for every code path that consumes the in-flight filenames, and the next save persists the cleared state to Keychain — so the heal is permanent across sessions even without an explicit migration. **(f) Show a one-time toast / banner "Your profile photo was reset because the file was missing" so the user knows the inconsistency was healed.** Rejected — would scare the user about a state they were never aware was broken. The avatar already gracefully renders the placeholder; healing silently is the better posture. **(g) Front-load the existence check before the seeding so the Keychain row's stale value never makes it into `inFlightPhotoFilename` in the first place.** Rejected — the seeding has to happen BEFORE the existence check to give the check something to validate. The order is: seed (set the in-flight from existingProfile) → validate (nil if missing). Reversing the order would require knowing what to validate against, which IS the in-flight value. **The implementation order.** Commit 1: spec amendments (this file CL-143 + spec.md US-44 amendment to AC-44.8 noting the file-existence validation + tasks.md T-746 entry). Commit 2: `ProfilePhotoStoring.exists(filename:)` + `existsOriginal(filename:)` protocol additions + the production `ProfilePhotoStore` + `InMemoryProfilePhotoStore` impls + L1 tests for both. Commit 3: `ProfileEditView` `.task` modifier that validates `inFlightPhotoFilename` + `inFlightPhotoOriginalFilename` against the store's existence check and nils stale references + integration test that drives a stale filename through view-mount and asserts the nil-out. **Numbering note.** CL-143 / T-746 is the next free CL slot above CL-142 (T-745, DUT-39). The `e2e` label IS applied — the picker-entry routing was visibly wrong (action sheet on placeholder) and is now correct (picker direct from placeholder), and the dev-rebuild reproducer is end-to-end user-observable. **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendment — AC-44.8 amended (conditional picker entry is file-existence-validated at view mount; stale references nil out before the conditional is evaluated). Implementing PR: T-746 (`fix/T-746-stale-photofilename`). Tests: L1 `ProfilePhotoStore.exists` (true for a real file, false for a missing filename, false for empty string); L1 `ProfilePhotoStore.existsOriginal` (mirror); L1 `InMemoryProfilePhotoStore.exists` / `existsOriginal` (parallel via the in-memory dictionary). L1 `ProfileEditView` integration: stale filename + missing file → view-mount task nils the in-flight (assert via injected mock store + manual mount); real filename + extant file → view-mount task leaves the in-flight populated. Estimate: ~10 min spec + ~15 min impl + ~5 min verify ≈ 30 min total.
- **CL-176 (US-12 amendment / T-779, 2026-06-15)** — **Record a recent search only on commit (Return or keyboard dismissal), not on every debounced search.** Closes Linear DUT-85. **Ask (Spencer).** A "Recent" chip should appear only when the user *commits* a search — hitting Return/Enter or dismissing the keyboard in any way — not on every mid-typing pause. **Current behavior (the bug).** Recents were recorded inside the search-completion path (`SearchViewModel.recordRecentAndTelemetry`, called from the `+T643` finalize hop), which runs on every 150 ms debounce — so any pause while typing (≥ 2 chars) dropped a partial query into Recent (typing "chicken" could leave "ch", "chic", "chicken" all under Recent). **The change. (1) Decouple recording from the live search.** Split `recordRecentAndTelemetry` → `sendSearchTelemetry` (telemetry only — the AC-3.6 SHA-256 hash still fires on each completed search, unchanged) and add `SearchViewModel.commitRecentSearch()` (in a new `+Recents` extension so the main file stays ≤ 400 lines): it records the current trimmed query into the `RecentSearches` store, skipping curated-"Try" taps (REG-19 / CL-66) and queries under 2 chars. **(2) Wire the two commit moments.** `DODSearchField` (the shared Search/Categories field) gains an optional `onFocusChange: ((Bool) -> Void)?` callback backed by an internal `@FocusState`; `SearchView` passes `onFocusChange: { if !$0 { viewModel.commitRecentSearch() } }` (keyboard dismissal = focus loss) and applies `.onSubmit { viewModel.commitRecentSearch() }` (Return). `nil` (Categories) is a no-op, so that surface is unaffected. **What this does NOT change.** The live as-you-type debounced search + results, the search telemetry cadence, the curated "Try" pills, the recents store + its persistence/dedup/cap, Clear All, per-term remove. **Considered + rejected. (a) Keep recording in the search path but debounce harder** — wouldn't fix it; any pause is still a "search." **(b) Record on `.onSubmit` only** — misses the "dismissed the keyboard without hitting Return" path the user explicitly called out. **Verification.** Package build + tests green; lint/format clean; all files ≤ 400 lines (relocated `SearchView.layoutToggleToolbarButton` to a new `SearchView+Toolbar.swift` to make room for the focus wiring). Updated 3 `SearchViewModelTests` recents assertions to call `commitRecentSearch()` after the search; new `SearchViewModelRecentsTests` (live search records nothing until commit; commit skips curated + short queries). **Numbering note.** CL-176 / T-779 / DUT-85 are the next free slots above CL-175 / T-778 / DUT-84. No `e2e` label — local state + a focus-event wiring; L1-pinned. **Spec/trace:** US-12 AC-12.4 amended (recents recorded on commit, not the live search). Implementing PR: T-779 (`feat/T-779-search-recents-submit-dismiss`).
- **CL-175 (US-35 amendment / T-778, 2026-06-14)** — **Warn before removing a download while offline: a confirmation dialog ("Keep Download" / "Remove Download") shown only when there's no network; online removal stays immediate.** Closes Linear DUT-84. **Ask (Spencer).** When a downloaded recipe is un-downloaded while the device has no cell/Wi-Fi coverage, first warn that the recipe won't be openable until service is restored; offer "Keep Download" and "Remove Download"; when online, no dialog — remove immediately. **Why.** Removing a download clears the offline-pinned copy (CL-172 / T-775); offline, the text + hero image can't be re-fetched until service returns, so an un-tap would silently strand a recipe the user may be relying on (mid-cook, no signal). Online, removal is harmless (re-downloadable in a tap), so no prompt. **The change. (1) Gate both remove surfaces on connectivity.** The recipe-detail toggle (`RecipeDetailViewModel.toggleDownload()`) and the Saved-tab "Remove Download" menu (`SavedViewModel`) check `dependencies.isOnline()` before removing: online → remove immediately (unchanged); offline → raise a confirmation instead (`showOfflineRemoveDownloadWarning` on the detail VM; `pendingOfflineRemoveDownloadID` on the Saved VM). Confirm → the existing `removeDownload(...)` worker runs; "Keep Download" → dismiss, download intact. **(2) Shared alert.** A new `View.offlineRemoveDownloadAlert(isPresented:onRemove:)` (DODDesignSystem) renders a native alert — title "You're offline", body "If you remove this download, you won't be able to open this recipe until you're back online. You can always download it again once you're connected.", **Remove Download** (destructive) + **Keep Download** (`.cancel`, the bold default) — so both surfaces share identical copy + roles. **(3) Connectivity seam.** `SavedDependencies` gains `isOnline()` (default `true` so fakes read "online"; `LiveSavedDependencies` routes to the same `NetworkMonitor.shared` the detail fetch path already uses, injected from `AppDependencies`). The detail VM already exposed `isOnline()`. **What this does NOT change.** The remove logic itself (optimistic badge clear + store write), the online flow (still one-tap), the download flow, the Saved header, the "Downloaded" badge. The detail VM's `isOffline` computed moved to the `+Download` extension (connectivity-grouped) to keep `RecipeDetailViewModel.swift` ≤ 400 lines. **Unsave is intentionally NOT gated** (DUT-84 decision, recorded on the Linear issue): Save is a lightweight bookmark, not an offline pin (AC-35.7), and a download survives an unsave (AC-35.5), so unsaving never strands a guaranteed-offline copy — the guard is scoped to Remove Download. **Considered + rejected. (a) Branded custom dialog** (per the iCloud Sync confirm, T-757 / DUT-63) — rejected: a native alert is the right idiom for a destructive confirmation and is accessible by default; the branded dialog suits non-destructive toggles. **(b) Warn on every removal (online too)** — rejected: online removal is reversible in a tap, so a prompt would be pure friction; the warning earns its interruption only when removal is actually lossy (offline). **(c) Also confirm unsave of a downloaded/offline recipe** — rejected per the decoupling above. **Verification.** Build green (sim); lint/format clean; all files ≤ 400 lines. New L1 tests: 3 on `RecipeDetailViewModel` (offline → warn, no removal; online → remove, no warn; confirm → remove + clear) + 4 on `SavedViewModel` (offline → stash pending, no removal; online → remove now; confirm → remove + clear; cancel → keep), the latter in a split `SavedViewModelDownloadWarningTests` suite so neither file trips the `type_body_length` cap. The offline path is unit-tested rather than sim-verified (forcing the simulator's `NWPathMonitor` offline isn't practical without disconnecting the host); the native alert's appearance is standard iOS. **Numbering note.** CL-175 / T-778 / DUT-84 are the next free slots above CL-174 / T-777 / DUT-83. No `e2e` label — local state + a system alert; L1-pinned. **Spec/trace:** US-35 AC-35.8 added (offline remove-download confirmation); AC-35.1 (detail toggle) + AC-34.6 (Saved menu) amended with pointers. Implementing PR: T-778 (`feat/T-778-offline-remove-download-warning`).
- **CL-174 (DUT-48 polish / T-777, 2026-06-14)** — **Polish the Dutch Oven Heat Coach (DUT-48): plain-language copy, no em dashes, accent-tinted segmented selectors, verified in light + dark.** Closes Linear DUT-83. **Asks (Spencer).** (1) Read clearly + warmly for any skill level; (2) remove the em dashes throughout the interface; (3) every UI element on the app's style/color in both modes. **The change. (1) Copy.** Reworded the UI strings + the reference content (`DutchOvenHeatCoach+Reference` feel cues / coal-management habits / wind guidance; `HeatCoachModel` style + adjustment notes; `HeatCoachView` / `+Sections` intros + the result eyebrow) into plain, warm language: expanded the terse `=` shorthand in the feel cues to full sentences ("Bright orange glow = running hot" → "Glowing bright orange means it's running hot, so pull 1-2 coals off the bottom"), defined jargon inline (charcoal chimney, the 3:1 lid ratio, back-to-wind), and removed every em dash from user-facing strings. The pinned test substrings — cue titles; keywords chimney / quarter-turn / back-to-wind / windbreak / the hand-test temp bands; `coalHeadline`; "Even heat" / "3:1" / "pull/add 2-3 coals" / "fix the environment first" — are preserved, so `DutchOvenHeatCoachTests` (30) + `HeatCoachModelTests` (12) pass UNCHANGED; they now verify the substance survived the reword. **(2) Accent selectors.** Replaced the two `.pickerStyle(.segmented)` controls (Even/Baking, Hot/Mild/Cold) with a scoped `accentSelector` helper: the selected segment fills `DODColor.accent` with `labelOnAccent` text (vs. iOS's neutral grey), over a `surface` track with a `surfaceDivider` hairline so the control reads as a group in BOTH modes (in light, `surface` matches the card and a fill alone is invisible, so the hairline defines it). No global `UISegmentedControl.appearance()` hack; the `heat-coach-style` / `heat-coach-ambient` accessibility identifiers are preserved. **What this does NOT change.** The calculator (`startingCoals`, `ambientCoalDelta`, `cookTimeExtraMinutes`, `replenishMinutes`, `handTest`) + every number; the screen structure (inputs → result → adjustments → cook-by-feel → coal management); the result card's accent treatment; the Settings "Tools" entry. **Considered + rejected. (a) Tint the native segmented control via `UISegmentedControl.appearance()`** — rejected: a global side effect on every segmented control in the app + fragile to scope; the custom selector is local + fully brand-controlled. **(b) Strip em dashes from the doc comments too** — out of scope: "interface" means the rendered UI (now em-dash-free); developer-facing doc-comment prose left as-is. **Verification (iPhone-17 sim, light + dark).** The accent selectors, reworded copy, and result card all render correctly in both appearances (screenshots saved). Lint/format clean; the 4 changed files stay ≤ 400 lines; `DODSupport` + `DODFeatureFeed` Heat Coach tests green; the full app builds. **Numbering note.** CL-174 / T-777 / DUT-83 are the next free slots above CL-173 / T-776 / DUT-82. No `e2e` label — copy + presentation; on-device verified in both modes. **Spec/trace:** DUT-48 (Heat Coach) refinement; no AC text changes (DUT-48 ships as a DUT-tracked tool without a formal `spec.md` AC). Implementing PR: T-777 (`feat/T-777-heat-coach-polish`).
- **CL-173 (US-5 amendment / T-776, 2026-06-14)** — **Work around an iOS 26 NavigationStack bug where the Saved tab's large "Saved" title vanishes after a card context-menu action.** Closes Linear DUT-82. **The bug (confirmed iOS 26 Apple regression).** `.navigationTitle` + `.toolbar` + a List/grid inside a NavigationStack: the large title renders on first appear but DISAPPEARS after a state transition that re-renders the view — e.g. a long-press context-menu action (Unsave per CL-103, or Remove Download per CL-172) — and only returns on relaunch. Documented at [developer.apple.com/forums/thread/789557](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/789557) + DevelopersIO's iOS-26 NavigationStack large-title writeup. **PRE-EXISTING:** the Unsave menu (CL-103, long before CL-172) triggers it identically on `main`, so CL-172's Remove Download merely added a second action that hits the same OS bug. Two in-app fixes were tried + FAILED (synchronous optimistic mutation; stable `ToolbarItem` + pinned `.navigationBarTitleDisplayMode(.large)`) — the bug is below app code. **The fix.** Render "Saved" as a real large header inside the view — a `Text("Saved").font(.largeTitle).fontWeight(.bold)` at the top of a `VStack` wrapping `content` — and DROP `.navigationTitle("Saved")` on the Saved tab. A header that's ordinary content can't be dropped by the OS large-title machinery. The `.toolbar` "Make Shopping List" cart button stays in the nav bar; the header carries `.accessibilityAddTraits(.isHeader)` so VoiceOver still reads it as a heading. **Trade-offs.** (1) The header scrolls with the content instead of collapsing into the bar on scroll — negligible for the Saved tab's short list. (2) A pushed shopping list shows a "Back" button instead of "Saved" (no nav title to inherit). Both acceptable. **What this does NOT change.** The saved-list source/order, the cards, the context menus (Unsave + Remove Download still work — and the header now survives them), the cart action, the empty/error/loaded states, the view-model logic. **Scope.** Fixes the Saved tab (where reported + verified). The same OS bug can affect any large-title + `.toolbar` + list screen (Feed/Categories/Search); a sweep is deferred to a follow-up if they exhibit it. **Considered + rejected. (a) Remove the `.toolbar` (relocate the cart into content) keeping the OS large title** — viable, but it relocates a well-placed button; the manual header keeps the cart in its nav-bar home. **(b) Wait for Apple's fix** — not actionable; the bug ships in iOS 26.0. **Verification (iPhone-17 sim).** The "Saved" header now survives Remove Download (badge clears, header stays) — and by construction Unsave (the header is action-agnostic content, not the OS title). UI tests find the Saved tab via the tab-bar button (`tabBars.buttons["Saved"]`), not the nav title, so dropping `.navigationTitle` doesn't break them. Lint/format clean; the 31 DODFeatureSaved tests pass (view-model logic unchanged). **Numbering note.** CL-173 / T-776 / DUT-82 are the next free slots above CL-172 / T-775 / DUT-81. No `e2e` label — a presentation workaround; on-device verified. **Spec/trace:** US-5 AC-5.3 amended (the Saved-tab title renders as an in-content header). Implementing PR: T-776 (`fix/T-776-saved-header-ios26`).
- **CL-172 (US-35 / US-34 / US-5 amendment / T-775, 2026-06-14)** — **Make explicit downloads removable + visually stateful: the recipe-detail download button becomes a toggle (orange filled glyph when downloaded; tap removes), and the Saved-tab long-press menu gains a "Remove Download" action that clears the T-774 badge.** Closes Linear DUT-81. **Context.** AC-35.5 listed "explicitly removing the download" as out of scope for v1 (a deferred affordance noted in CL-61's deferred-work section); this CL graduates it.
