Since CL-158 (T-761) decoupled Save from Download, removing a download reverts a recipe to saved-only (`downloadedAt → nil`) — it stays saved + in the Saved tab, just without the "Downloaded" badge (CL-171). **The change.** (1) New `RecipeStore.removeDownload(id:) -> Bool` (in a new `RecipeStore+Download.swift` extension, self-contained via `modelContext` since `RecipeStore.swift` is at the 400-line cap) — clears `CachedRecipe.downloadedAt`; idempotent (returns `false` if not downloaded). The hero image stays **pinned**: the AC-4.9 / AC-5.2 offline guarantee is keyed to the *saved* state, not the download flag, so un-downloading must not drop the offline image (that is unsave's concern). (2) Recipe-detail toolbar button (AC-35.1) is now a **toggle**: downloaded → `square.and.arrow.down.fill` in `DODColor.burntOrange`, tap → `RecipeDetailViewModel.removeDownload()` (clears the pin via the dependency, flips `isDownloaded`, "Download removed" snackbar, a11y "Remove download"); not downloaded → outline `square.and.arrow.down`, tap → existing `downloadForOffline()`. Supersedes CL-61's always-outline glyph + the "Already downloaded" re-tap snackbar (AC-35.3 / AC-35.4) — the re-tap now removes. (3) `recipeCardContextMenu` gains optional `isDownloaded: Bool = false` + `onRemoveDownload: (() -> Void)? = nil`; when both are set, a second item — "Remove Download" + `square.and.arrow.down.badge.xmark` via `.tint(DODColor.burntOrange)` — renders beneath "Unsave". `SavedView` passes `isDownloaded: downloadedIDs.contains(recipe.id)` + an `onRemoveDownload` → `SavedViewModel.removeDownload(id:)`, which clears `downloadedIDs` **optimistically** (badge vanishes instantly, mirroring CL-104's optimistic unsave) then routes the store write through `SavedDependencies.removeDownload(id:)` (default no-op; live → store). **Scope: the Saved tab only.** Per CL-162 / AC-34.6, Feed/Categories/Search don't hydrate per-row download state — so the menu's download branch is wired only where `downloadedIDs` exists (the Saved tab); extending it to the other surfaces is deferred with the same per-row-state groundwork the save branch's CL-60 path-(c) covered. **What this does NOT change.** Save/unsave (CL-104 / CL-162); the download mechanics (`markDownloaded` + the image pin); the saved-list source/order; the widget surfaces; the Feed/Categories/Search menus. **Considered and rejected. (a) Unpin/evict the hero image on un-download.** Rejected — the pin (`pinnedToSavedRecipeID`) is the *saved*-offline contract (AC-4.9); the recipe stays saved, so dropping the image would regress "fully usable offline if saved". **(b) Wire "Remove Download" on all card surfaces.** Deferred — needs the CL-60 path-(c) per-row state on Feed/Categories/Search; out of scope here. **(c) Use the `.destructive` role for the menu item.** Rejected — destructive renders red, the ask is orange, and un-download isn't destructive (the recipe stays saved + re-downloadable). **Tests.** L1 `RecipeStoreSavedWidgetTests.removeDownloadClearsThePinAndIsIdempotent` (download → remove clears the flag, second remove no-op, re-download works). L1 `RecipeDetailDownloadTests.removeDownloadClearsStateButKeepsSaved` (toggle flips `isDownloaded`, "Download removed" snackbar, recipe stays saved — un-download ≠ unsave). L1 `SavedViewModelTests.removeDownloadClearsBadgeOptimisticallyAndRoutesStoreWrite` (optimistic `downloadedIDs` clear + dependency routing + card stays in the grid). No L4 — the toggle glyph + the context-menu item aren't isolated snapshot components; verified on the iPhone-17 sim. **Numbering note.** CL-172 / T-775 / DUT-81 are the next free slots above CL-171 / T-774 / DUT-80. No `e2e` label — local state + presentation; L1-pinned + on-device verification. **Spec/trace:** US-35 AC-35.1 (toggle) + AC-35.3 / AC-35.4 (re-tap supersession) + AC-35.5 (un-download graduated) amended; US-34 AC-34.6 (menu download branch) amended; US-5 AC-5.3 (badge clears) amended. Implementing PR: T-775 (`feat/T-775-download-toggle-remove`).
- **CL-171 (US-5 amendment / T-774, 2026-06-14)** — **Add a "Downloaded" badge to the Saved tab's recipe cards so the user can tell saved-only from saved + downloaded at a glance.** Closes Linear DUT-80. **Context.** Since CL-158 (T-761) decoupled Save from Download, a saved recipe is either saved-only (`CachedRecipe.downloadedAt == nil`) or saved + explicitly downloaded for offline use (`downloadedAt != nil`, set by `markDownloaded(id:)`). Every card in the Saved tab is saved by definition, so only the downloaded ones need a marker. **The change.** (1) New `RecipeStore.downloadedRecipeIDs() -> Set<Int>` (in `RecipeStore+SavedWidget.swift`, alongside `savedRecipeIDs()` since `RecipeStore.swift` is at the 400-line cap) — fetches `CachedRecipe` rows where `downloadedAt != nil` and returns their ids. Download state is local-only (NOT CloudKit-synced, per DUT-35), so this reads the cache table, not `SyncedSavedRecipe`. (2) `SavedDependencies.downloadedRecipeIDs()` (+ default `[]` so fake conformers keep compiling; `LiveSavedDependencies` routes to the store) — mirrors the `savedRecipeIDs()` seam pattern (CL-162). (3) `SavedViewModel` gains `downloadedIDs: Set<Int>`, hydrated via `try?` alongside `recipes` in `refresh()` (a download-state read failure just means no badges, never a failed Saved-tab load). (4) `RecipeCard` gains a defaulted `isDownloaded: Bool = false` param + a `downloadedBadge` (a burnt-orange capsule with an `arrow.down.circle.fill` glyph + "Downloaded", mirroring the `timeChip` capsule) overlaid **bottom-leading** on the hero — the time chip stays top-trailing, and at the Saved tab's ~180pt half-width the two won't both fit across the top row, so the badge drops to the opposite corner (a clean diagonal, no collision). Default `false` so Feed / Categories / Search cards render byte-identically. (5) `SavedView` passes `isDownloaded: viewModel.downloadedIDs.contains(recipe.id)`; the card's accessibility label appends "Downloaded." when set. **What this does NOT change.** The saved-list source/order (still `savedRecipes()`, newest-first); the optimistic-unsave path (CL-104); the download mechanics (`markDownloaded`); the Feed/Categories/Search cards (no `isDownloaded` passed → no badge); the widget surfaces. Saved-only recipes show no badge. **Considered and rejected. (a) A "Saved" badge on every card alongside the "Downloaded" one.** Rejected — every Saved-tab card is saved by definition, so a "Saved" badge is noise; the single "Downloaded" badge is the meaningful distinction the user asked for. **(b) Put the badge top-leading.** Rejected — it collides with the top-trailing time chip at the narrow half-width grid (caught by the L4 snapshot during development); bottom-leading clears it at any card width. **(c) Add download state to the `Recipe` domain type.** Rejected — `Recipe` is the core domain model; per-state flags belong in a lightweight id-set projection (the `savedRecipeIDs()` precedent), refreshed on appear, not threaded through every fetch. **Tests.** L1 `RecipeStoreTests.downloadedRecipeIDsReturnsOnlyDownloadedRows` (cache 3, download 2, assert the set excludes the un-downloaded one). L1 `SavedViewModelTests.refreshHydratesDownloadedIDsForBadging` (the fake returns a download set; assert the view model hydrates it). L4 `RecipeCardDownloadedBadgeSnapshotTests` (new file, half-width card with `isDownloaded: true` — pins the bottom-leading badge + non-collision with the time chip; 1 baseline recorded on the iPhone-17 sim, re-run green). The existing RecipeCard snapshots are byte-stable (default `false` → identical view tree). DODFeatureSaved (30 tests) + the new DODPersistence / DODDesignSystem tests pass; strict lint clean (RecipeCard.swift trimmed to stay ≤ 400 lines). **Numbering note.** CL-171 / T-774 / DUT-80 are the next free slots. No `e2e` label — presentation; L1 + L4 pinned + on-device verification (download a saved recipe, confirm its Saved-tab card gets the badge). **Spec/trace:** US-5 AC-5.3 amended (Saved-list cards show a Downloaded badge for downloaded recipes). Implementing PR: T-774 (`feat/T-774-saved-downloaded-badge`).
- **CL-170 (CI / test-infra / T-773, 2026-06-14)** — **Harden the CI iOS-Simulator destination so jobs stop failing intermittently with "Unable to find a device matching ... name:iPhone 17" when the GitHub macos-15 runner image hasn't pre-created that device.** Closes Linear DUT-80. **The flake.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` pinned `-destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17'` in five jobs (Build app, L4 Snapshots · DODDesignSystem, L1 · DODApp, L3 UI Smoke, L5 E2E). The macos-15 image intermittently ships without an iPhone 17 *device instance* pre-created — the iPhone 17 *device type* + iOS 26 runtime are present (via the pinned Xcode 26), but the named device isn't — so `xcodebuild` exits with "Unable to find a device matching the provided destination specifier { platform:iOS Simulator, OS:latest, name:iPhone 17 }". The same source builds/tests fine locally and on rerun: a pure runner-image race. Hit twice on 2026-06-13 (Build app on #190, L4 Snapshots on #191), each needing a manual `gh run rerun --failed`. **The fix (two shapes).** **(1) Build-only job (Build app):** switched to the device-agnostic `-destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator'` — a build needs no booted device, so there is no device name to match and the flake is impossible by construction (mirrors `release.yml`'s `generic/platform=iOS` device build). **(2) Test jobs (L4 / L1·DODApp / L3 / L5):** a new composite action `.github/actions/ensure-ios-simulator` resolves the iPhone 17 UDID and CREATES the device on the newest installed iOS runtime if the image lacks it (`xcrun simctl create "iPhone 17" <devicetype-id> <runtime-id>`), boots it, and emits `-destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,id=<UDID>'`; pinning by UDID also sidesteps name-match races. L3's prior inline "prefer iPhone 17, else any iPhone, else bare name" resolver is replaced by the action so there is one source of truth. **Why create iPhone 17 specifically (not fall back to any iPhone).** The L4 DesignSystem snapshot baselines are geometry-locked to iPhone 17 (logical 402×874pt; recorded on Xcode 26.5 / iOS 26.5 / iPhone 17), so substituting a different device would break every snapshot — the action guarantees that exact device. `.github/actions/**` was added to both `changes` path filters so a future edit to the action re-runs the iOS-Sim jobs that depend on it. **What this does NOT change.** Any product/app code (CI config only); the Xcode-26 selection; `OS=latest` flexibility; the snapshot baselines; L3's retry loop / L5's label-gating; the `ci-required` aggregator. **Verified on a real macos-15 runner.** Build app (generic) + L4 Snapshots + L3 UI Smoke + L1·DODApp all PASSED on run 27481706831; `CI required` passed; 0 bare `name=iPhone 17` destinations remain in `ci.yml`. The three resolver jq filters were also validated locally against Xcode 26.5 (existing iPhone 17 → `AE5DFF24…`; newest runtime → `…SimRuntime.iOS-26-5`; device type → `…SimDeviceType.iPhone-17`). **PROCESS NOTE — why this is a back-fill, not a fresh code PR.** The workflow + action code did NOT land via its own PR. While it was being written in a shared working tree, a CONCURRENT session (running as the same `spencer0706` account, mid widget-batch) swept the uncommitted `.github/` changes into PR #192 (T-766 "Saved widget heroImageFilename"), which squash-merged to `main` at 2026-06-13T23:17Z. So the correct, CI-verified fix is already on `main` — but undocumented, inside an unrelated widget PR. This CL-170 / T-773 entry back-fills the traceability the spec-driven contract requires; dad (#192's author/reviewer) is flagged in this PR's body. **Considered and rejected. (a) Revert the CI change from main and re-land it cleanly.** Rejected — the code on main is correct + CI-verified green; reverting would transiently re-introduce the flake and risk conflicts with the active concurrent widget / CI-hardening branches built on top of it. Back-filling the docs is lower-risk and is the user's chosen path. **(b) `simctl create` a uniquely-named device (e.g. "ci-iphone").** Rejected — naming the created device "iPhone 17" keeps it consistent with the device the snapshot baselines + the rest of the repo already reference. **(c) Trace via only a `ci:` commit + DUT (the DUT-29/32/33 precedent for CI changes).** Noted — historically CI-infra changes here carried only a DUT + `ci:` commit and no CL/T; this entry adds the CL/T records the task explicitly asked for. **Numbering note.** CL-170 / T-773 / DUT-80 is the next free slot above CL-169 / T-772 / DUT-79 (the concurrent widget batch). No `e2e` label — CI-infra only; verified by the green #192 run. **Spec/trace:** CI infrastructure for the test pyramid — AC-T1..AC-T5 are *enforced* by these runners; no AC text changes, because the fix hardens runner-device provisioning and does not alter any acceptance criterion. Implementing PR: T-773 (`ci/sim-destination-backfill`); the workflow/action code itself shipped (unintentionally) via #192.
- **CL-169 (US-17 / US-21 amendment / T-772, 2026-06-13)** — **Fix the Saved Recipes widget never rendering hero photos: its filename→URL resolver pointed at the wrong directory (the App Group container root instead of the `widget-images/` subdir).** Closes Linear DUT-79. **The bug.** `WidgetImageBridge.writeImage` writes bridged hero images to `<AppGroupContainer>/widget-images/<sha>.img` (the `imageSubdirectory`). The Featured widget reads them via `WidgetImageBridge.fileURL(forFilename:)`, which includes that subdir. But `SavedRecipesWidgetEntryView` had its OWN `heroImageURL(forFilename:)` that did `container.appendingPathComponent(filename)` — i.e. `<container>/<sha>.img`, one directory too high. So the Saved widget never found the file and fell back to `WidgetCard.Hero`'s gradient for every saved row — even though the snapshot carried the filename (CL-163) and the bytes were actively bridged (CL-167). **Why it stayed invisible.** The L4 snapshot tests pass a `file://` URL straight into `WidgetCard`, so they never exercised the entry view's path construction; the `WidgetImageBridge` round-trip tests only exercised the *correct* resolver. The Featured widget worked (it used the canonical resolver), so the failure read as "saved images just don't load." Confirmed on-device: the saved snapshot entry carried `heroImageFilename=4480f2e3….img`, that file existed at `…/widget-images/4480f2e3….img`, but the Saved widget resolved `…/4480f2e3….img` (missing the subdir). **The fix.** Delete the divergent resolver; `heroImageURL(forFilename:)` now delegates to `WidgetImageBridge.fileURL(forFilename:)` — the single canonical resolver both widgets share. One-line behavior change; the rest is a doc comment recording the trap. **What this does NOT change.** The bridge write path / subdir; the snapshot wire format; the Featured widget; the publisher prefetch (CL-167); the gradient fallback when a file genuinely is absent. **Tests.** No new unit test — the Widget extension target isn't unit-testable, and the fix routes the Saved widget through `WidgetImageBridge.fileURL`, whose `widget-images/` inclusion is already locked by `WidgetImageBridgeTests.fileURLReturnsContainerPlusSubdirectoryPlusFilename` (+ `imageDirectoryURLCreatesDirectoryIfMissing`, `imageSubdirectoryConstantIsStable`). The bug existed precisely because the Saved widget bypassed that tested resolver; the fix removes the bypass. App + widget extension build; strict lint clean; on-device confirmed (the saved snapshot's filename + the bridged file both present, only the resolution path was wrong). **Considered and rejected. (a) Add an L4/unit test for `SavedRecipesWidgetEntryView.heroImageURL`.** Rejected — the `DODAppWidget` extension target has no test bundle and isn't importable by the L1/L4 suites; the durable guard is removing the divergent resolver so there's nothing left to drift. **(b) Keep the custom resolver but append the subdir literally.** Rejected — hardcoding `"widget-images"` in a second place is exactly how the two paths drifted; delegating to the bridge keeps one source of truth. **Numbering note.** CL-169 / T-772 is the next free slot above CL-168. No `e2e` label — a one-line path fix; verified on-device + by the existing bridge tests. **Spec/trace:** US-17 AC-17.3 amended (the saved widget's filename→URL resolution now shares the canonical `WidgetImageBridge.fileURL`); US-21 AC-21.3 (resolver contract). Implementing PR: T-772 (`fix/T-772-saved-widget-image-path`). **This is the real completion of the DUT-76 "saved images" work — CL-163 set the filename and CL-167 bridged the bytes, but both were defeated by this resolver pointing at the wrong directory.**
- **CL-168 (US-22 amendment / T-771, 2026-06-13)** — **Add a circular Lock Screen "Saved" shortcut widget: a static `.accessoryCircular` `bookmark.fill` glyph that opens the Saved tab (`dod://saved`). PR3 of a 3-PR widget follow-up batch (completes it).** Closes Linear DUT-77. **The ask (Spencer, 2026-06-13).** "For the lock screen widgets, make a small circular one that has the 'bookmark.fill' icon and when tapped, it brings the user to their saved recipes section in the app." **Why this is new vs CL-37.** CL-37 deferred `.accessoryCircular` for the *latest recipe* because "there's no good single-glyph payload for a recipe" — true for the recipe, but a **bookmark shortcut** IS a perfect single-glyph payload: it carries no per-recipe data, just a static deep link to Saved. So circular ships as a SEPARATE widget, not as a circular face of the latest-recipe widget. **The implementation.** (1) `WidgetCard.LockScreenCircularBookmark` (new, in `WidgetCard+LockScreen.swift`) — the `bookmark.fill` SF Symbol at `.system(size: 24, weight: .semibold)`, centered via `.frame(maxWidth/maxHeight: .infinity)`. Stays WidgetKit-free (no `AccessoryWidgetBackground` / `.widgetAccentable` here) so it remains L4-snapshot-testable without linking WidgetKit (constitution §6 L4 — same convention as `LockScreenRectangular`). (2) New `Widget/SavedLockScreenWidget.swift` — a `StaticConfiguration` with `.supportedFamilies([.accessoryCircular])`, a **trivial** `TimelineProvider` (one fixed entry, `.never` refresh — no snapshot read, since the shortcut is identical at all times), and an entry view that wraps the glyph with `.containerBackground(for: .widget) { AccessoryWidgetBackground() }` (the wallpaper-aware disc) + `.widgetAccentable()` (present-time tint) + `.widgetURL(URL(string: "dod://saved"))`. The widget + provider + entry view share one small file (vs. the rectangular widget's three-file split) because the static provider has no snapshot-reading logic to justify a split. (3) Registered as the fourth kind in `DODAppWidgetBundle` (after Featured, Saved, LatestRecipeLockScreen). **The deep link.** `dod://saved` is ALREADY parsed (`WidgetDeepLinkParser`, US-17 / CL-29 — the home-screen Saved widget's chrome tap uses it) → routes to the Saved tab via `RootView.onOpenURL`. No new parser case, no new App Group key, no host-side change. **What this does NOT change.** The existing rectangular latest-recipe lock-screen widget (US-22 AC-22.1–22.6); the home-screen widgets; the deep-link grammar; the snapshot wire formats; widget ordering for existing installs (the new kind appends last). **The `.never` timeline policy.** The shortcut never changes, so the provider returns `Timeline(entries: [oneEntry], policy: .never)` — WidgetKit never wakes it for a refresh (a static glyph doesn't need the 4-hour cadence the data-driven widgets use). **Considered and rejected. (a) Add `.accessoryCircular` to the existing `SavedRecipesWidget` (home-screen) or `LatestRecipeLockScreenWidget`.** Rejected — the repo's pattern is a separate `WidgetConfiguration` per lock-screen surface (CL-37), and a static shortcut has nothing in common with the snapshot-reading list/rectangular widgets; mixing families in one widget would force its entry view to branch on `widgetFamily` for two unrelated render paths. (b) **Read the saved-count and badge the glyph (e.g. "3").** Rejected — out of scope (the ask was a plain bookmark icon), and it would drag the App Group snapshot read into an otherwise-static widget, forfeiting the `.never` policy. **Tests.** New L4 `test_lockScreenWidget_circularBookmark` in `LockScreenWidgetSnapshotTests` — pins the glyph at the ~76×76 `.accessoryCircular` frame (1 recorded baseline on the iPhone-17 sim; re-run green). The `AccessoryWidgetBackground` disc + the system monochrome tint are present-time chrome (not reproducible in the SwiftUI snapshot host — same limitation as the rectangular widget's vibrancy), so the disc/tint + the `dod://saved` tap routing are the on-device human checkpoints. App + widget extension build; strict lint clean. **Numbering note.** CL-168 / T-771 / AC-22.7 are the next free slots above CL-167 / T-770 / AC-22.6. No `e2e` label — presentation + a static deep link; L4-pinned (glyph) + on-device tap verification. **Spec/trace:** US-22 amended — AC-22.1 notes `.accessoryCircular` now ships as a Saved shortcut; new AC-22.7 specifies the circular Saved widget. Implementing PR: T-771 (`feat/T-771-saved-lockscreen-circular`). **PR3 of 3 (widget follow-up) — completes the batch:** PR1 (T-769 FeaturedLarge fit + eyebrow) → PR2 (T-770 Saved images) → this.
- **CL-167 (US-17 amendment / T-770, 2026-06-13)** — **Fix the Saved Recipes widget STILL showing gradient placeholders instead of hero photos: emit the bridged filename unconditionally + actively prefetch & bridge the hero bytes for saved recipes whose photos aren't cached yet. PR2 of a 3-PR widget follow-up batch.** Closes Linear DUT-76. **The bug (why CL-163 wasn't enough).** CL-163 (T-766) populated `heroImageFilename` but GATED it on `heroImageCached` (a `CachedImage`-table probe). After CL-158 (T-761) decoupled Save from Download, a pure **Save** no longer pre-downloads the hero — so a recipe saved from the feed (long-press → Save, never opening detail) has no cached bytes → `heroImageCached == false` → `nil` filename → the widget renders `WidgetCard.Hero`'s gradient forever. The Featured (Latest Recipe) widget doesn't have this problem because `LiveFeedDependencies.publishWidgetSnapshot` ACTIVELY prefetches + bridges its hero bytes; the Saved publisher had no such prefetch (CL-163 explicitly deferred it as "a future enhancement could pre-fetch saved-recipe images for recipes never viewed"). **This is that enhancement.** **The fix (mirror the feed).** (1) `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher.toSnapshotEntry` now emits `row.heroImageURL.map(WidgetImageBridge.filename(for:))` **unconditionally** — the deterministic bridged filename for every row with a hero URL, regardless of cache state (matching the Featured publisher). (2) The publisher gains an injected `ImagePrefetcher` (`@Sendable ([URL]) async -> Void`, same shape as `LiveFeedDependencies.ImagePrefetcher`); after writing the snapshot + the first reload, `publish()` fires it in a **detached** Task for the saved recipes whose bytes aren't cached yet (`rows.filter { !$0.heroImageCached }.compactMap(\.heroImageURL)`), then calls `reload()` a second time so the freshly-bridged photos appear. Detached = the save round-trip isn't blocked on the network; the `!heroImageCached` gate = only the missing photos are fetched (cached ones are already bridged via AC-21.2, so they're skipped). The prefetch closure routes URLs through `ImageLoader.data(for:)` + `RecipeStore.cacheImage`, which writes the App Group bridge file via `WidgetImageBridge` (no widget-side network — AC-17.6; the fetch is host-side). (3) Composition root: a new `AppDependencies.savedWidgetPublisher()` factory builds the publisher with store + reload + the prefetch closure (the same `ImageLoader` + `cacheImage` closure `feedDependencies()` uses); **both** save paths now use it — `recipeDetailDependencies()` and `TabStack.saveFromCard` (the card long-press path, which previously built a bare publisher inline). **What this does NOT change.** The widget render path (`WidgetCard.Hero` / `SavedRow`); the bridge (`WidgetImageBridge`) + `RecipeStore.cacheImage` / `savedRecipesForWidget`; the snapshot wire format (`heroImageFilename` was always in the schema); the deep links / families / empty state; the gradient fallback for the brief window before a freshly-saved photo is bridged (AC-17.5 / AC-21.3). The `heroImageCached` projection field stays — it now drives the prefetch gate instead of the filename gate. **Considered and rejected. (a) Block `publish()` on the prefetch (await before writing) so the first render already has photos.** Rejected — `publish()` runs after every save/unsave and must stay fire-and-forget-fast; blocking on N image downloads would jank the widget refresh and fail offline. Detached + second-reload is the feed's proven pattern. (b) **Keep the `heroImageCached` gate + only add the prefetch.** Rejected — the filename has to be set for the photo to appear once bridged; gating it means a just-bridged photo still shows nothing until the NEXT publish. Unconditional filename + prefetch is the coherent pair (and matches the Featured publisher exactly). (c) **Prefetch ALL saved hero URLs every publish.** Rejected — the `!heroImageCached` gate avoids re-downloading already-bridged photos on every save; saved photos are stable once bridged (unlike the feed's rotating snapshot set). **Tests.** `heroImageFilenameIsNilWhenBytesAreNotCached` split into `heroImageFilenameIsNilWhenThereIsNoHeroURL` (genuine nil — no hero URL) + new `heroImageFilenameIsSetFromURLEvenWhenBytesNotCached` (the new contract — filename from URL even when uncached); new `prefetcherIsInvokedForUncachedSavedHeroImages` (inject a recorder, save a recipe with an uncached hero URL, poll the detached prefetch, assert the URL was queued) + `prefetcherIsNotInvokedWhenHeroBytesAreCached` (the `!heroImageCached` gate — deterministic, no Task spawned). 12 `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher` tests pass; app + widget extension build; strict lint clean. **Numbering note.** CL-167 / T-770 is the next free slot above CL-166. No `e2e` label — L1-pinned (12 publisher tests) + on-device verification (save a never-opened recipe from the feed, confirm its photo appears in the Saved widget). **Spec/trace:** US-17 AC-17.3 amended (unconditional bridged filename + active hero-byte prefetch). Implementing PR: T-770 (`feat/T-770-saved-widget-image-prefetch`). **PR2 of 3 (widget follow-up):** PR1 (T-769 FeaturedLarge fit + eyebrow) → this → PR3 (circular lock-screen Saved widget).
- **CL-166 (US-9 amendment / T-769, 2026-06-13)** — **Two Latest Recipe (Featured) widget fixes: (1) stop the `.systemLarge` title/excerpt from clipping; (2) rename the eyebrow "Today on DOD" → "New on DOD" on the medium + large faces. PR1 of a 3-PR widget follow-up batch.** Closes Linear DUT-75. **The bug (clipping).** `WidgetCard.FeaturedLarge` stacks a greedy hero (`.frame(maxHeight: .infinity)`) above the text block; with a real-world long title (2 lines) + excerpt (2 lines) the hero squeezed the text block below its natural height, clipping the bottom of the excerpt / the `TimeChip`. **The fix.** Pin the content stack to its natural vertical size with `.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)` so the text always gets its full height and the hero takes only the remainder; drop the large title from `.title3` (≈20pt bold) to `.headline` (≈17pt semibold — matching the `Medium` title) so long titles fit two lines comfortably. **The copy change.** The eyebrow rendered "TODAY ON DOD" (uppercased from the source string `"Today on DOD"`) on both Featured faces (`WidgetCard.Medium` + `WidgetCard.FeaturedLarge`). The widget shows the newest/latest recipe, so "New on DOD" is more accurate — both source strings → `"New on DOD"`. The small Featured face has no eyebrow (full-bleed photo + title overlay); the Saved faces use the "Saved" eyebrow — neither changes. **What this does NOT change.** The `Medium` side-by-side split; the deep-link grammar / families / kinds; the Tinted/Clear treatment (CL-164); the `SavedLarge` layout; the accessibility labels (they describe the recipe — "Today's recipe: …" — not the eyebrow, so they're untouched). **Tests.** The L4 `WidgetLargeSnapshotTests` fixture is bumped to a deliberately long title + excerpt ("Dutch Oven Garlic Herb Butter Roasted Chicken" + a 2-line excerpt) so the re-recorded `FeaturedLarge` baseline actively guards the clipping fix. 6 baselines re-recorded on the iPhone-17 sim: `FeaturedLarge` (fullColor + tinted) + `Medium` (fullColor / dark / tinted / vibrant — the eyebrow text changed the pixels). Verify re-run green for all widget classes (the lone `test_moderationBadge_eachKind` local diff is pre-existing/environmental — that component is untouched by this branch and is green on CI, which recorded its baseline). App + widget extension build; strict lint clean. **Considered and rejected. (a) Make the hero a fixed fraction of the height instead of `.fixedSize` on the text.** Rejected — `.fixedSize` is the minimal, intent-revealing fix (it says "give the text its natural height"); a magic fraction would re-clip at larger Dynamic Type. (b) **Edit the CL-165 historical note's "Today on DOD" wording in place.** Rejected — changelog notes are dated history; the base AC text + this CL-166 note carry the current truth. **Numbering note.** CL-166 / T-769 is the next free slot above CL-165. No `e2e` label — presentation; L4-pinned + on-device verification. **Spec/trace:** US-9 AC-9.1 amended (eyebrow copy "New on DOD"; large text-fit). Implementing PR: T-769 (`feat/T-769-featured-large-fit`). **PR1 of 3 (widget follow-up):** this → PR2 (Saved-widget images actually load) → PR3 (circular lock-screen Saved widget).
- **CL-165 (US-9 / US-17 amendment / T-768, 2026-06-13)** — **Add `.systemLarge` to both home-screen widgets (Latest Recipe + Saved Recipes). PR3 of the 3-PR widget overhaul; supersedes CL-26's small+medium-only deferral.** Closes Linear DUT-74. **The change.** Both widgets shipped `.systemSmall` + `.systemMedium` only. Add `.systemLarge` to each config's `supportedFamilies` + two new layouts in a new `WidgetCard+Large.swift` (split out so the near-cap `WidgetCard.swift` / `+Saved.swift` aren't touched): **(1) `WidgetCard.FeaturedLarge`** — a hero-forward "magazine cover": a full-width `Hero` photo on top (greedy `.frame(maxHeight: .infinity)`) with the "Today on DOD" eyebrow + title (`.title3` bold) + excerpt + `TimeChip` stacked beneath it. **(2) `WidgetCard.SavedLarge`** — the "Saved" eyebrow + up to **5** `SavedListRow` thumbnail rows (vs. medium's 3). Both follow the CL-164 (PR2) rule — NO inner background, so the `containerBackground` tints in Tinted/Clear — and place text on the container (not over the image), so they're legible in `.accented` without a scrim (AC-23.7). The hero/thumbnails keep their `fullColor` opt-out. **Wiring.** `FeaturedRecipeWidgetEntryView` routes `.systemLarge` → `FeaturedLarge`; `SavedRecipesWidgetEntryView` routes `.systemLarge` → `largeBody`, and `mediumBody`/`largeBody` now share a `savedListBody(maxRows:)` (3 vs 5) so the per-row `Link` deep-link wiring stays in one place. `SavedRecipesWidgetSnapshotConfig.maxEntries` is raised 3 → 5 so the host snapshot carries enough saved rows for the large list (small/medium still `prefix(N)` client-side). **What this does NOT change.** The small + medium layouts; the Featured `Medium` side-by-side split; the deep-link grammar (`dod://recipe/<id>?source=saved`, chrome → `dod://saved`); the snapshot wire format (the entry array already held up to `maxEntries`); the Tinted/Clear treatment (PR2). **Tests.** New L4 `WidgetLargeSnapshotTests` — `FeaturedLarge` + `SavedLarge` (5 rows) in both `.fullColor` and `.accented`, wrapped in the simulated container background (the CL-164 convention); baselines recorded on the iPhone-17 sim and committed (4 PNGs). Re-run is green (deterministic). App + widget extension build; strict lint clean (new code housed in `WidgetCard+Large.swift` + `WidgetLargeSnapshotTests.swift` to respect the type/file-length caps). **Numbering note.** CL-165 / T-768 is the next free slot above CL-164. No `e2e` label — presentation; L4-pinned + on-device verification (add the large widgets, confirm in the gallery). **Spec/trace:** US-9 AC-9.1 amended (Featured `systemLarge`), US-17 AC-17.2 amended (Saved `systemLarge`, up to 5). Implementing PR: T-768 (`feat/T-768-large-widgets`). **Completes the 3-PR widget overhaul:** PR1 (T-766 Saved images) → PR2 (T-767 Tinted/Clear) → PR3 (this — Large sizes).
- **CL-164 (US-23 / US-17 amendment / T-767, 2026-06-13)** — **Fix Tinted/Clear home-screen widget rendering (Apple News pattern): move the widget background from an inner `.background(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` to the `containerBackground` so the system tints it, instead of flattening a solid inner fill into a tint silhouette. PR2 of the 3-PR widget overhaul.** Closes Linear DUT-73. **The bug.** In `.accented`/`.vibrant` (Tinted/Clear) mode the widgets didn't follow the Apple News look (tinted background following the user's tint + white text + full-color images). Both widget configs already declared `containerBackground(for: .widget) { DODColor.surfaceElevated }`, but the `WidgetCard` content ALSO painted an inner `.background(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` at 6 sites (`Medium`, `Placeholder`, `SavedSmall`, `SavedMedium`, `SavedEmpty`, and `SavedRecipesWidgetEntryView.mediumBody`). In accented mode the system flattens that solid inner fill into the default-group tint silhouette, which covers the tinted container background — so the widget looked like a flat tint blob instead of tint-bg + content. **The fix.** Remove the 6 inner `.background(DODColor.surfaceElevated)`; the `containerBackground` is now the sole background. In Standard (full-color) mode this is visually identical (the container is the same `surfaceElevated` color the inner fill used). In Tinted/Clear the container tints, text renders white (default group), and the hero/thumbnail images keep their existing `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.fullColor)` opt-out → the Apple News pattern. The small Featured widget is untouched (full-bleed photo, per Spencer's call). **Snapshots.** The L4 `WidgetCard` snapshot tests render the components in isolation (no widget container), so each render site is wrapped in `.background(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` to simulate the container — the wrap fills the same frame the old inner fill did (and the Featured `Medium`'s Hero still covers its half), so the Standard + dark baselines are byte-stable (no re-record). `SnapshotTests.swift` was at the `type_body_length` cap, so the 3 added wrap lines are offset by collapsing a verbose `assertSnapshot` call (the moved-out approach was avoided to keep the shared `tolerantImage` helper in one place). **The true Tinted/Clear appearance can't be snapshot-tested** — the system's accent pass isn't reproduced in the SwiftUI snapshot host (AC-23.7) — so it is verified on-device (the human checkpoint constitution §7 mandates). **What this does NOT change.** The `containerBackground` declarations; the Hero `fullColor` opt-out + the `TintSafeScrim` (AC-23.7); the small Featured full-bleed layout; widget kinds / families / deep links / accessibility; the snapshot baselines (Standard + dark unchanged). **Considered and rejected. (a) Conditional inner background via `@Environment(\.widgetRenderingMode)` (paint `surfaceElevated` in `.fullColor`, `.clear` in accented).** Workable but adds the env + iOS-18 availability gating to 5 component structs for a redundant in-`.fullColor` paint; relying purely on `containerBackground` is simpler and the Apple-recommended mechanism. (b) **Restructure the small widgets into tinted cards.** Rejected — Spencer chose to keep small widgets full-bleed photo (idiomatic, like Apple Photos). **Tests.** No new unit test (presentation). The L4 widget snapshot baselines (Standard + dark) are unchanged by construction (wrap = old inner fill); the Tinted/Vibrant baselines under `__Snapshots__/WidgetCardTintedAppearanceSnapshotTests/` stay as the regression net; app + widget extension build; strict lint clean. **Numbering note.** CL-164 / T-767 is the next free slot above CL-163. No `e2e` label — presentation + on-device verification. **Spec/trace:** US-23 AC-23.2 amended (background owned by `containerBackground`; Tinted/Clear fix for all home-screen widgets), AC-23.7 unchanged (scrim contract intact). Implementing PR: T-767 (`feat/T-767-widget-tinted-styling`). **PR2 of 3:** PR1 (T-766 Saved images) → this → PR3 (Large sizes).
- **CL-163 (US-17 amendment / T-766, 2026-06-13)** — **Fix the Saved Recipes widget showing the gradient placeholder instead of recipe hero photos: populate the snapshot's `heroImageFilename` from the App Group bridge (PR1 of the 3-PR widget overhaul).** Closes Linear DUT-72. **The bug.** `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher.toSnapshotEntry` hardcoded `heroImageFilename: nil` — a documented T-322 v1 deferral ("future work: write image files to the App Group container … and use that filename here"). So every Saved-widget row rendered ``WidgetCard.Hero``'s gradient fallback, never the photo — unlike the Featured (Latest Recipe) widget, which shows photos. **Why the bytes already exist.** `RecipeStore.cacheImage(url:bytes:)` mirrors every cached hero JPEG into the App Group container under `WidgetImageBridge.filename(for:)` (AC-21.2), and `savedRecipesForWidget` already computes `heroImageCached` (a `CachedImage`-table probe). The widget's render path (`SavedRow.heroImageURL` → `WidgetCard.Hero`, with `.widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.fullColor)`) already resolves a bridged filename into a `file://` App Group URL. The only missing link was the publisher never setting the filename. **The fix.** `toSnapshotEntry` now emits `row.heroImageCached ? row.heroImageURL.map(WidgetImageBridge.filename(for:)) : nil` — mirroring `LiveFeedDependencies.publishWidgetSnapshot`, which the working Featured widget uses. A cached recipe's row now carries the deterministic bridged filename; the widget reads the local file (no network — constitution §9 / AC-17.6) and renders the full-color photo. **What this does NOT change.** The widget render path (`WidgetCard.Hero` / `SavedRow`); the bridge (`WidgetImageBridge`); `RecipeStore.cacheImage` / `savedRecipesForWidget`; the snapshot wire format (`heroImageFilename` was always in the schema — just always nil); the empty-state + deep-link behavior; the gradient fallback for uncached recipes (AC-21.3). **Considered and rejected. (a) Set the filename unconditionally (no `heroImageCached` gate), like the Featured publisher.** Rejected — Featured prefetches+bridges its top entries async, so its unconditional filename is backed by a deliberate fetch; the Saved publisher has no prefetch, so gating on `heroImageCached` (which directly reflects AC-21.2's bridge write) avoids pointing at files we have no reason to believe exist. (b) **Actively bridge bytes in the publisher (read `CachedImage`, `WidgetImageBridge.writeImage`).** Rejected for PR1 — unnecessary when cached (cacheImage already wrote the bridge file); a future enhancement could pre-fetch saved-recipe images for recipes never viewed, but that's separate scope. **Tests.** `heroImageFilenameIsSetWhenBytesAreCached` (new — cache a hero via `RecipeStore.cacheImage`, assert the snapshot carries `WidgetImageBridge.filename(for:)`); `heroImageFilenameIsNilWhenBytesAreNotCached` (retained, comment refreshed — still nil when uncached). 9 `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher` tests pass; app + widget extension build; strict lint clean. **Numbering note.** CL-163 / T-766 is the next free slot above CL-162. No `e2e` label — L1-pinned + manual on-device verification. **Spec/trace:** US-17 AC-17.3 amended (the saved snapshot's `heroImageFilename` is now populated from the bridge). Implementing PR: T-766 (`feat/T-766-saved-widget-images`). **Part of the 3-PR widget overhaul:** PR1 (this — Saved images) → PR2 (Tinted/Clear Apple-News styling on all widgets) → PR3 (add Large sizes).
- **CL-162 (US-34 amendment / T-765, 2026-06-13)** — **Resolve the long-standing Feed/Categories/Search TODO: the recipe-card long-press menu is now STATE-AWARE on every surface (correct Save vs Unsave), fixing a bug where an already-saved recipe showed "Save" and tapping it silently un-saved it.** Closes Linear DUT-71. **The bug.** Since T-634 / CL-103 (2026-05-29), `FeedView` / `SearchView` / `SearchView+IngredientSection` / `CategoryRecipesView` hardcoded `recipeCardContextMenu(isSaved: false)` (only the Saved tab was correct, `isSaved: true`). The action routes through `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` which TOGGLES, so long-pressing an already-saved recipe on those tabs showed "Save" + filled bookmark, and tapping it removed the recipe from Saved — a mislabeled, surprising un-save. Deferred at T-634 because `RecipeListItem` has no `isSaved` field; the cards needed a viewmodel-owned saved-id set (CL-60 path-(c)). **The fix (state-aware, all three surfaces).** (1) New `RecipeStore.savedRecipeIDs() -> Set<Int>` (in `RecipeStore+SavedWidget.swift`) — a lightweight id projection over the synced `SyncedSavedRecipe` source of truth (same rows `isSaved`/`savedRecipes` read). (2) Each feature dependency protocol (`FeedDependencies` / `SearchDependencies` / `CategoriesDependencies`) gains `savedRecipeIDs() async throws -> Set<Int>` with a default `{ [] }` protocol-extension impl (so existing test fakes keep compiling — mirrors `FeedDependencies.publishWidgetSnapshot` + Search's existing `recentlyViewedRecipeIDs()`); the `Live…` structs route to the store. (3) Each view model (`FeedViewModel` / `SearchViewModel` / `CategoryRecipesViewModel`) owns `savedRecipeIDs: Set<Int>`, hydrates it via `refreshSavedRecipeIDs()` on every appear (ungated, so a save on recipe detail / the Saved tab / CloudKit reflects on return), and exposes `applyOptimisticSaveToggle(id:)` that flips membership the instant the user taps the menu — mirroring `SavedViewModel`'s optimistic-removal pattern so the label is correct on re-open before the async store toggle round-trips. (4) The four card sites pass `isSaved: viewModel.savedRecipeIDs.contains(item.id)` and call `applyOptimisticSaveToggle(id:)` before `onSave?(item)`. The stale "TODO: thread per-card isSaved" comments are removed. **What this does NOT change.** The Saved-tab path (CL-104 optimistic-removal, still `isSaved: true`); the `recipeCardContextMenu` DesignSystem helper (untouched); `TabStack.saveFromCard` (still the real toggle + widget republish); the card tap + accessibility identifiers; the label/icon mapping. **File-length compliance (SwiftLint `--strict`, 400-line cap).** `RecipeStore.swift` + `SearchViewModel.swift` + `SearchView.swift` were at the cap, so the new code goes in extensions idiomatically: `savedRecipeIDs()` → `RecipeStore+SavedWidget.swift`; the two `SearchViewModel` methods → new `SearchViewModel+SavedState.swift` (the stored property stays on the class, relaxed to `internal(set)` so the extension can flip it — matching `items`). FeedViewModel/CategoryRecipesViewModel kept their methods inline (those files had headroom). **Considered and rejected. (a) Idempotent-save (always "Save", never unsaves) via `markSaved` instead of `toggleSaved`.** Rejected — removes the surprise but not the wrong label, and gives no Unsave-from-Feed; the user asked for full Save/Unsave parity with the Saved tab. (b) **Own the saved-id set in `TabStack`.** Rejected — escaping-closure `@State` mutation is awkward + not L1-testable; VM-owned state is the codebase pattern (`SavedViewModel`) and unit-testable. (c) **Add a `RecipeListItem.isSaved` field.** Rejected — `RecipeListItem` is a network/cache DTO; saved-state is per-user session state that belongs in the view model, and threading it through every fetch is heavier + staler than an on-appear set refresh. **Tests.** `SyncedSavedRecipeTests.savedRecipeIDsReflectsTheSavedSet` (the store projection + unsave removal); `FeedViewModelTests.onAppearHydratesSavedIDsAndOptimisticToggleFlips` (hydration on appear + optimistic flip — the shared VM pattern). Search (76) + Categories (10) + Feed (6) + persistence (28) suites pass; app builds; strict lint clean. The Search/Category VMs share the identical (extension-housed) logic; one VM test covers the pattern. **Numbering note.** CL-162 / T-765 is the next free slot above CL-161. No `e2e` label — L1-pinned; the existing Saved-tab L5 journey is unaffected. **Spec/trace:** US-34 AC-34.6 amended (the Feed/Categories/Search TODO resolved — state-aware label on all card surfaces). Implementing PR: T-765 (`feat/T-765-context-menu-saved-state`).
- **CL-161 (US-5 / US-10 amendment / T-764, 2026-06-13)** — **Reword the stale "save it for offline" Save copy to the favorite/find-later framing, finishing the T-761 / T-762 Save-vs-Download decoupling.** Closes Linear DUT-70. Follow-up to CL-158 (T-761 — Save became a lightweight favorite, not an offline pin) + CL-159 (T-762 — the splash bullet was already reworded). Four sites still framed **Save** as "for offline": **(1)** the Saved-tab empty state (`SavedView.swift`) — "Tap the bookmark on any recipe to save it for offline." → **"Tap the bookmark on any recipe to find it again later."** (matches the T-762 splash caption); **(2)** the Siri/Shortcuts `OpenSavedRecipesIntent` description (`App/RecipeAppIntents.swift`) — "Opens the list of recipes you've saved for offline cooking." → **"Opens the list of recipes you've saved."**; **(3)** the `EmptyState` `#Preview` default + **(4)** the `OnboardingSheet` `#Preview` bullet ("Save for offline" / "cook it without Wi-Fi") → both updated to match (dev-only previews). **What this does NOT change.** Behavior — copy only. The Saved empty-state **title** ("No saved recipes yet", which the L3 smoke + L5 journey assert via `app.staticTexts["No saved recipes yet"]`) is untouched; the Saved snapshot tests render only the loaded state (no empty-state pixel baseline), so there is no L4 regen. **Deliberately left:** the DODDesignSystem snapshot **fixtures** (`SnapshotTests.swift` / `SnapshotTests+AppearanceAudit.swift`) that hardcode a "Save for offline" sample bullet to render the generic `OnboardingSheet` component — those are component-render fixtures, not shipped copy, and changing them would force an L4 baseline regen for zero user benefit. **Considered and rejected. (a) Also update the snapshot fixtures for full string-purity.** Rejected — they're test render samples, not user-facing; the churn + baseline regen isn't worth it (noted here so the inconsistency is intentional, not an oversight). **(b) Keep "for offline" since saved recipes are still eviction-protected.** Rejected — eviction protection ≠ a guaranteed offline copy (the hero image isn't force-pinned on Save anymore per CL-158), and the user explicitly reframed Save as a findability favorite. **Tests.** Copy-only — no new test. DODFeatureSaved (29) + DODDesignSystem (19) suites pass; the app builds; lint/format clean. **Numbering note.** CL-161 / T-764 is the next free slot above CL-160. No `e2e` label. **Spec/trace:** US-5 AC-5.8 amended (Saved empty-state message); US-10's `OpenSavedRecipesIntent` description reworded. Implementing PR: T-764 (`feat/T-764-reword-save-favorite-copy`).
- **CL-160 (US-36 amendment / T-763, 2026-06-13)** — **Rename the Settings → App Appearance explicit-scheme option labels for brand personality: "Light" → "Flour", "Dark" → "Cocoa" ("Match System" unchanged).** Closes Linear DUT-69. One-enum change in `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/SettingsPreferences.swift` — `AppearancePreference.displayName` maps `.light` → "Flour" and `.dark` → "Cocoa". **The request (Spencer, 2026-06-13).** Give the App Appearance options more personality — "Light" becomes "Flour", "Dark" becomes "Cocoa" (the cast-iron-baking palette: pale flour vs dark cocoa). "Match System" stays as-is (explicitly out of scope today). **What this does NOT change.** The `AppearancePreference` cases (`system` / `light` / `dark`); the persisted `rawValue`s (`"system"` / `"light"` / `"dark"` — so an existing user's saved choice is preserved, no migration); the `colorScheme` mapping (Flour still forces light, Cocoa still forces dark); the default ("Match System"); the `settings-picker-appearance` identifier; the live-theming behavior (T-756 / CL-153). Labels-only. **Considered and rejected. (a) Also rename "Match System".** Rejected — the user explicitly scoped it out ("Match System remains the same as it has been"). **(b) Rename the enum cases / rawValues to match (`.flour` / `.cocoa`).** Rejected — that would break persistence for existing users (the stored rawValue is `"light"` / `"dark"`) and muddy the `colorScheme` semantics; the personality belongs in the display label, not the wire format. **Tests.** No new test — the labels aren't asserted by any unit/UI test, and the `.menu`-style picker renders only the selected value (default "Match System"), so the L4 Settings snapshots are unaffected. The 50 `DODFeatureFeed` `SettingsViewModel*` tests pass unchanged. **Numbering note.** CL-160 / T-763 is the next free slot above CL-159. No `e2e` label — a one-string-pair label change. **Spec/trace:** US-36 AC-36.2 amended (the picker reads "Match System" / "Flour" / "Cocoa"). Implementing PR: T-763 (`feat/T-763-appearance-flour-cocoa-labels`).
- **CL-159 (US-8 / US-41 amendment / T-762, 2026-06-12)** — **Consolidate the first-launch flow to ONE splash: remove the second sheet (the iCloud-Sync opt-in) and surface iCloud Sync as an informational capability bullet + a save-a-favorite bullet on the single welcome sheet. Supersedes CL-89's first-launch opt-in-prompt decision — the prompt moves entirely to the Settings toggle (AC-41.3).** Closes Linear DUT-68. Touches `App/RootView.swift` (collapse to one sheet + rework `welcomeBullets`), `App/DODApp.swift` (drop the opt-in-prompt UI-test overrides), and deletes `App/CloudKitOptInPromptGate.swift` + `AppTests/CloudKitOptInPromptGateTests.swift` + `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Sources/DODDesignSystem/Components/CloudKitOptInSheet.swift`. **The request (Spencer, 2026-06-12).** "One splash screen — the second one with turning on iCloud sync will be removed, and iCloud Sync will have a spot on the first one. No toggle to turn it on from the splash (that's done in Settings), but the spot mentions that sync is a capability of the app. Also mention how you can save a favorite recipe to find them easier later." **The change. (1) One sheet.** Pre-T-762, `RootView` drove two sequential first-launch sheets via a `FirstLaunchSheet` enum + `.sheet(item:)`: `.onboarding` (the US-8 welcome) chained to `.cloudKitOptIn` (the AC-41.2 opt-in) from the "Get cooking" CTA, and upgraders hit `.cloudKitOptIn` directly from `init`. Now there is a single `@State showOnboarding: Bool` + `.sheet(isPresented:)` rendering only the `OnboardingSheet`; the CTA sets `dod.onboardingCompletedV1 = true` and dismisses (no chain). `init` shows the sheet iff onboarding isn't done — the `CloudKitOptInPromptGate().shouldShow` branch is gone. **(2) Reworked bullets.** `welcomeBullets` goes 3 → 4: Browse + Search are unchanged; the bookmark bullet is reworded from "Save for offline" / "…cook it without Wi-Fi." to **"Save your favorites" / "Tap the bookmark on any recipe to find it again later."** (the save-a-favorite-for-findability framing the user asked for — T-761 / CL-158 decoupled Save from the offline download, so the offline wording is dropped); a new **`icloud.fill` "Sync across devices" / "Turn on iCloud Sync in Settings to keep your saved recipes on every device."** bullet surfaces sync as a capability with NO toggle (opt-in is Settings-only). **(3) Dead-code removal.** With the sheet gone, `CloudKitOptInPromptGate` (the `dod.cloudKitSyncOptInPromptShownV1` once-only gate) + its unit test + the generic `CloudKitOptInSheet` DesignSystem component (zero remaining call sites, no snapshot test) are deleted, and `DODApp.applyTestLaunchOverrides` loses the `-DODForceCloudKitOptInPrompt` / prompt-shown launch overrides that kept the opt-in sheet out of UI-test golden paths. **What this does NOT change.** The Settings → iCloud Sync toggle (AC-41.3 / T-759's direct flip) — the sole opt-in surface now; the non-destructive enable/disable (saved recipes stay local when sync is off, per T-759's verification); `dod.cloudKitSyncEnabled` + `setCloudSyncOptIn` (still wired through `LiveSettingsDependencies` + `SettingsViewModel`); the `dod.onboardingCompletedV1` flag + the "Get cooking" CTA + the `OnboardingSheet` component; the `-DODForceFreshOnboarding` / `DOD_SUPPRESS_ONBOARDING` onboarding overrides; the onboarding L3 smoke (`test_onboardingShowsOnFirstLaunchAndDismisses` — welcome → "Get cooking" → tab bar, which now lands on the feed with no second sheet to traverse). **Considered and rejected. (a) Keep the opt-in sheet but auto-dismiss / fold it into onboarding as a fourth interactive step.** Rejected — the user explicitly wants the second sheet GONE and the toggle only in Settings; an informational capability bullet (no toggle) is exactly the ask. **(b) Keep `CloudKitOptInPromptGate` + `CloudKitOptInSheet` dormant (just stop presenting).** Rejected — dead code rots (same posture as CL-156 / T-759); zero remaining callers or tests, so a clean delete is clearer. **(c) Add the download-also-saves behavior (T-761) to the splash too.** Rejected — the user explicitly said NOT to put the download-saves detail on the splash; only the save-a-favorite framing belongs there. **(d) Mention sync's iCloud sign-in nuance on the bullet.** Rejected — the bullet is a one-line capability teaser; the Settings row owns the `CKAccountStatus` sign-in nuance (AC-41.3). **Tests.** No new unit test — presentation + dead-code removal. The deleted `CloudKitOptInPromptGateTests` go with the gate. The onboarding L3 smoke is unchanged and still valid (one fewer sheet to dismiss). `xcodebuild build` + `build-for-testing` succeed (App + AppTests + UITests + E2E targets compile without the deleted files); DODDesignSystem's 19 component tests pass without `CloudKitOptInSheet`. **Numbering note.** CL-159 / T-762 is the next free slot above CL-158. The `e2e` label is NOT applied — presentation-only + dead-code removal, covered by the existing onboarding smoke + the compile gates. **Spec/trace:** US-8 AC-8.1 amended (four bullets — reworded Save + new iCloud-capability bullet); US-41 AC-41.2 amended (the first-launch opt-in sheet is removed; opt-in is Settings-only via AC-41.3). Implementing PR: T-762 (`feat/T-762-single-splash-screen`).
- **CL-158 (US-5 / US-35 amendment / T-761, 2026-06-12)** — **Decouple Save and Download on recipe detail: downloading a recipe now ALSO saves it (download = save + offline pin), and saving a recipe is a lightweight favorite that does NOT download. Supersedes CL-61's "a saved recipe is treated as already-downloaded" linkage.** Closes Linear DUT-67. Touches `Packages/DODPersistence/Sources/DODPersistence/RecipeStore.swift` (new `markSaved(id:)`), `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/RecipeDetailDependencies+Download.swift` (the live `downloadForOffline` now calls `markSaved` + drops the `isSaved` short-circuit), `RecipeDetailViewModel+Download.swift` (sets `isSaved = true` + republishes the saved widget), and `RecipeDetailViewModel.swift` (Save snackbar copy + `isSaved` set-access). **The request (Spencer, 2026-06-12).** "Make it so downloading a recipe saves it, but saving a recipe doesn't download it … make sure downloaded recipes still save." Two recipe-detail toolbar actions had overlapping-but-confusing semantics: **Save** (bookmark, AC-5.1) showed a "Saved for offline." snackbar (implying it pinned the recipe for offline use), and **Download** (AC-35.1) short-circuited to "Already downloaded" whenever the recipe was merely *saved* (CL-61's `isSaved == true` branch, justified back then by AC-5.2's save-driven pre-download). Spencer wants the two cleanly split: Download = the offline action (and, since you clearly want this recipe, it also adds it to your saved favorites); Save = a lightweight favorite to "find them easier later" (the T-762 splash copy), with no offline download implied. **The change. (1) Download also saves.** `LiveRecipeDetailDependencies.downloadForOffline(recipe:)` calls `RecipeStore.markSaved(id:)` (a new idempotent "ensure saved without toggling" method that mirrors the synced row exactly like `toggleSaved`, returning the unsaved→saved transition) right after `markDownloaded`. The view model flips `isSaved = true` and republishes the saved-recipes widget snapshot so the filled bookmark + the widget reflect the new save in the same frame. **(2) Download no longer treats "saved" as "already downloaded."** The `guard transitioned` short-circuit keys ONLY on the real `downloadedAt` pin (`markDownloaded`'s return), NOT on `isSaved`. So tapping Download on a merely-saved recipe performs a real first-time download ("Recipe downloaded for offline use") instead of the misleading "Already downloaded". A re-download of an already-`downloadedAt` recipe still returns `.alreadyDownloaded` (AC-35.4 idempotency preserved) AND still guarantees the recipe is saved (the `markSaved` call runs before the short-circuit) — satisfying "make sure downloaded recipes still save" even for recipes downloaded under the pre-T-761 behavior (it backfills the bookmark). **(3) Save is a lightweight favorite.** `toggleSaved()`'s success snackbar changes from "Saved for offline." to "Saved." — Save no longer claims offline availability (it never actually pre-downloaded the hero image from the detail screen: `RecipeStore.toggleSaved` only sets `isSaved` + mirrors the synced row; the dormant `LiveSavedDependencies.preDownloadImages` path has no call site). The offline-pin guarantee is now unambiguously owned by the explicit Download action. **What this does NOT change.** `RecipeStore.toggleSaved` (still just `isSaved` + synced-row mirror + eviction on un-save); `markDownloaded` (the `downloadedAt` pin + AC-35.4 idempotency); the hero-image pin path (`cacheImage(pinnedToSavedRecipeID:)`); the LRU eviction predicate (`isSaved == false && downloadedAt == nil` — saved OR downloaded rows survive); the four-flag state model (AC-35.5) — only the transitions change (a fresh Download now lands in state 4 "both"; "downloaded-only" is reachable only by later unsaving a downloaded recipe); the Save/Download/Share toolbar layout + identifiers + accessibility labels; the `recipeSaved` / `recipeUnsaved` telemetry (still Save-path only — Download stays un-telemetered per AC-35.6). To let the `+Download` extension set the flag, `RecipeDetailViewModel.isSaved` is relaxed from `private(set)` to `internal(set)` (mirrors `isDownloaded`; still read-only externally). **Considered and rejected. (a) Download sets `downloadedAt` but leaves `isSaved` alone (keep them fully independent).** Rejected — the user explicitly wants Download to also save; a download-without-save would still hide the recipe from the Saved tab, the opposite of intent. **(b) Make Save also pre-download the hero image (restore AC-5.2 on the detail Save).** Rejected — the user explicitly wants Save to NOT download; Save is the lightweight findability action now, Download is the offline action. **(c) Keep "Already downloaded" when a saved recipe is tapped for Download.** Rejected — under the decoupled model a saved recipe is NOT downloaded (no `downloadedAt`), so claiming "Already downloaded" is false; a real download must run. **(d) Add a `recipeDownloaded` telemetry event now.** Deferred (unchanged from AC-35.6) — out of scope; the constitution §9 allowlist stays tight. **Tests.** Persistence: `markSavedIsIdempotentAndPinsTheRow` (RecipeStoreTests) pins the new method's transition + idempotency + synced mirror. Recipe-detail (RecipeDetailDownloadTests, retargeted + expanded): `downloadForOfflineAlsoSavesRecipe` (download flips `isSaved` + mirrors the saved store), `downloadForOfflineOnSavedRecipeStillDownloadsFresh` (a merely-saved recipe downloads fresh — replaces the deleted pre-T-761 `…AlreadyDownloadedSnackbarWhenSaved` test that asserted the now-removed short-circuit), `reDownloadSurfacesAlreadyDownloadedButKeepsSaved` ("downloaded recipes still save" — already-`downloadedAt` recipe surfaces "Already downloaded" yet ends up saved), `savingDoesNotDownloadRecipe` (the inverse half — Save sets `isSaved` but leaves `isDownloaded == false` + no download pin + "Saved." copy). The `FakeRecipeDetailDependencies.downloadForOffline` is updated to mirror production (saves on download; only a real prior `downloadedAt` returns `.alreadyDownloaded`). All 189 recipe-detail + 21 RecipeStore L1 tests pass. **Numbering note.** CL-158 / T-761 is the next free slot above CL-157. The `e2e` label is NOT applied — the decoupling is fully unit-pinned at L1 + manually verified on device; no new end-to-end journey. **Spec/trace:** US-35 AC-35.3 amended (the "Already downloaded" snackbar keys only on `downloadedAt`, not `isSaved`); AC-35.5 amended (Download also sets `isSaved` via `markSaved`); new AC-35.7 (Save is a lightweight favorite that does not download); AC-5.1 / AC-5.2 / AC-5.4 amended (Save snackbar "Saved."; the offline-pin guarantee is owned by US-35 Download). Implementing PR: T-761 (`feat/T-761-download-saves-save-doesnt-download`).
- **CL-157 (US-40 / US-41 amendment / T-760, 2026-06-12)** — **Fold the Cook Mode Voice quality + preview into one cell, and the iCloud Sync status into one cell (DUT-66): each previously-separate sub-row moves INTO its parent control's cell as smaller inline detail.** Closes Linear DUT-66. Touches `Typography.swift` (new token) + `SettingsView+Voice.swift` + `SettingsView+CloudSync.swift`. **The request (Spencer, 2026-06-12).** Two Settings controls spread their secondary info across separate cells; consolidate each into one richer cell. **(1) Cook Mode Voice.** Pre-T-760: three cells — the gender `Picker` ("Cook Mode Voice"), the "Voice Quality" readout row, and the "Preview Voice" labeled button. Now ONE `voiceCell`: a `VStack` with the gender picker on top, then a single smaller line holding the voice-quality readout ("Voice Quality: Enhanced") + a speaker-icon-only preview button (the "Preview Voice" title is dropped — the label moves to VoiceOver). The dismissible download-voice nudge stays a separate conditional row. **(2) iCloud Sync.** Pre-T-760: the toggle cell + a separate "Status" row (shown only when sync is ON). Now ONE cell: a `VStack` with the toggle on top, then (when ON) a smaller inline "Status: …" line. **The size token.** A new `DODType.detail` (`.system(.subheadline)`, ~15pt) is the "between body (17) and caption (12)" size the user asked for — distinctly smaller than a row label but bigger than a caption/footer. Both folded lines + the preview icon use it, in `DODColor.labelSecondary` (accent for the preview icon). **What this does NOT change.** The gender picker behavior + identifier (`settings-picker-voice-gender`); the preview action + identifier (`settings-button-voice-preview`, now with an explicit `accessibilityLabel("Preview Voice")` since the visible title is gone); the quality readout identifier (`settings-voice-quality`); the iCloud toggle + status identifiers (`settings-toggle-icloud-sync` / `settings-icloud-sync-status`); the `cloudSyncStatusText` / `resolvedVoiceQuality` view-model values (every L1 test passes untouched). Only the cell composition + the detail text size change. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Keep the preview as an icon + small "Preview" text.** Rejected — the user explicitly wanted the title removed (icon only); the VoiceOver label preserves the meaning for assistive tech. **(b) Use `.caption` (12) for the folded detail.** Rejected — too close to the footer/description size; the user wanted it BETWEEN the row label and the caption, hence the new 15pt `detail` token. **(c) Put the quality + preview in the section footer.** Rejected — the user wanted them IN the cell (under the control), not as a section-wide footer. **Numbering note.** CL-157 / T-760 is the next free slot above CL-156. The `e2e` label is NOT applied — a presentation-only cell restructure; the regenerating L4 Settings snapshots + the unchanged L1 VM tests cover it. **Spec/trace:** US-40 AC-40.12 (voice quality + preview folded into the Cook Mode Voice cell), US-41 AC-41.7 (status folded into the iCloud Sync cell). Implementing PR: T-760 (`feat/T-760-settings-cell-consolidation`).
- **CL-156 (US-41 amendment / T-759, 2026-06-12)** — **Remove the per-toggle iCloud Sync confirmation popup — the toggle now flips directly. Supersedes CL-89 (the "confirm every flip" decision) + CL-154 (the custom branded dialog).** Closes Linear DUT-65. **The request (Spencer, 2026-06-12).** After CL-154 rebuilt the confirmation as a custom branded dialog (to get white buttons + fix a flip race), Spencer decided he doesn't want a confirmation popup at all on the iCloud Sync toggle — just flip it directly. **Why it's safe to remove.** The per-toggle confirmation was a self-imposed UX choice (CL-89 "no silent opt-in"), NOT an App Store / privacy requirement. Toggling is non-destructive in both directions: enabling starts syncing saved recipes to the user's OWN iCloud private DB; disabling calls `RecipeStore.recreateContainerAfterOptInChange` which switches the SAME local store from CloudKit-mirrored to local-only (`makeProductionConfiguration` → `syncedSavedConfiguration(cloudKit: false)` → `.none`) — saved recipes stay on the device, they just stop syncing (no data deleted). The flip is also deferred to the next cold launch ("Relaunch DOD to apply"), so nothing happens immediately. And the one-time AC-41.2 first-launch opt-in sheet (`CloudKitOptInSheet`) remains the informed-consent moment — only the redundant per-toggle popup is removed. **The change.** The toggle binding flips directly via a new `SettingsViewModel.setCloudSyncEnabled(_ enabled:) -> Task<Void, Never>?`: it no-ops on an unchanged value, else flips `isCloudSyncEnabled` synchronously (so the `Toggle` stays in its new position), sets `cloudSyncPendingRelaunch`, and persists the canonical opt-in flag via the dependency (returning the persistence `Task` so tests can await it; production discards it via `@discardableResult`). Removed: the `CloudSyncConfirmationDialog` + `CloudSyncConfirmationDialogModifier` views + the `cloudSyncConfirmationAlert(viewModel:)` modifier (T-757); the `requestCloudSyncOptIn` / `confirmCloudSyncFlip` / `cancelCloudSyncFlip` methods + the `cloudSyncConfirmationRequest` `@Observable` property + the `CloudSyncConfirmationRequest` value type. **What this does NOT change.** The toggle row + Status row + relaunch-pending sublabel + identifiers (`settings-toggle-icloud-sync` / `-status`); the dependency write (`setCloudSyncOptIn`) + the T-702 container-rebuild seam; `refreshCloudSyncStatus`; the AC-41.2 first-launch opt-in sheet (separate code path — unaffected). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Keep a confirmation for the OFF direction only (in case disabling is destructive).** Rejected — verified disabling is non-destructive (saves stay local); a one-sided confirmation is inconsistent + the user asked for none. **(b) Keep the machinery dormant (just stop presenting the dialog).** Rejected — dead code rots; a clean removal is clearer, and the first-launch sheet covers consent. **Tests.** The cloud-sync L1 tests are retargeted to drive `setCloudSyncEnabled` directly (`togglingOnCallsRecreateContainerAfterFlagWrite`, `togglingOffCallsRecreateContainerAfterFlagWrite`, `flippingSyncMarksRelaunchPendingInSublabel`, the two `SettingsViewModelCloudSyncStatusTests` cases). The obsolete `confirmationFlowCanBeCancelled` is replaced by `settingSyncToTheCurrentValueIsANoOp` (pins the no-op-on-unchanged-value contract). The L4 `test_settings_iCloudSyncSection_alertVisible_light` snapshot (which captured the pending-confirmation surface) is removed along with `makeHostedView`'s `pendingFlip` param. All 81 feed tests pass. **Numbering note.** CL-156 / T-759 is the next free slot above CL-155. The `e2e` label is NOT applied — the direct flip is unit-pinned + manually verified on device. **Spec/trace:** US-41 AC-41.3 amended (the iCloud Sync toggle flips directly with no per-toggle confirmation; the first-launch opt-in sheet remains the consent moment). Implementing PR: T-759 (`fix/T-759-remove-icloud-sync-confirmation`). Estimate: ~15 min investigation + ~25 min removal + ~10 min verify ≈ 50 min total.
- **CL-155 (US-36 amendment / T-758, 2026-06-12)** — **Two small Settings layout tweaks (DUT-64): (1) move "Clear Cached Recipe Images" below "Share Anonymous Usage Data" in the Data & Privacy section, and (2) add a line break to the "Recipe Step Temperatures" picker label so its value sits to the right instead of wrapping below.** Closes Linear DUT-64. Bounded to `SettingsView.swift`. **(1)** The Data & Privacy section order becomes iCloud Sync → Share Anonymous Usage Data → Clear Cached Recipe Images (the Clear Cache button moved below the telemetry toggle — Spencer's preferred order). The combined section footer is unchanged (it covers all three rows regardless of order). **(2)** The temperature picker (`.menu` style in a `Form`) renders its selected value to the right of the label only when the label is short enough; "Recipe Step Temperatures" (24 chars) + "Recipe Default" (14 chars) overflowed the row width, so SwiftUI stacked the value below the label. Adding an explicit `\n` ("Recipe Step\nTemperatures") makes the label a two-line, narrower column so the value sits to its right (the standard iOS picker-row layout). The App Appearance picker didn't need this (its label is short enough). **What this does NOT change.** The temperature preference values / persistence / observability (T-756); the telemetry + clear-cache behavior + identifiers; the Measurements & Units / Data & Privacy headers + footers. No `e2e` label — pure layout. **Spec/trace:** US-36 AC-36.4 / AC-36.5 row order + DUT-47 picker layout. Implementing PR: T-758 (`fix/T-758-settings-layout-tweaks`).
- **CL-154 (US-41 amendment / T-757, 2026-06-12)** — **Fix two iCloud Sync confirmation bugs (DUT-63): (1) the dialog's buttons rendered orange (need white), and (2) the toggle never actually flipped ON. Root cause for both: the confirmation was a system `.alert`. Fix: replace it with a custom branded confirmation dialog.** Closes Linear DUT-63. Bounded to `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/SettingsView+CloudSync.swift` (view only — the `SettingsViewModel` confirm/cancel/request flow is unchanged). **Bug 1 — orange buttons.** The app applies `.tint(DODColor.accent)` (brand orange) at `RootView`; system `.alert` buttons inherit the tint, and Apple exposes no per-button foreground override, so white button text is impossible while it stays a system alert. **Bug 2 — the toggle never flips (a SwiftUI alert race).** The toggle binding routes a flip through `requestCloudSyncOptIn(_:)`, which stashes a `cloudSyncConfirmationRequest` (the toggle springs back to its prior state, waiting for confirmation). The system `.alert`'s primary button ran `Task { await viewModel.confirmCloudSyncFlip() }` (async, deferred to a later tick). But tapping any alert button ALSO dismisses the alert, which drives the alert's `isPresented` binding to `false` **synchronously** — and that binding's `set` called `cancelCloudSyncFlip()`, which reverts `isCloudSyncEnabled = false` and clears `cloudSyncConfirmationRequest = nil`. So the synchronous cancel-on-dismiss ran BEFORE the async confirm task, and confirm then hit `guard let request = cloudSyncConfirmationRequest else { return }` → nil → no-op. Net: tapping "Turn On" reverted instead of confirming. (The view-model itself was always correct — the L1 `togglingOnCallsRecreateContainerAfterFlagWrite` test, which calls `requestCloudSyncOptIn` then `confirmCloudSyncFlip` directly, passed before + after this fix. The bug lived entirely in the view's alert wiring.) **The fix — a custom branded dialog.** The system `.alert` (+ its `isPresented`-binding cancel) is replaced with a `CloudSyncConfirmationDialog` view presented via an `.overlay` driven PURELY by `viewModel.cloudSyncConfirmationRequest != nil`. There is no `isPresented` binding, so there is no synchronous cancel-on-dismiss to race the confirm — the only state mutations are the explicit button actions (`confirmCloudSyncFlip` on the primary, `cancelCloudSyncFlip` on Cancel + on backdrop tap). The dialog is a dimmed backdrop + a centered `DODColor.surface` card (matching the `RatingsProfileGate` brand pattern): direction-aware title ("Turn on/off iCloud Sync?") + body copy + a filled `DODColor.castIronBrown` primary button with `DODColor.cream` (white) text ("Turn On" / "Turn Off" — fixes the orange-button complaint) + a plain `DODColor.label` "Cancel". Tapping the backdrop cancels. A `.transition(.opacity)` + `.animation(value: cloudSyncConfirmationRequest)` fades it in/out. **What this does NOT change.** `SettingsViewModel.requestCloudSyncOptIn` / `confirmCloudSyncFlip` / `cancelCloudSyncFlip` / `cloudSyncConfirmationRequest` (all unchanged — every L1 cloud-sync test passes untouched); the toggle binding; the Status row + relaunch-pending sublabel; the CL-89 "confirm every flip" contract (still honored — the dialog is the confirmation, just branded). The button accessibility identifiers (`settings-icloud-sync-alert-confirm` / `-alert-cancel`) are preserved. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Keep the system `.alert`, override the button tint to white.** Rejected — system alert buttons take no per-button color, and a blanket white tint would be invisible on the light alert background in light mode. **(b) Keep the system `.alert`, fix only the race (drop the `isPresented`-set cancel + make confirm synchronous).** Would fix Bug 2 but not Bug 1 (buttons still orange); the orange-button requirement forces leaving the system alert anyway, and the custom dialog fixes both at once. **(c) `.confirmationDialog` (action sheet).** Rejected — its buttons are ALSO tint-colored (same orange problem) and it's a bottom sheet, not the centered confirmation the flow wants. **(d) Make the destructive "Turn Off" button red.** Rejected — turning sync off is not destructive (data already on iCloud stays per AC-41.5); the brand-filled primary + the explanatory copy convey the action without a scary red. **Tests.** No new unit test — the VM flow was already pinned by the existing `togglingOnCallsRecreateContainerAfterFlagWrite` / `togglingOffCallsRecreateContainerAfterFlagWrite` / `confirmationFlowCanBeCancelled` tests (all still pass), and the fix is a pure view-presentation swap (the race lived in the deleted `.alert` `isPresented` wiring, not in any unit-testable VM path). The L4 `test_settings_iCloudSyncSection_alertVisible_light` snapshot now captures the custom dialog (it's in the SwiftUI tree, unlike a system alert) and regenerates its `record: .missing` baseline. **Numbering note.** CL-154 / T-757 is the next free slot above CL-153. The `e2e` label is NOT applied — the toggle flip is unit-pinned by the existing VM tests + manually verified on device. **Spec/trace:** US-41 AC-41.3 amended (the confirmation is a custom branded dialog driven by `cloudSyncConfirmationRequest`, superseding CL-89's system-`.alert` presentation). Implementing PR: T-757 (`fix/T-757-icloud-sync-confirm-dialog`). Estimate: ~25 min investigation + ~25 min impl + ~10 min verify ≈ 60 min total.
- **CL-153 (US-36 amendment / T-756, 2026-06-12)** — **Fix two App Appearance bugs (DUT-62): (1) the picker's selected-value label didn't update on change, and (2) the Settings sheet didn't re-theme live (only on close + reopen). Root cause: the picker-backed preferences were computed-over-store (not `@Observable`-trackable), and the Settings `.sheet` doesn't inherit `RootView`'s live `preferredColorScheme`.** Closes Linear DUT-62. **Bug 1 — non-observable preferences.** `SettingsViewModel.appearance` (+ `temperaturePreference` + `voiceGender`) were computed properties over `UserDefaults` / `VoicePreferenceStore`. SwiftUI's `@Observable` macro only instruments STORED properties; a computed property reading an external store emits no observation change on mutation, so setting it never re-rendered the `Picker` → the collapsed selected-value label stayed stale. (The same latent bug affected the Recipe Step Temperatures picker + the Cook Mode Voice picker — fixed in the same pass since they share the exact pattern.) **Fix:** convert all three to `@Observable` STORED properties — a stored `var` with a `didSet` that persists to the backing store, seeded from the store in `init`. `init` assigns each once (Swift property observers don't fire for in-init initial assignments, so no redundant write); subsequent picker mutations fire `didSet` (persist) + emit an observation change (re-render). `temperaturePreference` moved from the `SettingsViewModel+Temperature.swift` extension into the primary class declaration because stored properties can't live in an extension (the `temperaturePreferenceKey` static stays in the extension). The persistence contract is unchanged — `didSet` writes the same `UserDefaults` key the old setter did, and `init` seeds the read-back — so every existing round-trip test passes untouched. `shareFormat` stays computed-over-defaults: T-750 removed its UI picker, so nothing reads it reactively and it doesn't need the treatment. **Bug 2 — sheet doesn't follow `preferredColorScheme`.** Settings is presented as a `.sheet` (`App/SettingsToolbar.swift`). `preferredColorScheme` applied on `RootView` (via its `@AppStorage(appearancePreferenceKey)`) re-themes the main window live, but iOS does NOT propagate a presenting view's `preferredColorScheme` into an already-presented sheet — the sheet captures the scheme at present time and only re-captures on re-present, which is exactly "only switches on close + reopen." **Fix:** give the Settings sheet its OWN live color scheme by applying `.preferredColorScheme(viewModel.appearance.colorScheme)` directly to `SettingsView`'s content. Now that `appearance` is observable (Bug 1 fix), changing the picker re-renders `SettingsView` and updates the sheet's `preferredColorScheme` in the same frame → the sheet re-themes live. A new `AppearancePreference.colorScheme: ColorScheme?` helper (`.system` → `nil`, `.light` → `.light`, `.dark` → `.dark`) is the single source of truth, reused by both `RootView` (main window) and `SettingsView` (sheet) so they map identically. **Why both fixes are needed.** Bug 1 (observability) is necessary for the picker label AND to drive the sheet's live `preferredColorScheme`.
