Bug 2 (sheet theme) is independent — even with RootView re-theming the main window, the sheet needs its own `preferredColorScheme` to follow live. **What this does NOT change.** The persisted `UserDefaults` keys + wire format; `RootView`'s main-window theming (still `@AppStorage`-driven, now delegating to `appearance.colorScheme`); Recipe Detail's `@AppStorage` read of `temperaturePreference`; `SystemSpeechSynthesizer`'s read of the voice store; the picker bindings in `SettingsView` (get/set still work, now over an observable property); every round-trip test. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Apply the sheet's `preferredColorScheme` in `SettingsToolbar` (the presenter) via its own `@AppStorage`.** Workable (mirrors RootView), but the VM is recreated each access there, and driving the sheet theme from the SAME observable `appearance` the picker mutates (inside `SettingsView`) is tighter + guaranteed in-sync. **(b) Keep the computed properties + force re-render with a manual observation token (a stored `changeCount` the setter bumps).** Rejected — hacky; the value itself should be observable, which a stored property gives cleanly. **(c) Fix only `appearance` (the reported picker), leave temperature + voice.** Rejected — they share the identical root cause and sit in the same Customization / Measurements sections; shipping a half-fix invites immediate follow-up reports. **(d) Make the toggles (`useMetricUnits` / `notificationsEnabled` / `telemetryEnabled`) observable too.** Deferred — toggles mask the bug via their own interactive state, weren't reported, and converting them is out of scope for this fix (a future consistency pass can finish the set). **Tests.** New L1 `SettingsViewModelObservationTests` — `withObservationTracking` registers a read of each picker property, then a mutation must fire `onChange`; these FAIL on the old computed code (no observable access registered) and PASS once stored, so they're true regression pins. Plus a persistence-preservation test (set on one VM, read back on a fresh VM). All existing `SettingsViewModelTests` / `SettingsTemperaturePreferenceTests` / `SettingsViewModelVoiceTests` round-trips pass unchanged. **Numbering note.** CL-153 / T-756 is the next free slot above CL-152. The `e2e` label is NOT applied — the fix is unit-pinned by the observation tests + manually verified on device; no new end-to-end journey. **Spec/trace:** US-36 AC-36.2 amended (the appearance preference is observable; the Settings sheet carries its own live `preferredColorScheme`). Implementing PR: T-756 (`fix/T-756-appearance-observable-live-theme`). Estimate: ~20 min investigation + ~30 min impl + ~15 min verify ≈ 65 min total.
- **CL-152 (US-36 amendment / T-755, 2026-06-12)** — **Rename the Settings → Customization "Appearance" picker row label to "App Appearance" (DUT-61).** Closes Linear DUT-61. One-string change in `SettingsView.swift` — the `settings-picker-appearance` identifier + the option values ("Match System" / "Light" / "Dark") + the persistence are unchanged; only the visible row label gains the "App" qualifier for clarity. No `e2e` label. **Spec/trace:** US-36 AC-36.2 row label.
- **CL-151 (US-44 amendment / T-754, 2026-06-12)** — **Enlarge the centered profile-photo header from 88pt → 120pt (DUT-60).** Closes Linear DUT-60. One-constant follow-up to CL-150 — Spencer reported the 88pt header still left a lot of dead space at the top of the edit form, so `ProfileEditView.headerAvatarDiameter` bumps to 120 (Apple-ID-scale prominent avatar). Quality-safe: 120pt @3x = 360px, within the 512×512 saved JPG, so no upscaling. Everything else (the centered float-out-of-box layout, circle clip, caption-below, the picker/crop/edit/remove flows) is unchanged. The `e2e` label is NOT applied — a single presentation constant. **Spec/trace:** US-44 — refines the CL-150 header sizing. Implementing PR: T-754 (`feat/T-754-profile-photo-larger`).
- **CL-150 (US-44 amendment / T-753, 2026-06-11)** — **Profile edit photo header: move the profile picture out of the boxed "Profile Picture" row into a large, centered, circular, tappable header at the top of the form (above the display-name + email fields), with the tap-action caption directly below it, and update the caption to mention Edit alongside Replace + Remove.** Closes Linear DUT-59. Bounded to `Packages/DODFeatureProfile/Sources/DODFeatureProfile/{ProfileEditView,ProfileEditView+Photo}.swift`. **The request (Spencer, 2026-06-11).** Clean up the Profile edit page: take the photo out of its boxed row, make it bigger, move it to the top centered above the name/email fields, put the description right below it, mention that the user can now edit (not just replace/remove) the photo, and keep the circle shape. **The change.** `profileEditPhotoSection` is rewritten from a labeled `HStack` row (the text "Profile Picture" + a 44pt trailing avatar inside a `DODColor.surfaceElevated` cell, with the caption as the section footer) into a centered header `VStack`: a tappable 88pt circular ``ProfilePhotoView`` (Button → `handleProfilePictureRowTap`, identifier `profile-edit-photo` preserved) with the caption `Text` directly below, both centered via `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .center)`. The section uses `.listRowBackground(Color.clear)` + `.listRowSeparator(.hidden)` so the avatar + caption float on the page surface (`DODColor.surface`) instead of sitting in an elevated cell box — the Contacts / Apple ID avatar-header pattern. In `ProfileEditView`'s `Form`, `profileEditPhotoSection` moves to FIRST (above `identitySection`). **The caption.** State-dependent: populated → "Tap the photo to replace, edit, or remove it." (was "Tap the photo to replace or remove it." — now names the Edit Photo action that shipped in T-745 / CL-142, matching the action sheet's Replace / Edit / Remove options); empty → "Tap to add a profile picture." (unchanged); macOS slice → "Photo upload requires UIKit." (unchanged). The caption also feeds the avatar Button's VoiceOver `accessibilityHint` (with `accessibilityLabel("Profile Picture")`). **The circle.** Preserved for free — ``ProfilePhotoView`` clips both the loaded JPG (`.clipShape(Circle())`) and the ``InitialLetterAvatarView`` fallback to a circle, so bumping the `diameter` to 88 keeps the round shape (per AC-44.15). **What this does NOT change.** The `handleProfilePictureRowTap` direct-picker gate (T-745 / CL-142 / DUT-39 — no photo → picker, photo → action sheet); the picker / crop / Edit / Remove flows; the photo store; the dirty-state tracking; the Save / Sign Out / Delete sections. Only the photo row's presentation (position, size, box chrome, caption copy) changes. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Render the header above the `Form` in an outer `VStack` (fixed, non-scrolling header).** Rejected — a fixed header that doesn't scroll with the form fights the natural scroll on small screens / large Dynamic Type; the first-section-with-clear-background approach scrolls naturally and is the iOS-idiomatic avatar-header pattern (Contacts edit, Apple ID). **(b) Keep the "Profile Picture" text label as a section header above the centered avatar.** Rejected — a large centered avatar with a tap caption is self-evident; a redundant "Profile Picture" label adds clutter the user explicitly asked to remove ("take the profile picture out of the box"). **(c) Bump the avatar to a very large size (120pt+).** Rejected — the user asked for "a bit bigger"; 88pt reads as a prominent header avatar (roughly Apple ID scale) without dominating the form. **Numbering note.** CL-150 / T-753 is the next free CL slot above CL-149 (T-752, DUT-58). The `e2e` label is NOT applied — a presentation-only restructure of an existing row with no behavior change; the regenerating L4 profile snapshots + the existing UIKit-gated `ProfileEditView` logic tests (dirty-state, photo-validation) cover it. **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendment — AC-44.3 photo-row layout (centered 88pt circular header above the identity fields, caption below mentioning replace / edit / remove). Implementing PR: T-753 (`feat/T-753-profile-photo-header`). Tests: existing `DODFeatureProfileTests` (44 macOS + the UIKit-gated view tests) unchanged + still pass; no new unit tests (presentation-only change). Estimate: ~5 min spec + ~10 min impl + ~10 min verify ≈ 25 min total.
- **CL-149 (US-32 / US-40 / US-41 amendment / T-752, 2026-06-11)** — **Settings layout, round 4 (DUT-58): combine Appearance + Cook Mode Voice into a "Customization" section; combine iCloud Sync + Clear Cached Recipe Images + Share Anonymous Usage Data into a "Data & Privacy" section (in that order, between Tools and About); reword the Heat Coach caption to be em-dash-free.** Closes Linear DUT-58. Touches `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/{SettingsView,SettingsView+Voice,SettingsView+CloudSync,SettingsView+Tools}.swift`. **The request (Spencer, 2026-06-11).** Continuing the T-750 / T-751 Settings work: group the remaining loose rows into two more titled sections, and clean up the Heat Coach caption. **(1) "Customization" section.** The Appearance picker (US-36 AC-36.2) + the Cook Mode Voice rows (US-40) — previously two separate sections (Appearance headerless, Voice with its own footer) — are now one `Section` headed "Customization" with the voice clarification ("The Cook Mode voice reads recipe steps aloud.") as the footer. **(2) "Data & Privacy" section.** iCloud Sync (US-41) + Clear Cached Recipe Images (US-36 AC-36.4) + Share Anonymous Usage Data (US-36 AC-36.5) — three previously separate single-row sections — are now one `Section` headed "Data & Privacy", in that exact order, positioned between the Tools (Heat Coach) section and About. The header "Data & Privacy" umbrellas all three: iCloud is data-sync, Clear Cache is cached-data cleanup, telemetry is usage-data privacy. The section footer is a three-sentence, em-dash-free note covering all three rows ("Saved recipes sync across your devices when iCloud Sync is on. Cached images stay on this device. Anonymous usage data never includes personal information."). **(3) Heat Coach caption.** Reworded from "A starting point for coals — then cook by feel." (em dash) to "Estimate how many coals you need for your target temperature, then cook by feel." (no em dash, more descriptive of what the tool does). **The structural mechanism — Section → rows refactors.** SwiftUI `Section`s cannot share a header, so to put two formerly-independent sections under one header their rows must live in a single `Section`. Two sub-views were refactored from rendering their own `Section` to providing loose rows (a `Group`): `VoiceSection` → `VoiceRows` (the gender picker + quality readout + Preview button + download nudge; the footer + `.listRowBackground` + the `.onDisappear { stopVoicePreview() }` move to / stay reachable from the parent), and `CloudSyncSection` → `CloudSyncRows` (the iCloud toggle + the conditional Status row; the state-description footer folds into the Data & Privacy section footer, the state-dependent `accessibilityHint` stays on the toggle for VoiceOver). The parent `SettingsView` composes Appearance + `VoiceRows` into the Customization `Section` and `CloudSyncRows` + Clear Cache button + telemetry toggle into the Data & Privacy `Section`, each applying one `.listRowBackground(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` to the whole section. The `.cloudSyncConfirmationAlert(viewModel:)` modifier + the `.task { refreshCloudSyncStatus() }` stay on the host `SettingsView` body (unchanged — they were never part of `CloudSyncSection`). **The iCloud state-description handling.** Pre-T-752 the iCloud state description ("Saved recipes stay on this device." / the across-devices copy) was the iCloud section's own footer (T-750 / CL-147). Now that iCloud shares a section with two other rows, it can't have its own footer; the state-neutral essence ("Saved recipes sync across your devices when iCloud Sync is on.") folds into the combined Data & Privacy footer. The on/off state is still conveyed by the toggle position + the Status row (when on) + the retained VoiceOver `accessibilityHint`. This is consistent with the T-750 intent (descriptions belong in the footer, not the row). **New section order (top → bottom):** Profile → Measurements & Units → Notification Settings → **Customization** → Tools → **Data & Privacy** → About → version footer. **What this does NOT change.** The voice gender picker / quality readout / Preview button / download nudge behavior + identifiers (US-40 — only the surrounding Section wrapper changed). The iCloud toggle binding through `requestCloudSyncOptIn` + the confirmation alert (CL-89) + the Status row + the toggle identifier (`settings-toggle-icloud-sync`). The Clear Cache button identifier (`settings-button-clear-cache`) + the telemetry toggle identifier (`settings-toggle-telemetry`) + their behavior. The Measurements & Units + Notification Settings groups (T-750). The `sectionHeader(_:)` `heading` + `label` styling (T-751). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Keep the iCloud state description as an inline subtitle under the toggle (revert T-750's footer move for iCloud only).** Rejected — mixing an inline-subtitle row (iCloud) with footer-described rows (cache, telemetry) in the same section is inconsistent; folding all the descriptions into the single section footer is cleaner and honors the T-750 "descriptions in the footer" intent. **(b) A "Data & Storage" or "Privacy" header instead of "Data & Privacy".** Rejected — "Data & Storage" undersells the telemetry row's privacy framing; "Privacy" alone undersells the sync + cache (data/storage) rows. "Data & Privacy" is the tightest umbrella for all three. **(c) Keep the three rows as separate sections but visually tighten the spacing.** Rejected — the explicit ask was "grouped up into one section"; SwiftUI inset-grouped sections each render their own card, so separate sections can't read as one group. **(d) Per-row footers via nested structure.** Rejected — SwiftUI gives one footer per `Section`; there's no per-row footer primitive, and faking it with inline captions reintroduces the inconsistency from (a). **The implementation order.** Commit 1: spec amendments (this file CL-149 + spec.md AC-41.3 note + tasks.md T-752 entry). Commit 2: the `VoiceSection`→`VoiceRows` + `CloudSyncSection`→`CloudSyncRows` refactors + the two new combined sections in `SettingsView` + the Heat Coach caption reword + the stale-doc-comment cleanup. **Numbering note.** CL-149 / T-752 is the next free CL slot above CL-148 (T-751, DUT-57). The `e2e` label is NOT applied — a layout regroup of existing rows with no new behavior; the regenerating L4 Settings snapshots + the existing L1 voice / iCloud tests (which go through the full `SettingsView`) cover it. **Spec/trace:** US-32 / US-40 / US-41 — AC-41.3 amended (iCloud Sync rows now live in the shared "Data & Privacy" section, no own footer). Implementing PR: T-752 (`feat/T-752-settings-groups-customization-data-privacy`). Tests: existing `SettingsViewModel*Tests` (77 feed tests) unchanged + still pass; L4 `SettingsViewSnapshotTests` regenerate via the full-`SettingsView` host. No new unit tests (pure view-composition change). Estimate: ~15 min spec + ~40 min impl + ~15 min verify ≈ 70 min total.
- **CL-148 (US-32 amendment / T-751, 2026-06-11)** — **Settings section subheaders ("Measurements & Units", "Notification Settings", "Tools") bumped to `DODType.heading` (17pt semibold) + primary `DODColor.label` so they read as distinctly larger + bolder than the section footer descriptions (which stay `DODType.caption` 12pt medium + `DODColor.labelSecondary`).** Closes Linear DUT-57. Refines the T-750 / CL-147 header treatment. Bounded to `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/{SettingsView,SettingsView+Tools}.swift` (2 style swaps). **The problem (Spencer, 2026-06-11).** T-750 / CL-147 styled the new grouped-section headers with `DODType.caption` + `DODColor.labelSecondary` — the SAME tokens the section footers use — so a section subheader was visually indistinguishable from a section description. Spencer asked for "a clean distinction between description and section subheadings," with the subheaders "larger and bolder." **The fix.** Headers move to `DODType.heading` (`.headline` — 17pt semibold) + `DODColor.label` (primary). Footers are unchanged (`DODType.caption` — 12pt medium — + `DODColor.labelSecondary`). The result is a three-axis distinction — size (17 vs 12pt), weight (semibold vs medium), and color (primary vs secondary) — so the hierarchy reads cleanly at a glance. Applied at both header sites: the `sectionHeader(_:)` helper in `SettingsView` (Measurements & Units + Notification Settings) and the inline "Tools" header in `SettingsView+Tools.swift`. The iCloud Sync / Appearance / Profile / etc. headerless single-row sections are unaffected (they have no header to restyle). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) `DODType.displayMedium` (22pt bold) for headers.** Rejected — 22pt bold is oversized for an inset-grouped section header (it competes with the nav-bar title); `heading` (17pt semibold) is the right "section title within a list" weight, matching the app's `RecipeCard` title register. **(b) Keep `caption` but bold it + darken to primary.** Rejected — same size as the footer means the distinction rests on weight + color alone, which is subtler than Spencer asked for ("larger AND bolder"); bumping the size is the clearer signal. **(c) Uppercase the headers (iOS-stock grouped-list style).** Rejected — the app's Settings headers are already custom-styled brand `Text` (not the system uppercased grey, per CL-147 / the ToolsSection precedent); uppercasing would fight the Title Case the same revamp just standardized. **Numbering note.** CL-148 / T-751 is the next free CL slot above CL-147 (T-750, DUT-56). The `e2e` label is NOT applied — a pure type-token swap on static headers; the regenerating L4 Settings snapshots cover it. **Spec/trace:** US-32 (Settings) — no numbered AC governs the section-header font tokens, so this CL is the canonical record of the subheader (`heading` + `label`) vs footer-description (`caption` + `labelSecondary`) treatment, refining the T-750 / CL-147 header styling. Implementing PR: T-751 (`feat/T-751-settings-header-hierarchy`). No new unit tests (pure view-token change with no branching to cover; L4 snapshots regenerate). Estimate: ~5 min spec + ~5 min impl + ~5 min verify ≈ 15 min total.
- **CL-147 (US-32 / US-36 / US-41 amendment / T-750, 2026-06-11)** — **Settings page revamp (DUT-56): regroup scattered rows into titled sections, Title Case every row label + section header, clean up the iCloud Sync section, remove the Default Share Format UI row, and add a "When Someone Replies to My Comment" notification toggle alongside the renamed recipe-drop toggle in a new "Notification Settings" group.** Closes Linear DUT-56. Touches `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/{SettingsView,SettingsView+CloudSync,SettingsView+Voice,SettingsViewModel,SettingsViewModel+Notifications (new),SettingsPreferences}.swift` + tests + `UITests/SmokeTests.swift`. **The request (Spencer, 2026-06-11).** The Settings page accumulated rows over many tasks (US-32 skeleton → US-36 expansion → US-40 voice → US-41 iCloud → US-44 profile → DUT-47 temperature → DUT-48 tools) and the rows "feel like some of the things are just being thrown all over." Spencer asked for specific grouped sections, consistent Title Case, and several targeted cleanups. **The changes.** **(1) "Measurements & Units" group.** The "Use Metric Units" toggle (US-32 AC-32.4) and the "Recipe Step Temperatures" picker (DUT-47) were two separate, non-adjacent single-row sections; they're now grouped into one `Section` headed "Measurements & Units" with the temperature-conversion note as the shared footer. **(2) "Notification Settings" group.** The recipe-drop toggle (renamed "Notify me when new recipes drop" → **"When New Recipes Drop"**) + a **new "When Someone Replies to My Comment"** toggle grouped under one header. The recipe-drop key (`dod.settings.notificationsEnabled`) + identifier (`settings-toggle-notifications`) are unchanged so the persisted preference survives the rename. The reply toggle (key `dod.settings.commentReplyNotificationsEnabledV1`, identifier `settings-toggle-comment-reply-notifications`) is a persisted preference that requests notification authorization on enable, exactly mirroring `setNotificationsEnabled` (`setCommentReplyNotificationsEnabled` — persist on grant, revert + snackbar on deny, OFF never calls auth). **NOTE: comment-reply alert DELIVERY needs a server-side push trigger that doesn't exist in v1** (the same backend gap DUT-15 covers for new-post pushes); T-750 ships the UI + the stored preference + the secured permission so the future delivery path needs no second permission round-trip. **(3) iCloud Sync cleanup.** Dropped the redundant `Section("iCloud Sync")` header (the toggle row already self-labels "iCloud Sync" — headers are for multi-row groups, not self-describing single rows; the same principle leaves Profile / Appearance / Clear Cache / Telemetry / About headerless), and moved the state description ("Saved recipes stay on this device." / the across-devices copy) out of the toggle row's inner `VStack` into the section footer (the standard place for a descriptive caption). **(4) Removed the Default Share Format UI row** (AC-36.3) — it read as random/unnecessary and the `linkAndText` payload was never wired to a consumer. The `ShareFormatPreference` type + `shareFormat` property + `dod.settings.shareFormat` key + the L1 round-trip tests are **retained** (no consumer removed) so a future link+text share feature can re-surface the control. **(5) Title Case everywhere.** All row labels + section headers go Title Case: "Use metric units" → "Use Metric Units", "Recipe step temperatures" → "Recipe Step Temperatures", "Share anonymous usage data" → "Share Anonymous Usage Data", "Cook Mode voice" → "Cook Mode Voice", "Voice quality" → "Voice Quality", "Preview voice" → "Preview Voice", and the picker option values "Recipe default" → "Recipe Default" + "No preference" → "No Preference". **Footers stay sentence case** — they're prose descriptions, not labels; Title-casing a full-sentence caption would read wrong. **New section order (top → bottom):** Profile (no header) → Measurements & Units → Notification Settings → Appearance (no header) → Cook Mode Voice → Clear Cached Recipe Images → Share Anonymous Usage Data → iCloud Sync (no header) → Tools (Heat Coach) → About → version footer. This pulls the two new grouped sections + Appearance up near the top (user-facing prefs first, like iOS Settings) and keeps Voice→Cache→Telemetry→iCloud→Tools→About in their established relative order. **Section header styling.** New headers use a custom `Text(...).dodFont(DODType.caption).foregroundStyle(DODColor.labelSecondary)` (via a `sectionHeader(_:)` helper) rather than the `Section("…")` string form — this matches the established `ToolsSection` header treatment (brand font + color instead of the system's uppercased grey), so the Title-Cased headers render as authored rather than being force-uppercased by the system. **File_length management.** Two splits were needed to stay under the SwiftLint 400-line cap: the two notification setters moved from `SettingsViewModel.swift` into a new `SettingsViewModel+Notifications.swift` (mirroring the `+CloudSync` / `+Voice` / `+Temperature` splits — `requestNotificationAuthorization` widened `private`→`internal` + `snackbarMessage` widened `private(set)`→`internal(set)` so the sibling-file extension reaches them), and the new reply-toggle L1 tests moved into a new `SettingsViewModelNotificationsTests.swift`. **What this does NOT change.** The iCloud confirmation-alert flow (CL-89 — unchanged), the voice nudge / quality readout (US-40 — only labels Title-Cased), the Profile row (US-44 — unchanged), the Tools / Heat Coach section (DUT-48 — unchanged), the version footer, the brand `surfaceElevated` row backgrounds (T-647), the cache-clear snackbar. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Give every section a header (including Profile, Appearance, iCloud).** Rejected — headers on self-describing single rows are visual noise + redundant (exactly the iCloud "iCloud Sync header over an iCloud Sync row" problem Spencer flagged). Headers are reserved for multi-row groups. **(b) Make the reply toggle a pure persisted Bool with no auth request.** Rejected — every notification toggle turning ON should ensure permission so the future delivery path works; iOS only prompts once, so a second toggle requesting auth is harmless (returns the existing status). Mirroring `setNotificationsEnabled` keeps the two toggles' behavior consistent. **(c) Fully delete `ShareFormatPreference` + the `shareFormat` property + key.** Rejected — more churn (the L1 round-trip tests would have to go too) for a preference that was always planned to gain a consumer (the link+text share). Removing just the UI row is the minimal, reversible change. **(d) Keep the iCloud description inside the toggle row but shrink it.** Rejected — the description is a state caption, and SwiftUI section footers are the idiomatic home for state captions (every other descriptive Settings caption is already a footer). **(e) Title Case the footer descriptions too.** Rejected — footers are full-sentence prose; Title-casing a sentence ("Converts Temperatures Shown In The Steps…") reads as broken. Only labels + headers (noun phrases) are Title-Cased. **The implementation order.** Commit 1: spec amendments (this file CL-147 + spec.md AC-36.1 / AC-36.3 / AC-41.3 amendments + tasks.md T-750 entry). Commit 2: the view reorg + VM additions + the two file splits + tests + UITest label update. **Numbering note.** CL-147 / T-750 is the next free CL slot above CL-146 (T-749, DUT-55). The `e2e` label is NOT applied — the page is static content + layout + a persisted preference toggle with no deliverable end-to-end behavior yet (the reply delivery is backend-gated); the L1 preference tests + the regenerating L4 Settings snapshots + the updated L3/L5 smoke (toggle-label) cover it. **Spec/trace:** US-32 / US-36 / US-41 amendment — AC-36.1 (rename + new reply toggle + Notification Settings group), AC-36.3 (share-format UI row removed, preference retained), AC-41.3 (no header + footer description). Implementing PR: T-750 (`feat/T-750-settings-revamp`). Tests: L1 `SettingsViewModelNotificationsTests` (reply preference default-off round-trip; grant persists ON; deny reverts + snackbar; OFF never calls auth); existing `SettingsViewModelTests` share-format + notifications round-trips unchanged + still pass; UITest label updated to "When New Recipes Drop". Estimate: ~15 min spec + ~50 min impl + ~15 min verify ≈ 80 min total.
- **CL-146 (US-32 amendment / T-749, 2026-06-11)** — **Two changes to the About Dutch Oven Daddy destination (`AboutNedView`): (a) the 160pt hero photo of Ned clips to a `Circle()` instead of a `RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: DODSpacing.sm)` (same size + centered position preserved); (b) three story paragraphs are appended below the existing intro paragraph, and the whole hero + copy stack is wrapped in a `ScrollView`.** Closes Linear DUT-55. Bounded entirely to `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/AboutNedView.swift` (1 source edit) + `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Tests/DODFeatureFeedTests/AboutNedViewTests.swift` (test additions). **The request (Spencer, 2026-06-11).** Two polish asks on the About page: circle-crop the hero photo (it matches the app's avatar register — every other photo-of-a-person surface in the app clips to `Circle()` per AC-44.15, so the About hero reading as a rounded square was the odd one out), and add Ned's fuller origin story below the one-line intro. **(a) Circle the hero.** `Image("AboutNed").resizable().scaledToFill().frame(width: 160, height: 160).clipShape(Circle())`. The frame is already square (160×160) so `Circle()` inscribes cleanly with no distortion; `.scaledToFill()` + the square frame mean the circle samples the center of the photo. The size + centered position (`.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .center)`) are unchanged per the explicit "keep the image right where it is" instruction. **(b) Three story paragraphs.** The intro (`aboutNedCopy`, unchanged + still pinned) is followed by a new `aboutNedStoryParagraphs: [String]` static carrying three verbatim paragraphs — Ned's origin story (the gifted skillet + post-surgery meal-prep that birthed D.O.D.), his growth in the cast-iron community + his educational focus, and his TV appearances + classes + love of cast iron's multi-generational durability. Each renders as its own `Text` via a shared `paragraphText(_:)` `@ViewBuilder` helper (the brand body register: `DODType.body` + `DODColor.label`, full-width leading-aligned, `.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)` so it grows vertically to fit wrapped content) inside a leading-aligned `VStack(spacing: DODSpacing.md)` so the paragraph breaks read as clean blank-line gaps. **(c) `ScrollView` wrap.** Pre-T-749 the view was a plain `VStack(alignment: .center).frame(maxWidth: .infinity, maxHeight: .infinity, alignment: .top)` with a trailing `Spacer(minLength: 0)`. With one short paragraph that fit every device; with the 160pt photo + four paragraphs (~one screen-and-a-half of copy at default Dynamic Type, more at accessibility sizes) it would clip the tail on shorter devices (iPhone SE) and at larger Dynamic Type sizes on every device. Wrapping the content `VStack` in a `ScrollView` makes the overflow scrollable; the `Spacer` is dropped (a `ScrollView`'s content sizes to fit, so the bottom-spacer-to-push-up trick is unnecessary). `.background(DODColor.surface)` moves from the inner `VStack`'s frame to the `ScrollView` so the brand surface still fills the viewport behind the scrolling content. **The `paragraphText(_:)` helper.** Extracted so the intro + each of the three story paragraphs share one modifier chain rather than repeating the four-modifier stack four times — keeps the body readable + guarantees the intro and the story paragraphs render identically (same font, color, alignment, wrapping). **Verbatim pinning.** Both `aboutNedCopy` (unchanged) and the new `aboutNedStoryParagraphs` are pinned by L1 tests (`aboutNedView_copy_matchesDUT14Verbatim` + the new `aboutNedView_storyParagraphs_matchVerbatim`) that hold a private copy of the expected strings as the source of truth — a copy-paste edit on the production constant can't accidentally pass; any drift forces a deliberate spec amendment + CL bump. A second new test (`aboutNedView_storyParagraphs_hasThreeInOrder`) pins the count + first/last ordering independently of the verbatim text so a dropped or reordered paragraph trips CI even if both verbatim constants are updated in lockstep. **Copy normalization.** Spencer's source text carried a couple of double-spaces (sentence-join typing artifacts — "using cast iron.  Dutch" and "classes.  I love"); these were normalized to single spaces since SwiftUI `Text` renders literal double-spaces as visible gaps and they're clearly unintended. The words are otherwise verbatim. **What this does NOT change.** The intro copy (`aboutNedCopy` byte-identical — the existing `aboutNedView_copy_matchesDUT14Verbatim` test still passes unedited). The `AboutNed` image asset (same bundled asset). The navigation title ("About") + inline display mode + `settings-about` accessibility identifier. The Settings → About `NavigationLink` call site (`SettingsView`). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Render all four paragraphs as one `Text` with `\n\n` separators.** Rejected — a single `Text` with embedded double-newlines works but couples the paragraph-break spacing to the literal string (harder to tune the gap independently of the line spacing) and makes the verbatim test assert one giant blob rather than per-paragraph strings (a single-character drift anywhere fails the whole assertion with a less useful diff). The `VStack` of per-paragraph `Text`s gives `DODSpacing.md` control over the inter-paragraph gap + cleaner per-paragraph test pins. **(b) Keep the intro in `aboutNedStoryParagraphs[0]` and drop the separate `aboutNedCopy` constant.** Rejected — would break the existing `aboutNedView_copy_matchesDUT14Verbatim` pin + the DUT-14 provenance of the intro copy. Keeping the intro as its own pinned constant preserves the T-738 / CL-134 contract. **(c) Use a `List` instead of a `ScrollView` + `VStack`.** Rejected — `List` imposes row insets, separators, and a grouped background that fight the centered-hero + flowing-prose layout; `ScrollView` + `VStack` is the right primitive for a read-only prose page. **(d) Cap the content with a fixed-height + internal scroll only when overflowing.** Rejected — SwiftUI has no clean "scroll only if needed" without measuring; an always-on `ScrollView` is simpler and behaves identically when the content fits (no scroll indicators, no bounce past the content) while gracefully handling overflow + large Dynamic Type. **The implementation order.** Commit 1: spec amendments (this file CL-146 + spec.md US-32 AC-32.6 amendment + tasks.md T-749 entry). Commit 2: `AboutNedView.swift` (circle clip + `aboutNedStoryParagraphs` static + `ScrollView` wrap + `paragraphText(_:)` helper) + `AboutNedViewTests.swift` (verbatim pin + count/order pin). **Numbering note.** CL-146 / T-749 is the next free CL slot above CL-145 (T-748, DUT-54). The `e2e` label is NOT applied — this is a static content + layout change with no new interaction surface; the existing L4 SettingsView About snapshots (`record: .missing`, regenerate on next pass) + the new L1 verbatim pins cover it. **Spec/trace:** US-32 amendment — AC-32.6 amended (circle hero clip + the three story paragraphs + `ScrollView` wrap; supersedes the single-paragraph + `RoundedRectangle` contract from T-738 / CL-134 + T-741 / CL-135). Implementing PR: T-749 (`feat/T-749-about-circle-photo-story-paragraphs`). Tests: L1 `aboutNedView_storyParagraphs_matchVerbatim` (the three paragraphs verbatim, in order); L1 `aboutNedView_storyParagraphs_hasThreeInOrder` (count + first/last prefix); existing `aboutNedView_copy_matchesDUT14Verbatim` + `aboutNedImageAsset_existsInBundle` + `aboutNedView_rendersWithoutCrash` unchanged + still pass. Estimate: ~10 min spec + ~10 min impl + ~10 min verify ≈ 30 min total.
- **CL-145 (US-44 amendment / T-748, 2026-06-07)** — **Units bug in `ProfilePhotoCropView/cropRect(for:offset:imageSize:cropSize:)` fixed by changing the helper signature to take screen-pt diameter + scaledToFit ratio, so screen-pt offset + crop circle diameter convert to source pixels via `displayedImageScaleFactor * scale`.** Closes Linear DUT-54. Bounded entirely to `Packages/DODFeatureProfile/Sources/DODFeatureProfile/ProfilePhotoCropView.swift` (1 source edit) + the matching test rewrite. **The symptom (Spencer, 2026-06-07 via Linear DUT-54).** "The profile picture cropping doesn't render after the profile pic is saved" — when the user crops a photo in `ProfilePhotoCropView` and taps Done, the saved profile picture shows the uncropped (or mostly-uncropped) photo rather than what was framed in the crop circle. **Root cause — two axes of units conflation in the pre-T-748 `cropRect` helper.** **Axis 1 (offset).** `DragGesture.translation` yields `CGSize` values in the view's screen-point coordinate space; `.offset(...)` applied to the image also operates in screen points. But the helper treated `offset` as source-image pixels: `translatedX = centerX - (offset.width / scale)`. For a 393pt-wide iPhone 17 Pro proxy showing a 3000px-wide source, the `.scaledToFit()` ratio is ~0.131 pt/px. A 100pt user drag should map to ~763 source pixels (`100 / 0.131`); the helper mapped it to 100. Pan gestures were ~7.6× too small relative to the source for typical phone-on-phone-photo configurations. **Axis 2 (cropSize).** The `cropSize` parameter was normalized as "fraction of source shorter side at scale 1.0". The call site passed `cropSize: 1.0` (a misuse — the parameter was meant to allow sub-1.0 fractions but no caller ever did). At `cropSize: 1.0` and `scale: 1.0` the helper returned `visibleSide = shorterSide`, i.e. the full inscribed square of the source. But the on-screen crop circle is `0.85 * min(proxy.width, proxy.height)` — and converting that screen diameter to source pixels via the scaledToFit ratio yields a value usually ~17% smaller than the source's shorter side (for portrait-source on portrait-screen). **Combined effect at zero pan / zero zoom (the default user interaction).** Saved crop = central inscribed square of the source ≈ ~83% of source visible inside the circle + ~17% extra source pixels OUTSIDE the visible circle bleed into the saved image. For a 3000×4000 portrait source: saved crop is 3000×3000 (just trimming 500px top + bottom); visible crop circle covers a 2549×2549 region. The user frames their face inside the circle, taps Done, and the saved photo has their face plus 17% of surrounding background — looks "uncropped" relative to what they intended. **The fix shape (post-T-748).** New helper signature `cropRect(for:offset:imageSize:cropDiameterInScreenPoints:displayedImageScaleFactor:)`. Internal math: `cropInSourcePixels = cropDiameterInScreenPoints / (displayedImageScaleFactor * scale)`; `visibleSide = min(cropInSourcePixels, shorterSourceSide)`; `offsetInSourcePixels = offset / (displayedImageScaleFactor * scale)`; centering + clamping math unchanged. The view captures `GeometryReader.proxy.size` into `@State` via `.onAppear { capturedProxySize = proxy.size }` + `.onChange(of: proxy.size) { _, new in capturedProxySize = new }` inside the GeometryReader closure (the closure has access to the proxy; the toolbar Done button outside the closure reads `capturedProxySize`). `renderCroppedImage()` computes `cropDiameterInScreenPoints` (`0.85 * min(capturedProxySize.width, capturedProxySize.height)`, the same formula the GeometryReader closure uses) + `displayedImageScaleFactor` (via the new `displayedImageScaleFactor(imageSize:proxySize:)` static helper) at crop time. **The companion helper `displayedImageScaleFactor(imageSize:proxySize:)`.** A pure static that returns `min(proxySize.width / imageSize.width, proxySize.height / imageSize.height)` (SwiftUI's `.scaledToFit()` picks the smaller per-axis ratio so the image fits entirely within the proxy on its limiting dimension). Returns 0 for degenerate inputs (zero-side image or proxy) so call sites can guard. Exposed `static` so the L1 test suite can pin the conversion without spinning up a view host (matches the `cropRect` pattern). **What this does NOT change.** The visual layout (the gestures, the dim overlay, the circle stroke, the proxy-sized image with `.scaledToFit().scaleEffect(scale).offset(offset)` — all unchanged). The on-disk format (still square 512×512 JPEG at 0.85 quality per `ProfilePhotoStore`). The clip-to-circle behavior at render time in `ProfilePhotoView` (CL-140 (2) — saved square is clipped to `Circle()` at every avatar render site). The Edit Photo / Replace / Remove flows in `ProfileEditView+Photo.swift`. The pinch-zoom 1.0..4.0 clamp via `clampScale(_:)`. The defensive guards for zero-side image + zero scale + new zero `displayedImageScaleFactor`. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Pass `cropSize: 0.85` instead of `cropSize: 1.0` as a one-character partial fix.** Rejected — would fix axis 2 (cropSize) but leaves axis 1 (offset) broken. The pan gestures would still be ~7.6× too small. Partial fixes are worse than full fixes because they ship a still-wrong product that LOOKS more correct, making the residual bug harder to diagnose. **(b) Compute `cropRect` inside the GeometryReader closure on every gesture change + capture the result into `@State`.** Rejected — would write to `@State` on every drag delta (chatty, re-renders the view), and races the Done tap (the user can tap Done before the last gesture's cropRect commits to state). The "compute at crop time using captured proxy size" approach is simpler + race-free. **(c) Refactor the crop view to a `UIViewRepresentable` wrapping a `CIImage` / `Core Image` pipeline.** Rejected — overkill for what is fundamentally a units bug in a pure-math helper. Constitution §3 default-no on new dependencies + the existing view already produces a 512×512 JPEG correctly when the math is right; the path forward is fixing the math, not replacing the rendering pipeline. **(d) Hardcode the conversion factor inside the helper based on a runtime measurement of `UIScreen.main.scale`.** Rejected — confuses screen scale (the `@1x`/`@2x`/`@3x` rendering density) with scaledToFit (the layout-time aspect-preserving fit ratio). These are unrelated; the helper needs scaledToFit which depends on `proxy.size` + `imageSize`, not on the screen's pixel density. **(e) Add a `cropSize: CGFloat = 1.0` default parameter to the new helper for backwards compat.** Rejected — the call site IS the helper's only caller (no other consumers). Adding a defaulted parameter to ease a migration that doesn't need easing is dead weight + a future-trap if a future caller passes the wrong value. Just change the signature. **The implementation order.** Commit 1: spec amendments (this file CL-145 + spec.md US-44 amendment to AC-44.3 + tasks.md T-748 entry). Commit 2: refactor `cropRect` to the new signature + add `displayedImageScaleFactor` companion helper + add `capturedProxySize` `@State` + wire `.onAppear` / `.onChange(of:)` on the GeometryReader closure + update `renderCroppedImage()` to read captured proxy + compute scaleFactor + call new helper + rewrite `ProfilePhotoCropMathTests` for the new signature with truth-table coverage of identity, zoom, pan-conversion, edge clamps, and defensive guards. **Numbering note.** CL-145 / T-748 is the next free CL slot above CL-144 (T-747, DUT-41). The `e2e` label IS applied — the saved crop visibly differs from what the user framed; the fix is end-to-end user-observable on the Profile Picture row's avatar. **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendment — AC-44.3 amended (the saved profile photo matches the contents of the visible crop circle; pan + zoom gestures move the saved crop in proportion to the screen-pt gesture amount; supersedes the `cropSize: 1.0` contract from T-740 / CL-137). Implementing PR: T-748 (`fix/T-748-crop-units-bug`). Tests: full rewrite of `ProfilePhotoCropMathTests` — identity centered crop, clamp-to-shorter-source when circle exceeds source, zoom shrinks the crop proportionally, max-scale samples the proportional region, screen-pt → source-px conversion at scale 1.0 + scale 2.0, large-pan clamps to source edges, defensive guards (zero image, zero scale, new zero scaleFactor). Estimate: ~15 min spec + ~15 min impl + ~15 min verify ≈ 45 min total.
- **CL-144 (US-44 amendment / T-747, 2026-06-03)** — **Delete the `Rectangle().fill(.ultraThinMaterial)` dim overlay from `RecipeDetailRatingsSection.gatedRateAndReviewCard`'s ZStack so the gate composes only the blurred-and-disabled composer + the popup card on top — no sibling Material layer reading as a grey frame around the popup in dark mode.** Closes Linear DUT-41. Bounded entirely to `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/{RecipeDetailRatingsSection,RatingsProfileGate}.swift` (2 source edits) + 1 stale L4 baseline deleted. **The bug shape (Spencer, 2026-06-03 via Linear DUT-41 with screenshot evidence).** After T-744 / CL-141 made the `RatingsProfileGate` popup card itself a solid `DODColor.surface` background, Spencer's dark-mode screenshot showed the popup card rendering correctly with its solid deep-brown fill — but a translucent warm-grey rounded-rectangle "frame" was still visible AROUND the popup card, sitting between the popup and the blurred composer beneath. The frame is NOT the popup card; it is the SIBLING `Rectangle().fill(.ultraThinMaterial)` view that the `gatedRateAndReviewCard` ZStack composes between the blurred composer (z-index 0) and the popup card (z-index 2). The Material's job was to dim the composer further — but in dark mode the `.ultraThinMaterial` reads as a translucent warm-grey wash whose perimeter is exactly what Spencer described as the "grey square". T-744 fixed the popup card; T-747 removes the popup's grey neighbour. **Why T-744 didn't catch this.** T-744's framing was "the popup card is reading grey; make the card opaque." That fix landed correctly (the card IS opaque now). But the grey Spencer sees is two layers down — it was always the Material doing the framing, not the card itself. The two-layer composition (Material under, card over) made it visually ambiguous which surface was the offender; T-744's hypothesis was "the card is partially transparent and the Material is bleeding through the card's corners," which would have been resolvable by hoisting the shadow out of the fill chain (which T-744 did, correctly improving the card's opacity guarantee). What T-744 missed is that the Material itself — when visible AROUND the card, not under it — was the user-visible frame even after the card became fully opaque. T-747 attacks the root: the Material is redundant, not just a compositing artifact. **The chosen fix (delete the Material entirely; keep the blur).** The composer's `.blur(radius: 10)` modifier (applied directly on the `rateAndReviewCard` view, not via a sibling) renders the composer's text + controls as an unreadable haze. That blur alone is sufficient to communicate "this is gated" — the user can see something IS there beneath the popup, can see it is unreadable, and can see the popup's CTA inviting them to set up a profile. The Material layer was redundant with the blur; dropping it loses no semantic information and gains a clean visual posture (no grey frame around the popup). The composer's `.disabled(!viewModel.hasProfile)` + `.accessibilityHidden(!viewModel.hasProfile)` gating semantics are unchanged — these are modifiers on the composer view itself, NOT on the deleted Material rectangle. **What this does NOT change.** The popup card's own background composition (the T-744 / CL-141 `.background(RoundedRectangle.fill(.surface)).shadow(...)` hoisting is unchanged — the popup card stays solid on its own merits, just now without a grey neighbour). The composer's blur + disabled + accessibilityHidden modifiers (unchanged — those are on the composer, not on the deleted Rectangle). The `RecipeDetailViewModel.hasProfile` derivation (unchanged). The `.sheet`-presentation + `refreshProfile()` `.onDisappear` path that swaps the gate for the live composer after profile save (unchanged). The `RatingsProfileGate` popup's icon + heading + body + CTA + accessibility identifiers (unchanged). The ZStack's `if viewModel.hasProfile` collapse path (unchanged — when the user has a profile, the ZStack still collapses to just the interactive composer, no popup, no blur, byte-identical to the pre-Phase-c shape). **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Swap `.ultraThinMaterial` for `.thinMaterial` / `.regularMaterial` / `.thickMaterial`.** Rejected — all four Material variants render with a similar grey-on-brown perimeter in dark mode; thicker materials are MORE grey, thinner ones less but still present. The complaint is the frame's existence, not its density. **(b) Tint the Material with `DODColor.surface` via `.background(DODColor.surface.opacity(0.X))` instead of `.fill(.ultraThinMaterial)`.** Rejected — would lose the blur-behind-the-overlay effect that Material provides for free; without `.fill(.ultraThinMaterial)` the rectangle becomes a flat-tinted overlay, NOT a Material. If we wanted that, we could just delete the rectangle (which is what we did) since the composer's own `.blur(radius: 10)` already provides the blur. **(c) Shrink the Material's frame to just the area inside the popup card's rounded-rectangle, so the Material can't show OUTSIDE the popup.** Rejected — the popup card is `maxWidth: 320` and `padding(.lg)` whereas the Material rectangle fills the ZStack's full bounds; matching the popup's exact frame from the Material's call site would couple the dim layer to the popup's layout decisions and require duplicating the rounded-rectangle path. Even if the Material were clipped to the popup's exact shape, it would still appear UNDER the popup card — which is already opaque after T-744 — so it would be invisible and could just as well not exist. **(d) Replace the Material with `Color.black.opacity(0.3)` for a non-translucent dim.** Rejected — would still create a perimeter (just a black one instead of grey), and would also blanket the entire composer area rather than letting the blur convey the gated state. The blur-only treatment is simpler and reads as more intentional. **(e) Lower the Material's opacity to near-invisible.** Rejected — at low opacity the Material no longer dims the composer (defeating its only purpose), so we'd be paying the compositing cost for no visible effect. Either it's visible (and reads as a grey frame) or it isn't (and shouldn't exist). **The implementation order.** Commit 1: spec amendments (this file CL-144 + spec.md US-44 amendment to AC-44.10 noting the Material removal + tasks.md T-747 entry). Commit 2: delete the `Rectangle().fill(.ultraThinMaterial).allowsHitTesting(false)` view from `RecipeDetailRatingsSection.gatedRateAndReviewCard` + amend the `gatedRateAndReviewCard` doc-comment + trim the `RatingsProfileGate` doc-comment paragraph that still framed the Material as the popup's load-bearing neighbour + delete the stale `test_section_gatedState_renders.1.png` baseline so the next test pass regenerates it. **Numbering note.** CL-144 / T-747 is the next free CL slot above CL-143 (T-746, DUT-40). The `e2e` label IS applied — the grey frame is visibly wrong on a real device in dark mode and is end-to-end user-observable. **Spec/trace:** US-44 amendment — AC-44.10 amended again (the gate composes the blurred + disabled composer + the popup card; no Material dim layer between them). Implementing PR: T-747 (`fix/T-747-remove-grey-material-overlay`). Tests: existing `RecipeDetailRatingsViewSnapshotTests.test_section_gatedState_renders` re-records its baseline (the snapshot test uses `record: .missing`, so the deleted baseline regenerates with the Material-free composition on the next test pass). No new unit tests — the change is a pure-view-graph deletion with no new branching to cover. Estimate: ~10 min spec + ~5 min impl + ~5 min verify ≈ 20 min total.
